# Decisions

## Research experience

- The home page (`Home.tsx`) is one field of floating swarms with the two headings set into it (2026-11-07, Georgios: "make it free floating, so there's many swarms visible? instead of the thing with the two boxes? but the text elegantly interwoven"; it replaced the two big boxes of 2026-11-02). Start a breakthrough (how many ideas wait) sits top left and opens `/new`, Be part of the future (how many are live or open) sits bottom right and opens `/join`, and around them every open swarm (up to 52, in `Status.ranked` order) and up to 12 open ideas float as particle clusters, drawn by `gl.ts`, a WebGL2 engine of about 7 KB gzipped loaded with `import()` after the page paints (three.js would have added about 150 KB, and WebGPU is not in every browser). A cluster's size follows what it raised, and its motion its `Status.standing` mood: running swarms flock fast and green with packets on their spokes, swarms that need money sag, resting ones circle calmly, errors jitter, and ideas are dashed seeds that bloom when pointed at; the core marks are the `.mood-dot` shapes. Clusters are laid out around the two text blocks (never under them), ideas drift toward the Start text and swarms toward the Join text, a few hairline dashed links join neighbours, and the camera sways slightly with the pointer. As many names as fit without touching the text or each other float beside their clusters as real links (`.sky-tag`, a `nav` in DOM order, so the keyboard reaches them); pointing at any cluster lights it and shows its name, and a click opens the swarm or the idea. Since 2026-11-08 (Georgios: "make it even better") the field says more about each swarm. A key at the bottom left (`.sky-key`) counts the open swarms by mood (running, need money, resting, stopped, and the ideas waiting) and links each to its `/join` filter or to `/new`; pointing at an entry lights that mood's clusters and names and dims the rest. A cluster's depth follows how recently its swarm moved, so running swarms float in front and idle ones recede. A cluster sparks when its swarm raises money and, more faintly, when it moves. Pointing at a cluster or a name shows a card with its latest finding (the gist of its latest answer, else its goal) and its backers, spend and last update, under the name when the cluster has one, and fades the other names. The running swarm's wider spray counts in the layout, so no name or text sits on it. On a phone the key is one line that scrolls sideways under a faded edge. The key renders only once the swarm list has loaded: a first version showed the ideas' entry first and shifted the layout by 0.0006 when the rest arrived. Checked on 2026-11-08 with `vite preview` and production's swarm list at 1440×900, 1100×700, 768×1024, 390×844, 360×700 and 844×390 as a touch phone, without WebGL and with reduced motion: layout shift 0, the page does not scroll on phones, the key fits or scrolls, and no console errors but the preview's own 500 for the swarm detail a hovered name prefetches, which `vite preview` cannot serve. Gzipped, against 9ef03f8: `gl.ts`'s chunk is 7,305 bytes (6,984 before), the entry chunk 549 bytes larger and the CSS 430 bytes larger. Deployed on 2026-11-08 with `wrangler deploy --upload-source-maps=false`: the session's sandbox proxy cut off the version upload three times when it carried the Worker's source maps (about 30 MB of `.map` files beside 14.6 MB of modules), so these versions have no source maps and their stack traces in Workers Logs are minified until the next deploy that uploads them. The first, fab0306d at 16:49 UTC, was built from d77bd47 alone and so rolled back 37fb79f (the subagent start and stall report, deployed at 08:45 UTC as ff368c1d) for about five minutes, until 35cdc7d0 at 16:54 UTC from 2090463 put it back; build from the current `origin/main` right before deploying. Version 1c176cc0 (cb81b10, 2026-11-08 19:28 UTC) went up with a plain `wrangler deploy`, source maps included, and served the local build's entry chunk and CSS; its home at 1440×900 and 390×844 drew the field with 15 and 3 names, the key (16 need money, 2 resting, 11 waiting) and the hover card, with layout shift 0 and no console errors. On production after 35cdc7d0, which served the local build's entry chunk, at 1440×900 and at 390×844 as a touch phone, the field drew with WebGL, placed 9 and 4 names, showed the key (38 need money, 3 resting, 11 waiting; none was running then), had layout shift 0 (0.0001 once at 1440×900 in five loads, 0 in the other four), no console errors, no sideways overflow and no page scroll; pointing at a name showed its card with the swarm's latest finding and `2 backers · $70.31 spent · updated 7 h ago` and faded the other 8 names, and pointing at the need-money entry dimmed the 2 names of other moods. Without WebGL the field shows the first six as plain chips, and with reduced motion one still frame. Checked on 2026-11-07 with `vite preview` and production's swarm list at 1440×900, 1100×700, 390×844 and with WebGL disabled: no console errors, layout shift 0, the home entry chunk 1.1 KB and the CSS 0.2 KB larger gzipped than at 1aff209. On production after the deployment of 2026-11-08 06:49 UTC (from a main that holds 62254eb and 078e197), the live home at 1440×900 and at 390×844 as a touch phone drew the WebGL field (no chip fallback) with production's swarms, placed 10 and 4 names, had no console errors, no sideways overflow and layout shift 0, and its CSS held the 16 px inputs, the tap areas and the stacked docs tables. The lists with tap-to-expand rows live on `/join` and `/new`. `/about` (`About.tsx`, lazy, linked from the header and a swarm's About tab) says what Swarms is in five rows (Start, Fuel, Work, Steer, Finish), three plain facts (public, reported not reviewed, no returns) and links to start, join and the docs. Step headings carry no subtitles; the New flow's steps are Start, Fuel, Launch. Since 2026-11-07 (Georgios: keep the two doors about the breakthrough, but add short text explaining the swarm mechanism and the crowdfunding) each heading's link also holds one short paragraph (`.sky-text`, `.door-text` before the floating field): Start a breakthrough says what a swarm is (an AI agent that researches or builds in public while it has money, with branches and subagents, real machines, code and live artifacts), and Be part of the future how the crowdfunding works (anyone funds it, the money pays only for its work, a note with money steers it, every cent shows, no tokens, no returns). Checked on the fixture world at 1440×900 and 390×844: no sideways overflow, no text clipped, the page does not scroll on the phone.
- The idea bank (`problems` in `shared/ResearchBrief.ts`, the user's request of 2026-11-02) is a curated list of problems, each with an `id`, a short label and hint, a title and a full evidence brief. `open(swarms)` drops every problem whose title matches (case-insensitively) a listed swarm that is not done, so the Pick step, the Start door and the `/x` card offer only problems nobody is working on; a finished swarm frees its problem again. The Pick step shows six at a time with More ideas, `/new?q=<id>` opens one on the Fuel step, and when every problem is taken it points to Join. A swarm stores the problem it was started from in `swarms.problem` (migration 0010, backfilled by exact title for older swarms), set by `POST /v1/swarms` and `swarm_create` with `problem` (one of the bank's ids, 400 otherwise) and exposed on `Summary.problem`; the Pick step and the `/x` card send it, a custom idea sends none, and `open` matches on it, so editing the title does not free a problem. Checked locally on 2026-11-02: 0010 applied, a swarm created with `problem: "sat-solver"` answered it back, and an unknown id answered 400.
- Swarms are general, not an autoresearch app (2026-11-07, Georgios: "This is not an autoresearch app, this is a general thing; autoresearch is just a detail, a capability of the swarm"). A swarm's goal is whatever its agent can do with code, machines and the web: research a question, build and ship software, port or audit code, gather data, write a guide, or push one measurable number. So the idea bank mixes build ideas with the research ones (`port-to-rust`, `ship-a-tool`, `open-dataset`, `missing-guide`, interleaved so the Start door's first four and the Pick step's first six show both; no existing id was removed or renamed, since `swarms.problem` stores them), `briefInstruction`, appended to every custom idea, asks for a definition of done, a plan and published work with how to check it instead of "baselines, testable hypotheses and reproducible computational experiments", the persona opens with `# The work` (work the way the goal asks; experiments and a results table only when the goal asks for a measurement) and its `# Autoresearch` section is headed and introduced as only for goals that come down to one number, `/about`, the page and card descriptions, Telegram's description, the MCP instructions, the OpenAPI description and the README say research and build. `public/kit/autoresearch.js` stays as one artifact kind. The file keeps its name `ResearchBrief.ts` to keep the diff to what changed.
- Design principle (the user's, 2026-11-02): avoid explicit buttons where the row or card itself can be the click target. The home page's clusters and their names, the join tiles, the idea cards, the notes under Back it (the row backs that note, with a + affordance), the artifact announcements in the room, the swarm's status callout (the whole strip opens Back it or the result), the empty transcript (opens Back it), the empty and offline join list, and the not-found and offline pages (rows: Try again, Join one, Start one) follow it. The join list is compact rows (status dot, one-line title, raised; the status words, area and chevron were dropped at the user's request), about 46 px each, in one column up to 760 px wide (scrolling inside its box on a phone), instead of 168 px cards. They are sorted by status and then creation time, so rows do not jump on each poll. One row is open at a time: the first tap expands it (full title, goal, the latest report's first plain line, what is left, backers, last activity), a tap on another row moves the expansion there, and a tap on the open row goes to the swarm. The New flow's Pick step is the same list (the user's request of 2026-11-02): one row per open idea with its mark and label, More ideas and Your own idea as the last rows; the first tap expands an idea into its hint and its brief in an editable box (the user's request: a template's prompt must be editable), with Fuel it; a second tap on the row also moves to Fuel with the edited brief, and opening another idea replaces the draft with that idea's brief; and Your own idea expands into the question box with Fuel it. Rows sit flush in one bordered group with dividers and no gaps (the user's request of 2026-11-02): the join list, the idea list, the artifact list, the not-found rows, the More sheet and the room roster.
- The app has no footer and no "Demo data" line (the user's request of 2026-11-02). Desktop pages are centred: the home page's field fills the window under the header, Join's list is a 760 px column, New and Docs keep their widths centred with a top offset that grows with the window's height, and not-found pages fill the space under the header and centre in it. `/docs` is reached from `d`, the X cards and links; nothing in the header points to it.
- Funding is one component, `Fund` in `Contribute.tsx`, and one kind of payment (below): a dollar amount with $2, $5 and $10 and one button (Pay $2.00, or Launch · $2.00 on New) that sends a `transferWithMemo` of OUSD, USDC.e or pathUSD from Tempo Wallet to the swarm's wallet, or in MACH opens a channel the gateway spends for the swarm (below), and counts it (`send`, then `POST …/contributions`); a note behind the money shows as a removable chip. A swarm page opens it as a sheet from the head's Fund button, the callout, the empty transcript and the room (a phone sheet from the bottom, a centred card on a desktop). New uses the same component as its second step (Start, Fund). Errors and confirmations are `Alert` cards (`Alert.tsx`: a mark, a headline, an optional detail, an optional action), with `explain` turning wallet and network errors into plain headlines. The token is picked by `choose`: the first of OUSD, USDC.e, pathUSD and MACH (from `channel.least`, 0.05) that covers the amount, else the largest balance. A swarm row is created only when New's Launch is pressed, reused on a retry, and stays hidden from every list until money arrives.
- The swarm page's views are tabs, and each shows only itself (the user's request of 2026-11-02): Transcript, Room (its tab carries who is here and how many watch, `Presence`, instead of a Room header with backers and watching), Ideas (`Ideas.tsx`: the leaderboard of notes with the money behind each and its backers, then every backer with what they put in and how; tapping an idea drafts `@swarm <idea>` in the transcript's composer) and Artifacts, then on a desktop Code, Machines and About. Machines (`Machines.tsx`, `shared/Fleet.ts`, tested in `test/fleet.test.ts`) lists every machine the feed knows: a Modal sandbox from its `compute_create` call (image, CPU, memory, GPU, lease, timeout), a Browserbase browser from `browser_create`, and a contributor's reverse `nc hand` from its join line and `GET /hands` (label, price, what its owner was paid, requests), each with its state and uptime; opening one shows its settings and every command that ran on it (from `hand` on the call), each opening to its output. Since 2026-11-06 (the user's report of a machine showing `volumes [object Object]`), each setting is written out: volumes as `muse at /work (read-only)`, `env` by its names only (never its values), memory in GB, the network as open or blocked, other booleans as yes or no; CPU and memory are left out when a maximum is set, and so is a single GPU's count. A sandbox's commands are the `compute_*` calls that name its `sandboxId`, tied to the machine by `compute_create`'s output (a sandbox started without `attach` is named by that id), shown as `$ <script>` or `write <path>`; a Code Mode cell is no longer listed as a command, since the calls inside it are. A `compute_create` that failed shows as Did not start with the gateway's reason. Checked on swarm f7df8db3fe54's feed: its `h200` sandbox listed 96 commands, `judge` Did not start with the payer's 402, and `probe` showed as stopped. The tab reads `GET /v1/swarms/:id/machines`, which folds every row that names a machine (`Feed.machined`) and counts each machine's commands from the `runs` table over the swarm's whole life, in the swarm object (`Swarm.fleet`, `Fleet.folded`, `Runs.counted`), and adds the lent hands in the Worker (`Fleet.lend`), and falls back to the loaded transcript, where a phone showed only the machines in the last 30 entries (`h200` without `probe` and `judge`); it loads a machine's commands, their outputs and its logs only when they are opened (the performance bullet "Every command and log of a machine").
- The storyboard covers every flow the user has asked for: home, join, start, a swarm's states, the transcript, steering with @swarm (amounts, new money, approval, decline, failure), the room, ideas, fund, artifacts and machines, and the X cards and other pages.
- Steering is a leaderboard, not a funding form (the user's request of 2026-11-02: copy-trade an idea). The room opens on a leaderboard of ideas (each note with the money behind it, its backers and share) above the backers and what each put in. Tapping an idea, a message's $ chip or a $ row in the transcript drafts `@swarm <idea>` in the transcript's composer, which shows $0.50, $1, $5 or your own amount when there is text. Every steer is new money (the user's decision of 2026-11-02: what a backer put in when starting is separate, and each follow-on message pays again): sending first transfers the chosen amount as a one-off stablecoin transfer (one Tempo Wallet approval, token picked by `choose`), then posts the steer with exactly that amount behind it. An @swarm message typed in the room drafts it in the transcript's composer instead of moving existing backing. Until 2026-11-07 the code did not do this: `useCredit().cover` read the author's unassigned backing (`room.me.available`) and paid only what it lacked, so a backer with old backing steered with money the swarm had already spent. Georgios posted "@swarm Let's combine all our solutions from the branches so far and keep going" with the $0.50 chip on a swarm showing "Out of money: $0.028 left" and it stayed paused. Now an @swarm steer from the transcript's composer (a backer, `from: 'backing'`) is one contribution of the chosen amount with the idea as its note (`payIn` with `note`, the Fund card's path: Tempo Wallet's approval, a sign-in first when needed), which the swarm counts, steers on (`Swarm.contributed` steers the note) and wakes on; nothing is posted with `Api.say`, and the contribution shows as the "@handle funded $0.50 behind …" row. The composer says it: the placeholder reads "Steer the agent: adds money to the swarm", the chip row "adds $0.50 from your wallet" and the send button "Add $0.50 and steer". A branch steer, a branch fund and a side-pane steer still allocate with `Api.fund` or `Api.say`, but `cover` now always pays the whole amount in first (a retry of a payment that was sent but not counted records it and pays nothing more), so every one of them adds the money it names. Steering from a team budget (`from: 'team'`) is unchanged: it spends the swarm's money and says so ("from the swarm's money"). Previously contributed backing is no longer spent by any steer from the page; it is only the weight a contribution's own note carries. Checked under `vite` on the fixture world in headless Chrome, signed in: on dex-guide ("Out of money: $0.0038 left") the steer above with $0.50 added a funded row behind that note and the head went from "$0.0038 left, $0.80 raised" to "Running, $0.50 left, $1.30 raised"; on bench-coders, which was running, $9.04 raised went to $9.54. The swarm has no Fund tab any more; the head's Fund button and `?fund=` links raise the Fund card in the transcript.
- Funding lives in the transcript's flow (the user's request of 2026-11-02): the head's Fund button, the composer's $ button and every other Fund entry switch to the transcript and raise the Fund card from the bottom of the transcript pane in place of the composer (a tray, closed with × or after paying); the Fund tab keeps it inline beside the ledger. Each transfer shows in the transcript as a $ row ("@handle funded $2 behind …"), and the feed's own money entries are left out of the transcript. The phone dock (Live, Room, Artifacts, More) is gone; phones use the same tab bar as desktops. The room's "You are @… · $… to steer with", Pick a username and Sign out are gone.
- The transcript has a composer (the user's request of 2026-11-02: it should feel like a multiplayer room). What a backer types there is sent as a contribution of the chosen amount ($0.50 by default) with the text as its note, new money that steers the agent and wakes the swarm (the bullet above; a team member steering from the team's budget posts `@swarm <text>` with `Api.say` and `steer`); signed-out visitors and non-backers see a Steer the agent row that signs in or opens Fund. Room messages that steer (`Mention.steers`) are woven into the transcript by time as speech bubbles under their @handle; other room chatter stays in the room. People → opens the room (the Room tab under 1100 px, the room's composer beside it on a desktop). The room shares its messages, members and the visitor with the swarm page through `onMessages`, so the transcript needs no second socket.
- A swarm's head is one line (the user's request of 2026-11-02): a status dot (its title says Live, Paused, Not started or Finished), the title on one line, what it has raised and one button, Fund, or Result once it is done. The meta line, the Raised / Spent / Left / Backers figures and the status callout with its Why? are gone; the money is under the Fund tab, and why a swarm waits is the feed's own status line.
- The transcript (`Transcript.tsx`) reads like a conversation (the user's request of 2026-11-02, less is more): a thin bar with a Live dot (or Finished, Paused, Not started) and a quiet All / Agent / explorer switch; then each speaker's name once per run of entries, their messages as prose, thoughts as two grey italic lines, tool calls and outputs as one-line mono rows with a › mark that expand on tap to their code or output, errors in red, and swarm, money, machine and steering events as small centred lines. What a direction is writing or thinking now streams at the bottom with a caret. Times are on hover. The kind labels, activity pulse, money left and LIVE badge are gone. Since 2026-11-06 thoughts drop Markdown's `**`, `__` and backticks, and the errors that end a turn after it ran out of money (`The run failed.`, `stopped with an error`) fold into the money line above them, across the machine lines between them: f7df8db3fe54's last turn showed four lines saying it was out of money.
- Nothing should flash on load or navigation (the user's request of 2026-11-02). The swarm list's poll keeps its last answer in memory and `sessionStorage` (`usePoll(…, { keep: true })`, keyed by fixture scene too, so board frames do not borrow each other's data), so Home, Join and New draw with it at once and replace it when the poll answers; the filter counts keep their space, and the loading rows are the height of real ones. The header's wallet button remembers the last address, @handle and MACH balance in `localStorage` (`swarms.face`) and shows them on the first frame; `restore` checks the address with `eth_accounts` and clears it when Tempo Wallet no longer reports it, then reads the balance (before, a signed-in visitor's balance was never read on load). The Connect button is no longer disabled while the config loads. Checked in headless Chrome: Join from a reload and from Home showed its final chips and rows on the first frame, and no layout shifts came from them over 12 s; a remembered profile rendered at its full width at once.
- `New.tsx` is a three-step quest flow after the Path step, which links back home: Choose, Equip, Launch. Starter cards preserve the full scientific briefs in `shared/ResearchBrief.ts`; a custom question gets an editable evidence brief. No swarm is created until the final launch action. Back navigation preserves the budget and brief. Wallet permissions remain available before launch, and funding still requires Tempo Wallet approval. Token choices and priority notes are optional setup details.
- The X cards (`XFrames.ts`, redesigned 2026-11-02, the user's request: the home, starting or joining, and a room with its chat and funding) are one player each, switching views inside the frame. `GET /x` opens on two doors like the app's home, Start a breakthrough (open ideas as chips) and Be part of the future (how many are live or open, two swarms as chips); Start shows four open ideas and Your own idea as flush rows beside a big dollar amount with $2, $5 and $10 and Launch, which creates the swarm only when pressed; Join lists open swarms, each opening its own card `/x/s/:id`. A swarm's card is its room in miniature: status dot, title, raised and backers and Fund (Result ↗ once done), Transcript and Room tabs (the room's tab shows how many back it), the transcript from `/feed` with what the agent is writing streamed from `/live`, steers woven in with their $ chips, the chat from `/chat`, and an @swarm composer that shows $2, $5 and $10 once there is text. Paying opens the swarm's page with the Fund card filled in (`/s/:id?fund&amount=&note=`, creating the swarm first when Launch picked an idea), because a card cannot send a transaction: the card shows Open the swarm to pay and then You're in with Watch it and Share; an unknown swarm answers 404 with Start one and Join one. `?view=`, `?tab=chat`, `?pick=`, `?note=`, `?demo` and `?scene=wait` with `go` put a card into any state without the API, for the storyboard's X cards section. The pages publish `twitter:card=player`, Open Graph metadata and an 800×450 preview image, follow xframes' opaque-origin sandbox and no-scroll conventions, and stack into one column under 560 px.
- X cards are served the way tempoxyz/xframes serves frames (`src/frames.ts` `documentHtml` and `public/player.js`, 2026-11-02, the user's request): `/x` and `/x/s/:id` answer a trusted player page, with the card tags, no CSP sandbox (`object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'`) and one iframe sandboxed with `allow-scripts allow-forms allow-popups allow-popups-to-escape-sandbox` at the same URL with `xframes-content`, which is the card itself under the CSP sandbox, an opaque origin. X's player iframe has no `allow-popups-to-escape-sandbox`, so a popup from the card would inherit the sandbox and Tempo Wallet would open as an opaque origin (checked in Chrome under X's nested sandboxes: from production's card the wallet popup had origin `null`). The card posts `{type: 'swarms:open', url}` for every `target=_blank` link and the player opens it, only for Tempo Wallet with a `user_code` (a 460×760 popup, or navigating itself when popups are blocked), this app's `/s/` and `/x/` pages and X's post intent. Links to other cards keep `xframes-content` so they stay inside the player. Checked on 2026-11-02 under `wrangler dev` inside X's two sandboxes: the card loaded with origin `null`, Open Tempo Wallet opened `https://wallet.tempo.xyz/?user_code=…` with its own origin, and Join one stayed in the inner frame.
- `/`, `/join` and `/new` are player cards too (2026-11-02: a post of the home page showed a static image of the old design), playing `/x`, `/x?view=join` and `/x?view=start`, and `/s/:id` plays `/x/s/:id`. Their preview images are drawn per page since 2026-11-08 (the link previews bullet below); until then every page shared `public/xframe-card.jpg`, one 800×450 screenshot of `/x?xframes-content&demo`, so a swarm's post looked like every other one. Telegram re-crawls a link sent to @WebpageBot, which a messenger that kept an old preview needs.
- Telegram Mini App (2026-11-06, the user's request; Telegram previews never run a page's script, so `twitter:player` does nothing there): the bot is @TempoSwarmsBot, created with @BotFather through computer use, its token in the Worker secret `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` and `~/.config/swarms/telegram-bot-token` (mode 600), and `TELEGRAM_BOT` in `wrangler.jsonc`. `/tg` and `/tg/s/:id` serve the X card's views directly at Swarms' origin (no player, no CSP sandbox, `telegram.org` allowed for `telegram-web-app.js`), with `?tgWebAppStartParam=` opening a swarm (or `join` and `start` opening those views), Telegram's own back button instead of the ← (hidden on the home view), `openLink` for outside links, `t.me/share/url` sharing `t.me/TempoSwarmsBot?startapp=<id>`, and Watch it staying inside the app. Paying opens the swarm's own page inside the app with the Fund card filled in, as the X card does since pledges went away (before, it was a MACH pledge through Tempo Wallet's device flow, opened with `openLink`). `POST /telegram` is the bot's webhook (`Telegram.ts`, secret header checked against `TELEGRAM_WEBHOOK_SECRET`): `/start [swarm|join|start]` in a private chat answers with a `web_app` button. Set through the Bot API: the webhook (messages only), the menu button (Open, `/tg`), the `/start` command and both descriptions. Checked on production in headless Chrome with a stub of `telegram-web-app.js` that records each call: ready, expand and colours on load, the back button shown on join and hidden again on home, a start parameter opening the swarm and back going to the join list, the Telegram share link, and `/x` still loading no Telegram script. The Main Mini App is set in BotFather (Bot Settings, Configure Mini App, `https://swarms.tempo-dev.workers.dev/tg`), which `?startapp=` links need; `getMe` then said `has_main_web_app: true`. In Telegram for macOS the same day, `/start` answered with Open Swarms, the app opened on the two doors with production's swarms, a tap opened a swarm's transcript with Telegram's Back in the header, and @gakonst steered swarm f7df8db3fe54 from it: the composer's device code was approved in Tempo Wallet, the 2 MACH pledge was delivered and the room said `@gakonst pledged 2 MACH behind "Be more ambitous!"`. Telegram's terms on payments inside Mini Apps (Stars for digital goods, TON for crypto) were not checked.
- Link previews (2026-11-08, Georgios: "make the opengraphs for everything look great across all platforms esp when you're sharing a particular swarm - should also show og video"). Every page and card names its own picture, drawn by the Worker from the same data the page shows: `Share.ts` picks the subject of a URL (a swarm, an idea from `/new?q=`, the home, `/ideas`, `/new`, `/about`, `/docs`) and writes one set of tags for `Page.ts` and `XFrames.ts` (the `/x` and `/tg` cards): canonical `og:url` (the swarm's `/s/:id` even from its card), `og:site_name`, title, description (a swarm's status, what it raised from how many backers and the first sentence of its goal), a 1200×630 PNG `og:image` with its type, size and alt text, a 1280×720 MP4 `og:video` (`og:type` `video.other`) for every page but `/about` and `/docs`, `twitter:card` `player` with the 16:9 poster as `twitter:image` and the MP4 as `twitter:player:stream` beside the existing `twitter:player`, `twitter:label1/data1` and `2` (status and raised for a swarm, which Slack shows under the title), and an oEmbed link. `GET /oembed?url=` (CORS `*`) answers `type: video` with an 800×450 iframe of the page's `/x` card (scaled to `maxwidth` and `maxheight`) for pages that have one, which Mastodon, Discourse and Notion embed, and `type: link` for `/about` and `/docs`; a URL off this origin or a missing swarm is 404. `index.html` and the cards also link `/favicon.ico` (16, 32 and 48 px) and `/apple-touch-icon.png` (180 px), drawn from `favicon.svg`, since iMessage, Slack and Discord ignore an SVG icon.
  - Pictures are `/og/<key>/card.png`, `poster.png` and `video.mp4` with `?v=` a hash of what they show (`Share.version`: title, state, raised, backers, pitch, and what is left in twentieths, so a swarm spending cents keeps its URL and a new backer or a state change gives a new one, which X and Facebook need because they keep a picture by URL). An asset at its current version is `immutable` for a year; one asked at an older version is drawn from current data and kept 5 minutes. Each lives in the isolate (up to 48 and 12 MB) and the colo's Cache API, one draw at a time per URL. A crawler's page view (`Share.bot`: Twitterbot, facebookexternalhit, Slackbot, Discordbot, TelegramBot, WhatsApp, LinkedInBot, Mastodon and others) draws its three assets in `waitUntil`, so the crawler's own fetch of the image or video a moment later is a cache hit. `/og/*` answers `Range` with 206 and 416 (Safari and iMessage fetch an MP4 by ranges), `If-None-Match` with 304, and `HEAD`.
  - Drawing (`Cards.ts`, `Film.ts`, `Paint.ts`, `Glyphs.ts`): a page's static layer is an SVG in the site's own look (white, Paradigm Serif and SemiMono, the cover gradients, pills, gauge and `Back it` button of `Card.tsx`) rasterized by `@resvg/resvg-wasm` 2.6.2 with the site's fonts; satori was not used because its Yoga layout instantiates WebAssembly from bytes, which Workers refuse, and the cards need only line wrapping, which `Glyphs.wrap` does from the fonts' `hmtx` and `cmap` (with an ellipsis on the last line). The fonts are `public/fonts`' WOFF2 files merged with their Extended parts and decoded to TTF with fonttools (`src/worker/fonts/*.bin`, about 100 KB each). Everything that moves is painted in JavaScript on that layer: a swarm's cover cluster (its dots, spokes and dashed far links in its mood's colour, slowly turning, with packets running out along the spokes and a ring pulsing while it is working), the gauge filling to what is left, the pill's pulsing dot, the goal's sentence typed in with a caret, and on the home page the field of clusters around the hero (working swarms as bursts, swarms that need money as amber smudges, ideas as seeds) with dashed links fading in. The home, `/ideas` and `/new` pictures count and show swarms as the home page does: open ones only, without the e2e test swarms it hides (`Status.test`, moved from `Home.tsx`), so the image says what the page says. A still is the film's last frame, PNG-encoded in `Paint.png` with the runtime's `CompressionStream`.
  - Video: H.264 Constrained Baseline, level 3.1, yuv420p, 24 fps, 6 s, a key frame every 2 s, from lieff/minih264 (CC0, b0baea7, single header vendored in `src/worker/h264/`) compiled to a 85 KB `encoder.wasm` with no imports, which converts RGBA to BT.601 YUV and encodes; `Mp4.ts` writes a fast-start MP4 (`ftyp`, `moov`, `mdat`, with `stss` from the IDR slices and `colr` and `pasp`). `Film.film` yields every 8 frames so a draw never holds the isolate. Rebuild the encoder with Homebrew's LLVM (Apple's clang has no wasm target): `cd src/worker/h264 && /opt/homebrew/opt/llvm/bin/clang --target=wasm32 -O3 -msimd128 -mbulk-memory -nostdlib -ffreestanding -Iinclude -fuse-ld=/opt/homebrew/opt/lld/bin/wasm-ld -Wl,--no-entry -Wl,--export=start -Wl,--export=pixels -Wl,--export=output -Wl,--export=encode -Wl,--strip-all -Wl,--initial-memory=1048576 -o encoder.wasm encoder.c`. `test/og.test.ts` checks the tags, escaping, versions, page keys, crawler detection, wrapping, the PNG and the MP4's boxes against its stream.
  - Checked on 2026-11-08 under `wrangler dev` on the build, with local D1 holding two swarms shaped like production's Megakernel ($80 from 2, $2.31 left) and US perps ones: every page answered its tags as Twitterbot; cards drew in 27 to 110 ms (35 to 108 KB), posters in 30 to 70 ms, videos in 0.7 to 1.5 s (130 to 345 KB) on this Mac, and a second fetch in 3 ms; a Discordbot view of a swarm page left all three assets cached 4 s later; a new backer changed the version and the crawler's next view drew the new one; `Range: bytes=0-1` answered 206, a range past the end 416, a matching `If-None-Match` 304; `/og/about/video.mp4`, an unknown swarm and an unknown idea 404. ffprobe read the video as Constrained Baseline 1280×720, 144 frames; headless Chrome played it (`canPlayType` probably); AVFoundation (`AVAssetReader`, what iMessage and Safari use) decoded all 144 frames and Quick Look drew its thumbnail. The Worker's gzipped upload grew from 803 KB to 1,863 KB, 1,060 KB of it resvg's WebAssembly.
  - Checked on production on 2026-11-08 after deploying b1942be (version 17bfa834, with `CTX=tempo wrangler deploy`: the `~/.local/bin/wrangler` wrapper keeps one login per context, and the `tempo` context is georgios@tempo.xyz on Tempo Development Resources), with `wrangler tail` running: as Discordbot and Twitterbot every listed page answered 200 with its own `og:title`, an `og:video` on all but `/about` and `/docs`, and every image and video it names answered 200 with its type. A crawler's first view of a page drew its three assets in the background in 2.4 to 5.5 s of CPU, and the crawler's own fetches of them right after used 0 to 2 ms; later views of the page used 6 to 100 ms. A cold draw (a never-seen `?v=`) took 127 ms of CPU for a card, 194 ms for a poster and about 2.7 s for a video (2.8 s wall), and the same URL again 0 ms. `Range: bytes=0-1` answered 206 with `accept-ranges`, a matching `If-None-Match` 304, `/oembed` the swarm's card and iframe, `/x/s/:id` the swarm's canonical `og:url`, image and video, and `/favicon.ico` and `/apple-touch-icon.png` their types; 111 tail events, all ok, no exception. Not checked: how X, Discord, Slack, Telegram, iMessage, WhatsApp, LinkedIn and Mastodon actually show a posted link.
- A card is laid out at 800×450 and, in any landscape box smaller than that, scaled down whole with CSS `zoom` (`fit` in the card's script), as xframes' SDK does, because X's timeline plays it at about 598×336 and the bottom of the 800×450 layout was cut off (2026-11-02, a post of `/x`). The one-column layout applies only to narrow portrait boxes.
- The swarm head is three rows across the whole width (2026-11-02): the title, the energy bar (what it is doing, the bar, the total raised, +) and the tabs, so the transcript and the room start on one line and are the same height.
- Phones (2026-11-02, checked on an emulated iPhone 13 for page scroll, sideways overflow, clipped content and tap targets): the page never scrolls, only its panels do, each with `overscroll-behavior: contain`. The Code tab wraps long lines in files and diffs instead of scrolling them sideways, and the file grows inside the panel's one scroll instead of a nested 60vh scroll box. The tab says just Code. Touch screens get 32 px speaker chips, invisible larger tap areas around the $ chips, mentions and Copy path, no tap highlight and no sticky hover states. The join page's filter chips bleed to the screen edges on purpose and scroll sideways.
- Caching (2026-11-02, the user's request that reopening a swarm be instant): every poll's last answer stays in memory for the session (`remember` in `hooks.ts`), ahead of the server's boot data, and is shown at once and revalidated immediately; a swarm's merged transcript is kept too (`trails` in `Swarm.tsx`, its last 400 entries). Touching, hovering or focusing a swarm link or tile prefetches its detail and the swarm page's code (`prefetch`/`warmer` in `router.tsx`), and the page's code also loads when the browser is idle. Measured on production in emulated iPhone 13 from /join: opening Solve P = NP took 1,065 ms the first time and 107 ms and 103 ms on the next two after going back. `public/_headers` caches `/assets/*` (hashed) for a year as immutable and fonts for 30 days (before, everything was `max-age=0, must-revalidate`), and every JSON `GET` under `/api` and `/v1` gets a weak ETag and `no-cache`, answering 304 when it matches, so an unchanged poll costs no body.
- Streaming instead of polling, in the browser (2026-11-07, the user's request: "reduce the need for polling as well when a system can be instead streaming"; the plan is polling.txt of the same day's audit, items 3, 4, 6, 7 and 8):
  - One room socket per swarm per tab (`wire.ts`): the swarm page, the room, the roster (`useRoster`), the transcript, the side pane, Machines and the Code tab all join it, and it stays open 5 s after the last of them leaves, so a tab switch or a back navigation reuses it. `/s/:id/code` opens its own with `?listen=1`, which the server must pass to the swarm object and leave out of presence.
  - The client counts the socket as streaming only once `welcome` arrives. Until then (at most 4 s), or on a server that never sends it, every fallback poll keeps running, so this client works against the Worker before it.
  - `pulse` is merged into the detail with no request (`usePoll` now also returns `set`), `artifacts` and `roster` replace their lists, and `changed` refetches only the parts it names: detail (`?feed=0`), feed (one `?after=` read when `/live` is closed), members (the room's `chat?after=`, which also refreshes `me`), branches, roster, hands (the lent hands and the machine list), code (the snapshot, and one git log read on `/s/:id/code`) and artifacts. A hidden tab holds the parts and fetches each once when it is shown again.
  - A `welcome` after a reconnect, or whose version differs from the last one the tab applied, refetches every part once; an unchanged part answers 304. On a first visit the parts are read at mount and again at the first welcome; data the page already had (memory, session storage or the server render) waits for the welcome instead.
  - Fallback polls run only while the socket is down: the detail every 10 s, the room every 15 s (once a minute while its socket is open without a welcome, as before), the feed every 5 s while running and 10 s otherwise, the artifacts, branches and roster every 60 s, the code log every 60 s while the tab is visible. While it streams: the branches every 60 s only while the swarm runs (a branch's spending has no push), the lent hands every 60 s while Machines is open (their requests and charges have no push), and the machine list at most every 10 s, and only after new machine, tool or output entries arrived. Everything else waits for a message.
  - `usePoll` no longer fetches when its pace changes: the next fetch is due at the last one's start plus the new pace, `null` pauses it, and `reload` fetches at once, after a fetch already in flight. Before, every pace change (running to paused, stream up or down) fetched immediately.
  - `/live` is always followed with `?after=` (the page used nothing else, and the other branch is gone from `api.ts`). `useLive` is one state, off, following or live with its streams, where it kept `pushed` and `connected` flags that disagreed after an idle frame. The feed is not polled while `/live` is open: it delivers every entry with its seq and replays the gap on reconnect. A stream that delivered entries or lasted 30 s reopens at once; one that failed sooner waits 3 s.
  - The transcript and the side pane read one branches store (`useBranches` in `Branches.tsx`), where each ran its own poll. A `changed` naming branches refetches 5 s later, because `/branches` is `Edge.cached` fresh for 5 s and an immediate read can return the copy from before the change.
  - The fixture world sends `welcome`, a `pulse` when a swarm's summary changes, `changed` for a paused swarm's feed and for new updates, contributions, steers and branch moves, and `roster` on team changes; its `/live` follows like the server's (each entry once with its seq after the cursor, `start` and `end` around a turn), and its lobby sends `welcome` and a `swarm` for each changed listed swarm.
  - Requests in 60 s, counted in headless Chrome under `vite` on the fixture world (`?fixture`, 1440 px, after the page hydrated), before (388f6fb) and after: a running swarm 14 to 1 (6 detail, 4 feed, 2 artifacts, 1 roster, 1 branches before; 1 branches after), a paused swarm 15 to 0, the home page and the join page 11 to 0 each, the code page 8 to 0 (4 detail and 4 git log reads; git prefetches of the 8 newest commits, which happen once, left out). In a hidden tab the code page still read the git log 4 times a minute before and reads it none now; a hidden swarm tab made 1 request before and none now. With a server that never sends `welcome`, a running swarm's tab falls back to 8 (6 detail, 1 artifacts, 1 branches; its room socket still carries messages, so the room reads once a minute), a paused one to 14 (6 detail, 6 feed, 1 artifacts, 1 branches), the home page to 1 and the code page to 2. The first welcome refetches what the page read at mount: a running swarm's page made 9 reads in its first second against 8 before (a part whose read is still in flight is not read twice).
- The lobby (`Lobby.ts`, binding `LOBBY`, one object for the world, `getByName('lobby')`, Durable Object migration `v2` in `wrangler.jsonc`): `GET /api/swarms/live` and `/v1/swarms/live` are hibernatable WebSockets that send `welcome` when they open and `{ type: 'swarm', swarm }` whenever a listed swarm changes. The swarm object tells it with `Lobby.tell(env, Store.summary(row))` (the RPC `changed(summary)`), which the lobby filters itself: it drops a swarm with nothing raised (the list hides it), cuts the answer with `Answer.lead` as `/api/swarms` does, skips a summary equal to the last one it sent, sends a change of status, why, title, goal, problem, steering or answer at once, and sends money-only changes at most once per 30 s per swarm (the latest one, from its alarm). It does nothing while nobody listens. Home, join and new keep the list from it (`useSwarms` in `wire.ts`), read `/api/swarms` once on each welcome, and poll it every 60 s only while it is down; before, home and join polled every 5 s and new every 30 s. It takes at most 10,000 sockets, a number chosen without measurement. Checked on 2026-11-07 under `vite` with a temporary route that called `Lobby.tell`: a socket got `welcome` in 18 ms and `pong` to `ping`, a repeated summary was not sent again, two money-only changes within 30 s arrived once, 30.0 s later, as the latest, a status change arrived at once, and a swarm with nothing raised never arrived. Until the swarm object calls it, the lobby sends only `welcome`, so the pages show the list as of each (re)connect.
- Checked on production on 2026-11-07 after the deploy of the query, state and streaming work (8d5b1e8, then bc3807e), in headless Chrome over 60 s per tab: a running swarm's page (f7df8db3fe54) made 1 request (its branches) with its room socket open, the home page 0 with the lobby socket open, and a swarm woken from the top-up bug 2, with no console errors; before, the same pages made about 15, 12 and 16. A Worker tail over 100 s showed 19 events, all ok: 7 swarm-object invocations for two running swarms, where the 2 s event paging alone used to make about 30 a minute each. Every read route, `/wait`, the lobby and room sockets' welcome, `/live` follow mode and the 17 MCP tools answered. The 8 swarms that F1 had stuck (33a66a5137ee, 71ae8f963b9b, a8efb916f435, fc98b729378e, 2452f9b2260d, 3a05762b5254, ae64c263f6a1, c40012cbf6af) counted their channels again on their next read and went back to running with $0.04 to $0.14 each. The deploy merged the shared worktree's uncommitted Telegram app (b19475c) so it stayed live.
- Pushes after hibernation, checked on production on 2026-11-07: a room socket opened on the paused swarm 98eb38605e98 got `welcome` and `presence`, sat idle for 90 s, and then a signed-in `@swarm` steer posted with the wallet's access key reached it as `said` and as `changed` with `detail` and `members` 1.3 s after the post, so the coalescing timer runs in the object woken by the post.
- The X card follows `/live?after=` from the newest entry it shows and stays open between turns (it reopened every 5 s with no cursor, up to 12 requests a minute per view, and missed entries logged between turns); `end` only clears the line being written, and it reconnects only on an error. It also read `/feed?limit=40`, which answers a swarm's oldest 40 entries, so a long-running swarm's card showed its first minutes; it reads `?before=<max>&limit=40` now, and drops an entry it already showed by its seq or its gateway event id. Since 2026-11-07 it holds that stream only while the card is on screen: it closes it when the page is hidden or an IntersectionObserver on the card says it scrolled out of view, and reopens from the last entry it showed, because an open `/live` keeps the swarm's object awake for up to 15 minutes per viewer, and an X timeline leaves scrolled-past players loaded.
- A contribution the wallet sent but `POST …/contributions` did not count (a 5xx, a dropped connection, a receipt still missing after the retries) is its own state of the Fund card (`unrecorded`): Pay is disabled, and Record it posts the same hash, or for MACH the same signed channel open, again; a 409 `conflict` ("already counted") counts as done. Before, any such failure became a plain error and the button sent a new transfer. A MACH post the server refused with a 4xx moved nothing and stays an error, and so does one the gateway refused as expired. Steering and side questions (`useCredit`) keep an unrecorded payment and record it before paying anything new. The storyboard has both states (fixture scene `uncounted`).
- One derivation each: `Status.standing` in `shared/Status.ts` names a swarm's state (word, mood and call to action) for the head, the dot, the join list and the X card, where `Status.word` and `Visual.standing` disagreed (a funded swarm before its first step was Not started on the join list and Starting on its page, and a paused swarm with no why and money left was Resting on one and Needs money on the other; both now say Starting and Resting, and the join list's Not started filter includes Starting). `Steering.gate` decides who may post in the room and who may steer and ask on the side (checking, signed out, unnamed, not a member, open from backing or from the team budget with what is left), where the room, the composer and the side pane each wrote the chain of checks.
- The wallet keeps addresses lowercase in one place (`set`, and the initial state read from storage); sign-in ends the pending phase. Transactions still use the address Tempo Wallet returns.
- The web's branches for a finished swarm are gone (the Result button, the read-only room, the hidden composer and Fund): nothing calls `Swarm.done`, and on 2026-11-07 production had 7 funding, 41 paused and 4 running swarms and none done. `done` stays in the public `Status` type.
- The web app's swarm detail (`/api/swarms/:id`, and the boot data of `/s/:id`) carries the last 80 feed entries rather than 200, and its updates without their text (`Pushed`), which the page never showed: the Code tab needs only their times, spend and commits, and Earlier loads older entries. For Solve P = NP on 2026-11-02 that took the detail from 347 KB to 93 KB (22 KB gzipped), the 5 s poll (`?feed=0`) from 127 KB to 14 KB and the page HTML from 509 KB to 190 KB, and opening it from /join on an emulated iPhone 13 from 1,065 ms to 195 ms (84 to 108 ms on a revisit). `/v1` and MCP keep the full updates.
- On phones a swarm page hides the site header (Swarms, About, Start, Join and the wallet), the user's request of 2026-11-02: a ← before the title goes back to /join, and signing in stays in the composer. Desktop keeps the header.
- The swarm head shows an energy bar instead of "raised" and a Fund button (`Energy` in `Visual.tsx`, 2026-11-02, the user's request): the bar is what is left to spend, out of everything raised, with the amount left on the bar and the total raised to its right; a line above it says what the swarm is doing (working for how long, resting, out of energy, charging up to start), and the whole bar is the Fund button. When money arrives it animates the bar filling back up.
- A swarm rests when an update came with no tool call or machine (`resting`, pause reason `idle`): it stops sending the agent "keep going", which before cost about $0.05 to $0.12 a minute for nothing (swarm 06cf5d7c8f92 posted five "nothing spent, no tool calls" updates in a row on 2026-11-02, each costing that much in model calls, and kept needing funding), and spends nothing until money or a note wakes it. The prompt tells the agent so, and tells it there are no rounds; the page and the excerpts strip a leading "Round N update" from older updates (`Answer.unround`), and old "Round N started" status lines are hidden. (Superseded on 2026-11-08: a swarm stops only when it is out of money; see "Only money stops a swarm" under Campaign experience.)
- The transcript stays at the bottom while it is live: a `MutationObserver` on the list follows streamed text as it grows (before, only new rows and resizes moved it, so a long thought streamed off screen), and only a scroll the viewer made, not one the page made, switches it to reading.
- The agent is a stock gateway agent with no Swarms prompt on top (2026-11-03, the user's request: "no system prompts or custom things on the swarms code on top of the default gateway code upstream for cloud agents/subagents"; before, 2026-11-02, "the standard nanocode-agents stuff, no mods, no special prompts"): `POST /v1/agents` with only a name and the payer, so the gateway's default harness, model, thinking, tools, subagents and instructions apply (tempoxyz/gateway `src/Agents.ts`, nanocode-ts upstream prompts). The swarm sends only the people's own words: the first message is the swarm's title and goal followed by its notes, each continuation is `Keep going.` followed by notes it has not sent yet, and a note that arrives during a turn is steered in as the note's text alone. Gone: the money lines, "this is a crowdfunded swarm", the public-storage line, the repository instructions and the $1.19 launch gate that waited for repository money, the lent-machine rules and the steer announcing a newly connected hand, and the last three reports when an agent is recreated. Stored items are still made public after the fact (`expose`), and a repository the agent creates on its own is still found and shown (`repo`). One exception since 2026-11-05, the user's request ("append in the system prompt that you can use artifacts for visualizing progress, or explaining things, and how close we are … must have taste about things for progress reporting"): the artifacts convention below is appended to the agent's system prompt, since 2026-11-08 as the swarm's own instructions, frozen when the swarm first runs (a shared gateway persona before; next section), after the upstream prompt, which stays whole.
- A swarm's repository clones without a key (2026-11-03): the gateway serves public repositories read-only over smart HTTP at `/p/<owner>/<app>/git/<name>.git` (upload-pack only; a push answers 403), and `code.clone` in the detail, `/v1` and MCP carries that URL. The Code tab and the full browser show `git clone <url> <name>` with a copy button. Checked on 2026-11-03: `git clone` of Solve P = NP's URL fetched its 32 commits, and a push answered 403. The owner is the operator key's wallet, 0x9759…e122 (0x975937feafc6869a260c176854dda8764a78e122).
- The payer pays vendors itself when the gateway asks (`operation: "vendor"`, 2026-11-03: the gateway's treasury was empty, so searches and paid cell `fetch`es failed): `Swarm.vendor` takes only a Tempo USDC.e charge of at most $0.25 (`vendorCap`, equal to `passCall` since 2026-11-06; it was $1, for a Code Storage repository, but `passable` already refused anything over $0.25), buys the USDC.e it lacks with the wallet's OUSD on the DEX (`Vault.buy`, exact out, 0.5% slippage), signs the charge with the wallet (`mppx` `tempo.charge`) and counts it in `banked:pool`, so it is spent like any other payment, and logs the vendor and amount.
- A swarm also rests after three updates in a row that each came within a minute, spent under $0.01 and pushed nothing (stuck, stuckTurns): on 2026-11-03 swarm 236cb54576c8 retried a failing git_create every minute for about 35 minutes, because each update made a tool call and so never counted as idle. (Superseded on 2026-11-08: a swarm stops only when it is out of money; see "Only money stops a swarm" under Campaign experience.)
- A channel that never opens is logged at most once per source every 10 minutes (`quietMs`). Swarm fb3840ed48a5 logged it every 8 s for an hour on 2026-11-03, while the gateway's fee sponsor was out of funds. Gateway #175 now has an agent payer pay its own fee in OUSD when the sponsor is starved; a MACH pledge key still cannot, since its limits hold only MACH.
- A swarm that rests because its agent stopped working (`idle`, `rest`) shuts down the sandboxes it left running; one that pauses for money leaves them on their prepaid lease, so a long experiment finishes and its results wait for the next round. `sweep` used to return unless a hand was listed, so a sandbox created without `attach` was never shut down; it now also runs when the feed logged a `Starting machine` since the last sweep. Swarm fb3840ed48a5 had four Modal sandboxes training models on 2026-11-03, renewed up to 55 minutes each, none attached. (Superseded on 2026-11-08: a swarm stops only when it is out of money; see "Only money stops a swarm" under Campaign experience.)
- What a source can pay counts its channel's unspent deposit only once the channel is confirmed on chain. Swarm fb3840ed48a5's prompts said $3.95 was payable from a $2 pledge on 2026-11-03, while its channel kept failing to open: the unopened channel's deposit was counted beside the key's untouched limit.
- Model calls now hold close to their real cost (gateway #179: the prompt at its cached rate where the conversation was seen, 1,024 output tokens, overruns charged on the next voucher), so `Store.perCall`, a call's hold for the busy and smallest-payment checks, is 0.02, down from 0.04.
- The code viewer reads a snapshot the swarm keeps (`Swarm.snapshot`, `GET /api/swarms/:id/code`): the last 50 commits on main, up to 2,000 file paths and the README (or first file) at the head, up to 64,000 characters, read from the gateway's `/p` routes in parallel and refreshed when `watch` sees a new commit, or in the background when a read finds it more than a minute old. The swarm page fetches it, the `Code.tsx` chunk and its highlighter (`preloadHighlighter` with markdown and pierre-light) 300 ms after the page loads when the swarm has a repository, and renders the loaded module directly rather than through `lazy`, because React 19 holds a suspended boundary's reveal for 300 ms. The Code tab then opens from memory: `Code` starts from the snapshot (`Api.coded`) and `Viewer` from the README text, falling back to the three `/p` requests only without one or when the newest update's commit is missing from it. `/s/:id/code` serves the snapshot in its server-rendered data, seeds `Repo.tsx`'s log and head tree from it, and is prefetched with its chunk when the Full browser link is hovered. Measured on 2026-11-03 in headless Chrome against the deployed app: before, the Code tab waited for its 174 KB chunk, then `log` and `files` (about 0.5 s), then the README `read` (0.4 s), then five highlighter chunks (0.5 s), about 1.7 s after the page's scripts and 4.4 s from a cold load; after, 60 ms from the tab's click to the highlighted README (84-284 ms including Playwright's click), 2-3 s from a cold load (most of it this machine's 0.25-0.6 s per request), and 70-190 ms from the Full browser link to its tree, commits and diff. `/s/:id/code` itself answered 404 when loaded directly, because only `/s/:id` was server-rendered; it is now.
- The viewer reads the repository through this app, `GET /api/swarms/:id/git/{log,files,read,diff}` (`Code.proxy`), not the gateway's `/p` routes directly: same origin, so no second TLS connection, and cached in the colo's Cache API. A `files`, `read` or `diff` at a full 40-character sha is immutable (`public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, so the browser keeps it too); `log` and refs like `main` live 5 s. The page keeps every immutable answer in memory (`read`, `peek`), so a file or commit seen once opens in the same frame, and warms the first 40 text files at the head and the diff and tree of the newest 8 commits while idle (`prefetch`, `warm`, 3 to 4 at a time, none for trees past 400 files). The snapshot also carries the head commit's diff, so `/s/:id/code` shows its Changes without a request. For `/s/:id/code` the HTML head carries `modulepreload` links for `Repo.tsx`, `Code.tsx` and the highlighter's chunks (`preload.json`, which a Vite plugin in `vite.config.ts` writes from the client bundle), and the response starts once D1 says the swarm exists rather than after its data is gathered. Measured on 2026-11-03 in headless Chrome on swarm 06cf5d7c8f92 (32 commits, 40-odd files): `/s/:id/code` ready in 1.1 s from a cold browser (2.3 s before; this machine's requests take 0.25-0.6 s), a file opened in 9-33 ms and a commit in 9-19 ms; a cached `read` answered in this connection's round trip (0.32-0.39 s by curl), an uncached one in 0.8-2.5 s.
- `Chain.client` batches the `readContract`s it is given at once into one Multicall3 call (0xcA11…CA11, deployed on Tempo): `Vault.balances` went from three RPC requests to one, measured on 2026-11-03. That swarm's follow loop had hit rpc.tempo.xyz's `Request exceeds defined limit`.
- A swarm is never done (2026-11-03, the user's request: "I can always keep steering a swarm, a swarm just produces artifacts and git code as it goes"): the prompt no longer asks for STATUS: done, a turn's answer never ends the swarm, and contributions, steers and hands are no longer refused for a done swarm. A swarm that finished before this is reopened (reopen) on its next read or step: it forgets refunded and refundAt and rests (paused, why idle) until a note or new money wakes it, and its agent carries on in the same thread. Its earlier refund, closed channels and ended budgets stay as they were.
- Everything a swarm produces is public (2026-11-02, the user's request, after the gateway made stored items private unless made public one at a time, tempoxyz/gateway AGENTS.md on `Public`): the prompt asks for `public: true` on `kv_set`, `bucket_put` and `git_create`, and after every turn that called one of them, and once for every swarm, `expose` lists the app's kv values, bucket objects and repositories (`/v1/kv/list`, `/v1/bucket/list` at 100 a page, `/v1/git/list`) and makes each private one public with `/v1/<kind>/visibility`, paid by the operator key at 0.0005 a call. Anyone reads them at the gateway's `/p/<operator wallet>/swarm-<id>/`. For swarms from before this, `heal` (every read of a swarm) runs it once; an `exposing` mark older than 60 s does not block it, because the first deploy left one behind when its run was cut off and the swarm never exposed. On 2026-11-02 Solve P = NP held 8 kv values (one, `round32-scratch`, private), 8 objects and one repository, all public otherwise; its feed then logged "Made 1 private item the agent stored public", and of the 16 listed swarms every item was public except one value in the running swarm 97d3c0be73ea, which its next turn end exposes. Since 2026-11-07 the agent is told so: the persona's Artifacts section says that everything in the swarm's app is published after each turn whatever public flag it sets, and to keep secrets out of it. Before, an agent reported the publication as a gateway bug (fb_644e8201c07447deb2cacc81b50fbd0d: keys it stored with `public: false` were public).
- One swarm is one agent in one long thread (the user's request of 2026-11-02: "no notion of rounds anywhere", "no lead researcher, explorers or directions", "the upstream mechanism handles it"). Each gateway turn of the agent ends with a reply, and that reply is an update (`Update`: `at`, `text`, `done`, `spent`, `base`, `commits`). Unless its last line is `STATUS: done`, the Durable Object tells the agent to keep going (`next`), with the money left, the repository, the artifacts, lent hands and the notes by weight; a turn that ended less than 60 s after it started waits out the rest of that minute first (`quickMs`), so an agent that only says it is waiting cannot loop many times a minute, and a steer while it waits wakes it at once. A steer while a turn runs goes into it (`steer: true`, `Steer.live`); otherwise its note is in the next continuation. `since` marks an unbroken stretch of work, from the first turn after the swarm was idle until it pauses or finishes, and `Detail.working` (`{since}`) is set for that whole stretch, between turns too, so the page says "Working for 3h 06m" rather than counting turns. Machines persist between updates while their lease lasts (the prompt says so); sandboxes are swept only when the swarm pauses or finishes.
- Each update's spend is what its turn cost by the gateway's ledger (2026-11-08, found by the session watching swarm f3a42cf87339, whose updates at 01:22, 01:25, 01:26, 01:32, 01:34 and 01:39 UTC said "Posted an update after spending $0" although each of those turns paid for model calls, and the next update caught up). `Turns.finish` reads the turn's report, `GET /v1/agents/:id/turns/:turn`, which since tempoxyz/gateway 34737b8 answers `spent_usd`, the net of the charges ledger rows that carry the turn's id and whose calls have settled (`Turns.settledUsd`), and stores it as the turn's `spent`; without `spent_usd` it falls back to the change in the swarm row's `spent` since the turn started, as before. That change follows the gateway's snapshots of the payer channels, which count the hold of every call in flight (a branch's model call held about $0.37 and kept about $0.02), so a turn that started while a branch's call was in flight and ended after it settled read below zero, shown as $0, and the next one read the difference. By the ledger those turns cost $0.084 to $0.134 each (turn 10, reported $0: $0.092138; turn 14, reported $0.588862: $0.086668; turns 17 to 58, when nothing else ran, equal to what was reported). A branch's calls carry the turn that started it, so spend of branches that outlive that turn is in the swarm's total and the branches' spend but in no update, and a turn's figure is what had settled when it ended.
- Subagents are the runtime's own: the agent spawns them with `spawn_agent` when it wants help (the prompt does not ask for any), and their events reach the turn's event stream as `source: "subagent"` with `update.type` `added`, `event`, `status`, `completion` or `message` (gakonst/nanocode `crates/runtime/agent/src/session_events.rs`, commit f6dfa4e). `Trail.subagent` names each by its role (`role #<id>` when two share one), narrates its tool calls, outputs, messages and thoughts like the agent's, and logs its completion once with its output (up to 20,000 characters); the feed stores the name in `who` (null for the agent itself). The researcher tracking that came before (`shared/Research.ts`, the `researchers` table, `Detail.researchers`, money-sized explorer parallelism with `perWorker` and up to three explorers, the lead's output contract) is gone.
- The swarm page is a workspace (the user's request of 2026-11-01: the transcript is the main view, the room to its right): a compact head (status, how long the agent has been working or when it last posted, title, raised, spent, left, backers and a Back it button), then tabs over the main column, Transcript (`Transcript.tsx`, kept mounted), Artifacts (below), Back it (the contribution box, the ledger, the notes and the funding record), Code, Machines (lent hands and Connect) and About (the question and how a swarm works), with the room (`Room.tsx`) as a column on the right. From 1100 px wide the room is a column; narrower, it is a tab after Transcript, kept mounted (hidden) so its socket stays open, with a count of messages that arrived while it was hidden. The agent's updates are its replies in the transcript, and the API still serves them (`/updates`, `swarm_updates`); the page has no Updates tab since the user's request of 2026-11-02. The tab is the URL's hash (`#about`, `#fund`, `#room`…), and `?fund=` links open Back it.
- The transcript is the thread: a LIVE badge while the agent works, how long it has worked, its activity over the last 30 minutes and the money left; a chip per speaker (Everyone, Agent and each subagent, `?who=` in the URL) that filters the timeline; and the timeline itself (thoughts, commands, their output, messages, steers, money and errors, each tagged with its speaker, newest at the bottom and pinned there until the viewer scrolls up), with what each speaker is thinking, writing or typing into a tool call right now below it. Earlier pages load with `GET /api/swarms/:id/feed?before=<seq>&who=`. `/live` streams `start`, `delta` (`who`, `channel`), `entry` (`seq` 0, replaced by the polled copy by `event` and kind) and `end` or `idle`, reduced by the same `shared/Trail.ts` code the object uses.
- Phones (the user's request of 2026-11-02: "make mobile work properly, make it look great, no scrolling obviously no zoom in"): under 800 px the page is a fixed shell (`.app` sized by `--screen`, which `main.tsx` sets from `visualViewport` and scrolls back to the top on every resize, so the on-screen keyboard shrinks the shell instead of scrolling the page), and only the panels scroll inside it; `index.html`'s viewport has `maximum-scale=1`, `viewport-fit=cover` and `interactive-widget=resizes-content`, and inputs are 16 px, so iOS neither zooms nor pans. The tab strip becomes a dock at the bottom (Live, Room with its unread count, Artifacts with their count, and More for Back it, Code, Machines and About), respecting the safe area; the head shrinks to the status, title, figures and Back it; while the room's composer has focus the site header, the swarm head, the banner and the dock hide, so the keyboard leaves the conversation and the composer on screen; the transcript's moments put time and kind on one line above a full-width body; the home page's join tiles scroll inside their box; there is no footer. Since 2026-11-07 (Georgios: "make it also look great on mobile and fix the entire website to work great on mobile") an audit of every storyboard frame at 390×844 and 360×700 as a touch phone (`isMobile`, `hasTouch`) checks for sideways overflow, text that reads `undefined` or `NaN`, inputs under 16 px and tap areas, the last measured with `elementFromPoint` from each control's centre so invisible `::after` areas count. It found inputs at 12 to 15 px (the stake box, the steering amount, a tile's edit box, the branch and side-thread selects), which iOS zooms into, now 16 px under `pointer:coarse`; text links and small buttons 14 to 20 px tall (the side-thread link, speaker names, branch links and Stop, a result's summary, the clone Copy, the repository's Open link, the wordmark), now with invisible tap areas of at least 24 px; the stake note cut off at the right edge on a 390 px phone, now on two lines; and the docs tables of tools, routes and errors squeezed into columns a letter wide (`invalid_request` broke after each character), now one row per entry with its name above its text under 641 px. After the fixes the audit found no input under 16 px and no control whose tap area is under 24 px except those under an open side thread's drawer.
- `/board` (`Board.tsx`, lazy, outside the app shell) is the storyboard the user asked for on 2026-11-02 ("a view of all the states the system can get in"): every state as a live iframe of the fixture app at phone (390×844) or desktop (1440×900) size, grouped by flow (home, start, a swarm, the room, back it, around a swarm, X cards and the rest), each frame linking to its URL. Frames marked "scrolled" scroll the nearest scrolling box of a selector (or the page) to the bottom or top, so long lists, the transcript, the room's history, the ledger and the docs show their scrolled state; `?scene=many` adds a swarm per idea to the list. Frames that need input carry scripted steps (click, type, wait) the board runs inside the same-origin frame. `?scene=` puts the fixture world into a state: `wait` (requests never answer, loading), `down` (they fail), `empty` (no swarms), `in` (signed in), `hang` and `deny` (Tempo Wallet never answers, or declines), `broke` (an empty wallet), and `costly`, `rest` and `repo` (the pause reasons); links inside the fixture keep it. The same day's copy pass ("less text, more clickable things, 'Start a breakthrough', 'Be a part of the future'") replaced the home wizard with a headline, Start one and Join one buttons, idea cards and filter chips (Open, Live now, Needs fuel, Finished); the New flow is Pick, Fuel, Launch; a swarm that is not running shows a one-line callout with its action (Fuel it, Be the first, Read the result) and a Why? for the full reason; the funding modes are chips above the amount; not-found and offline pages have buttons. Checked in headless Chrome on the board at both sizes: no frame overflows sideways and phone frames do not scroll the page. X cards render in the board but come out blank in a full-page screenshot.
- The swarm page's tabs are Transcript, Room, Artifacts, Contributors, Code, Machines and About (on a phone the strip scrolls sideways). Ideas and Artifacts were taken out of the UI on 2026-11-02 at the user's request, to come back later, and Artifacts came back on 2026-11-05 ("bring back artifacts in the transcript / room"), with Contributors ("a Contributors leaderboard with the money ppl have given in so far"): `Contributors.tsx` ranks the room's members by what each put in (`Member.backed`, ties by who came first), with their share of the total, a bar against the top contributor, how they gave, what they put behind ideas and since when, the top three marked and the reader's row highlighted; there is no new endpoint, because the room already loads every member. `Ideas.tsx` stays out of the UI. The Code tab (`Code.tsx`) is a repository card: name, commits, files and age, the tree beside the open file (README first) with a sticky path bar, and the commits as rows that open their diffs. A streaming thought, message or tool input is shown whole as it grows, never as its last few hundred characters (the page kept the last 400 and the X card the last 600, so a long thought rotated in place).
- Pages render for any request that does not ask for something other than HTML (no `Accept`, `*/*` or `text/html`). Until 2026-11-02 they rendered only for `Accept: text/html`, so Twitterbot, which sends none, got 404 for `/s/<id>` and every other page and X showed no card, and `/robots.txt` was the app's HTML; `public/robots.txt` now allows everything.
- The fixture world (`?fixture`) plays a scripted live stream (`Fixtures.live`) through the same listener, with the agent and two subagents, so the transcript can be inspected without a running swarm.
- A completed turn is reported execution progress, not independently verified science. No fake research percentage, XP, token reward or investment return is shown.
- Storage keeps its old names where renaming would only cost a migration: D1 `swarms.rounds` counts turns, `reports.round` is a turn's number, and the object's `feed.round` column holds it. The object's constructor upgrades a swarm from the rounds model once: it renames its `rounds` table to `turns`, rewrites `feed.who` from researcher ids to their names from `researchers` and drops that table, and moves kv `round` to `n`, deleting `workers`. Each round became one update. Checked on 2026-11-02 under `vite` on a local swarm whose object was given the old schema by hand (a `rounds` table with two rounds, a `researchers` row, six feed entries, kv `round` 2 and `workers` 1): after a restart its tables were `turns`, `feed`, `chat` and `steered`, kv held `n` 2 and no `round` or `workers`, the subagent's entries read `who: "rust-subset"`, and `/v1/swarms/:id`, `/updates`, `/answer`, `/wait`, `/feed?who=&before=`, MCP `swarm_updates` and `swarm_feed` answered with the two rounds as updates, their `STATUS:` lines stripped. The rename is one way: the code before it would find no `rounds` table and start an empty one.
- Checked on 2026-11-02 in headless Chrome against the fixture world under `vite`, at 1440×900 and at 390×844 with iPhone 13 emulation (touch, mobile viewport): no page overflow on a phone (`scrollHeight - innerHeight` and `scrollWidth - innerWidth` both 0) on the home page, the New wizard, and a running, a paused, a funding and a done swarm, with each dock tab and each More item open; the speaker chips filter and set `?who=`; signing in with the fixture wallet, focusing the composer and shrinking the viewport to 500 px (the keyboard) left `--screen` at 500 px, `scrollY` 0, the room list pinned to its newest message and the header, head and dock hidden. Not run: a real agent turn on the gateway in this model (the continuation, `quickMs`, a real `spawn_agent` subagent's events and the 20,000-character completion), Safari or a real phone, and the real Tempo Wallet sign-in.

## How it works

- A swarm's money is three numbers: its `balance`, what it `spent`, and what is left (`remaining`), which is the balance less what was spent (2026-11-10, Georgios: "remove the notion of raised, just call it balance. that way its obvious that balance == all the money the system has capacity to pull, left is that minus what it has used up so far"). `raised` is gone: `Summary.balance` replaces it in `/v1`, MCP and `/openapi.json` (a breaking rename), and the page, the cards, the X and Telegram cards and the link previews say "$x balance" (`twitter:label2` is `Balance`; the previews' versions change once, so each picture is drawn again). The balance is every contribution: a transfer to the swarm's credit at its amount and an approved backing at its limit, or at what it paid once it ended, so it falls when a backing ends unspent. `remaining` was the gateway's figures (`pool_left + sponsor_left`) and is now `balance - spent` in D1's totals (`Store.ts`) and in the row `Money.refresh` keeps, so a contribution shows in what is left as soon as it is counted, before the next refresh reads the gateway; the swarm still starts and pauses turns on the gateway's own figure (`Money.refresh`'s `left`).
  - Production disagreed with that on 2026-11-10 (read-only selects of D1). 29 of the 47 listed swarms (36 of all 71) had more contributed than spent and left, $2.45 ($4.51 in all): money the old swarm wallets kept or lost to swaps and refunds, which was never moved to credit (`spent_before` holds only what their channels spent). And 7 swarms funded by the MACH channels of 2026-11-06 to 11-08 kept those channels' spend, $137.20 (f3a42cf87339 $84.69, f7df8db3fe54 $42.42), in `sponsor_spent`, which `Money.refresh` has overwritten with the backings' spend since 7cf2cce, so the next refresh of any of them would have dropped it from `spent`; none had refreshed since.
  - Migration 0030 moves `sponsor_spent` into `spent_before` for every swarm without a backing, then counts as spent, in `spent_before`, what a swarm's contributions exceed what it spent and has left, at most what its contributions from before the credit cutover (2026-11-10 01:20 UTC) exceed `spent_before`, so money counted after the cutover and not yet read from the gateway is never counted as spent. It runs before the deploy: until it has, this Worker shows that old money as left. Simulated in node:sqlite on a copy of production's money columns, afterwards every one of the 71 swarms' balance less spent equals the gateway's figures; `test/store.test.ts` covers the derivation and the migration (old channels, an old wallet's leftover, old and new money, a backed swarm, a contribution not yet refreshed). For up to 9 s after the deploy the swarm list may still come from the colo copy the old Worker cached (`Edge.cached`, 3 s fresh and 9 s stale), with `raised` and no `balance`; pages are cached per build. The persona's outside-services sentence now says "a twentieth of the swarm's balance" where it said "raised"; that limit was `Paying.passable`'s, which went with the credit cutover, so the sentence describes a limit Swarms no longer enforces, and it was left as it was otherwise. Checked under `vite` on the fixture world in headless Chrome at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone: the home line ("$13.47 balance"), All ideas, two idea pages ("$9.04 balance" over "$7.17 left"), Start and the X card, with no "raised", NaN or undefined, no console errors and no sideways overflow; `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and the node tests (136) pass. On production on 2026-11-10 (b12201d, version 8e531abb, deployed with `CTX=tempo wrangler ctx exec pnpm run deploy` right after 0030 was applied): D1 then read every one of the 71 swarms' contributions less spent equal to the gateway's figures; `/v1/swarms` listed 47 swarms, each with `balance` and none with `raised`, and every one added up; f3a42cf87339 answered $84.685281 balance, $84.685281 spent and $0 left, and the Fermat swarm (6f4541d2f211) $27, $17.237445 and $9.762555; `/openapi.json`'s Summary has `balance`, MCP `swarm_get` answers it, and a Twitterbot view of the Fermat swarm read "Needs funding · $27.00 balance from 1 backer" with `twitter:label2` Balance and its card answering 200.
