In headless Chrome at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone, the home ("33 ideas · $255.75 balance"), All ideas, two idea pages and Start showed no "raised", NaN or undefined, no console error and no sideways overflow. A 90 s tail held 171 events, all on the new version and none with an exception; its only error log was `/new`'s server render failing with `ReferenceError: location is not defined`, from the header in `App.tsx` reading `location.search` since 6ea3f53, so `/new` has rendered only on the client since 2026-11-08.
- Money is the swarm's credit at the gateway (2026-11-09, Georgios: "In my ideal world there'd be no intermediate wallet", then "I am OK with doing it at backing, that's simpler UX"). Everything below in this file about a swarm's own wallet, its sealed key, `Vault`, `Payer`, `Paying`, the `/pay` route and its secret, lanes, swaps, vendor purchases from the wallet, hand-owner channels, MACH channels a backer opens (`config.channel`, `Gateway.sponsor`), the `kv/get` probes, squeezes and `costly` describes code that is gone. What replaced it:
  - Backing is one `transferWithMemo` of MACH, OUSD, USDC.e or pathUSD from the backer's wallet to the gateway's recipient (`config.credit.recipient`, 0x40de…ECFE, the one address MACH can also reach). The memo is 32 bytes (`shared/Credit.ts`): the first 16 of `keccak256("gateway:credit:4217:<operator wallet>:swarm-<id>")`, which names the swarm's gateway app, then the first 16 of the old memo (`Transfer.memo`: `keccak256("swarm:<id>")`, or `keccak256("note:<id>:<note>")`), which commits to the note. `POST …/contributions { hash, note? }` reports the hash to the gateway (`Gateway.credited`, `POST /api/keys/credit` with the operator key in the swarm's app), which reads the receipt, credits the transfer once and answers who sent how much of what with which tag; Swarms stores the contribution from that answer and reads no chain itself. A note is kept only when the tag commits to it, a counted transfer can get its note later, a repeat is 409, a hash without a receipt 404, a transfer that does not name this swarm 400. `transaction` (a signed MACH open) is no longer taken. Every token counts at $1.
  - The page has one path (`Contribute.send`): `Wallet.send(token, recipient, amount, memo)`, then the hash. `choose` takes the first of MACH, OUSD, USDC.e, pathUSD whose balance covers the amount, and the page refuses before asking the wallet when the balance it read is short, where it used to fall back to a USDC.e transfer the wallet could not make (the bug of 2026-11-09: a config that came without MACH made a $25 backing a USDC.e transfer, and Tempo Wallet tried to swap MACH for it). `/v1/config` always lists the four tokens and has `credit: { owner, recipient }` in place of `channel`; it is null only while the operator's wallet cannot be read. A wallet that holds only MACH still needs someone to pay its network fee: Tempo Wallet sponsors fees for its partner origins only, and Swarms is not one.
  - The agent is created with `payer: "credit"` (tempoxyz/gateway's app credit, its AGENTS.md): the gateway pays everything the agent and its subagents do from the app's credit and from nothing else, and answers 402 when it is short. An agent from before is moved once, on its swarm's next turn (`Turns.lead` sends `PATCH payer: "credit"` and keeps `url: 'credit'` in the agent record). The gateway's `budget` tool answers `usd`, `spent` and `deposited`; the room's URL, which the payer's budget answer used to carry, is now in the first message (`Turns.roomed`), and in every Keep going for swarms whose frozen instructions still point at the budget tool.
  - `Money.refresh` reads `GET /api/keys/credit` (cue `credit`, in place of `chain` and `sponsors`) and writes `left` and `spent` to `pool_left` and `pool_spent`; the totals SQL is unchanged. A turn starts while the credit covers `Store.reserveOf(row)`. A failed turn pauses the swarm for money when the credit is under that, or when the turn's error is the gateway's credit refusal (`Turns.broke`). `Turns.settle` runs once, 15 s after a money pause and once no subagent is running: it reads the credit again, since the gateway's `spent` counts the holds of calls in flight, and puts the swarm back to work when enough came back.
  - Gone with it: `swarms.wallet` and `sealed_wallet` are no longer written or read (the columns and the old keys stay until someone needs the dust in those wallets), `SWARMS_SECRET` is no longer required, `Summary.wallet` and `Prepared.channel` are gone and `Prepared.wallet` is `to`. A swarm object's old payer keys (`channel:*`, `mppx:*`, `secret` and the rest) are left in its storage, unread. Local development needs no tunnel any more, since the gateway never calls back.
  - Migration 0029 folds each swarm's `pool_spent` into `spent_before` and zeroes `pool_spent`, `pool_left` and `sponsor_left`: it runs right before the deploy, after the backers' MACH channels were ended with `DELETE /api/keys/sponsors/:channel` and their contributions lowered to what each channel spent (`.scratch/cutover.mjs`, not tracked). What the old swarm wallets and their channels still held, $1.18 over 25 swarms on 2026-11-09 (23 of them operator checks under $0.05; 48dfb55dc725 held $0.31), was not moved to credit.
  - Checked on 2026-11-10 UTC under `vite` with local D1 against the production gateway (5370042), no tunnel: `/v1/config` answered the four tokens and `credit`; a swarm created with `template: free` and backed with 0.05 MACH from the test wallet (0x45cab3aa…f074, memo as `swarm_contribute` prepared it, which equals `Credit.memo`) was counted with its note (201; again 409; an unknown hash 404; a transfer for another app 400), started within 5 s, answered on DeepSeek V4.1 Flash for $0.001454 and paused as out of money with $0.048546 left, under the $0.05 a step needs. In headless Chrome on the fixture world at 1440×900 and 390×844, Back it · $5 answered "You backed it with $5" and an empty wallet "Not enough in your wallet" with nothing sent, with no console errors and no sideways overflow. `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and the node tests (118) pass.
  - On production on 2026-11-10 (4a2d342, version 4dc0f8bd, deployed at 01:20 UTC with `CTX=tempo wrangler ctx exec pnpm deploy`): before it, `.scratch/cutover.mjs --apply` ended the 19 live MACH channels of 7 swarms, which returned 0.644766 MACH to their backers, and lowered each contribution to what its channel spent (f3a42cf87339 then read raised $84.685281, spent $84.685281, $0 left), and 0029 was applied. After it the listed pages answered 200, `/v1/config` had `credit` and the four tokens, and `/api/swarms/:id/pay` answered 404. A new swarm, 06c34f19f11c (`template: free`), backed with 0.05 MACH from the test wallet (0x2c5efa7b…4d9d), was counted with its note, started 5 s later, answered for $0.000167 and paused as out of money with $0.049833 left; the gateway's credit and the swarm's row read the same. An older swarm whose agent still had the payer route, 98eb38605e98 (34 updates), backed with 0.06 MACH (0x8493ebf8…a0a0), woke, had its agent moved to the credit, posted two more updates for $0.02902 and paused with $0.03098 left. The gateway's `charges` for those turns are payer `credit` and sum to exactly what the credit spent; the operator key paid only the listings that publish stored items ($0.0005 each, $0.004 in all), and its channel moved by that much. Not run: a backing from the page with a real Tempo Wallet, and a wallet that holds only MACH.
- The templates name models the gateway serves through Boundless (2026-11-10, Georgios: "Let's make Swarms use the Gateway's new Boundless providers, since these don't require me to do anything", after the gateway's Vercel balance ran out that night and every `vercel/` model answered 402 for a while). `GET /v1/models` names who serves each model in `owned_by`; on 2026-11-10 Boundless served DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, GLM-5.3, GLM-5.3 Flash, Kimi K3 and K3.1 Pro, Claude Opus 5.5, Claude Fable 5.1 and GPT-6 Luna, and Vercel the rest (Haiku and Sonnet 5.5, GPT-6.1 Sol and Terra, GPT-6 Astra, Gemini, Grok). So `cheap`, still the default, is Codex on DeepSeek V4.1 Flash with subagents on DeepSeek V4.1 Flash or GLM-5.3 Flash (it led with GPT-6 Luna and offered Luna and Haiku 5.5), and `frontier-mix` is Claude Code on Opus 5.5 with subagents on Opus 5.5, Kimi K3.1 Pro or GLM-5.3 (it offered GPT-6.1 Sol and GPT-6 Astra); `frontier-anthropic` was already Opus only. `frontier-openai` (Sol, Astra) and what `free` and `custom` allow still reach Vercel, and a swarm keeps the setup it was created with, so the one `cheap` swarm of before still leads with Luna.
  - Checked on 2026-11-10 with credit agents in the gateway app `credit-check`, one turn each, the provider read from the gateway's `charges`: Boundless served DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, GLM-5.3 Flash, GLM-5.3, Kimi K3.1 Pro and Opus 5.5 under both Codex and Claude Code ($0.0009 to $0.078 a turn). GPT-6 Luna is listed as Boundless's but Boundless answered its agent calls 400 `upstream_rejected` ("the model rejected the request; you were not charged") and Vercel served them, which is why it is out of the templates. Not run: Kimi K3 and Fable 5.1.
  - Boundless is paid per call by the gateway, for a swarm's agent from the gateway's fee payer (tempoxyz/gateway's AGENTS.md on fronted keys: one OUSD session with Boundless, bought from its pathUSD). Nothing in the code refills that wallet: on 2026-11-10 it held 9.49 pathUSD (14.53 the day before), while the recipient the backers' money goes to held 875 MACH, 39 OUSD and 308 USDC.e. When the fee payer is empty Boundless is not paid and the gateway falls back to the model's other provider.
  - On production after the deploy of 269ffb0 (version 81a51fdc, 2026-11-10 02:05 UTC): a swarm created with no template, 7fe8cda84a22, answered `cheap` with Codex on DeepSeek V4.1 Flash and the two subagent models, was backed with 0.05 MACH, and its one model call was charged to its credit ($0.003077) with provider `boundless` in the gateway's `charges`.
- New swarms run on Anthropic, Claude Code on Fable 5.2 for the lead and every subagent (2026-11-10, Georgios: "the default should always be on the highest tier most frontier model types - for now lets make it be fable 5.2 only. so its anthropic, openai, frontier mix, abliterated (soon)"). `frontier-anthropic` is now Fable 5.2 only (it was Opus 5.5 only) and `Setup.fallback`, the API's default; its labels are Anthropic and OpenAI. Start offers Anthropic, OpenAI and Frontier Mix with their step costs ($0.066, $0.019 and $0.038 a step), then Abliterated, disabled and marked Soon; `cheap`, `fable` and `free` stay in the API, and `?template=` picks only an offered one. A swarm keeps the setup it was created with.
- One operator API key runs every swarm. `SWARMS_GATEWAY_KEY` is a `sk-tempo-` gateway API key that owns every swarm's agent, hands, machines and code repository, each swarm in its own `Gateway-App`, `swarm-<id>`, so swarms never see each other's data. It pays for none of a swarm's work: the agent is created with an agent payer (`POST /v1/agents` with `payer: { url: <SWARMS_URL>/api/swarms/<id>/pay, secret }`, tempoxyz/gateway 82d56b6), so the gateway asks this app to pay for every model call, MCP tool call (`compute_*`, `git_*`, `kv_*`) and subagent request the agent makes. A swarm keeps one agent for its whole life; `lead` creates it when there is none or the gateway answers 404, and `PATCH /v1/agents/:id` moves the payer when `SWARMS_URL` changes. The operator key pays only the app's own requests: `compute/list` and `compute/terminate` when a swarm that pauses or finishes leaves machines running. On 2026-10-31 the operator key was created from the test wallet 0x9759…e122 by its admin key with the gateway checkout's `.scratch/admin-install.mjs` (`REQUEST='{"limit":2,"days":365,"deposit":0.1,"topUp":0.1}'`): access key 0x34e15c9ba6ea1a7061b6077a6520adcc9e298b8d, installed in 0xdd043fd77d3cbaff1df73e37a16c62ee0e698a180211445bf9cedf3d998362dc, expiring 2027-10-31. On 2026-11-06 a test `compute_create` sent with this key needed more than the 2 MACH limit had left. The gateway answered `SpendingLimitExceeded` by retiring the key and closing its channel and hands, which stopped every swarm. It was renewed the same day with `POST /api/keys/update` (mode replace, so the `sk-tempo-` token and the agents stayed). The new access key is 0xa47b45c12b7588b56e3a11fe3e808fc1b72577b2, with a 20 MACH limit and 20.02 of each stablecoin, a 0.5 deposit and top-up, and the same expiry. Its key authorization went in an admin-signed transaction for the gateway's fee payer, whose only call is `getKey`, because the wallet's stablecoins could not pay the fee. Anything that spends from this key can retire it again once its limit runs out, so read `GET /api/keys/me`'s usage before spending from it. Agents expire with their key, so a new operator key means new agents, each primed with the last three updates. After three local swarms its `/api/keys/me` still showed no channel and its whole 2 MACH limit: it had paid for nothing.
- A swarm runs on frontier models by default, and its creator picks them through a template (2026-11-07, the user's request: "Swarms should use frontier models by default, and a swarm creator picks the lead model and the models its subagents may use, through templates"). `shared/Setup.ts` holds the templates: Frontier Mix (the default; Claude Code on Claude Opus 5.5 leads, and subagents may use Opus 5.5, GPT-6.1 Sol or GPT-6 Astra on Claude Code or Codex), Frontier Anthropic (Claude Code on Opus 5.5 only), Frontier OpenAI (Codex on GPT-6.1 Sol leads, subagents on Sol or Astra), Free For All (Codex on DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, the gateway's default that every swarm ran on before, with any subagent model) and Custom (the lead's harness and model and the subagents' harnesses and models, every model under every harness, or none checked for any). Leads: Opus 5.5 for the Claude templates, because at the gateway's prices ($4 input, $0.20 cached, $20 output per million tokens) it costs a third of Fable 5.1 and Astra ($10/$0.25-$1/$50), and a lead re-reads its whole context every call; Sol for Frontier OpenAI, a fifth of Astra's input and output price and a tenth of its cached reads, with Astra offered to subagents for the hardest branches; Frontier Mix as the default because Claude Code on Opus is the strongest lead at a middle price and Sol gives it a cheap parallel worker. `POST /v1/swarms` and `swarm_create` take `template` (default `frontier-mix`) and, for `custom` only, `harness`, `model` and `subagents` (`{ harnesses, models }`, or null for any; 400 otherwise). Creating reads the gateway's prices (`GET /v1/models`, kept 10 minutes per isolate as a settled map, `Gateway.prices`; 503 when they cannot be read) and stores the setup with its costs as JSON in `swarms.setup` (migration 0028, a column the Worker reads, so it runs before the deploy): `step`, a typical step of the lead (one call at a 50,000-token context, 48,000 of it cached, 2,000 fresh and 1,000 output tokens: $0.0376 on Opus, $0.0188 on Sol, $0.118 on Astra, $0.0017 on DeepSeek V4.1 Flash), `most`, an uncached step (50,000 input and 1,024 output tokens, at least the $0.05 `reserve` every swarm used: $0.2205 on Opus, $0.1102 on Sol; 1,024 output tokens because that is what the gateway holds for an agent payer's model call, tempoxyz/gateway's "Holds for keys and payer agents", so a swarm needs `most` on hand for the gateway to take its next call; the first version used 4,096, $0.282 on Opus, which no payer hold asks for), and `steps`, a typical step of each subagent model. `lead` creates the agent with the setup's `harness`, `model` and `subagents` (tempoxyz/gateway#477: the SDK lists only those in `spawn_agent` and refuses any other, and the gateway refuses a subagent request outside them). `most` replaces `Store.reserve` wherever a cheap model was assumed: a turn starts, and the swarm pauses as out of money, against `Store.reserveOf(row)`, the pause says "one step can cost up to" that (the money pause carries it as `needs`), the follow loop funds ahead from it, and `step` (at least the old $0.02) replaces `perCall` in the payer's busy and small-source checks (`Store.callOf`); swarms without a setup keep $0.05 and $0.02. `Status.fueled` (Resting against Needs money on the cards) compares what is left with the setup's `most` instead of $0.05. The first prompt (`brief`) tells the agent its setup, each allowed model's cost per call and to give each subagent a `budget_usd` of at least 20 of its model's calls (`Setup.told`), since a gateway subagent budget below its model's first call is refused. The swarm persona has a short `# Models` section after `# The work` (Georgios: "probably all these need appending to the system prompt a bit so the model knows what to do"): the first message names the setup, a refused spawn lists the allowed models to pick from, and since subagents do not get the persona (the gateway appends it to root calls only), each subagent's task carries which models it may give its own subagents. `/v1/config` lists `models`, every gateway model that calls tools with its price, which the New flow uses: its Fund step has a Models row (`web/Models.tsx`) showing the chosen template, its cost per step and what a step needs to start, which opens into the five templates with their costs and, for Custom, the harness and model pickers and checkboxes; the Fund card's minimum becomes the template's `most`, and Launch waits for a complete Custom choice. The swarm's About tab shows the template, lead and allowed subagent models with their step costs (`Facts`), and `Summary.setup` carries it (null for older swarms, whose About says they run on the gateway's default). `test/setup.test.ts` checks the templates, the costs, the reserve and call, the pause text and the brief. Checked under `vite` on the fixture world with headless Chrome at 1440×900 and as an iPhone 13: the Fund step showed Frontier Mix (default) at about $0.038 a step and "Needs $0.282 to start a step" (before `most` took 1,024 output tokens) with Minimum $0.28 and no page overflow at either size while closed (opened, the list scrolls the desktop page by 101 px, Custom by 337 px); Custom listed every model with its step cost; Frontier OpenAI launched a swarm whose About read "Frontier OpenAI, about $0.019 a step · Codex on GPT-6.1 Sol · Codex on GPT-6.1 Sol ($0.019), GPT-6 Astra ($0.118)", with no page errors. On production on 2026-11-07 (Swarms 850ed5d, deployed at 23:24Z after 0028 was applied; gateway 36260aa, which has tempoxyz/gateway b2bc40c's subagent policy and its migration 0076): swarm 3eaac25332a3, created with `template: frontier-mix` (its setup answered `most` 220480, `step` 37600 and steps of 37600 for Opus, 18800 for Sol and 118000 for Astra) and funded with a 0.25 MACH channel from the test wallet's admin key, ran its lead on Claude Code with vercel/anthropic/claude-opus-5.5 (its own report, and its cost: $0.1586 for the lead's turn against $0.0331 for the subagent); `spawn_agent` with harness codex and vercel/openai/gpt-6.1-sol ran and answered `SOL-OK vercel/openai/gpt-6.1-sol`; `spawn_agent` with @cf/deepseek-ai/deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017 was refused before anything ran with `model @cf/deepseek-ai/deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017 (claude) is not offered to subagents; offered: ...`, listing the six allowed harness and model pairs; and with $0.058 left the swarm paused as "Out of money: $0.058 left, and one step can cost up to $0.22". The test wallet's `requestClose` of the channel (0xf2a232463052d07e70240347fd87fdc091e29c0b53bc90f9970b9584a2909cf3) brought the 0.058291 MACH left back within two minutes; the check spent $0.1917.
- One kind of payment (2026-11-06, the user's requests "remove the pledge keyword altogether that's a huge complexity" and "we remove subscriptions. we have 1 kind of payment. we just send money. we dont mind breaking anything in the public api"): a contributor sends USDC.e, pathUSD or OUSD from any wallet to the swarm's wallet with `transferWithMemo`, then posts its `hash` and an optional `note` to `POST /api/swarms/:id/contributions` (or `/v1`, or `swarm_contribute`). The Worker reads the receipt and counts the TIP-20 `Transfer`s to the swarm whose `from` is the transaction's sender (so a swap's or a channel close's transfers into the wallet are not contributions); the contributor is that sender. A note counts only when a `TransferWithMemo` to the swarm carries `keccak256("note:<swarm>:<note>")` (`Memo.memo`; without a note the page sends `keccak256("swarm:<swarm>")`), so nobody can attach a note to someone else's transfer; a hash counted without a note can get its note later, and each hash counts once (409 after).
  - Gone: pledges (spending keys the app held, in MACH or a stablecoin, approved in the browser or through Tempo Wallet's device flow), daily and monthly budgets, the per-wallet spending key of `/v1/me/key` and `/v1/swarms/:id/backing`, and for a few hours MACH (next bullet). Their routes (`/pledges*`, `/budgets*`, `/me/key*`, `/backing`), the MCP tools `swarm_pledge`, `swarm_pledge_status` and `swarm_budget`, `Keys.ts`, `Devices.ts`, `Pledge.tsx`, `config.budget`, `config.mach`, `config.minPledge`, `Summary.daily` and `Detail.pledges` and `budgets` went with them, and the payer's sources are the swarm's wallet and the owners of lent hands.
  - MACH goes through the gateway (2026-11-06, the user's request "I still want MACH working everywhere, since we're using it via the gateway"; MACH cannot reach a swarm's wallet, next bullet, and the MACH swapper's `swapTo` reverted with `0xfc1faa65` both to a swarm's address and to the sender's own, simulated from 0x9759…e122). A contributor opens a TIP-1034 channel in MACH whose payee is the gateway's recipient, operator the gateway's fee payer and `authorizedSigner` the operator key's access key, with the swarm's memo as salt (`shared/Channel.ts`, `config.channel`), and the Worker registers it as a sponsored channel of the swarm's app (`Gateway.sponsor`, the gateway's `POST /api/keys/sponsors`). The gateway pays the swarm agent's requests from it before asking the payer and returns what is left to the contributor when it closes; the swarm holds no key. A client that can sign for a fee payer posts the open unsent as `transaction`; the gateway co-signs, pays the network fee and sends it, so a wallet holding only MACH can fund a swarm. A channel its contributor sent is counted by `hash` from its `ChannelOpened` event, and the Worker registers it with its descriptor alone. The page does the latter (2026-11-07): Tempo Wallet sends the open with `eth_sendTransactionSync` (`Wallet.open`), paying the network fee in a stablecoin the contributor holds, and the page posts its hash. Until then the page asked Tempo Wallet to sign the open for a fee payer (`eth_signTransaction` with `feePayer: true`), and a real contribution from production was refused with "The gateway did not open the channel: The channel's open transaction was refused: Invalid transaction: fee-sponsored transaction feeToken is not allowed". Tempo Wallet honours an app's `feePayer` only for first-party and partner origins (tempoxyz/wallet `apps/web/src/routes/remote/-lib/Sponsorship.ts`: gateway.tempo-dev.workers.dev, mpp.e2b.dev and xframes.nanocode.xyz), and the app's `accounts` provider sends no fee payer URL, so for swarms.tempo-dev.workers.dev it drops `feePayer` and signs a transaction that pays its own fee in its own fee token. Such a transaction deserializes with `feePayerSignature` undefined and a `feeToken`, while one signed for a fee payer has `feePayerSignature` null and no `feeToken`, because its sender does not commit to the fee token (checked with viem's `Transaction.deserialize` on both); mppx's fee payer then refused the wallet's fee token, which was neither the treasury's nor MACH, and could not have co-signed it anyway, since the sender's signature covers the self-paid payload. `contribute` now answers such a `transaction` with 400 before reaching the gateway: send it from the wallet and post its hash. A wallet holding only MACH cannot fund a swarm from the page until Tempo Wallet sponsors this origin. `contribute` refuses a channel whose payee, operator, token or signer differ, whose salt is not this swarm's memo (so a channel cannot be moved to another swarm or carry someone else's note), or under `least` (0.05). The contribution is the deposit, recorded with its `channel` (migration 0023). Each `refresh` reads the swarm's channels (`sponsorships`, `GET /api/keys/sponsors`), counts what they hold toward what the swarm can pay, keeps `sponsor_left` and `sponsor_spent` in D1 for the totals, and lowers a closed channel's contribution to what it spent. When the gateway asks the payer anyway and the swarm's own sources cannot pay, the refusal says why from that listing (`sponsor_left`, and `sponsoredMost`, the largest channel's unspent money, in the object's kv) instead of "Every contribution to this swarm is spent": no channel holds the request's price at once (with the total and the largest; the price is kept as `wanted`, as a `small` source's is), or the channels still hold money and a retry may find it, or, for `agent.fetch` and `vendor`, outside payments come only from the swarm's wallet. On 2026-11-06 the agent of f7df8db3fe54 was told "Every contribution to this swarm is spent" for its `judge` sandbox while its channels held money, and concluded that a per-call cap limited its H200 leases to about 280 s. The gateway refused a sponsor that is the key's own wallet, which is the wallet that owns the operator key and holds the user's MACH; tempoxyz/gateway#311 allows it. An expiring nonce lasts 25 s from signing and the gateway wants 15 s of it left, so a client signing for a fee payer must post within about 10 s; a later post is refused, and nothing is charged. `feePayer: true` alone did not get an expiring nonce. viem picks one only for a plain account, not for an access key or a configurable account, so on 2026-11-06 a real contribution from Tempo Wallet was refused with "fee-sponsored transaction must use an expiring nonce". The swarm wallet's swaps and sends ask for an expiring nonce (`Vault.ts`, where viem sets `validBefore`). The page asked for one too, from 2026-11-06 to 2026-11-07, with `validBefore` 240 s ahead, which Tempo would have refused: an expiring nonce's `validBefore` must be within 30 s of the block's timestamp (tempoxyz/tempo `EXPIRING_NONCE_MAX_EXPIRY_SECS`; the opens counted on 2026-11-06 had 22 and 25 s). Transfers still went through, apparently because Tempo Wallet's `eth_sendTransactionSync` dropped those fields (two pathUSD contributions to 98eb38605e98 on 2026-11-07, signed by a Tempo Wallet p256 key, had `nonceKey` 0 and no `validBefore`), and the open never reached the chain. The page now asks for none, since nothing it sends has a fee payer. The gateway's own transactions already did. Checked that day on production: the page's Fund card with a 1 MACH contribution, through a stub wallet that prepares with viem from the page's parameters and signs with the test wallet's admin key, and a 9 MACH open posted to `/v1/swarms/:id/contributions` as `transaction`. Both were counted, and the agent ran on them.
  - Checked on production on 2026-11-06: a 0.05 MACH open from 0x9759…e122, signed by its admin access key for a fee payer, was registered in app `mach-check` in 2.9 s (0xe0e483d2ef4a71d46fdc3d2ed77d78943b9b7bca888473a0b7d6ab067f2f73eb), paid three `kv/get`s while the key's own channel did not move, and closing it as operator returned the rest (0x92a9dd3af0f5141043fc543e706d873e7a58b113372f5b0ea4676e493796282b). After the deploy (db2b7452), `swarm_contribute` with `token: mach` prepared the open, the same key's signed open posted as `transaction` to a new swarm, 44926f7cc4e7, was counted in 3.4 s (0xc3a54b6b69bd1b38b8a573e2b6b4ecf5c88fdf1a7439c4eba3729134e67913fd, raised 0.05), the swarm started, its agent wrote its three haiku for $0.003658 paid from the channel, and it paused with $0.046 left, under the $0.05 a turn needs to start. Not run: Tempo Wallet signing the open in a browser.
  - The whole API headless, on 2026-11-06 against production (`/tmp/mach/headless.mjs`, the wallet's admin access key signing and the `tempo` CLI's access key sending): reads, the 16 MCP tools and the removed routes' 404s; a new swarm (98eb38605e98) funded by a MACH open posted as `transaction` over REST with a note (4.8 s; the wallet paid exactly the 0.05 deposit and the gateway the fee), one over MCP, one the wallet sent itself paying its fee in pathUSD and counted by `hash`, a pathUSD `transferWithMemo` with a note, and a USDC.e `tempo wallet send` by hash; a repost (409), another swarm's salt, a note the salt does not commit to, a deposit of 0.01, another signer, garbage, an empty body and an open posted 16 s after signing (the gateway's `needs at least 15 seconds of remaining validity`) all refused with no MACH moved; the swarm started from the channels and spent from them; sign-in with the access key, a username, a room post, an `@swarm` steer of 0.01, a side question, funding a subagent's branch with a message, a hand issued and attached with `nc hand`, every read route, `/live` and seven MCP reads. The first side questions answered 502 `agent runtime disposed` from the gateway twice while the agent moved between turns, and later ones answered. The contributor's `requestClose` of its never-spent channel then sat failing in the gateway's close supervision with `no voucher to close with` until tempoxyz/gateway#321 released such channels; the next pass closed it (on-chain deposit 0), the wallet got its 0.05 MACH back, and the swarm lowered that contribution to 0. A closing channel is now counted at what it spent (`sponsorships`), since the gateway takes no more vouchers on it and its row is ended only by the key.
  - A MACH open the gateway refuses is reported as refused, not as sent (2026-11-07, after the user's own MACH contribution from the Swarms page said "Sent, but not counted yet. The gateway did not open the channel: The gateway sponsors at most 5 channel opens a day for each wallet and signer…" and offered a Record it that failed the same way all day). The gateway refuses before it sends anything, so `contribute` passes its refusals through: 404, 409 and 429 (`rate_limited`) as themselves, its other 4xx as 400, and its 503 (it cannot pay network fees) as 503 `unavailable`; only another 5xx is still 502 `upstream_failed`. `record` in `Contribute.tsx` throws any answer under 500, and 503, for a `transaction` instead of keeping it as unrecorded, `explain` shows a gateway reason that starts "Nothing was sent or charged" under that title with no action, and an unrecorded MACH open is titled "$x is not counted yet" ("Not counted yet." in its text), since a 502 there does not prove the gateway sent it; a stablecoin transfer, which the page itself sent, keeps "went to the swarm". The gateway's limit was a flat 5 sponsored opens a day per wallet and per signer, and every swarm's MACH channel names the operator key's access key as signer, so 5 a day bounded every contributor together; tempoxyz/gateway#461 scales it with what the wallet spent through the gateway (5 for a fresh wallet, up to 200) and says when it resets and what works for MACH. The fixture scene `capped` (with `&token=mach`) answers that refusal, and the storyboard has it as "MACH open refused, nothing sent". Checked under `vite` on the fixture world in headless Chrome: `capped` showed "Nothing was sent or charged." with the gateway's reason and no Record it, `uncounted` with MACH "$0.10 is not counted yet." with Record it, and with USDC.e "$2.00 went to the swarm, but it is not counted yet." as before.
  - Signing in instead of failing (2026-11-07, the user's request "instead of saying no account, log the user in instead of that error"): `Wallet.connect`, which every payment calls, signs in with `wallet_connect` (`signIn`) when `eth_requestAccounts` answers no account or fails for any reason but the user declining, where it threw "Tempo Wallet returned no account.", and goes on with the payment once signed in. Every action that needs a session (`rename`, `say`, `steering`, `seat`, `unseat`, `fund`, `stop`, `move`, `btw` and `hand` in `api.ts`) goes through `signed`: a 401 (a session that expired or was revoked while the page was open) opens the same sign-in, one at a time for concurrent actions (`renewing` in `wallet.ts`), and runs the action again with the new session; only declining shows an error. Reads never open it, so a page load never pops up Tempo Wallet. The fixture scene `expired` holds a session whose actions answer 401: in headless Chrome under `vite` a room message posted with it appeared after the sign-in, and with `expired,deny` the room said "You declined the request in Tempo Wallet." and kept the message in the composer.
  - The old money was retired first (`Swarm.retire`, deployed in f1376b9 and run on 2026-11-06 by reading each of the 50 swarms): every pledge channel closed and every budget ended, returning what was not spent; f7df8db3fe54 returned 8.306698 MACH, and 1.9999 of a 2 MACH pledge made that evening, and five other swarms between 0.020674 and 0.112365. D1 then held every budget `ended` but four `pending` ones that never had a channel, and every legacy pledge ended or spent. The next deploy refused new pledges, keys and budgets with 410.
  - Migration 0021 adds `swarms.spent_before`, what legacy pledges and MACH budgets spent, folds what each of them delivered into `contributions` as `Store.totals` counted it (a pledge's spent part, a MACH budget's pulled part; ids `pledge:<id>` and `budget:<id>`, no hash), and sets every contribution's kind to `transfer`. `raised` is the sum of contributions, `spent` the pool channel's plus `spent_before`, `remaining` the pool's and `contributors` the distinct contributors. 0022 drops `pledges`, `budgets`, `pledge_devices` and `wallet_keys` once no code reads them. The spending keys contributors approved stay authorized on chain until they revoke them in Tempo Wallet, and nothing holds their private keys any more.
  - Checked on production on 2026-11-06 after 0021 and the deploy (3d198645): every one of the 34 listed swarms answered the same `raised` and backer count as before (only three running swarms' `spent` moved, by their own work), `/v1/config` listed only OUSD, pathUSD and USDC.e, the removed routes answered 404, MCP listed 16 tools, and a Worker tail over a minute of reads and the cron showed no error; 0022 ran after it. That deploy broke the X card: `'Finish on the swarm\'s page'` inside the card's template literal reached the browser as a bare quote, so the card's script did not parse and Fund did nothing. `card scripts parse` in `test/xframes.test.ts` now parses every inline script of a card, and failed on that commit. In Brave the fixed card's Fund then showed Open the swarm to pay linking `/s/<id>?fund=&amount=2`, and `/s/<id>?fund&amount=1&note=…` opened the Fund card with $1 and the note (it dropped the note before). Not run: a paying send through the page, because the test wallet held 0.004 of stablecoins and its 84 MACH cannot become any.
- MACH cannot be pooled. MACH's TIP-403 policy (compound policy 583: any sender, recipients from whitelist 582, any mint recipient) lets MACH reach only whitelisted merchants such as the gateway's recipient and the escrow. A `transferWithMemo` from a funded wallet to a fresh swarm address reverted with `PolicyForbids`, and the MACH swapper's `swapTo` reverted for any recipient but a merchant (checked on Tempo mainnet on 2026-10-30). USDC.e (policy 1), pathUSD and OUSD (policy 2) can reach a fresh address. That is why MACH reaches a swarm only through a channel to the gateway (above).
- Every swarm has its own wallet: a secp256k1 key generated when the swarm is created (or on first read for older rows), AES-GCM-sealed with `SWARMS_SECRET` in `swarms.sealed_wallet`, its address in `swarms.wallet`. It swaps the USDC.e and pathUSD it receives to OUSD on the stablecoin DEX (`swapExactAmountIn` with `minAmountOut` 0.5% under `getSellQuote`, paying the fee in the input token and leaving 0.002 of it) and pays the gateway from its own OUSD session channel, signed by its root key. Measured on 2026-10-30: 0.1 pathUSD quoted 0.1 OUSD (fee 0.000052 to 0.000351 per swap, 584,841 gas). Before each turn, and during one whenever less than `reserve` + `headroom` (0.1) is payable or the wallet holds unswapped stablecoins, `fund` swaps them.
- The payer is `POST /api/swarms/:id/pay` (`Operations.pay`, then `Swarm.pay`, one request at a time per swarm). It answers only the bearer secret the swarm generated for its agent (64 hex, in the Durable Object, compared in constant time) and only for the swarm's own app, and signs only a `tempo` session challenge to the gateway's recipient 0x40def0Cf3B5d28B6e9a7976BA10591e515AfECFE on chain 4217 for at most $10 (`Chain.cap`), or, for a lent hand's charge, an OUSD session challenge to the owner the gateway names in `recipient` (below), so a leaked secret buys gateway requests and hand operations from this swarm's money and nothing else. The cap was $1, what `git_create` costs, until 2026-11-06. An H200 sandbox with 8 cores and 64 GiB on its default 300 s lease costs $1.05 in a region the gateway prices at 1.75 times, so swarm f7df8db3fe54's `compute_create` calls were refused with "No single contribution to this swarm can pay this much at once", which named the wrong cause. A request above the cap is now refused before any contribution is tried, with what it costs and the cap (`Payer.priced`). A leaked secret could already spend the same total $1 at a time, so the higher cap does not widen what it exposes. Its source is the swarm's wallet (OUSD). Each source has its own mppx session client and channel in the object's storage (`mppx:<source>:`, `channel:<source>`). A channel opens with 0.1 or the request's price, whichever is more, bounded by what the source can put in (the wallet's OUSD). A voucher is the larger of the gateway's `requiredCumulative`, one past the highest voucher still in flight (signed in the last 10 s and above the accepted one) or else the accepted one, and `spent` plus this source's vouchers not yet accepted plus the price (without a snapshot, one past the last voucher signed); before 2026-10-31 it was always at least one past the last voucher signed, so every voucher the gateway refused or that crossed a refund raised the floor for good and the deposit had to cover it, the arithmetic the gateway's payer contract recommends, so refunded reservations are not paid twice; when the deposit is short the answer carries a `topUp` of at least 0.1 beside the voucher. A source that cannot pay is skipped for this request when its channel still holds a call's reservation (0.04, so it is only busy) or it could pay a smaller request, and rests otherwise: a wallet short of the token for 15 minutes, anything else for 10. A source whose credential the gateway refused on its third attempt rests, and a channel the gateway reports `closing` or refused with 410 is dropped so the next payment opens another. A source whose channel lacks deposit is also busy for 90 s after it last paid, because the shortfall is then mostly other calls' reservations, which the gateway refunds when they end: on 2026-11-01 a web search reserved about 0.18 (0.09 once the SDK sent `max_tool_calls`), two subagents searching at once held all of swarm 48ba542731e3's 0.4, and the next model call was answered `limit_reached`, which rested the wallet for 10 minutes and paused the swarm with 0.37 of it about to come back. A channel with a call's reservation (0.04) unspent counts as busy only until 10 minutes after its source last paid (`heldMs`): before 2026-11-02 it counted as busy for good, and swarm 06cf5d7c8f92's 2 MACH pledge, whose backer's wallet held 0 MACH so it could add nothing, kept 0.04 or more unspent but less than its next model call's reservation, with nothing else in flight; the payer answered 429 busy from 15:09 to past 16:18 UTC while the harness retried that one call every 15 s, and the turn never ended, so the swarm never paused. When the payer answers `limit_reached` because no single source can pay a request whose price it knows, it records that price (`wanted`); a failed turn then pauses the swarm as waiting for money rather than counting toward the three-error rest, and no turn starts until one source can pay that much (`costly` is the reason shown); a completed turn forgets it. Without it, that swarm, its wallet holding $0.003 and its pledge's channel $0.21 that could not grow, failed three turns, rested and woke at the wallet's 10-minute rest, which cost $0.0006 and posted two room messages every 10 minutes (16:26 to 16:28 UTC on 2026-11-02). When the only sources left are busy the answer is 429 with `retry-after: 2`, which the harness retries; otherwise 402 `limit_reached`, which fails the turn's request, and a turn that failed with less than `reserve` payable pauses the swarm with the amount and the time a resting source comes back (its alarm). New money wakes it. After a turn the object pays one `kv/get` (0.0001) from each source with a channel to read `spent` from its receipt, because the gateway reports `spent` only with each ask, and it includes reservations it refunds after the call.
- The lead learns its money from the gateway's free `budget` tool (tempoxyz/gateway, `Payments.budget`): the gateway posts `{ operation: 'budget', agent, app, … }` to the pay route with the payer secret, and `Swarm.budget` answers `{ usd, spent, raised, daily }` from the swarm's totals (`daily` is 0 since budgets are gone) (`usd` is `remaining`, what the swarm page shows as left). Before it, a lead running on the bare prompts knew only what earlier turns' prompts had said: on 2026-11-04 swarm d22c348174de, woken by a 2 OUSD transfer with $1.90 payable, thought "Budget: ~$0.25 payable" and then "essentially $0", and spent the next eleven minutes on code review and documentation it called free work, while about $0.03 a minute of model calls left the wallet.
- A swarm never decides to rest. It runs turn after turn while its money can pay, stops when the money runs out (`money`, `costly`) or after three failed turns in a row (`rest`), and new money or a note starts it again, steering a running turn when it carries a note. The user's rule of 2026-11-04 ("the swarm doesn't decide to rest - the thing captures the errors and stops when it's out of money, and when new money arrives it steers the swarm"). Before, a turn with no tool calls, or three quick turns without commits under $0.01, paused it as `idle` ("Resting") with money left, as "Solve P = NP." did with $9.22, and a reopened swarm rested too. When it stops for money its reason says what is left and that one step can cost up to $0.05 (`Store.reserve`), (`broke`): "Out of money: $0.021 left, and one step can cost up to $0.05. Fund it to keep going." Until 2026-11-06 it also named the next pull of a monthly budget (`coming`). Before 2026-11-05 it said "Out of money: $0.021 left, less than its next step needs. Adding money wakes it. More comes in at 21:32 UTC.", where the time was the earliest of every retry the swarm had scheduled (`wake`), so on "Solve P = NP." it promised money from a daily budget whose wallet held no USDC.e; the user asked what it meant. `Answer.spoken` rewrites the old reasons that paused swarms still carry. Swarms paused as `idle` before this have no alarm; `heal`, which every read of a swarm runs, sets one and the next step runs them. `heal` also sets an alarm for a working swarm (one with a turn) that has none, at most every 5 minutes: on 2026-11-05 the local swarm c77fde6b935c sat as running for 13 minutes after its payer route answered 500 while the dev server could not load the Worker, because the turn's failure was only noticed by a step, and no alarm was left to run one (an alarm that keeps throwing is retried a few times and then dropped); the next read after this change recorded the failed turn and the swarm went on. Its agent can decide to wait (2026-11-08, asked for by the session watching swarm f3a42cf87339 overnight): an update whose last line is exactly `Waiting for backers.` (markdown emphasis allowed, `Turns.waiting`) pauses the swarm as `idle`, which the page shows as Resting and `Phase.reason` as "Waiting for backers: new money, a steer or a message in its room wakes it.", once the gateway lists none of its agent's subagents as pending, queued or running (a subagent's completion would start a turn of the agent that Swarms does not follow). It shuts the swarm's machines down as a `rest` pause does. New money (`contributed`), a steer or a contribution with a note (`wake('note')`) and any message in its room (`say`) wake it with the usual Keep going. The persona's Wishlist section tells the agent when and how to say it. Before, nothing let it wait: f3a42cf87339 (Frontier Mix, Opus 5.5 lead) chose at 01:40 UTC to hold at 1.316x over SGLang with about $8 left until its Wishlist was funded, and Swarms answered each of its "still holding" updates with Keep going, about once a minute at $0.035 to $0.06 each. Checked on production after the deploy of 87cd3ae (version cd612fbf, 02:32 UTC), without steering or funding the swarm: f3a42cf87339's next turn ended its update with Waiting for backers. at 02:32:42, the swarm paused as idle at 02:32:43 with $2.86 left (its machines already shut down, so the sweep's listing found none), and 15 minutes later it was still paused with the same spend and the gateway held no charge of its app since. That one turn cost $0.48, where its holding turns before it cost $0.048, because the new persona text changes the system prompt every running lead caches, so each lead's first call after a persona change reads its context uncached. Not run on production: the wake on a room message, on a steer or on new money, which only the swarm's backers should send. (Superseded on 2026-11-08: a swarm stops only when it is out of money; see "Only money stops a swarm" under Campaign experience.)
- A top-up no longer makes the swarm's own channel count as $0 (2026-11-07). A top-up used to write the channel back as unconfirmed with the new deposit, and the money counted only confirmed channels, so a swarm whose last payment was a top-up paused "Out of money" with money still in its channel and never resumed, because only another payment confirmed it again. A read of the escrow and of D1 found 8 swarms in that state on 2026-11-07 (33a66a5137ee, 71ae8f963b9b, a8efb916f435, fc98b729378e, 2452f9b2260d, 3a05762b5254, ae64c263f6a1 and c40012cbf6af, all paused `money`), holding about $0.77 in their channels between them, each at least the $0.05 a step needs; five had paused within a minute of their last `TopUp`. Now a channel keeps its confirmed deposit and records the top-up as pending (`Payer.topped`), so what it counts never drops; every `refresh` reads the escrow for channels that are opening or have a pending top-up, in the same Multicall3 call as the wallet's balances (one viem client, `Payer.confirm`); and a channel is forgotten only when that read succeeded and showed no deposit 8 s after it was opened, where an RPC error used to read as a deposit of 0 and drop a live channel. `test/payer.test.ts` checks that a top-up never lowers the money counted. The 8 swarms resume spending what they hold on their next step after the deploy (a page view or any contribution).
- A swarm object's lifecycle is one record (2026-11-07, the user's request: "remove redundant states in our state machines and make any implicit state machines explicit"). `Phase.Run`, under the kv key `run`, is `new`, `waiting` (between turns, with when it runs next, the pause it was woken from and the last retry), `starting` (a turn id chosen and not yet confirmed), `working` (the turn, when it started, the stretch's start and the price no source could pay) or `paused` (the pause as data: `unfunded`, `money` with what is left and when a resting source comes back, `costly` with the price, `rest` with the failures). `Phase.check` allows only new or waiting to starting, starting to working or back to waiting, working to waiting or paused, waiting to paused, and paused to waiting; `Swarm.move` writes the record and D1's `status` and `why` through one projection (`Phase.project`: paused is `paused`, or `funding` before the first turn, starting and working are `running`), so D1 is written only when the projection changes. Before, the lifecycle was eleven kv keys (`turn`, `started`, `since`, `paused`, `why`, `failures`, `wanted`, `checked`, `n`, `refundAt`, `refunded`) read by `Phase.of`, with the pause stored as its sentence and parsed back by `Answer.spoken`, and every change in the money left rewrote D1 and logged another line. The sentence is now rendered when read (`Phase.reason`), and a pause logs only when its kind changes.
  - Waking is one rule (`Phase.wakes`): a pause for money or before any funding wakes on new money, a pause for one costly request (`costly`) or after three failed turns (`rest`) wakes on new money or a note, and a money pause also wakes when a resting source comes back (`Phase.due`). Waking moves the run to `waiting` and sets the alarm, and `costly` is forgotten then, so a turn tries again; there is no timed cooldown (the user's decision). Before, a room note woke only a swarm D1 said was running, so a `rest` pause ignored notes despite its own sentence, and `costly` stayed until a turn completed, which no turn could do: 48dfb55dc725 had been paused `costly` since 2026-11-06 15:52 with $0.314 left. `heal` arms an alarm for a waiting, starting or working run that has none, at most every 5 minutes, and never for a paused one, where it used to re-step every paused swarm (fund, refresh, pause: about five subrequests) every 5 minutes while anyone viewed it. `alarm` never pushes back an earlier alarm set during the step, so money that arrives while a step runs is acted on at once instead of at the step's own next time (up to 10 minutes).
  - Starting a turn is idempotent: the run records `starting` with the id `<agent>:<n>` before `POST /v1/agents/:id/turns` (the gateway's turn `id`), and a 409 for that id counts as already started, so an object that resets between the call and its bookkeeping no longer queues a second turn the swarm does not track.
  - The agent is one record, `agent` (`id`, `url`, `persona`, `cursor`, `briefed`, `unreachable`), replaced whole when the swarm starts a new agent, where six keys went stale: a new agent created without the persona (its handle still held by the old one) kept `persona: swarm` and never got the artifacts instructions.
  - One lock (`locked`) covers the wallet's swaps (`refill`) and a vendor purchase's buy and signature, and `refill` leaves USDC.e alone for 2 minutes after a vendor purchase so the vendor can pull it; before, a refill between the purchase and the pull swapped the USDC.e back to OUSD and the charge failed.
  - The settlement machine is gone: `done`, `settle`, `close`, `sponsor`, `refund`, `reopen`, `Payer.requestClose`, `Vault.send` and Phase's `settling` and `settled`, which nothing could reach since swarms are never done (D1 had no `done` row on 2026-11-07), and whose `reopen` left a `done` swarm in `settling`, where a step sent a prompt and then threw. The bullet above that starts "Done:" describes removed code. Operations no longer refuses a swarm whose status is `done`; migration 0025 turns any such row into `paused` and can run before or after the deploy.
  - Dead state is deleted once per object by a schema version (kv `v`), migrated in the constructor with the synchronous kv and SQL APIs only: the old lifecycle keys become `run`, a money pause becomes a `waiting` run woken from it (so the 8 swarms of the top-up bug re-check once on their next view), the agent keys become `agent`, and the keys nothing reads are deleted (`shelved`, `terms`, `available:*`, `left`, `expected`, the pool-key and pledge keys, the settlement keys, and every per-source key of retired pledges and budgets), and the feed's `round` column is dropped (`ALTER TABLE feed DROP COLUMN round`), which cost a kv read per logged entry and was never selected. The drop is one way: a Worker from before it would fail to log. The turn count is `MAX(n)` of the object's `turns` table rather than a kv copy.
  - A transfer that gets its note after it was counted is logged and announced as a note (`kind: 'noted'`) rather than as the same money again; a failed agent creation or prompt logs once per error per 10 minutes rather than every 30 s; and the payer tries the swarm's wallet whenever it has one rather than when D1's `pool_left`, a value `refresh` writes, says it holds something.
  - Schema version 2 (2026-11-07, the user's request to remove the object's redundant state) deletes kv `sponsored` and every `delivered:<channel>`, and marks the feed for indexing (`unindexed`, below). `sponsored` said that the swarm had a MACH channel contribution, which D1 says itself, and `delivered:<channel>` kept what `contributions.amount` already holds once a channel ended, only to skip writing it again. `sponsorships` now reads the swarm's channel contributions from D1 (`Store.channels`, through the covering partial index `contributions_sponsored` on `(swarm, channel, amount) where channel is not null`, migration 0026), asks the gateway for its listing only when there is one, and lowers a contribution to what its ended or closing channel spent only when D1's amount differs. `refresh` starts that read beside the wallet's balances. A swarm without channels now costs one index seek that finds nothing per sponsors poll (every 10 s while someone watches a turn, 30 s otherwise, and at each turn's start and end) where the kv flag cost nothing; without the index the read went through `contributions_by_swarm` and every contribution row of the swarm.
- The swarm object's own reads (2026-11-07, from a read-only query audit at a synthetic scale of 30,000 feed entries and 730 turns in one object, plans checked with SQLite 3.51):
  - `/wait` and MCP `swarm_wait` read the latest update newer than their start through `Swarm.latest`, the last five completed turns ended after it (a backward primary-key scan of 6 rows), where each 2 s pass read and parsed every turn (`pulse`, 730 rows and 732 pages) and ran `Store.find`; D1 is read once, when it answers. `/updates` and `swarm_updates` read only the last `limit` + 10 completed turns (`Swarm.recent`) and no longer resync search on every call, since every turn's end already indexes its update.
  - The detail poll's turns with commits use a partial index `turns_committed` (`ON turns (n) WHERE commits != '[]'`) and a `UNION ALL` instead of an `OR`, which kept scanning the table even with the index: 91 pages against 732.
  - A feed read filtered by speaker uses `feed_who` (`ON feed (who)`): 21 rows and 50 pages for a speaker with 21 entries, against every row of the feed through its detail overflow pages. `Swarm.feed` also takes `kinds`, so `/machines` can read only the kinds `Fleet` folds.
  - A search scoped to one swarm reads a contentless FTS5 index of the feed in the object's SQLite (2026-11-07, item 2 of the audit's db.txt), `feed_search` (`body`, `content=''`, `contentless_delete=1`, and the tokenizer of D1's `search`, porter on `unicode61 remove_diacritics 2`): each entry's `text` and the first 4,000 characters of its `detail`, under its `seq` as rowid, written by `log()` in the same synchronous block as the entry. The query is the terms quoted and joined with `OR`, as for D1, takes the `limit` best by `rank` (bm25), the newest first among equals, and reads those entries by key, best first. Without the tie-break, FTS5 hands equals over oldest first: on production a search of 2d4a1a9790ba for `standing` answered entries 298, 295, 292, … rather than its newest `Standing by.` at 5105. The audit measured 178 pages and 0.031 s on its 30,000-entry object against 0.096 s for the bounded scan and 0.51 s for the whole feed, 0.5 ms to index a 15 KB entry, and an external-content table over whole details 150 MB larger. Matching is by word stem rather than by substring (`kernels` finds `kernel`; `box` no longer finds `sandbox`), and only a detail's first 4,000 characters are searched.
  - Objects from before it are backfilled: schema version 2 sets kv `unindexed` to the feed's last `seq`, and each alarm indexes the 1,000 entries below it, newest first (`index`), and deletes the flag once none are left. Until then `search` falls back to the old scan of the newest 5,000 entries (`instr` over the lowercased text and detail: 279,067 VM steps and 0.096 s in the audit). A paused swarm has no alarm of its own, so `heal` and `search` arm one for a paused object with `unindexed` set (`reindex`), and its alarm comes back every second (`indexMs`) while entries are left; a waiting or working swarm indexes one chunk at each of its own alarms, because an early alarm would start a waiting swarm's next turn early. A chunk of 1,000 entries took 4-9 ms of a request to a Durable Object under `vite` (a third of them with 3-10 KB of detail; 2,000 took 15-40 ms), and the audit's CLI run indexed 63 µs an entry, so a chunk holds the object for at most about 65 ms. The index is one way like the dropped round column: a Worker from before it logs entries without indexing them, and rolling forward again leaves those out of the index.
  - `PRAGMA compile_options` and `sqlite_version()` answer `not authorized` (`SQLITE_AUTH`) inside a Durable Object, so the check was to use the table: under `vite` (workerd 1.20261023.1, whose SQLite source id is dated 2026-07-24) a Durable Object created `feed_search` with `contentless_delete=1`, inserted, matched `sandbox` to `sandboxes`, `train` to `training` and `cafe` to `café`, deleted by rowid and replaced a row. A throwaway Worker to run the same check on production could not be deployed: the account is at its limit of 500 Workers.
  - Checked on 2026-11-07 under `vite` with local D1 and a fake gateway (`/tmp/sw-e2e`, outside the repository): two swarms run by the code before, given 6,000 and 2,500 more feed entries, kv `sponsored` and a `delivered:` key, and for one a MACH channel contribution of 0.05 in D1, then 0026, then this code on the same state. Both objects read `v` 2 with neither key. Both were paused, and indexed their 6,006 and 2,503 entries in 7 and 3 alarms a second apart; a third, made by this code and set back to version 1 with 6,001 entries, kept the fallback while it was unfunded (`new`), and once funded indexed at its turns' alarms, about 5 minutes for 6,001 entries with a 6 s turn and a minute between turns. A word only in the oldest entry, outside the newest 5,000, was not found until its chunk was indexed, and then was. Entries logged meanwhile were indexed as they were logged (feed and index counts equal throughout). Woken by a contribution, the swarm with the channel lowered that contribution from 50,000 to the 30,000 its ended channel spent and set `sponsor_spent` 30,000, and only its app asked the gateway for sponsors (14 listings over about 8 minutes, none for the other two). New contributions were saved with `kind` and `budget` null after 0026 and without the columns after 0027.
  - Checked on production on 2026-11-07 after 0026 and the deploy of 5f1224a (version 55228352): a tail of the first 7 minutes held 59 events, every one `ok` with no exception. The paused 2d4a1a9790ba (5,109 feed entries), read once, indexed them in 6 alarms a second apart (26-34 ms wall and 2-4 ms CPU each), and its scoped search then found `keep going` in entries 65 and 83, outside the newest 5,000, while `sandb` found nothing and `sandbox` three entries. The running f7df8db3fe54 took the new code only at 07:19:48 UTC, about 7 minutes after the deploy (no event of its alarms reached the tail before then, while its turn went on), then indexed its 3,500 entries at its own alarms; a minute later `sandb` found nothing there either. Its `remaining` (3.9 to 4.7, with nothing in its wallet) kept coming from its four MACH channels, which `Store.channels` read with `rows_read` 4 (and 1 for a swarm with none). A 0.0005 pathUSD transfer from 0x9759…e122 to 98eb38605e98 (0xf14e37a54b5d99feafeefe99348e2b0857cd772843dcca26c73c080083e07783) was counted (raised 0.1008 to 0.1013) and stored with `kind` and `budget` null, and posting it again answered 409. Then 0027 ran and 138598d (the tie-break) was deployed (version 33e50585): production's `contributions` has `id, swarm, contributor, token, amount, note, note_id, created_at, channel` and `swarms` no `workers`; a second transfer of 0.0003 pathUSD to 98eb38605e98 (0xab7d411840aadac78a42ca0b0addfa3c81d37b9e4d3a7c23e822d648278011ae) answered 201 and raised it to 0.1016; the list, details, backers and the global and scoped searches answered, and `standing` on 2d4a1a9790ba answered 5105, 5102, 5099, … newest first. A tail of the next 3 minutes held 53 events, all `ok` but a browser's cancelled artifact read and one alarm cancelled by the deploy itself, after which f7df8db3fe54 went on logging its turn.
  - The turn-end check for stored items and the sweep's check for started machines count feed entries after a position (`working.from`, kv `sweptSeq`), a primary-key range, where they scanned the whole feed by time once per turn and per pause.
  - `Store.epoch`, which invalidates the object's memo of its D1 row and is part of the detail and chat ETags, is kept per swarm. It was one counter per isolate, so a neighbouring swarm object's write every 10 s dropped every co-located idle swarm's memo and its early 304s. Writes that change no swarm row (names, notes on counted transfers, removed steers, `delivered`) no longer count, and the object marks itself after `delivered`.
  - After `refresh` writes the pool figures, the object keeps the row it wrote (`remember`) instead of reading it back, and reads issued hands from D1 only when a hand it did not know connects, where it read them every 10 s while any hand was connected.
  - A turn's end settles only the closed branches D1 does not already show as settled (one read), with every claim in one batch (`Store.claim`), where it wrote a claim on the primary for every closed branch at every turn's end, one round trip each, forever. `release` claims a replaced agent's branches in one batch too.
  - A steer that cannot reach a running turn describes its note with one note's sums (`Store.note`) rather than weighing every note, and `Swarm.budget` reads the memoized row.
- A running swarm streams instead of polling (2026-11-07, the user's request: "reduce the need for polling as well when a system can be instead streaming"):
  - The object follows the agent's session through the gateway's event stream (`GET /v1/agents/:id/events` as `text/event-stream`, `Swarm.listen`) in windows from its cursor that last until the next read is due (at most 5 minutes, at least 10 s; below), digesting each stored event as it arrives, where it paged the events every 2 s. Deltas are asked for (`live=1`) only while someone watches `/live`; a watcher that arrives mid-window ends the window so the next opens with them. The same stream feeds the watchers, so there is one upstream per swarm and each entry is sent once, with its seq, where a watched swarm opened a second upstream and sent every entry twice (once with seq 0). A stream that does not open, or closes within 5 s, twice in a row falls back to paging every 2 s until a window works again.
  - The reads the 10 s refresh made are triggered by what the events show (`Follow.planned`, a cue debounced 5 s and at most one read of a kind per 30 s): the turn's outcome from the stream's `result` event, which carries the turn's status, message and error (read with `GET …/turns/:turn` only when it does not, else every 5 minutes), the repository after `git_create`, `git_commit`, `git_remote` or a `git push`, artifacts after a `kv_set` or `bucket_put` under `artifacts/`, lent hands after a `compute_*` machine call, the wallet's balances after the object's own channel opens, top-ups, swaps and contributions, and sponsored channels after a contribution; besides the cues, lent hands every minute while any is connected or one was issued in the last hour, sponsored channels every minute while a room socket is open, and everything else every 5 minutes. A cue that arrives during a window closes it when its read is due (`Swarm.cue`, `Follow.until`), so a window lasts until the earliest due read.
  - Event-driven turns, 2026-11-07 (the user's request: "make the swarm↔gateway interaction event-driven end to end … make an idle swarm cost ~nothing"). Each window used to last 10 s and every read had a fallback of 10 s to a minute, so a running swarm made about 10 gateway requests and 6 alarm invocations a minute whatever its agent did; and every turn's start and end re-read things that had not changed. Now:
    - The persona is set only when its text changed (`Persona.ensure`): a 64-bit FNV-1a hash of the description and instructions is kept in the object's kv `personaSet` once the gateway accepted that text, and the isolate remembers the version it set (a settled string, no shared promise). Before, every isolate set it again on its first `lead`: the gateway counted 845 `personas.set` (0.0005 each from the operator key, $0.42) in the 24 hours to 2026-11-07 07:00 UTC, about 0.8 per turn (294 in the hour with 359 turns).
    - A turn's start lists hands only when the hands read is due, and the hand caps reuse that listing (`Money.cap` with the listing from `refresh`), listing only when a hand is known to be connected; before, every turn listed hands twice, connected or not. The end lists hands only when due.
    - Without a repository, `Repo.located` lists the app's repositories only after a git cue or every 30 minutes (kv `repoLooked`), where it listed them at every turn's start and end and every 5 minutes. With one, a turn's start uses the head the object last saw (kv `seen`, `seenAt`, `Repo.saw`) unless a git cue came since, and its end reads the commits only when a git cue came during the turn.
    - A turn's end lists subagents to settle closed branches only when a subagent was added, changed status or completed since the last settlement (`Swarm.branched`, kv `settledAt`), publishes artifacts only after an artifacts cue, and pays the `kv/get` probe of the swarm's wallet channel only when that channel signed a voucher since the last probe (kv `probed:<source>`): a swarm paid from contributors' MACH channels made two `kv/get` requests and a voucher of 0.0001 every turn for nothing. `expose` lists only the kinds the turn stored (kv, bucket, git), where it listed all three.
    - A cue's reads skip nothing that matters after a restart: `Follow.cued` counts anything since the object started (`Swarm.born`) as cued.
    - Measured under `vite` against a stub gateway (`/tmp/swarms-res`, outside the repository: it plays a turn of tool calls every 20 s, an artifact `kv_set` at 30 s and a `compute_create` at 45 s, and logs every request), one funded swarm each, no watcher. Turns of 150 s for 7 minutes: 121 gateway requests before (42 event streams, 19 sponsor listings, 17 hand listings, 9 turn reads, 7 repository lists, 6 artifact reads, 9 `expose` lists) and 44 after (11, 7, 4, 2, 1, 4, 5), with the same feed entry for entry and the same 2 updates; with a room socket open the whole time, 143 before (43 sponsor listings, 41 event streams) and 55 after (12, 17), with the same feed and the same room messages. One 10-minute turn, counting from 2 minutes in to the end: 84 requests before (47 event streams, 16 sponsor listings, 9 turn reads, 8 hand listings, 2 repository and 2 artifact reads), about 10.5 a minute, and 7 after (3 event streams, one each of the rest), under one a minute; the windows (and so the object's alarm invocations) went from 47 to 3. Not measured: a production turn, since no swarm ran after the deploy.
  - Measured on 2026-11-07 under `vite` against a fake gateway and Tempo RPC (a Node server that plays a turn's events, answers the multicall and forges a contribution's receipt), counting its requests for 60 s in the middle of a 400 s turn: the code before made 30 event pages, 6 turn reads, 6 hand listings, 6 repository reads, 6 balance multicalls and 2 artifact listings (56 requests, 30 alarms), plus one more upstream with a watcher; this code made 6 stream requests, 1 turn read, 1 hand listing and 1 multicall (9 requests, 6 alarms), with or without a watcher. With the stream answering 503, it fell back after two tries and paged; the turn ended and `/wait` answered `updated`.
  - `/wait` and `swarm_wait` park one call in the object (`Swarm.waited`), which every transition of the run releases, instead of a 2 s loop of object and D1 reads: a wait opened during a 12 s turn answered `updated` when the update was posted, with one object call and one D1 read.
  - The room socket pushes what the page used to poll for, as typed in `shared/Api.ts`: `welcome` with the stamp's version when it opens; `pulse` (the summary, reason, why, working and hands) on every transition of the run and every change in the money or machines `refresh` writes; `changed` with the parts to refetch (`detail` and `members` on a contribution, `detail` on an update, a steer or a found repository, `feed` when an entry is logged between turns, `branches` on a subagent's start, status or completion and on a fund, move or stop, `hands` on a hand that joins, leaves or is issued, `code` on a new commit); `artifacts` with the list when it changes; and `roster`, which `Swarm.rostered` broadcasts for Operations' team changes. `pulse` and `changed` are coalesced to one each per 2 s, and nothing is computed while no socket is open. A socket opened with `?listen=1` (forwarded by `Operations.chatter`) gets every message but is not counted as watching and takes no `hello`. The object's version (the detail and chat ETags) now moves only for the kv keys a view reads (`run`, `hands`, `repo`, `owner`, `id`) and for its SQL writes, where every cursor, buffer and bookkeeping write moved it, so a running swarm never had a quiet stamp and its polls never got the early 304. Checked under `vite` with a room socket and a listening one: a contribution brought `said`, `pulse` (paused, then running with the new money 2 s later), `changed` (detail, feed, members), and each update `changed` (detail); presence counted one watcher.
- A turn (`steer: false`) starts when the sources can pay at least `reserve` (0.05) now, together. While it runs the object pages `GET /v1/agents/:id/events` every 2 s into its feed and every 10 s reads the turn, the sources and `GET /v1/hands`. Nothing limits a turn's length or the number of turns, so a swarm runs until its agent reports the goal met (`STATUS: done`) or its money is gone. Three failed turns in a row pause it, except turns that failed because the money ran out. Sandboxes the agent left running are terminated when the swarm pauses or finishes (`sweep`), not between turns. Before 2026-11-02 a swarm worked in rounds, one turn each, and a round got one explorer per `perWorker` (0.1) beyond the reserve, up to 3; every round's end terminated the sandboxes.
- Contributors steer with money. A transfer may carry a note; contributions with the same note (normalized) back it together, and a note's weight is the money sent behind it, stablecoins at par. A note that arrives during a turn steers it (`steer: true`) with its weight and its share of everything contributed, and every continuation lists the notes by weight.
- Done: there is no settlement any more (2026-11-07; nothing had called `done` since 70c199a on 2026-11-02, and its reopen path could start a turn nothing tracked). A contributor gets unspent MACH back by closing its own channel, which the gateway's close supervision answers; money sent to a swarm's wallet stays the swarm's.
- A swarm object starts with `initialize` (the old `upgrade`): it creates the payer secret on first use. The pool-key branch that revoked main's OUSD gateway keys of 2026-10-31 is gone, since every swarm has long passed it.
- Code: a swarm's code lives in a public repository on the gateway's `/v1/git` (code.storage over MPP), in its own app and owned by the operator key, when the agent decides to make one with `git_create` (the swarm's money pays the $1 through the payer). Nothing in the swarm's messages asks for one since 2026-11-03 ("remove the (2) stuff"): the repository instructions, the $1.04/$1.19 launch gate (`Store.repoAt`, `Store.launch`, pause reason `repo`) and the $2 floor that existed so one contribution could pay for the repository are gone. Before, from 2026-11-02, the prompt asked for the repository first, as soon as one source could pay $1.04, and no swarm started until $1.19 was payable. The URL carries a token, so every feed entry, report and live delta is redacted (`https://***@`, and any `eyJ…` JWT becomes `[redacted]`). The app reads the repository without a key through the gateway's public routes (`GET /p/<operator wallet>/swarm-<id>/git[/<name>/log|files|read|diff]`, gateway 5b5c47d): after each turn it takes the first public repository of the app (an agent once named one `wc` instead of the name it was given), records the head before the turn as the update's `base` and the commits after it as its `commits`, and the update's feed line names them. While a turn runs, every 10 s check (`follow`) also logs each new commit on main as a feed entry (`watch`: `Pushed <sha> to <repo>: <title>`, from the turn's `base`, kept in `seen`), so commits show up in the activity log as they land. The page's Code section (`Code.tsx`, loaded lazily) reads the same routes from the browser: a file tree with @pierre/trees, the selected file at the head with @pierre/diffs' `File`, and each update's diff (`diff?ref=<last commit>&base=<base>`, or each commit's own diff for a first update with several) with `PatchDiff`. `/p` answers `Cache-Control: public, max-age=60`, so the page adds the time of the latest update to its URLs. `/s/<id>/code` (`Repo.tsx`, lazy) is the full code browser: a searchable @pierre/trees tree of the selected commit, marked with that commit's changes (`gitStatus` from its diff), the commit's diff with `PatchDiff` or a file at it with `File`, and every commit on main (50 at a time with `cursor`); `ref` and `path` stay in the URL. While the swarm is not done it polls the log every 15 s with a 15 s bucket in the URL, past `/p`'s cache, follows main unless a commit is pinned, and highlights new commits for 12 s. Checked on 2026-11-02 in headless Chrome under `vite` with the fixture swarm pointed at a local server answering `/p`'s log, files, read and diff from a git repository: the tree, the head commit's diff, a root commit with its added files marked, and a commit made while the page was open, which appeared and was selected within one poll.
- Checked end to end on 2026-10-31 under `vite` against the deployed gateway, with agent payers and public reads live, the pay URL reached through a cloudflared quick tunnel, every key approved by the test wallet's admin key, Codex leads on the gateway's default model:
  - Swarm 2d8a3a0efd0e, funded by 0.25 pathUSD (0x9569dcc52eb9ae5432726fd76fa78e4ba843e21026326dd224a472e8f3008322, swapped in 0x5fc4d701ac485991c325070a05ba151cc308bf720485135a443a307dfbd53fe1), ran one round on the swarm's wallet for $0.028264 and reported done without a repository (its goal did not require one). Its wallet's channel closed through `requestClose` (0x7b7864531353e79ea6ad97516664200f219320ea716082dcb4f133a6f215f0de) and 0.215709 OUSD went back (0x499058e86b4c1d9dafa261fcaf8af4d2fa245b5055a096d4e7e06682fed1a6de).
  - Swarm 21ad54110b8e, funded by 0.25 pathUSD (0xb35420f5025d8e644179e4872666d5b98be6ab68f5826e29cdeb1b39c1e3a9f1), a 1.3 MACH pledge and a 0.25 pathUSD-a-day budget, ran two rounds. The wallet paid the first calls; `git_create` ($1) was more than the wallet held, so the payer moved to the pledge (`Paying from 0x9759…e122's pledge now`), whose channel opened with exactly $1, and then back to the wallet. A 0.05 pathUSD transfer with the note "Also support a -c flag that prints byte counts" steered round 1, which implemented it; the follow loop then pulled 0.086055 pathUSD from the budget (0x0e22f4a6d28e40fcbbb5bfdfa22f4b41129395b33085267d502f4ff7977c8cf2) and swapped it (0x462de2cf7e8808863805c2f46ccb1990682c04ef151cdd07233c1b510b360fd0). Round 1 created the public repository `wc` and pushed wc.py ($1.028919, commit 90b3db0); round 2 pushed tests and a README ($0.024071, b0af684) and reported done. The page's Code section showed the tree, wc.py and round 2's diff (headless Chrome). At done the pledge's channel was asked to close (0x7ec09a780373882f4a294592c722ef1945d684479d53f21f0fb72c737b3e4f11, nothing to return: its $1 was spent) and the wallet's (0xb07b2d9953709ae5fe1dd7ebee1715f2fb0126cdaecaeeacd1baf15095d0d0a7). That run refunded 0.179642 OUSD at once (0x63d277e97373897fef62073061403f7bb1ebc01406588fa847919f2632d91477), before the channel's 0.147 had come back, because it waited for an expected balance the OUSD held outside the channel already met; the late 0.146988 OUSD was returned by hand (0xf66297da4b7e68c22cc42ce3c0b4cc9963fb2c3aedcfa142a6dca6a7fe258883), and the refund now waits for the channel's on-chain deposit to reach 0.
  - Swarm bc0470739d55, funded by a 0.25 MACH pledge and 0.05 pathUSD, showed rotation when a source runs out: the pledge paid until the transfer was swapped (0x030b27d6b20555f02aa952451367dec5369af69ce697b1618abfd511f1874ae8), the wallet paid next, then could not cover the next reservation (`It can add 0.000000 now, less than the 0.044925 its channel needs`), rested and the pledge paid the rest. The round cost $0.003074. At done the pledge's channel (0xff2f80fae42c0627b83f8e58164f4821755925fef6fdd027b261d98b7e30988f, 0.09742 MACH back) and the wallet's (0xb503714db04ece216c0f95227f5dec67832bee3d1a7bb53cc8d58c895335f318) closed through `requestClose` sponsored by the swarm's wallet, and the refund waited for the wallet's channel to close before returning 0.043444 OUSD (0x8fb6b612d2bbef41e14ff30eff9f71d985955a0e6d99a8be345249f0bbc66083).
- `Swarm` (one Durable Object per swarm, binding `SWARMS`) runs it all on alarms.
- The page funds a swarm one way (`Contribute.tsx`): Tempo Wallet's `eth_sendTransactionSync` with a `transferWithMemo` of USDC.e, pathUSD or OUSD to the swarm's wallet, whose memo is `Memo.memo(swarm, note)`, then `POST …/contributions` with the hash, or in MACH the channel open above, sent the same way and counted by its hash. The swarm's wallet links to the explorer. The box prefills from the link `swarm_contribute` hands out: `?fund` opens it, and `token` (`usdce`, `pathusd`, `ousd`, `mach`), `amount` and `note` fill the fields.

- A subagent's start is logged once, and a lead that stops making progress is noticed (2026-11-08).
