The gateway re-sends a subagent's `added` update whenever its agent session reopens, so swarm f3a42cf87339's feed logged "Started a subagent: autoresearch branch: attention4" about every 45 s from 05:24 to 08:30 UTC (200 identical entries in a row) while its lead's session was stuck in a reset loop (tempoxyz/gateway's `spawner` bullet); `Trail.subagent` now answers no step for an `added` of a subagent the crew already names (`crew` is kept in the object's kv, so this holds across reconnects and deploys). The same swarm showed "On it for 3 h" the whole time, because any event, those replays included, counted as the agent being reached. Now the follow loop records the time of the last model call, tool result, message or finished run of the lead or a subagent (`progressed` in kv, `Follow.advanced`), `working` answers `progressed` and `stalled` (no progress for `Clock.stallMs`, 20 minutes, chosen without measurement), the feed logs one error line saying so (`Follow.stalled`, once per stall), and the swarm's status line reads "On it for …, no progress for …". `test/trail.test.ts` and `test/follow.test.ts` check both. On production after the deploy of 37fb79f (version ff368c1d, 08:45:51 UTC), swarm f3a42cf87339's feed logged no further "Started a subagent" for a known branch, also across the gateway deploy at 08:56 that reset its lead's session and reconnected its stream (it had logged attention4 again at 08:42:21, before the deploy), and `GET /v1/swarms/f3a42cf87339` answered `working` with `progressed` and `stalled: false` while the lead worked again.

## API and MCP

- Every operation lives in `Operations` as a function of `(env, input)` returning a `Problem.Result`. The web app's `/api` routes, the public `/v1` routes (`Rest`) and the MCP tools (`Mcp`) are thin adapters over these functions, so validation, errors and side effects match on every surface. That includes transfers (`contribute`, `transfer`), which `/v1` serves under `/v1/swarms/:id/contributions`, and the wallet a swarm from before 0003 gets on its first read. Each operation parses its own input with Zod, and the schemas have no transforms: the MCP SDK validates a tool's arguments with the same schema and passes on the parsed value, so a transforming schema (the pledge amount to atomic units) failed when the operation parsed that value again.
- Every page reads only what the public API serves (the user's request of 2026-11-02, so anyone can build another UI or scrape it): `/v1` has everything `/api` does, plus `GET /v1/problems` (the idea bank, each problem marked open or with the swarm on it), `GET /v1/swarms/:id/machines` (`Fleet.fleet` over the feed and the lent hands: the Machines tab, with each machine's settings and command history) and `GET /v1/swarms/:id/backers` (the Ideas tab: ideas ranked by money and every backer), with MCP tools for each (`problems_list`, `swarm_machines`, `swarm_backers`).
- State machines made explicit so bad states cannot happen: `Swarm.fund` is single-flight (`funding`), so two swaps of one balance never run at once; the Fund component cannot pay again after a payment until Fund again is tapped; Lend your machine is a `Step` union (closed, naming, issuing, failed, issued).
- Pages render on the server and stream (the user's request of 2026-11-02). `src/worker/Page.ts` answers `/`, `/join`, `/new`, `/about`, `/docs` and `/s/:id` for HTML requests (listed in `run_worker_first`). It writes the head of `index.html` at once, then the page's own title, description and Open Graph and X tags, then React's `renderToReadableStream` output of the same `App`. The page's data comes from the same `Operations` the API uses, bounded at 1.5 to 2.5 s so a slow read degrades to client loading: the swarm list, or a swarm's detail and its room. It is followed by `window.__SWARMS_BOOT__` with that data, the config, the server time, the visitor's time zone (cookie `swarms_tz`), whether the device is a phone, and the remembered wallet (cookie `swarms_face`, which `wallet.ts` mirrors from localStorage). The server uses `#ssr` (`src/web/server.tsx`, aliased in `vite.config.ts`), and its components read the request through `boot.ts`: `here()` for the URL, `seeded()` for data (a failure is seeded as `!key`), and the server snapshots of `useMedia`, `useWallet` and `usePath`. `main.tsx` loads the route's module and hydrates (`pages.tsx` keeps the code split while letting the server and hydration render a page without Suspense), so the first paint is the final layout, and polls seeded on the server wait one interval before refetching. Times are formatted in the visitor's zone on both sides. A missing swarm answers 404 with its page. Fixture and storyboard URLs (`?fixture`, `?scene`) stay client-rendered. Checked on 2026-11-02 under `wrangler dev` on the build: every listed page renders with JavaScript off, and hydrates on phone and desktop with no React warnings.
- Anonymous pages are edge-cached (2026-11-03, `Edge.page`): `/`, `/join`, `/new`, `/about`, `/docs`, `/s/:id`, `/s/:id/code`, `/x` and `/x/s/:id` for a `GET` or `HEAD` without `authorization`, without the `swarms_face` or `swarms_d1` cookie and without `?fixture` or `?scene` are kept in the isolate (48 pages) and the colo's Cache API under `/__page<path>?<sorted query>&__variant=<build>|<phone or desktop>|<swarms_tz>` (`/x` pages have no variant), fresh for 5 s and served stale up to 10 minutes (60 s before 2026-11-06; below) while one redraw per key runs in `waitUntil`; the response says `x-page-cache: hit|stale|miss`. A miss streams as before and tees its body into the cache, and only a 200 whose data was all gathered in time and rendered without error is kept (`whole` from `Page.draw`), so a 404, an error page or a degraded render is never cached; a signed-in visitor (face cookie) and a visitor who just wrote (bookmark cookie) always render fresh, so no cached copy carries a wallet. Polls seeded from a cached page wait only what is left of their interval after the page's age (`boot.now`), so a page drawn 40 s ago refetches at once. Measured on 2026-11-03 under `wrangler dev` on the build with local D1 and a swarm holding Solve P = NP's 200 feed entries, 98 messages and 47 updates: a cached `/s/:id` answered in 7.7 ms p50 (`/robots.txt`, a static asset, 5.3 ms), against 36.6 ms rendered before.
- A server-rendered swarm page ships the last 30 feed entries (Earlier loads the rest) and the last 30 room messages (the room loads only what came after them once its socket opens, or after 3 s without one; before 2026-11-06 it reloaded its 100 on mount), only the updates with commits and the newest (all `Code.tsx` reads), and `/s/:id/code` ships no feed (its own poll reads none). `/api/swarms` and the list in boot data carry each answer cut after its first telling line (`Answer.lead`; the tiles show `Answer.gist`, which it keeps); `/v1` and MCP keep the whole answer. Measured the same day: `/s/:id` 176 KB to 66 KB (30 KB to 14 KB brotli), rendered in 22 ms instead of 37 ms; `/s/:id/code` 91 KB to 12 KB; production's 22-swarm list 46 KB to 20 KB (17.5 KB to 6.5 KB brotli). HTML and JSON were already brotli-compressed by Cloudflare. Headless Chrome at 1440 px and on an emulated iPhone 13 hydrated every listed page, cached or not, with no console warnings, and Earlier took the transcript from 25 beats to 181.
- Performance pass of 2026-11-06, after a read-only audit of production (headless Chrome at 1440 px and on a 390 px phone profile with 150 ms latency, 1.6 Mbit/s and 4x CPU, against main bac2718):
  - The 5 s detail poll (`/api/swarms/:id`) shipped every update. On 1cadc9cc84d4, 55,147 of its 57,139 bytes were 707 `updates`, and the swarm object read and parsed every turn row on each `view`. `Operations.stamped` now asks for `pushed`: the object reads only turns with commits and the last three completed ones and sends the updates with commits and the newest, the subset `Code.tsx` reads and the server-rendered page already shipped. `/v1` and MCP keep every update. `view` also sends only the last 20 ended turns, and `Store.ledger` reads only the last 40 reports, for `Search.lags`, which compares only turns at or after the oldest of those 40, so an object still on older code, which sends every ended turn, does not start a sync on every poll; before, both read every turn and report on each poll. There is no cron: an unscoped search's `Search.lagging` checks the last 40 turns of every swarm (the D1 queries bullet of 2026-11-07).
  - Polling while the live streams are open: in 120 s on a running swarm, a tab made 51 API requests: 24 detail, 13 feed, 10 chat, 9 team and 4 artifacts. Now:
    - The detail poll runs every 10 s instead of 5 s while `/live` pushes entries.
    - The feed poll no longer restarts on each detail change while `/live` pushes entries; it still runs every 15 s.
    - `useLive` counts a follow stream as pushing once any event arrives on it. A follow stream gets every logged entry with its seq. Before, it counted only after the first entry, so a running swarm between turns had the feed poll run every 2 s. In 120 s on 1cadc9cc84d4 after the first deploy of this pass, a tab made 28 feed requests; a minute after this fix it made 4.
    - The room polls every 15 s only while its socket is closed, and otherwise once a minute. Every message goes through `speak`, which broadcasts it.
    - `useRoster` shares one request and one 60 s timer per swarm. Before, each of its three callers fetched `/team`, so three requests went out at once.
  - `/branches` p95 was 3.6 s on 1cadc9cc84d4 and 6.0 s on 06cf5d7c8f92, against a p50 of about 220 ms: a 5 s cache per isolate fell through to the gateway's subagents call. Branches are now `Edge.cached`, fresh for 5 s. After that, a read waits up to 1.5 s for the refresh (`patience`) and otherwise gets the copy kept in the isolate or the colo, up to 10 minutes old. A fund, stop or move writes the list it reads afterwards (`Edge.put`). A read whose isolate copy is past fresh also checks the colo and takes the newer copy. Without that check, the e2e below failed twice: after a steer raised alpha's budget to $0.08 and beta was stopped, a reloaded Branches tab showed both at their first state ($0.03 budgets, no funders, Working). The stale copy came from another isolate's memory.
  - The edge page cache hit on none of 44 browser loads. A page was kept for only 60 s, so a swarm with less than about one view a minute per colo always missed, and a miss took 800-1,144 ms to first paint on desktop against 520-590 ms for a stale hit. `pageStale` is 10 minutes. Polls seeded from an older page revalidate at once (`boot.now`). The time-zone variant stays, because times render in the visitor's zone on the server.
    - The key now starts with a hash of the build's `index.html` (`Page.built`). A page drawn before a deploy names chunks that the new deploy no longer serves. Right after the deploy that changed `Swarm.tsx`, a stale `/s/1cadc9cc84d4` loaded in headless Chrome and made no API request in 120 s: it never hydrated. Loaded again once redrawn, it polled as usual. The previous build's `Swarm` chunk (`Swarm-D6CMg54P.js`, rebuilt from 00e4a41) answered 404 on production once the new build was live. With the old 60 s window this could only happen in the first minute after a deploy.
  - Layout shifts:
    - The swarm page's `chat` (handles, steers) started empty until the room mounted, so the transcript's rows changed from short addresses to @handles and moved 18 px about 0.3 s after hydration (CLS 0.128 on the phone for 06cf5d7c8f92). It now starts from the server's room data.
    - Tab buttons grew 2 px when a count badge arrived (CLS 0.0106 on every desktop swarm). They now have a minimum height that fits the badge.
  - First paint of a swarm whose last 30 entries are all hidden status lines (06cf5d7c8f92) said "Nothing here yet." above its money rows, with no Earlier button, because `first` came from the shown entries. The empty line now needs no rows at all, `first` falls back to the raw entries, and the transcript loads one earlier page by itself when nothing is shown, keeping the view pinned to the bottom. The server render also leaves out the status lines the transcript hides (`Answer.settled`, now shared). When fewer than 10 entries are left, it adds what an earlier page of up to 200 holds (`Page.shown`), so such a swarm renders rows at once. Filling on the client alone shifted the page by 0.077 on the phone: the short list sat at the top, and the full one was pinned to the bottom. That swarm's 30 hidden lines are "Out of money … More comes in at <time>", logged every 15 minutes because the time changed each retry. The wording of 2026-11-05 has no time, so `pause` no longer logs it again.
  - `Artifacts.list` now fills its isolate memo on a colo cache hit, with the time the colo copy was written (`x-listed-at`).
  - Found by the web e2e of 2026-11-06 (swarm f331c7adcb98):
    - Right after a $0.50 launch, the head said "Not started" and "Add $2 to start it. Its funds cannot pay for a first step yet." The swarm was still `funding` with money in and no `why`, and its first step ran seconds later. `Visual.standing` now calls that "Starting".
    - The custom amount box (`.say-own input`, 4ch wide with border-box padding) cut "0.05" to "0.0". It now sizes to its content between 2 and 10 characters, and is 6 characters wide where `field-sizing` is unsupported.
    - Right after signing in, the account is unknown until `/api/me` answers, so the composer is open and the username form is not shown yet. A steer sent then made its transfer, and the steer itself was refused with `username_required` (on edbd09aeed99, 0.05 USDC.e reached the swarm as a plain contribution). `payIn` now calls `Wallet.named` before any money moves: it waits for the account and refuses an unnamed one, which brings up the username form.
    - Sign-in started two `/api/me` reads at once: `signIn` and `WalletButton` each called `identify`. If a read answered after a rename, it put the unnamed account back. `identify` now shares one read per token and ignores an answer that started before a rename.
  - Measured on production after these changes, with the same tools as the audit:
    - Detail poll bodies: 1cadc9cc84d4 went from 57,139 to 2,236 bytes, and 2d4a1a9790ba from 77 KB to 2,322 bytes. `/v1/swarms/:id` still carries every update, for example 729 on 1cadc9cc84d4.
    - A 60 s trace of a desktop tab on 1cadc9cc84d4 made 15 API requests: 6 detail, 4 feed, 2 artifacts, 1 team, 1 chat and the live stream. The audit's tab made 51 in 120 s.
    - `/branches` p50 was 165-174 ms. A miss still took up to 1.6 s on 1cadc9cc84d4 and 7.0 s on 06cf5d7c8f92.
    - Pages: the second load of a swarm page was `x-page-cache: hit`.
    - Phone CLS on 06cf5d7c8f92: 0.128 in the audit, then 0.0775 with the client-only fill, then 0.0004 after the server render reached back. Desktop CLS there went from 0.016-0.034 to 0-0.0002. 1cadc9cc84d4 measured 0 on the phone and 0-0.0024 on desktop.
  - Web e2e (`/tmp/pw/e2e-web.mjs`, Playwright with headless Chrome against production; the Tempo Wallet chunk is replaced by a stub that signs with a fresh local key, so no access keys, pledges or monthly budgets):
    - The scenario: a new wallet launches a swarm from /new with its own brief, then signs in and picks a username. It steers with @swarm and new money, and posts in the room. On the Branches tab it steers a branch, asks it on the side, shares the answer to the room, steers the lead with the side thread, moves $0.01 between branches and stops one as its biggest backer. Then it lends a machine and opens every tab, and the page is checked at 390 px. Throughout, it watches for console errors and 5xx responses.
    - On 2026-11-06, swarm fc98b729378e (launched with $0.30 in OUSD) passed 26 of 26.
    - The runs before it found the bugs above. One of them, 2d8a96dc7e56, failed the side-thread steer on Workers AI's per-minute rate limit (`3021`), which tempoxyz/gateway#294 now retries and reports as a rate limit.
    - `/tmp/pw/e2e-pages.mjs` loads `/`, `/join`, `/new`, `/about`, `/docs`, a swarm, `/x`, `/x/s/:id` and an unknown path at 1440 and 390 px: 18 of 18 passed, with no console errors, no 5xx and no horizontal overflow.
    - The e2e wallets' leftover money was sent back to the test wallet 0x9759…e122.
  - Not changed: the minute salt on the detail ETag (budgets' views depend on the time), the font preloads and the markdown stack (about 60% of the 235 KB raw Swarm chunk).
    - The audit's phone experiment cut first paint by 250-370 ms without the font preloads and with `fetchpriority=low` on module scripts. Run again with four cold loads per variant on 06cf5d7c8f92, the rewritten HTML was no faster: first paint median 1,644 ms as served, 1,936 with low priority, 2,224 without font preloads and 1,920 with both. The rewritten HTML went uncompressed, so the experiment cannot tell.
  - The audit also found the two running test swarms posting "Standing by." or "No new task." about once a minute for 12 hours, each a paid turn ($0.00044 each on 2d4a1a9790ba). That is the user's rule of 2026-11-04 above: a swarm never rests on its own.
- Cold paths, 2026-11-06 (the user's request: "Optimize all cold things w/ better queries / ways to talk to the backends"):
  - Every `/api` and `/v1` response carries `Server-Timing` (`Timing`): the time spent in D1 (`d1.<region>` with the region that answered, or `d1` for `first()`, which reports none), in swarm-object calls (`do.<method>`), in gateway calls (`gw.<path>`) and in the colo cache (`cache`), each with its count, and `total`. `Timing.database` patches each D1 statement's methods rather than wrapping it in a Proxy, because `batch` needs the real statements. `Timing.object` wraps the swarm-object stub. It calls methods with `Reflect.apply`, because `method.apply` on a JavaScript RPC stub asks the remote object for a property named `apply`: every call through the first version answered 500.
  - What one D1 round trip costs: reads came from `wnam` at 40-50 ms from a Worker in CMH. That replica is in the primary's own region (`served_by_colo` LAX), and no nearer one answered. `/team` and `/branches` were not in `Replica.readable`, so their reads went to the primary itself, at 70-80 ms each. They now go through the read session too. So the work went into making each cold request one round trip, with D1 and the swarm object queried at once:
    - `/team` read the swarm, then its members: 147-170 ms of D1. Now `Store.rostered` reads both in one batch, and a roster held in the isolate answers without D1 (`Team.cached`). 39-50 ms.
    - `/backers` read the swarm, then its members and notes: 130-156 ms of D1. Now `Store.backed` reads all three in one batch, beside the swarm object's view. 41-51 ms of D1.
    - `/branches` read the swarm and the subagents, wrote `Store.adopt` (an update on the primary that seldom changes anything) and then read the money and the members: 282-334 ms of D1. Now the swarm row, the subagents and every branch ledger row of the swarm (`Store.ledgers`, which then picks the agent's own and legacy rows) are read at once, and the adoption runs after the answer. About 85 ms of D1, beside the subagents.
    - `/chat` and `/hands` check the swarm and read their D1 rows at once. The swarm list adds its ids to the isolate's known swarms (`Store.know`), so later detail, backers, room and branches reads of those swarms ask the swarm object straight away instead of after D1.
  - Gateway calls:
    - `Gateway.wallet` (the operator's wallet, from `GET /api/keys/me`) cost 510-992 ms on every new isolate, and `/artifacts`, `Artifacts.serve` and `Code.proxy` waited for it. It is now kept in the colo cache for a day (`operator/<hash of the key>`) and refreshed in the background for 30 days. A failure falls back to asking the gateway.
    - The calls now use the `GATEWAY` service binding. Gateway `wrangler tail` then showed the swarms' requests without `cf-connecting-ip` or `cf`, where before they came from a Cloudflare address in ORD. It did not make `/p` reads faster, though: `gw.p.kv` stayed at 83-117 ms. On a cache hit at the gateway, `/p` took 260-330 ms from the Mac against 85 ms for a static asset. That is the gateway's own time (its rate limiter and its D1 in WNAM, read replication `disabled`), not the trip there. `/p` limits reads per `cf-connecting-ip`, so `Artifacts.serve` and `Code.proxy` now pass on the viewer's address. Reads with no viewer behind them share one bucket, as they shared Swarms' egress address before.
    - The gateway's subagent listing took 114 ms to 4.7 s, and `/branches` waited for it on every isolate and colo with no copy. The swarm object now shares one listing in flight and keeps the last list for 2 s in memory and 10 minutes in storage (`subagents`, under 1 MB). A read returns the stored list at once and refreshes it in the background; a write (`fund`, `stop`, `move`) always asks the gateway. Once stored, `do.subagents` answered in 25-34 ms instead of 336-1,025 ms. A list can be one refresh behind, and the page polls every 15 s.
  - Wasted D1 work:
    - D1 insights for the day before showed 10,077,448 of 11.2M rows read coming from `delete from search where swarm = ? and kind = 'report' and ref = ?`. That statement ran 3,833 times and read about 2,630 rows each time. `search` is FTS5 and its `swarm`, `kind` and `ref` columns are unindexed, so every delete scanned the whole table, once for each turn that ended. Migration 0019 adds `search_rows`, which maps `(swarm, kind, ref)` to the FTS rowid. `Search` deletes by that rowid (`SCAN search VIRTUAL TABLE INDEX 0:=` against `INDEX 0:`), and inserts the mapping in the same batch from `last_insert_rowid()`, only when the row at that rowid has the same `swarm`, `kind` and `ref`. Checked with `wrangler d1 execute --local`: a report replaced twice left one row and one mapping. On production, all 4,758 mappings pointed at their own row. Swarm objects still on the previous code added 7 rows without a mapping before they restarted. `insert or replace into search_rows ... where rowid not in (select row from search_rows)` mapped them.
    - The swarm object's `refresh` (every 10 s during a turn) wrote `update swarms set machines, pool_left, pool_spent` and one `update budgets` per source each time, changed or not: 19,855 swarm updates that day. Every write also invalidated the object's memoized rows (`Store.epoch`), so the next step read them again. Both writes now happen only when a value changed. `updated_at` therefore moves when money or machines move, not every 10 s.
  - Sessions:
    - Every signed-in request read its session from the primary (`Sessions.store`), 70-90 ms from CMH. Now a session is read from the replica first and from the primary only when the replica does not have it, as it does not just after sign-in.
    - The isolate keeps a session it read for 30 s, so a request signed in on that isolate no longer reads D1 for it. A sign-out deletes the session at once on its own isolate. Another isolate can still accept the token for up to 30 s, which was judged acceptable for a random bearer token.
  - The swarm object read its active and its spent MACH keys as two queries, the spent ones on every `refresh` with no memo: 22,714 and 20,879 runs that day. `Store.held` reads both in one query, kept in the object's memo (`mach:<id>`).
  - A desktop swarm page with a repository no longer loads the Code viewer and the highlighter when the browser is idle: that was 247 KB brotli of JavaScript on every such visit, used only by those who open Code. Hovering, focusing or touching the Code tab still loads it first (`warm`), and the snapshot (`Api.code`) is still fetched at once.
  - The Worker build is minified (`environments.swarms.build.minify`), and `/mcp` and `/openapi.json` import their modules on first use. The upload went from 19,607 KiB (3,668 gzipped) to 14,300 KiB (2,940). Cloudflare reported a Worker startup time of 105 ms before. It reported 129 ms with minification alone, and 79 ms with the lazy imports too. `wrangler check startup` measured 46-124 ms both before and after, so most of that spread is noise.
  - Fixed from a read-only bug hunt run the same day, each checked against the code:
    - A server render that failed (`Page.draw`'s catch) left the HTML stream open: the browser got `<div id="root">` and nothing more. An anonymous page's background redraw then waited on that body for good, so its isolate never redrew the page again. The catch now ends the document with the boot data emptied, so the client renders from scratch, and closes the stream.
    - A background subagent listing that finished after a write's fresh one stored its older list over the newer one in the object's storage. That brought a stopped branch back as Working for one cache cycle. Only the latest listing is stored now. It is written without touching the object's stamp, so detail and chat ETags no longer change on every branch refresh, and it is stamped with the time it started.
    - `stop` handed back what the branch had left in the listing read before closing it, but the branch kept spending until the gateway closed it. It now uses the branch's budget and spending from the gateway's answer to the close, as the object's own `settled` does.
    - Two concurrent fundings or moves of one capped branch each set the gateway budget from a total they read before the other's steer landed, so the last call to arrive could set the lower total. `Swarm.rebudget` now reads the branch's base and funding from D1 at the time it sets the budget, one call at a time per object. Checked on production: two $0.01 fundings of f57d8e240d4b's branch 1, sent at once from existing backing, both answered 200 with a budget of 0.06, and the gateway's budget read 0.06 (base 0.03 plus 0.03 funded).
    - A branch tray chose its move target when it mounted. A second branch that appeared later could not be chosen, so Move stayed disabled. A target that was stopped stayed chosen while the select showed another branch. The target is now derived on each render from the branches still live.
    - Two submissions of the same transfer at once both announced it and both steered the agent with its note, though `insert or ignore` counted it once. Only the request whose insert or note update changed a row now tells the swarm object, and only it answers `created`.
    - Monthly budgets showed what was left of the period as "left today".
  - Checked after each deploy with `/tmp/pw/cold.mjs`, which reads each endpoint of 8 swarms once from fresh isolates, and with the web e2e below.
- Tempo Wallet connection (fixed 2026-11-02 after "That did not go through. No accounts connected."): the accounts provider keeps its own connected accounts in IndexedDB, separate from the app's remembered address and sign-in session. A visitor could therefore be signed in (a session token) or remembered (the face cookie) while the provider held no account, and `eth_sendTransactionSync` then threw `DisconnectedError`. `Wallet.connect` no longer trusts the app's state: it asks the provider for `eth_accounts` and falls back to `eth_requestAccounts`, and `send` always goes through it, so paying reconnects Tempo Wallet when needed. A different account than the session's ends the session. `explain` names a disconnected wallet plainly.
- Disconnect (2026-11-06, the user's request: "allow disconnecting my account when i hover on it … so i can log in with another"): hovering or focusing the header's wallet button shows a Disconnect row under it; on a touch screen the first tap opens it and a second tap on the button goes to the explorer, as the join rows do. `Wallet.disconnect` signs out, forgets the address, @handle, balance and the remembered `swarms.wallet`, and sends the accounts provider `wallet_disconnect`, because otherwise its `eth_accounts` would hand the old account to the next payment and the next Sign in; that also drops the access key the browser held, and the next account is offered one again (`keyless` resets); a `restore` still waiting for `eth_accounts` drops its answer. Tempo Wallet's own session at wallet.tempo.xyz stays signed in, so choosing another account happens in its prompt. Checked in headless Chrome on the fixture world at 1440 px (hover, keyboard Tab, a click still opening the explorer) and on an emulated iPhone 13 (tap opens without navigating, a tap outside closes, no overflow), and on a page with a stored session, where Disconnect cleared `swarms.session`, `swarms.face`, `swarms.wallet` and the face cookie and a reload showed Sign in. Not run: a real Tempo Wallet sign-in afterwards with another account.
- More explicit state: `Phase.ts` holds the swarm's run record and its transitions (the bullets after "A swarm never decides to rest." describe it). The Fund component is a `Flow` union (idle, busy, unrecorded, done, failed). The wallet has a `pending` phase: a remembered profile shows at once but is not a link, and the room gate and composer show a neutral placeholder, until Tempo Wallet confirms it, so a signed-in backer never sees Sign in or Fund to steer for a moment.
- The rest of the 2026-11-02 state audit is in: a paused or unfunded swarm carries `why` (`unfunded`, `money`, `costly`, `repo`, `rest`) beside its `reason` text, set where `Swarm.pause` is called and served by `/v1`, MCP and OpenAPI, so no client parses the sentence; the transcript's follow mode is `live` or `reading`, and only `live` scrolls with new rows or a resize.
- `.scratch/video.mjs` records an end-to-end run of the fixture world (home, join, a swarm, steering with $1, ideas, the Fund tray, machines and their logs, lending a machine, artifacts, starting a breakthrough, and the X card through You're in); the 2026-11-02 recording is `~/Desktop/swarms-e2e.mp4`.
- `/v1` is the public REST API, described by `/openapi.json` (OpenAPI 3.1, request bodies from `z.toJSONSchema`). `/v1/*`, `/openapi.json` and `/mcp` answer CORS for any origin, and the web app's `/api` stays same-origin. Errors are `application/problem+json` with a stable `code` (`Problem.codes`). `run_worker_first` lists these paths, because the assets' single-page fallback would otherwise answer them with `index.html`.
- The thread model changed the public API on 2026-11-02, without a version bump (the user's request was to have no rounds anywhere): `GET /v1/swarms/:id/rounds` and MCP `swarm_rounds` are gone, replaced by `GET /v1/swarms/:id/updates` (`limit`, the newest last, with `working`) and `swarm_updates`; `Detail` and the overview lost `history`, `running`, `researchers`, `rounds`, `workers` and `parallel` and gained `updates` and `working`; `answer` is `{at, done, text}`, without `next` and `round`; feed entries lost `round` and the feed takes `before` and `who` (a subagent's name, or `agent`) instead of `round`; search hits of kind `report` are `update` and lost `round`; `wait` takes `after` in milliseconds and answers `reason` `updated`, `idle` or `timeout` with the latest `update`; `/live` events are `start`, `delta`, `entry`, `end` and `idle` (no `round`, `text`, `thinking`, `message` or `researcher`); a room steer's `round` is `live`, a boolean; `/v1/config` lost `perWorker`. Any outside client of those fields breaks.
- Vite's dev server answers `OPTIONS` itself, without `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, so preflights are checked against the build (`wrangler dev -c dist/swarms/wrangler.json`). There on 2026-10-30, `OPTIONS /v1/swarms` and `OPTIONS /mcp` answered 204 with `*`, and `/mcp` exposed `mcp-protocol-version` and `mcp-session-id`.
- `POST /mcp` is a stateless streamable-HTTP MCP server (`WebStandardStreamableHTTPServerTransport` with `enableJsonResponse`, one server per request). `GET` and `DELETE` answer 405, because there are no sessions and no server-sent notifications. It has 22 tools: `swarms_list`, `swarm_get`, `swarm_answer`, `swarm_updates`, `swarm_feed`, `problems_list`, `swarm_machines`, `swarm_machine_commands`, `swarm_machine_command`, `swarm_machine_logs`, `swarm_machine_job`, `swarm_machines_export`, `swarm_backers`, `swarm_branches`, `swarm_artifacts`, `swarm_chat`, `swarms_search`, `swarm_create`, `swarm_contribute`, `swarm_hand`, `swarm_feedback` and `swarm_wait`. Its instructions explain swarms, the agent's thread and its updates, sending money, notes, rooms and lent machines.
  - `swarm_contribute` without `hash` prepares a one-off transfer (`Operations.transfer`). It answers:
    - the swarm's wallet, which a swarm from before 0003 gets now;
    - the token's address and the memo, `Memo.memo`, the same one the page signs;
    - with `amount`, the `transferWithMemo` call data;
    - a link `/s/<id>?fund=&token=&amount=&note=`, where the page sends it with Tempo Wallet and counts it.
    With `hash` it counts a transfer already sent (`Operations.contribute`). The server never sends money: the user's wallet does.
  - The contribution box reads those parameters (`asked` in `Contribute.tsx`). Checked on 2026-10-30:
    - The transfer link opened Send once with pathUSD, 0.500000 and the note.
    - The prepared call for 0.5 pathUSD decoded to `transferWithMemo(swarm wallet, 500000, keccak256("note:<id>:Add HTTP/2"))`. An `eth_call` of it from the test wallet succeeded, and nothing was sent. The server runs no model: the client's own model answers questions about the data by calling the tools, so reading costs nothing. An argument the SDK rejects comes back as an `isError` result with `MCP error -32602`. An operation's failure comes back as an `isError` result holding its problem JSON.
- `swarm_wait` and `GET /v1/swarms/:id/wait` wait at most 55 s per call (`maxWait`). The MCP SDK client's default request timeout is 60 s (`DEFAULT_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MSEC` in `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.32.2). A caller that wants to keep waiting calls again. This bounds only the wait: the swarm itself has no time limit.
- Search is a D1 FTS5 table, `search` (porter on `unicode61 remove_diacritics 2`, migration 0004). It indexes swarm titles and goals, notes and updates (kind `report` in the table, `update` in hits), and ranks by `bm25` with titles weighted 2 and bodies 1. A query keeps its words minus stopwords, each quoted and joined with `OR`, so a query's punctuation can never reach FTS5 syntax. Like the list, search hides swarms that nothing funds: no contribution.
  - When rows are indexed: a swarm when it is created, a note when its transfer is counted, and an update when its turn ends (`Swarm` calls `Search.report`; every ended turn goes into `reports`, only completed ones into the index).
  - `Search.sync` fills in reports that are missing or changed. Reads of a swarm call it, and so does every search: an unscoped search checks the 5 most recent swarms whose last 40 turns have fewer reports than ended turns (`lagging`), and a scoped search checks its own swarm the same way.
  - Feed entries stay in the Durable Object. A search scoped to one swarm also searches that swarm's feed there (`Swarm.search`, through its own FTS5 index `feed_search`, under the swarm object's own reads below).
  - Checked on 2026-10-30 on a scratch D1 database on the Tempo account (`swarms-fts5-check`): created, migrated with 0001, 0002 and 0004, queried with `Search.query`'s exact SQL, then deleted. Porter stemming, `bm25` and `snippet` worked; production D1 refused read-only pragmas, so this was the check.
- D1 read replication (2026-11-03): the primary is in WNAM and each D1 round trip from elsewhere cost about 85 ms (measured from CMH), so read-only requests read through a D1 session (`Replica.ts`) that may use a nearby replica. A middleware in `index.ts` swaps `c.env` for one whose `DB` is `env.DB.withSession(bookmark ?? 'first-unconstrained')` for `GET` and `HEAD` of `/`, `/join`, `/new`, `/about`, `/docs`, `/s/:id`, `/s/:id/code`, `/x`, `/x/s/:id`, `/api/swarms`, `/v1/swarms`, `/api/me`, `/v1/me`, `/v1/search`, `/v1/problems`, and `/api|v1/swarms/:id` with `feed`, `code`, `git/:read`, `live`, `artifacts`, `chat`, `chat/live`, `hands`, `answer`, `updates`, `search`, `wait`, `machines` and `backers` (`Replica.readable`); MCP's reading tools get the same env and its writing tools the primary. Everything else stays on the primary: every `POST` (under `/api` and `/v1` in a `first-primary` session of its own since 2026-11-07), the pay route the Swarm Durable Object (its own env, all money accounting) and sign-in (`Sessions.store` unwraps with `Replica.primary`, so a fresh token is never missed by a replica). Writes a read path makes (a wallet for an old row, `answer` backfill, `Search.sync`) go through the session, which sends them to the primary. Read-your-writes: after a successful write under `/api` or `/v1` (`Replica.writes`) the Worker answers the bookmark of the write's own session (until 2026-11-07 it read `select 1` in a new `first-primary` session for it) as `x-d1-bookmark` (exposed by `/v1`'s CORS) and the cookie `swarms_d1` (5 minutes, HttpOnly); a request carrying either reads from a replica at least that fresh. A bookmark not shaped like D1's (`00000000-00000010-00000000-<32 hex>`) is ignored. A swarm a replica does not have yet (`Store.exists`, `find`, `steering`, `repo`, `ledger`, `backed`, `rostered`) is read again on the primary before answering 404, so a swarm shared or polled over MCP right after its creation is found. Checked on 2026-11-03 under `wrangler dev` on the build with local D1: a create answered the bookmark header and cookie, a failed write none, the listed pages and routes answered 200 with no bookmark, with it and with a malformed one, MCP `swarm_get` answered, and a warm detail made one D1 call (the ledger batch), the room one, `/s/:id` two in parallel and search two. Read replication must be enabled on the database itself (the REST call in the deploy notes); until then sessions all go to the primary and behave as before.
- D1 queries, 2026-11-07 (the user's request: "optimize all database queries, dont add caches where the database can be just faster", after the read-only audit in /tmp/swarms-analysis/reports/db.txt). Plans were read with `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` and `.stats` on a local SQLite 3.51 database built from `migrations/` and that audit's seed (60 swarms, 3,000 contributions with 400 on the biggest swarm, 15,734 reports, about 20,000 search rows), without `sqlite_stat1`, since production D1 has none (`select count(*) from sqlite_stat1` answers "no such table"). Where a new query needs no new index, production D1's own `rows_read` was read with read-only SELECTs. `test/store.test.ts` runs the real queries on `node:sqlite` over `migrations/` and checks each plan named below. No cache was added, and no column stores a value the database can derive (the user's decision: such a copy is a redundant state).
  - Migration 0024 adds `contributions_backing` on `contributions (swarm, contributor, amount, note_id, created_at)`. A swarm's totals, a steer's check of its author's backing, refund shares (`Store.shares`) and the members query's contributions arm read it without touching the table. `contributions_by_swarm` stays: the newest 200 contributions (`Store.gifts`) read it in order with no sort.
    - A swarm's totals (`Store.find`, and the detail, team, backers and room batches) keep their two correlated subqueries, now index-only, and `count(distinct contributor)` no longer needs a temp b-tree: 31 → 18 page misses and 5,383 → 4,268 VM steps on the biggest swarm. A join to one grouped pass was tried for it and is slower: SQLite materializes every swarm's group (3,000 rows) instead of pushing the id into it.
    - The list (`Store.list`) evaluated the raised subquery twice for each swarm, once for `raised > 0` and once for the column, and the contributor count a third time: 8,001 contribution rows, 168 misses, 59,178 VM steps. It now joins swarms to one grouped pass over the index: 3,000 index entries, 75 misses, 46,707 VM steps. On production, which lists 36 swarms over 120 contributions, it read 233 rows before and 201 with the new query (before the index exists).
    - `Store.steer`'s availability check read the swarm's 400 contributions; it now seeks `(swarm=? AND contributor=?)`: one index entry, 45 → 24 misses, 2,219 → 221 VM steps. `Store.shares` loses its GROUP BY temp b-tree (28 → 15 misses).
  - Search ranks with the search table's own rank, set by 0024 (`insert into search (search, rank) values ('rank', 'bm25(0, 0, 0, 2, 1)')`), and `order by search.rank`. FTS5 then hands rows over best first (plan `SCAN search VIRTUAL TABLE INDEX 32:M5`, no temp b-tree), so the swarm join, the contributions check and `snippet` stop once the limit fills. Locally a three-word query matching 18,842 rows read 10 of them, 1,631 → 146 misses and 508,906 → 375 VM steps, with the same ten hits. On production (FTS5's default rank, the same plan) it went from 4,860 to 33 rows read and 28.1 to 2.7 ms, and scoped to 06cf5d7c8f92 from 1,275 to 316. 0024 can run before or after the deploy: an older Worker orders by its explicit `bm25(...)`, and without 0024 `order by search.rank` weighs titles and bodies alike.
  - Lagging reports: `Search.lagging` counted every report of every swarm on every unscoped search (6,088 rows read on production, growing with every turn), then shipped every turn of each lagging swarm from its object and read all its reports. It now counts only the reports after a swarm's `rounds - 40` (`Search.recent`, the 40 reports the detail batch already reads for `Search.lags`), a range on the reports primary key: 1,006 rows read on production, at most 40 a swarm; locally 15,734 → 1,984 index entries for the same three lagging swarms. The meaning is kept for those 40 turns: more turns ended, by D1's `rounds` less a running one, than reports. A lagging swarm is synced from `turns(after)`, the object's turns after the window's start, against `select round, ended_at from reports where swarm = ? and round > ?` (1,360 → 49 rows for 2d4a1a9790ba's 1,359 reports), and the detail path syncs from the oldest of its last 20 ended turns the same way. A scoped search syncs its swarm only when it lags; before, every scoped search shipped all of that swarm's turns. A gap more than 40 turns back is no longer found: on production on 2026-11-07 no swarm lagged and every swarm's newest report round equalled its report count. A swarm whose `rounds` counts a turn its object never ended stays lagging, as before, but now costs each unscoped search one object call for its last 40 turns and no D1 read. `Swarm.turns` took this optional `after` (one line in `Swarm.ts`); `Search.sync` ignores turns at or before `after`, so an object still on older code that sends every turn writes nothing twice.
  - A write's bookmark: a request that `Replica.writes` names now runs in a `first-primary` session of its own (`Replica.write`), and the Worker answers that session's bookmark, with no query of its own. Before, every successful `POST` and `DELETE` under `/api` and `/v1` read `select 1` in a new `first-primary` session for it, one more primary round trip (70-80 ms from CMH). Reads later in the session may come from a replica at least as fresh as its own writes, so the write env counts as replicated, and a swarm it does not find is read again on the primary; sign-in still reads the primary directly (`Replica.primary`). A swarm object's own D1 writes are in the bookmark only when they committed before the request's last query; no write route's answer depends on one, since the objects write D1 from their alarms and the pay route. Locally under `vite` with local D1, `POST /api/swarms` made 4 D1 calls before (insert, `Store.find`, the search rows, `select 1`) and 2 now (the insert, which hands its row back with `returning`, and the search rows), with the bookmark header and cookie still set; a refused write (401) sets none.
  - `GET /branches` read the swarm's totals on every request, cache hit or not, for its 404 and its `steering`. It now takes `steering` from the team roster the isolate already holds (`Team.cached`), else one primary-key read (`Store.steering`, which is also the 404 check). Locally, a branches read with the roster held made no D1 call (1 ms), and one without it one call. `Store.adopt`, its per-isolate `adopted` set and the fallback in `Store.ledgers` that counted agent `''` rows for whichever agent asked are deleted, and a branch's money is only its own agent's rows: on production on 2026-11-07, 0 branch rows had agent `''` and 0 branch steers had agent `''` or null, and `Store.steer`, `allot`, `shift`, `base` and `settle` always write the current agent. Before, each new isolate ran adoption's two primary `UPDATE`s on its first branches read, for nothing.
  - Writes read back less. `Store.give`, `Store.annotate` and `Store.insert` hand their row back with `returning`, so a counted contribution no longer reads it again (only the request whose insert lost a race does) and a created swarm skips `Store.find`; the check for an already-counted hash now runs beside the receipt fetch. `Operations.say` and `btw` read the swarm and its members in one batch (`Store.rostered`, two sequential round trips before) and pass the members to `Team.seat`, and a steer of one's own backing derives the author's new `available` and `steered` instead of reading every member again. `Operations.machines` checks the swarm with `Store.exists` and reads `view.hands`; before, it loaded the row, provisioned a wallet and could backfill the answer from every turn.
  - Index audit: 0024 drops `swarms_problem`, which no query reads (the problem bank filters the list in JavaScript), in this code or in the deployed main. Every other index has a reader: `contributions_by_swarm` (`Store.gifts`), `contributions_channel` (`Store.channel`, `delivered`), `contributions_sponsored` (`Store.channels`, from 0026), `steers_by_swarm` (the steered sums), `steers_budget` (`allot`, `allotted`), `steers_branch` (ledgers, shift, settle, replaced) and `sessions_by_expiry` (expired-session deletes). `Hands.count` had no caller and is gone.
  - Dead D1 columns, dropped on 2026-11-07: `swarms.workers` (from 0001, read and written by nothing, nonzero on 2 rows), `contributions.kind` (always `channel` when `channel` was set and `transfer` otherwise, read by nothing) and `contributions.budget` (null on all 120 rows, read by nothing); no index, view or trigger names them (`contributions_channel`'s WHERE names only `channel`). `Store.give` no longer writes `kind` or `budget`. `kind` was NOT NULL with no default, so a Worker that leaves it out cannot insert while it stands (and `insert or ignore` skips such a row without an error), and a Worker that writes it cannot insert once it is gone. So the order is: 0026 drops `kind` and adds it back nullable, before the deploy (the Worker before it still writes it), then the deploy, then 0027 drops `kind`, `budget` and `workers`, after the deploy and once the old Worker's isolates have gone. 0027 is committed only after that deploy, so a `pnpm db:migrate` before it cannot apply it.
- Resources, 2026-11-07 (the overnight resource audit; Cloudflare's GraphQL analytics read with the deploy login, D1's with `wrangler d1 insights`). The audit's numbers covered 2026-10-31 to 11-07, mostly code since replaced, so each was measured again by hour:
  - `delete from search where swarm = ? and kind = 'report' and ref = ?` (10.6M of that week's D1 rows) is the statement 0019 replaced: in the 24 hours to 20:50 UTC on 11-07 no search statement read more than 12,534 rows in all, and the delete by rowid read too few to rank among the top 100 statements (486,106 rows read by those 100 in all).
  - The listing's 18.4M rows over 48,752 runs came from the list that summed `pledges` and `budgets` (gone since 0022) for every swarm; the current list (one grouped pass, behind `Edge.cached`) ran 136 times in those 24 hours at 205 rows each. It returns `s.*`: 83,880 bytes for 36 swarms, `answer` 41,004 of them and `sealed_wallet` 4,680. Narrowing it was left, since D1 does not bill bytes and the list runs only on cache misses.
  - D1 rows read: 11,799,351 on 11-06 (450k to 490k an hour from 12:00 to 16:00 UTC), 256,811 on 11-07 up to 20:44, 23 to 56k an hour after 08:00 with every swarm paused.
  - Swarm objects' alarms: 392 to 420 an hour on 11-06 from 12:00 to 16:00 UTC and up to 7,439 while swarms ran that evening; 0 to 42 an hour between 08:00 and 20:00 on 11-07, all paused (36 and 42 in the hours f7df8db3fe54 ran turns 39 to 43, on the code before 25f4590, about six an active minute), and none in a 150 s `wrangler tail` at 20:40. 25f4590 was deployed at 20:27 and no swarm has run since, so its per-turn count is measured only locally (its bullet). SQLite rows the objects read: 340k to 430k an hour on 11-06 from 12:00 to 16:00, 45 to 125k an hour after 08:00 on 11-07 (page views, `/machines`, and the feed index backfill, which wrote 25,090 rows after the 20:27 deploy).
  - What Cloudflare counts as the objects' errors, 2026-10-31 to 11-07: 1,003 alarms `clientDisconnected`, 28 alarms `scriptThrewException`, 1,092 `http` `clientDisconnected` (and 67 of the lobby's) (a `/live` viewer that left; the X card and `/tg` follow `/live` whatever the status, the swarm page only while it runs) and 54 `jsrpc` `clientDisconnected`. On 11-07, 56 of 4,960 alarms ended `clientDisconnected`, after 20 to 150 ms each and spread through the hours swarms ran rather than at deploys; the 11-06 production tail in /tmp/sw-e2e held one (`canceled`, 716 ms, no log, no exception, scheduled 6 s after the alarm before it). Workers Logs answer 403 to the deploy login, so their cause is not known; the runtime retries a canceled alarm. The 5 `scriptThrewException` alarms of 11-07 each ran 9.8 to 9.99 s, the length of the 10 s windows before 25f4590, and were not diagnosed either.
  - An open `/live` does not count as the swarm object's active time (what Durable Object duration is billed on): with five such streams of about 280 s held every 5 minutes from 20:50 to 21:10, the objects' active time was 97 s in the 20:00 hour and 8 s in the 21:00 hour.
  - `GET /v1/swarms/:id/machines` folds the feed in the swarm object. The Worker used to ask the object for a `view` (only for its hands) and for the newest 2,000 tool, output, error and machine entries, and fold them itself: on f7df8db3fe54 those entries were 1,711,451 bytes of JSON per uncached read, for an answer of 506,543 bytes. `Swarm.fleet` now answers `Fleet.folded` (the feed and the object's hands), read beside the lent hands, and `Fleet.lend` adds them in the Worker; `Fleet.fleet` is the two together. On production after 5d9607e (version 4435d5c2) the answer was the same 506,543 bytes (same SHA-1), and in Node the old fold and the new one gave the same JSON over the same 2,000 entries. `Server-Timing` on uncached reads: before, `do.feed` 128 and 132 ms (two reads); after, `do.fleet` and `total` 61 to 283 ms, median 113 over six (602 on the first read after the deploy). The first version (9abc35a) read the lent hands before the fold and took 125 to 282 ms, the hands' D1 read (about 45 ms) ahead of the object call.
  - The fold also sees every machine over the swarm's life (2026-11-08, found by the session watching swarm f3a42cf87339: its first H200, which the lead created at 00:32 UTC, showed `by: autoresearch branch: attention2`, a start at 00:44 and no settings, because its `Starting machine` row had fallen out of the newest 2,000 entries and the first command left in the window, a branch's, made the machine). `Swarm.fleet` reads, below the window, every row that names a machine: every `machine` row, every `compute_create` output or error and every `Called browser_create` (`Feed.machined`), through the partial index `feed_machines` (`ON feed (seq) WHERE` that expression, created with the other indexes), so who started a machine, when and with which settings stay right while its commands stay those of the window. And `compute_create`'s output reaches the feed wrapped in JSON more than once (three backslashes before the quotes around `sandboxId`), where `Fleet.sandbox` and `reason` took at most one, so a sandbox started without a name was never tied to its `Starting machine` row: f3a42cf87339's second H200 (01:57) showed no settings either. Checked in Node on f3a42cf87339's whole public feed (4,344 entries, read with `/v1/swarms/:id/feed`): before, the newest 2,000 of its 3,918 tool, output, error and machine entries folded to sb-E4MAfaPmjuC8gDgCptT4Md `by autoresearch branch: moe3`, started 01:10:58, and sb-M604ARCyrPpKM27hbL5Y01 started 01:57:24, both with no settings, as production's `/machines` answered at 02:53 UTC; after, in node:sqlite with the swarm object's feed schema, the two machine rows below the window (`EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN`: `SEARCH feed USING INDEX feed_machines (seq<?)`) and the window folded to sb-E4MA… by the agent, started 00:32:50, with CPU 8, GPU H200, the sglang image, lease 10 min, memory 64 GB and timeout 240 min (340 commands, those of the window), and sb-M604… started 01:57:18 with CPU 4, GPU H200, the image, lease 10 min, memory 64 GB and timeout 60 min. The fixture world's six swarms fold the same before and after (`fleet` over each one's whole fixture feed and lent hands). `test/fleet.test.ts` covers the wrapped output. On production after the deploy of c1e5acb (version 29bf1dcf, 03:05 UTC) `/v1/swarms/f3a42cf87339/machines` answered both H200s as the Node check above did (by the agent, started 00:32:50 and 01:57:18, with their settings), and f7df8db3fe54's ten machines answered in 57 to 77 ms of `do.fleet` on warm reads (553 to 624 ms on the first read after the deploy, which built `feed_machines`). The spend per update was checked on the gateway side only (its bullet in tempoxyz/gateway's AGENTS.md: a test agent's turn report answered its ledger spend), since no swarm ran after the deploys and none was woken.
  - Every command and log of a machine, complete (2026-11-08, Georgios: "commands to get all of a swarm's machine logs, complete"). Before, the Machines tab listed only the commands inside the newest 2,000 feed entries, each with the feed's copy of its output (the first 2,000 characters), all in one `/machines` answer: production answered f3a42cf87339's first H200 (sb-E4MAfaPmjuC8gDgCptT4Md) with 340 of its 830 commands and f7df8db3fe54's `h200` with 346 of 575 and `judge` and `judge2` with none of their 17 and 15, in 480,148 and 401,218 bytes (`do.fleet` 152 and 512 ms), at 03:38 UTC. Now:
    - The swarm object keeps a derived table `runs` (one row per command: the tool row's seq, machine, at, who, text, the output row's seq, failed, exit, ms, clipped and a compute_spawn's log path), indexed `runs_machine (machine, seq)`. `Fleet.track` (a pure step over one feed entry, `shared/Fleet.ts`) is the fold's command logic made incremental: the sandbox ids `compute_create` named, the pending `Starting machine` and the commands waiting for their output (the same speaker's next output or error within five entries, as `folded` pairs them) are its state, kept in the object's kv beside a cursor (`runs:cursor`, `runs:tracker`). `Runs.logged` applies each entry as `log` writes it (writing the state only when an entry changed it), and every read first runs `Runs.sync`, which catches up from the cursor in chunks of 2,000 entries, so the first read after the deploy backfills the whole feed (f3a42cf87339: 3,918 entries in 31 ms in Node) and later reads read nothing. A command's duration is the gateway's `duration_ns` on its `tool.result` (`Trail.step` carries it as `ms`, `Follow` passes it to `log`) for new commands; for older ones it is the gap between the call and its output when that is positive (a subagent's events often reach the feed in one batch with one time, so those stay null), until the command is opened, when the gateway event's `duration_ns` is written back to the row. Exit codes come from `exitCode` in the output, ` · exit N` on a hand's command output, and 0 for a hand's plain `Command output`; a non-zero exit or `timedOut` now also marks the command failed.
    - `/machines` folds only the rows `feed_machines` indexes (every machine row, `compute_create` output or error and `browser_create`) and adds each machine's `runs` (`count`, `failed`, `last`) and `sandboxes` (every sandbox id that name was given, oldest first: f7df8db3fe54's `h200` was 34 sandboxes) from `runs`, and the machines only commands name; it no longer carries commands (`commands: []`). `GET /v1/swarms/:id/machines/:machine/commands` (`?order=newest|oldest&cursor=<next>&limit=1..500`, 100 by default) pages them without outputs; `/commands/:seq` answers one with its whole output: the gateway's copy of the tool result (`GET /v1/agents/:id/events?after=<event - 1>&limit=1` with the operator key, free, read only when the feed's copy was cut or the duration is unknown, kept 30 days in the colo's Cache API under `/__cache/full/<swarm>/<agent>/<event>`), else the feed's (`source`, `truncated`), and its input; `/commands/:seq/log` reads a compute_spawn's log through the gateway's `POST /v1/compute/logs` (listening 8 s, from the offset it reached, at most every 15 s, $0.0001 a read, up to 4 MiB kept in `job_logs`; once the job exited, or the sandbox is gone or finished, it is never read again); `/logs` reads a sandbox's own log through `POST /v1/compute/output` (system, stdout and stderr, 1,000 lines a page, $0.0001 a page, up to 10 pages newer than what it holds and 10 older a read, at most every 30 s), keeps the lines in `machine_logs` (indexed by `(sandbox, at)`) and pages them back with `before`; once it reached the start of a sandbox that ended (10 minutes after its `Shutting down` row, or 24 h and 10 minutes after it started, Modal's longest timeout) or the gateway no longer knows it, it is `complete` and never asks the gateway again. `/machines/export` and `/machines/:machine/export` stream one NDJSON file (`export`, then per machine `machine`, every `command` with its whole output, `logs` and each `log` line oldest first, each `job`, then `end`), six gateway reads at a time. The gateway reads are made with the operator key in the swarm's app, as `compute/list` is. MCP has them as `swarm_machine_commands`, `swarm_machine_command`, `swarm_machine_logs`, `swarm_machine_job` and `swarm_machines_export` (the URL and a curl command). The Machines tab loads a machine's commands 100 at a time when it is opened (Newest first or Oldest first, Show more), each command's output when it is opened (shown as its stdout and stderr when it is a compute call's JSON, `Fleet.readable`), a spawned job's log on demand, Show the machine's log (with stream filters and older lines), and a download link for the machine and one for every machine; the fixture world, whose `/machines` is empty, keeps the local fold with outputs.
    - Checked in Node on f3a42cf87339's and f7df8db3fe54's public feeds (4,344 and 4,608 entries): `track` over the whole feed gave the same commands as `folded` over it (machine, text, who, output and failure; 906 and 622, none different), f3a42cf87339's first H200 830 and its second 76, f7df8db3fe54's `h200` 575, `judge` 17 and `judge2` 15; in node:sqlite a page of 100 read 102 rows (`SEARCH r USING INDEX runs_machine (machine=? AND seq>?)`), the new `/machines` answer was 1,027 and 5,531 bytes where the whole-feed fold with outputs would be 1,031,558 and 795,612, and the 830 commands in pages of 100 came to 248,270 bytes. `test/runs.test.ts` covers the tracker against the fold, exit codes, durations, the job path, the backfill, incremental logging, paging both ways, the counts and the index; `test/trail.test.ts` the duration. Under `vite` with local D1 and two swarms whose feeds were those two, posted through a temporary seeding route that called `log` (not committed): f3a's copy backfilled on its first read after 2,500 entries and logged the rest incrementally, then answered both H200s with 830 and 76 commands (`do.fleet` 11 ms), pages of 100 in 6 ms of `do.commands`, a command's output, the job and log routes with the gateway's 401 for the local dummy key (complete false, nothing kept), and both exports in full (992 and 685 lines). Headless Chrome on that copy at 1440 and 390 px opened the 830-command machine, showed 100 commands, a command's output, Show more (730 left, then 200 shown) and the log panel with the gateway's error, with no sideways overflow; the fixture world's Machines tab opened `bench-coders`' sandbox and its command output as before.
    - On production after the deploys of e5e52db (version 7d501373, 03:44 UTC) and afec234 (version c8f29fc0, 03:46 UTC), read-only and with no swarm woken: `/v1/swarms/f3a42cf87339/machines` answered both H200s with `runs.count` 830 and 76 (105 and 22 failed), by the agent, started 00:32:50 and 01:57:18, in 1,027 bytes, where it answered 480,148 bytes and 340 commands at 03:38; its first read backfilled `runs` in 551 ms of `do.fleet`, later reads 29 ms. The 830 commands paged oldest first in two pages of 500 came to 830 distinct seqs (739 with a duration, 460 with an exit code, 135 whose feed output was cut, 80 spawns); a page of 100 is 27,464 bytes in 228 ms of `do.commands` for 500. Opening seq 224 and 323 (outputs the feed cut at 2,000 characters) answered the gateway's whole tool result, 3,011 and 8,965 characters (`source: gateway`, one free events read of 29 to 106 ms), and again from the colo cache in 25 ms of total; seq 393's duration came from the gateway event (341 ms) and was written back. `/logs` of sb-E4MAfaPmjuC8gDgCptT4Md answered Modal's 7 lines (the CUDA banner on stdout and an Xid 31 GPU MMU fault on system at 01:18:28) and of sb-M604ARCyrPpKM27hbL5Y01 its 19, both `complete` since both sandboxes were shut down more than 10 minutes before, so the second read answered from the object in 26 ms without the gateway; spawned jobs on those stopped sandboxes answered done with nothing kept, without a gateway read. `/machines/export` streamed 992 lines in 2,197,564 bytes in 4.0 s (1.7 s again), every command's output whole (135 from the gateway, none truncated, the longest 16,384 characters, the gateway's stdout cap), the 26 log lines and the 80 jobs; one machine's export 144,137 bytes. f7df8db3fe54's `/machines` answered `h200` 575 commands over its 34 sandboxes, `judge` 17 and `judge2` 15 in 5,531 bytes. Headless Chrome on f3a42cf87339's Machines tab at 1440 and 390 px opened the 830-command machine, showed 100 commands, a command's output as its stdout, Show more (730 left, then 200) and the machine's log, with no console error and no sideways overflow. The gateway reads of the check were the operator key's compute/output pages, a few at $0.0001 each. f3a42cf87339 started working again at 03:48 (not woken by this check) and started a third H200 (sb-qystpfVMja9brNflheCsKu): its commands were tracked as they were logged (22 by 03:50, the subagent `moe5`'s with durations of 339 to 516 ms from the gateway's `duration_ns`, where every older subagent command's duration had to come from the feed's times), its `/logs` answered 11 lines with `complete: false` while it ran, and its spawned job's log (seq 4464) was read through `compute/logs` for 8 s and was empty, since that job sends its output to its own file.
- D1 export does not support databases with virtual tables (Cloudflare's import and export docs: drop them, export, recreate them). Before `wrangler d1 export`, drop `search`. Afterwards, rebuild it from `swarms`, the notes of `contributions` and `steers` (`pledges` and `budgets` are gone since 0022), and `reports`, which holds every indexed report, then set its rank again: `insert into search (search, rank) values ('rank', 'bm25(0, 0, 0, 2, 1)')` (migration 0024; without it `order by search.rank` weighs titles and bodies alike).
- `/docs` (`Docs.tsx`) documents the MCP server, the REST routes, sending money, contributor hands, and the error codes. The home page's "Use it from code" section links to it.
- Checked end to end on 2026-10-30, locally:
  - `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`'s client called every tool.
  - A script that answers questions through `swarms_search` and then `swarm_rounds` (now `swarm_updates`) answered "Which codec should I use for log archives?" with bz2, from a round report, and "Which Python HTTP client used the least peak RSS memory?" with urllib3.
  - The two local swarms behind those answers spent 0.017463 and 0.027562 MACH.

- Every public read streams (2026-11-24, `Watch.ts`): any GET under `/v1` that reads something answers server-sent events when asked for `text/event-stream` (an EventSource asks), at the same URL, so a third party builds a live view of any swarm from the routes it already reads. One pattern for all of them: `retry: 3000`, then `event: snapshot` with exactly the body the GET answers, then what changes, a `: keepalive` comment every 25 s, and `Last-Event-ID` (or `?since=`) to resume. Logs (a room, #general, the feed, the tree's and a node's deltas) send each new item as its own event (`said`, `entry`, `delta`) with its seq as the id and resume with only the items after it, read back through the same GET's `?after=` (the tree through the new `/v1/swarms/:id/deltas`), at most 10 pages before falling back to a snapshot; everything else sends a new snapshot whenever its body changes, with the body's hash as the id, so a resume at an unchanged body sends nothing. The list sends its snapshot and then `swarm` upserts as the lobby does. `/v1/swarms/:id/live` (the agent's words as it writes), `/wait`, the exports and the WebSockets are unchanged.
  - How: the Worker opens the swarm object's room socket (or the lobby's, or #general's) as a WebSocket client tagged `listen` and `watch`, subscribes before it reads the snapshot, and re-reads the resource through the same Hono route (`Rest.app.fetch`) when a signal touches it: `changed` parts, `pulse`, `artifacts`, `roster` and deltas, mapped per route in `Watch.topic`; reads that land while one runs fold into one more, and lobby-wide reads (a rider's page, health, search) and machines wait at least 5 s between reads. Items the snapshot already holds are skipped by seq. The room socket stays the one fan-out: no new Durable Object state, no cache. Each stream costs one socket on the object and one read per change it cares about; streams have their own 1,000 sockets, so third-party viewers cannot fill the 500 the room keeps for pages (both numbers chosen without measurement), the same reads the web app makes per tab on `changed`. A socket that closes (a deploy) ends the stream and the client resumes; a ping every 25 s without a pong in 10 s does the same.
  - New events where state changed silently: a `watch` socket gets each transcript entry as `{ type: 'entry', entry }` (during a turn the feed sent `changed` to nobody, so the feed could not stream), and a tree swarm now tells the lobby and pulses its room after every stored delta (`Nodes.emit` → `Store.find` → `beacon`): before, a tree swarm never called `beacon`, so its rides and spend reached neither the list socket nor a room's `pulse`. `/v1/swarms/:id/code` joins `/api`'s, so the Code tab reads only what `/v1` serves.
  - OpenAPI adds a `text/event-stream` response and a `Last-Event-ID` header to each of the 35 GETs that stream (from `Watch.topic`, so the two cannot drift), `/docs` has a Real-time section with `curl -N` and EventSource, and the MCP instructions point agents at the streams.
  - Checked: `test/watch.test.ts` (snapshot, items, dedupe by seq, re-read on change, fold, resume and heartbeat for the tree, chat, #general, list, money and feed streams, and the route table), and `test/e2e/streams.test.ts` under `vite` on the mocks: a tree swarm's list, tree, ledger, backers and room streams each opened with their snapshot in 16 to 44 ms; a $5 ride's `funded` delta was on the tree stream before the contribution POST returned (145 ms), the new books and riders snapshots and the list's `swarm` came 20 ms after it, a room post's `said` 27 ms after the POST began, and a tree stream resumed from the first snapshot's seq got the missed `funded` delta and no snapshot in 100 ms; a ledger stream resumed at the current hash sent nothing in 1.5 s. Not checked on production; nothing was deployed.

## Rooms

- Every swarm has a room where its backers talk (the user's request of 2026-11-01). Anyone reads it; a backer posts. A backer is an address with a counted contribution, or a hand issued while it was signed in (`hands.contributor`, migration 0008; hands issued signed out, over MCP or before 0008 have no owner and make nobody a member). `Store.members` lists them with `backed` (what they put in), `steered` (what of it is behind a note, from a note on the money or from the room) and `available`, the difference.
- Sign-in is accounts' `Handler.auth` (SIWE, the accounts `examples/authentication` pattern) at `/v1/auth`, `/v1/auth/challenge` and `/v1/auth/logout`, with `cookie: false`, so every session is a bearer token, 30 days, and `origin` pinned to `SWARMS_URL`. Its store is the D1 table `sessions` (`Sessions.store`: `take` is `delete … returning`, so a challenge nonce is used once, and expired rows go on every write) rather than the example's Durable Object. The page signs in with one Tempo Wallet prompt: `wallet_connect` with `capabilities.auth = { url: config.auth, returnToken: true }`, keeps `{ address, token }` in localStorage and sends `Authorization: Bearer` on every `/api` request. Funding does not sign anyone in, so sending money never depends on the session handshake. A room that answers `me: null` to a token the page holds signs the page out. A Tempo access key signs in as its wallet too (2026-11-06, the user's request to run everything headless with the wallet's access keys): accounts calls viem's `verifyMessage`, whose Tempo chain refuses a keychain envelope unless `mode: 'allowAccessKey'`, so `Sessions.auth` hands it a client whose chain's `verifyHash` sets that mode, which recovers the access key and checks on the AccountKeychain that it is authorized for the wallet, not expired and not revoked. Before, the admin access key's signature answered 401 `signature does not match address`. Locally with viem, the admin key's signature verified for its wallet, and a key the wallet never authorized and a plain EOA claiming the wallet did not.
- A backer's name in the room is their username. Since 2026-11-05 ("require people to set a username instead of the automatic guest usernames") posting, steering and side questions need one: `Operations.say` and `Operations.btw` answer 403 `username_required` to a signed-in account without a name, and the room's gate and the transcript's composer ask for one (`Username.tsx`) before they show a composer. Someone without a username, a backer who funded without signing in, shows as a short address (`Mention.short`, `0x73dd…c0c0`, written without `@` by `Mention.at`) in the room, the transcript, the contributors and the team roster, and is not offered in @-completion. Before, such a backer got a made name, `Mention.handle(address)`: one of 64 words picked by the address's first four bytes and its last four hex digits (`@quark-1a2b`), which still resolves as a mention in old messages. A signed-in account sets its username with `POST /api/me` or `/v1/me` `{ name }` (the user's request of 2026-11-02; `null` or `""` clears it) and reads it with `GET`, and the header's wallet button shows `@name`. Usernames live in D1 `names` (migration 0009), keyed by lower-case address, unique, stored in lower case, 3 to 24 letters, digits, `-` and `_`, starting with a letter and ending with a letter or digit (`Mention.invalid`). A name shaped like a made one (`word-1a2b`) is refused, so nobody can pass for another backer's made name, and so are `swarm`, `lead`, `all`, `everyone`, `here`, `admin`, `system`, `swarms` and `you`. A taken name is 409 `conflict`; a released name is free at once. Mentions resolve against both names, the username and the made one, when a message is posted, and are stored as addresses; a message's text keeps the name it was written with, so a mention of a username that was later changed no longer highlights. The page highlights messages that mention the reader.
- `@swarm` steers. A message with it must carry `steer`, an amount of the author's `available` backing that goes behind the rest of the message (`Mention.idea`, 3 to 280 characters) as a note: a row in D1 `steers`, inserted only when the author's available backing covers it, in one statement, so two concurrent steers cannot spend the same backing (409 `insufficient_balance`). `Store.notes` adds `steers` to the other notes, so a room steer weighs like a note on money, merges with the same note's money (`noteId` normalizes case and spaces), counts its author as a backer of the note, is indexed for search and is in every continuation prompt. While a turn runs `Swarm.say` steers it at once (`Swarm.steer`, returning the note it sent, `Steer.live` true); between turns it wakes the swarm, whose next continuation lists it. It moves weight, not money; to put more behind an idea than their backing allows, the page sends the backer to Back it with the idea as the note. Plain messages never reach the agent.
- Who steers (2026-11-05, the user's request: "each swarm can permission who can steer it … using gateway teams we make a swarm a team with a budget … by default maybe a swarm is public ie anyone can steer but u can make it so that the funds can only be steered by a given list of ppl"). A swarm's `steering` is `open` (the default: any backer steers with their own backing, as above) or `team` (migration 0014: `swarms.creator`, `team`, `steering`, and `steers.budget`). In team mode only the members of the swarm's gateway team steer it and ask it side questions, and they put the swarm's own money behind ideas, each within their gateway team budget (the team member's `limit`, USD per 30 days, null for none) instead of their own backing; anyone still funds it, and backers and members talk in its room. A member's steer is a `steers` row with `budget = 1` (`Store.allot`, one statement that checks the 30-day sum against the limit, 409 `insufficient_balance` past it), which weighs like any steer but is not taken from the member's backing (`steered` counts only `budget = 0`). The admins are the gateway team's admins (`Team.roster`, cached 30 s per isolate, the operator wallet left out); before a team exists, its admin is whoever created the swarm signed in (`creator`), else its biggest backer by delivered money (until 2026-11-05 its first backer, and a free hand row counted as backing, so whoever issued a hand on a swarm nobody had backed yet became its admin). The first policy change or member makes the operator key create the team (`POST /v1/teams/create`) and add that admin (`Team.ensure`). Routes, also under `/api`: `GET /v1/swarms/:id/team` (the roster: `steering`, `team`, `admins`, `memberBudget`, and `seats` with each member's `role`, `limit`, `spent` and `left` in these 30 days), `POST /v1/swarms/:id/team` `{ steering?, memberBudget? }`, `POST /v1/swarms/:id/team/members` `{ member, role?, budget? }` and `DELETE /v1/swarms/:id/team/members/:member`; only admins change the policy and add or remove others (403), anyone leaves, and the last admin stays (409). The room has a Steering strip (`Steering.tsx`): the policy and team size, and for admins the open/team choice, the default budget, add (wallet, member or admin, budget) and remove; a member's composer shows "from the swarm's money · $x of your team budget left" and skips the funding step, and a non-member's says only the team steers. The check that a policy change sends `steering` or `memberBudget` is in `Operations.steer`, not the schema: the OpenAPI document builds request bodies with `.omit({ swarm: true })`, which zod 4 refuses on an object with an object-level refinement, and the first deploy (fb803ded) answered 500 for `/openapi.json`. Not decided with the user: whether a team's budget should instead be money moved into the gateway team's own funds; here it is a share of the swarm's pooled money, enforced by Swarms. Checked on 2026-11-05 with this branch under `vite` against the deployed gateway on the local swarm c77fde6b935c with its first backer W and a fresh wallet X, both signed in: W without a username was refused posting and side questions (403 `username_required`) and showed as `0x73dd…c0c0`, `quark-1a2b` was refused as a name, and once named W posted; the roster said open with W as admin; X and a signed-out caller could not change the policy (403, 401); W made it team-steered with a 0.05 budget, which created a gateway team with W as admin; X could not steer, post or ask; W's 0.01 steer was taken from the team budget (W's own backing stayed 0.17, the roster said 0.04 left) and a 0.05 one answered 409; W added X with 0.02, X could not add others, posted and steered 0.01 without backing it, and a 0.02 steer answered 409; the last admin could not leave, X left and could no longer steer, and once open again X could not post. In headless Chrome on the fixture world: the strip's choice, add and remove, a member's composer note and steer, the username gate and its refusal of a made name, and no sideways overflow at 390 px.
- A swarm is steered by whoever starts it (2026-11-10, Georgios: "make swarms have modes where the swarm creator can only steer it but still allows backers to chat, and ways where a swarm creator can also allow others to steer it now with a whitelist, and later on we may make the conditions more programmatic … so that it's 1 swarm 1 rider", then "theyre all riders, dont worry about it"). Everyone who puts money in is a rider; who steers is a separate fact, the same `steering` as above. `POST /v1/swarms` takes `steering`, `team` by default when signed in and `open` otherwise (`team` signed out is 401; `swarm_create` has no session and leaves it out). A team-steered swarm with no gateway team has its creator, else its biggest backer, as its one seat (`Team.roster`: admin, no limit, `spent` from `Store.allotted`), so starting one creates nothing on the gateway: the gateway team is made only when an admin adds someone (`seat`, which takes a wallet or a `@username` resolved by `Store.named`, 404 for an unknown name before any gateway call) or sets a member budget, or switches a swarm nobody started signed in. One gate decides who steers, `Team.steerer`, used by the room, branch funding and moving, side questions, and a contribution's or backing's note (on a team-steered swarm a note on anyone else's money is not kept; the money counts). It is where programmatic conditions would go. On the web `gate`'s open state has `from: null` for a rider who chats but does not steer, and `steerer` decides whether money may carry a note: such a rider has no ✦, no @swarm completion, no "+ Steer this" and no note field. A seat's ✦ posts at once with the swarm's money, $1 a steer or what is left of their budget. Under the pitch, "Steered by @a" (or "@a and @b", "every rider") opens Who steers: the list, and for admins the same Steered by rows as Start (You and whoever you add / Every rider with their own money) and an @username or 0x… field. Start shows Steered by under Runs on, You by default, which signs in rather than only connecting. `Page.ts` seeds the roster so the line renders on the server. Seat handles come from `names`, so a seat who never put money in shows their username. The worker's and the API's words stay "team" and "backer".
