Checked on 2026-11-10 under `pnpm dev` against the local D1 and the deployed gateway, with three throwaway addresses signed in through sessions written to the local `sessions` table: signed out a new swarm was `open` and `steering: team` 401; signed in it was `team` on `frontier-anthropic` with one admin seat, the creator's name, and no gateway team; a rider with a $2 contribution in local D1 chatted but got 403 `not_member` for @swarm and for /btw; someone with no money got 403 for chatting; the creator chatted and steered $1 with no money of their own; a non-admin adding someone got 403, an unknown @username 404, the last admin leaving 409, removing a non-member 404; the creator switched to open and back to team with no gateway team made, and once open the rider steered $0.50 of their own; the server-rendered page said Steered by @e2e-alice. Not run: adding someone, which creates a real gateway team. In headless Chrome on the fixture world at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone (`?scene=in` with json-terabyte steered by mira, `scene=helm` making the visitor the steerer of every other swarm, and the storyboard's Who steers flow): no console error, no sideways overflow, no tap area under 24 px; the desktop swarm pages shifted by up to 0.05 on load as the Now tab already does. Deployed on 2026-11-10 (1040958, version 88bd379d, no migration: `steering` and `creator` are 0014's). On paradigm.xyz/swarms: `/openapi.json` answered 200 with `steering` (open, team) and `template` defaulting to `frontier-anthropic` in createSwarm's body and `member` as a string in addSteerer's; MCP `tools/list` answered 200 with swarm_create saying frontier-anthropic by default; `GET /v1/swarms/06cf5d7c8f92/team` said open with its creator as admin and no seats; `/v1/config` priced Fable 5.2. In headless Chrome at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone, home, Start, that swarm, its Who steers and its chat had no console error and no sideways overflow, and home, Start, the swarm, its chat, All swarms and the docs no 5xx; Start listed Anthropic $0.066, OpenAI $0.019, Frontier Mix $0.038 a step and Abliterated Soon, then Steered by with You chosen, and the swarm said Steered by every rider. Not run on production: creating a swarm signed in, steering as its team, and adding someone, which creates a gateway team.
- Branches (2026-11-05, the user's request: "each subagent can also have a budget … so that way we can give each subagent cluster a budget, or we can make a swarm increase the budget only of a subagent(s) … fund sub-populations of a given swarm or redirect our funding from a given sub-agent swarm"). The gateway's agents take `budget_usd` on `spawn_agent` and refuse a subtree's paid requests once its budget is spent (tempoxyz/gateway #283, gakonst/nanocode 6727a5f1e, nanocode-ts 6f9e58d). A branch is a subagent the swarm's agent started itself (`parent` null), with the subagents under it. `GET /v1/swarms/:id/branches` (also `/api`, MCP `swarm_branches`; `Swarm.subagents` lists the gateway's `/v1/agents/:agent/subagents`) answers every subagent with its role, task, state, `budget`, `spent` (what the subtree spent since it got a budget, from the gateway's `spent_usd`), `left`, `starved` (failed because its budget was spent), and `funded` and `funders`. `POST /v1/swarms/:id/branches/:branch/fund` `{ amount }` puts the caller's backing not yet behind a note or branch behind one branch, as a `steers` row with `branch` set (migration 0015: `steers.branch`, and `branches` with each branch's `base`), so it comes out of `available` as a steer does and, when only the team steers, out of the member's team budget (`Store.allot`); branch rows are not ideas (`weighing` skips them). The branch's budget is its `base` (the budget it had when first funded: what the agent gave it, or nothing for one without its own) plus everything put behind it, and `Swarm.direct` sets it on the gateway (`POST /v1/agents/:agent/subagents/:n/budget`), announces it in the room and the transcript, and sends a starved branch a message that starts it again. `POST /v1/swarms/:id/branches/:branch/move` `{ amount, to }` moves what the caller put behind one branch, at most what that branch has left, behind another (`Store.shift`: a negative and a positive row in one batch, so `available` does not change), lowering one budget and raising the other. A gateway call that fails takes the rows back, and so does a swarm object still running the code from before the deploy: right after f8a7f8bb, three swarms' objects answered `The RPC receiver does not implement the method "subagents"`, which `branches` turned into a 500 until `reached` made every such call a 502 with that detail. Sign-in and a username are required, a stranger who never backed the swarm gets 403, and on a team-steered swarm only its team directs the money. The Branches tab (`Branches.tsx`, after Room, replaced on 2026-11-06 by the transcript's branch filter below) showed each branch with its state, spent against its budget, its funders and the subagents under it; tapping one opened $0.50, $1, $5 or your own amount, which in an open swarm first sends new money from the wallet as a steer does, then funds the branch, and, for a backer with money behind it, Move to another branch. The room shows the fund and move announcements (`arrived` matches `put` and `moved`). Checked on 2026-11-05 with this branch under `vite` against the deployed gateway (aec044c6), the swarm's payer reached through a quick tunnel, on a fresh local swarm (3e740359642d) whose goal told the agent to start two subagents with `budget_usd` 0.02, funded with 0.3 OUSD by a throwaway wallet W (`/tmp/states-e2e/branches-swarm.mjs`, 20 of 20): both branches showed budget 0.02 after 19 s; signed out was 401, a nameless stranger 403 `username_required` and a named one 403, branch 99 404, $5 409; W's $0.05 behind alpha made its budget 0.07 at the gateway, W's available backing fell from 0.30 to 0.25, and no idea appeared; moving $0.06 (more than W put in) was 409, to the same branch 400, and $0.02 to beta left alpha at 0.05 and beta at 0.04 at the gateway with W's available unchanged; the room announced both; the branches finished at $0.0021 and $0.0030 within their budgets. The tab rendered that swarm's real branches, and in headless Chrome on the fixture world funded a starved branch (out of budget, then working with $1.00 left) and moved $0.40 to another, with no sideways overflow at 390 px. The swarm was then drained back to the test wallet.
- Steering a branch, stopping it, and the side pane (2026-11-05, the user's story map: "i want you to do it all", with "/btw … just a side pane that opens in the UI, it's not an inline prompt", steering costs new money every time for a branch as for `@swarm`, artifacts are one flat shelf for all branches, and the agent weighs prompts by the money behind each, $4 over $1, working it out from its own transcript).
  - `POST /v1/swarms/:id/branches/:branch/fund` takes `message` (3 to 60,000 characters). The money goes behind the branch as before, and the message reaches the subagent at once as an urgent `send_agent_message` (`Gateway.tell` with `urgent`: it steers a running turn or starts one), headed `[$X behind this, from @who; backers have put $Y behind your branch in all, and your budget is now $Z]`. The steer row's note is the message's first line; the transcript logs a steer under the branch's `who` (its role, or `role #id` when two branches share a role) and the room says `@who steered <role> (branch n) with $X: "…"`. In an open swarm the Branches tray and the side pane send that much new money from the wallet first (`money.ts` `payIn`, the composer's flow), so every steer is new money; on a team-steered swarm it comes out of the member's team budget.
  - `POST /v1/swarms/:id/branches/:branch/stop` (`Operations.stop`) closes a top-level branch and its subtree at the gateway (`/subagents/:n/close`); the transcript and the room say who stopped it, and while the agent works it is steered not to restart the branch or hand its work to another subagent unless backers ask again (the persona says the same): on 2026-11-05 on production, before that steer, the agent of swarm bbff259f5abb answered a stopped `gamma` by starting a new `gamma`, because its goal named three subagents; with the steer (67218a40), the same goal on swarm a8efb916f435 (`/tmp/states-e2e/stop-restart.mjs`) finished alpha and beta and left gamma closed, with no fourth subagent (one run each). Who may stop one is my choice, not yet the user's: whoever holds more than half of the money backers put behind it, or the swarm's admins (403 `access_denied` for others, 401 signed out, 404 for an unknown branch, 409 for a stopped branch or a finished swarm); until the audit below, any backer of it could. `Store.settle` then returns what the branch had not spent, at most what its backers put behind it, to them in proportion to their net shares as negative `steers` rows, so it is theirs to put behind something else; `branches.settled_at` (migration 0016) claims a branch's settlement once. After every round `Swarm.settled` settles each branch with a budget that the agent closed itself, and the room says how much went back.
  - `GET …/branches` adds `who` (the transcript's name for it: its role, or `role #id` for a later subagent with a role an earlier one took, as `Trail.subagent` names them) and `result` (the subagent's output, `status.output` or `last_output`, as its strings joined or as JSON, at most 4,000 characters). The Branches tab showed every branch's spend (one without a budget of its own shows what it spent of the swarm's money), its result once it finished, Its transcript (the transcript filtered to its `who`, which reads that name's newest 200 entries from `/feed?who=&before=` the first time it is chosen and adds it to the speaker chips: on production the filter first showed "Tuning in…" for a branch whose entries were older than the loaded page), Ask it on the side, a steer box with $0.50, $1, $5 or your own amount that reads Fund or Steer, Move, and Stop this branch. Propose a branch drafts `@swarm Start a new branch to ` in the composer.
  - The side pane (`Side.tsx`) opens from Ask on the side in the swarm's header (desktop), the composer's link, or `/btw …` typed in the composer, which prefills it; the last two ask the chosen branch when the transcript is filtered to one (a branch's own Ask it on the side until 2026-11-06). Its select targets the agent or one branch (`btw` takes `branch`, answered by the gateway's `/subagents/:n/side_question`), and each target keeps its own thread in localStorage (`swarms.btw:<id>:<lead or n>`). With a thread it offers Steer with this (the agent: the collapse as before; a branch: `fund` with the collapse prompt as `message`), Ask for an artifact (`say` with `artifact: true`, note `Artifact from /btw: <the first question>`, whose prompt asks the agent to publish or update an artifact showing what the thread asked about; for a branch the same text as its message), Share to room (the last exchange as a plain message) and Clear. Since 2026-11-06 (the user's request: "make it so /btw channels can ask for artifacts"), Ask for an artifact works with only a typed question: it asks it first, then asks for the artifact with that exchange; Steer with this, Share to room and Clear wait for a thread. A subagent does not get the swarm's persona, so a branch's artifact request is `fund` with `artifact: true`, which appends the persona's Artifacts section (`Persona.publishing`, the convention with its bucket and kv calls) to the steer the branch receives. Checked in headless Chrome on the fixture world at 1440 and 390 px, for the agent and for a branch.
  - The side pane says what each action costs (2026-11-07, Georgios: "the UI as I said in the /collapse should be fixed"). Audited at 1440 and 390 px on the fixture world (`/tmp/night/collapse-pw/audit.mjs` and `edge.mjs`, outside the repository) before the change: the words /collapse appeared nowhere; `/collapse` and `/close` typed in the composer showed a `/btw` chip and opened the pane with an empty question (neither collapsed nor closed anything), and `/collapse` typed in the pane was asked as a side question; the amount chips appeared as soon as a side question was typed, so asking looked like it cost $1, though answers are paid from the swarm's money; the collapse was a button "Steer with this" beside "Ask for an artifact" (both primary) under "New money from your wallet goes behind it.", without the amount, the note or that the thread goes with it; and after it the thread vanished with one grey line. Now: the pane is titled /btw and says asking takes nothing from the wallet and only /collapse costs; the money row shows only with a thread (or after "Or ask this and request an artifact of the answer, with money behind it"), as a card with two modes, "/collapse into a steer" and "Ask for an artifact", a sentence naming exactly what goes (the amount from the wallet, or from the team budget on a team-steered swarm, the note `Collapsed /btw: …` and how many questions and answers) and one button that names the money ("Add $0.50 and collapse", "Add $0.50 and ask for an artifact"); the default is $0.50, as in the composer. `/collapse [amount]` in the composer or the pane selects the steer, sets the amount and focuses that button (Enter then sends it; with no thread it says there is nothing to collapse yet), `/close` closes the pane, and the composer shows the command's own chip with a line saying what it does. A success is a ✓ card naming the money, the note and the thread; a decline or an empty wallet keeps the thread with the reason. What is sent is unchanged: the whole amount is paid in first (`useCredit().cover`, so it is new money since 114b976) and then `say` puts it behind the collapsed note with the thread as `btw`, which the swarm sends to the agent as the CLI's collapse prompt; a branch gets `fund` with the collapse as its message. Checked at both sizes signed in through the fixture (`scene=in`, `in,deny`, `in,broke`): no sideways overflow, no console errors, and the transcript then showed "@ada funded $0.50" and the "@swarm Collapsed /btw: …" steer. The storyboard has "Ready to /collapse", "Collapsed" and "/collapse before asking". On production after the deploy of d78e9db (version 89e28f65), the same audit through the fixture (`?fixture&scene=in`, no real money) gave the same answers at both sizes with no console errors, f7df8db3fe54's pane signed out read the new text with Sign in to ask on the side, `/v1/problems` listed the new ideas open, and `/`, `/about`, `/new` and `/docs` had no sideways overflow at 1440 and 390 px.
  - Every note the agent gets in a brief, a continuation or a steer reads `[$X behind this, from N backers] <note>` (`weighed`). The persona's "Money behind every request" asks it to weigh requests by that and keep the tally from its own transcript; "Branches" asks it to give each subagent a `budget_usd`, pass the weighing and the publishing instructions on to it, and keep artifacts one flat shelf with one overview of every branch; and it runs heavy computation on a machine, since a code cell stops after a few seconds of synchronous work (gateway #286, nanocode-ts e9e717d). Since 2026-11-07 it also renews a machine's lease only when less than about two minutes of it remain, never far ahead, and terminates a machine it no longer needs: the agent of f7df8db3fe54 renewed its H200 twice within 32 s, ten minutes ahead, and the $1.755 prepaid left no contribution channel room for its next model call (the squeeze below). The text reaches the gateway at the first turn a new deploy's isolate starts (`Persona.ensure`, called first in `styled`), and the gateway appends it to every root model call after that. Since 2026-11-08 the list of what a subagent's task must carry also has it call the budget tool to see its branch's spend (the gateway's budget tool answers a budgeted subagent's branch budget, what it spent and what is left). A persona change makes every running lead's next call read its context uncached once (about $0.48 against $0.048 on f3a42cf87339's Opus 5.5 lead when the Wishlist's waiting rule went in).
  - The room's `arrived` matches `steered` and `stopped`.
  - Checked on 2026-11-05 in headless Chrome on the fixture world under `vite`: steering swe-bench-lite with $1 raised its budget to $3.00 and the steer showed in its transcript, the pane targeted the branch and Ask for an artifact completed, `/btw how close are we?` opened the pane prefilled, funding and then stopping rust-subset showed it Stopped, a 390 px phone showed the pane 390 px wide without sideways overflow, and the console had no errors. On production (80589e39) the same day, on swarm bbff259f5abb funded with 0.4 OUSD by a throwaway wallet W whose goal told the agent to start alpha, beta and gamma with `budget_usd` 0.02, each writing haiku with 45 s waits (`/tmp/states-e2e/steer-stop.mjs`, 25 of 25): a two-character steer was 400; W's $0.05 steer of alpha to lighthouses made its budget 0.07, was logged in the transcript and announced in the room, and took W's available backing from 0.40 to 0.35, and alpha's next three haiku were about lighthouses; side questions to the agent and to beta answered, a stranger's was 403 and branch 99's 502; an artifact request with the collapse note was 400 and the right one 201, after which the agent published "Haiku branches"; stopping gamma signed out was 401, by a stranger 403, branch 99 404; W funded gamma $0.03 and stopped it, which returned exactly $0.03 to W's available backing, a second stop and a fund of the stopped branch were 409, and the room said who stopped it and what went back; every branch stayed within its budget. In headless Chrome on that swarm, signed in as W at 1440 and 390 px, the Branches tab showed each branch's spend and alpha's result, the side pane targeted beta from its card and answered "Old stone, younger stream —", Share to room posted it, and nothing overflowed.
- Branches live in the transcript's filter (2026-11-06, the user's request: "remove branches tab and make it part of the transcript filtering"). The swarm page has no Branches tab, and `#branches` falls back to the transcript. The transcript's speaker chips list every branch from `GET …/branches` in tree order (each top-level branch followed by the subagents under it, `tree`), then any other speaker; the chosen chip scrolls into view, and a branch's dot dims once it is done or stopped. Choosing a branch (`?who=<its who>`) puts `BranchBar` (`Branches.tsx`) above its entries: its state, its task on one line, spent against its budget (or what it spent of the swarm's money) and its funders. Tapping it opens the whole task, the subagents under it (each filters to itself), a link to the top-level branch above a subagent, What it found, Move and Stop this branch (for the same people as before). The composer then targets that branch, or the top-level branch above a subagent: the branch's name replaces @swarm, the amount row stays shown, and the button reads Fund $x with no text or Steer $x with a message (`Api.fund`, new money from the wallet first in an open swarm, the team budget on a team-steered one). `/btw` and Ask … on the side ask that branch, and a stopped branch or a finished swarm falls back to @swarm. Any draft (a $ chip, an @swarm from the room, + Branch) switches a branch filter back to All, and + Branch after the chips drafts `@swarm Start a new branch to `. The transcript polls the branches every 60 s, and every 15 s while a subagent is chosen. The storyboard's Branches frames use `?who=` now; they were not run. Checked on 2026-11-06 under `vite` in headless Chrome on the fixture world (`/tmp/pw/lane.mjs`) at 1440×900 and as an iPhone 13: the tabs no longer list Branches; steering swe-bench-lite with $0.50 raised its budget to $2.50 and showed the steer in its transcript; Fund $0.50 with no text on the out-of-budget latency-probe set it Working with $0.50 left; parser-tasks' composer targeted rust-subset, and its link went there; license-check showed its result; `/btw` with swe-bench-lite chosen opened the side pane on it; + Branch went back to All with the draft; signed out, the composer offered Sign in to steer or fund rust-subset; the phone had no sideways or page overflow, with the lane open too; the console had no errors. Not run: a real swarm's branches on the gateway.
- Audit fixes (2026-11-05, the user's request after an audit of the branch, side-question and hand work: "let's do EVERYTHING you've found"). Four holes were exploited first on production swarm bbff259f5abb (`/tmp/states-e2e/audit.mjs`, `audit2.mjs`): a fresh wallet with nothing in was refused a side question (403), issued a hand without connecting anything (201), showed as a member with `ways: machine` and $0, and then got an answer the swarm paid for; a signed-out caller took a hand slot (201); a $0.01 fund of omega whose message was 30,000 emoji (60,000 characters, 120 KB) was refused by the gateway (`message exceeds the 65536-byte limit`) after its budget had gone from 0.10 to 0.11, while the backer's available stayed 0.30 and the answer said nothing was put behind it; and a wallet that put $0.01 behind omega stopped it, though another backer had $0.05 behind it and the agent gave it $0.10. What changed:
  - Hands: a hand row makes its contributor a member only once the gateway has seen it connected or serving requests (`hands.seen_at`, migration 0017; `Hands.seen` marks it from the gateway's listing when the machines tab, an issuance or the lender's own room post reads it). Issuing stays open to anyone, MCP included, but when a swarm has its 32 hands the oldest that never connected is forgotten and detached at the gateway to make room, as is any that has not connected in a day (`Hands.evictable`), so unused slots can no longer block lending; only 32 connected hands answer 409.
  - Side questions need at least $0.01 of the asker's own money behind the swarm (or a team seat), not just membership; a swarm answers at most 120 an hour from everyone (`Btw.perSwarmHour`, kv `btw:*`, chosen without measurement) besides 20 per asker; a thread is at most 32 KiB of questions and answers (`Btw.maxThreadBytes`; the side pane drops the oldest exchanges to fit, `Btw.fit`), where 20 exchanges of 4,000 and 20,000 characters used to be accepted; and answers are redacted like the transcript.
  - Stopping a branch takes more than half of the money backers put behind it, or an admin; a branch nobody funded is an admin's to stop. Before a team exists the admin is the creator, else the biggest backer.
  - A fund's message is at most 48,000 bytes (`Btw.maxMessageBytes`, under the gateway's 65,536 with the header), and `Swarm.direct` puts the branch's previous budget back when the gateway refuses the message after the budget was raised.
  - A branch with no budget of its own is not capped by funding it: money put behind it, or moved to it, counts as backing (shown, told to it, returned when it stops) and no gateway budget is set, where a $0.01 fund used to cap an unlimited branch at $0.01, a stop that needed no permission. Moving out of an unbudgeted branch is limited only by what the mover put behind it.
  - Branch money is scoped to the agent: `steers.agent` and `branches.agent` (migration 0017, which rebuilds `branches` with primary key `(swarm, agent, branch)`), because subagent ids restart at 1 when a swarm starts a new agent and the new subagent 1 inherited the old branch 1's backers, base and settlement. Rows from before the migration had agent `''` and were adopted by the swarm's current agent the first time its branches were listed (`Store.adopt`), or released when it started a new agent first; adoption and the fallback that counted such rows for any agent were deleted on 2026-11-07 (the D1 queries bullet). When `Swarm.lead` creates a new agent, `Swarm.release` returns what backers put behind the old agent's unsettled branches (what each had not spent, from the old agent's listing, or all of it when the old session does not answer) and says so in the room. `direct` and `halt` refuse a call made against an agent the swarm has since replaced.
  - A settled branch takes no more money: `Store.steer`, `Store.allot` and `Store.shift` refuse rows for a branch with `settled_at`, inside the insert, so a fund racing a stop cannot land behind a closed branch, and `move` refuses a stopped source, where the share a stop left behind (when the branch had less left than its backers put in) could be moved to another branch after the stop had already returned money.
  - A team member's returned allotment is dated at their earliest allotment to that branch, not now, so a stop cannot lower their spending in a 30-day window that no longer holds the allotment it returns.
  - A collapsed side thread's note id hashes the note with the whole thread, so a second thread with the same first question is a separate idea: before, anyone could add their own thread, with answers they wrote, to another backer's note for $0.01 and have the agent read it with that note's full weight.
  - Money claims in text are disarmed: in every note, collapsed thread and branch message the agent reads, a bracketed segment with "behind" in it becomes `(a claim in the text: …)` (`Mention.claimed`), and the persona says only the bracketed line the swarm puts first states money.
  - Credentials are redacted (`Trail.redact`, which now also covers `sk-tempo-` keys and `nch_` hand tokens) in side answers, every room post, and each branch's `task` and `result`, which `GET /branches` serves publicly; a scan of the 19 listed swarms' branches found none exposed before the change.
  - Room announcements put the backer's words last, in quotes with their quotes and line breaks flattened (`Mention.quoted`), so a note cannot end the swarm's own sentence and continue as the swarm.
  - `GET /branches` is served from a 5 s cache per isolate, dropped by every fund, move and stop in that isolate.
  - The web flows pay new money once per amount: when a steer's payment went through and the steer then failed, the next try with the same amount uses that money (`useCredit` in `money.ts`) instead of asking the wallet again, and a branch message over the byte limit is refused before anything is paid.
- Second audit (2026-11-05, the user's "go and refind bugs and optimizations"): a read-only review of the whole codebase after the fixes above. Fixed here (the money findings, pledges and budgets counted before their money arrived, are the next bullet):
  - A branch's stop takes more than half of everything behind it, the swarm's own money included: its base (what the agent gave it) for a budgeted branch, what it spent of the pool for one without a budget. Before, on a branch no backer had funded, $0.01 was a majority, and the stop gave the $0.01 back.
  - Outside payments are governed in the payer (`Swarm.passable`, `passed`): an `agent.fetch` or `vendor` payment is at most $0.25 (`passCall`). The amount counted is the largest of the gateway's `amount` and the amounts in the challenges the swarm signs (`asked`). Before 2026-11-06 only `amount` counted, so a call without it was capped and counted as $0. all of them together at most the larger of $1 and a twentieth of what was raised in an hour (`passHour`), and one host at most half of that, all chosen without measurement; each `agent.fetch` payment is logged in the transcript. A prompt-injected agent could otherwise send the pool to any address $1 at a time, through any text it reads (a $0.01 note, a branch message, a lent hand's output). Sponsored pledges are registered with `passThrough: false` (existing ones are re-registered by `sponsorships`), so the gateway asks the payer for every pass-through instead of paying it from a sponsored channel unasked.
  - A lent hand earns at most a fifth of what was raised in a day, and at most the turn's cap within a turn: `Swarm.allowance` reads the hand's lifetime `charges.usd` from the gateway and keeps where it stood at the start of the turn and of the day, and a hand that reconnects gets what is left of the turn's cap, where it used to get a fresh window on every reconnect and every turn (ten quick turns allowed about 89% of the pool).
  - Hand slots: `hands.seen_at` is when the gateway last saw the hand connected or serving (refreshed at most hourly), and a hand not connected for a day, or the longest idle one when all 32 are taken, makes room for a new one; only 32 hands connected at once answer 409. Before, 32 brief connections held every slot forever.
  - A collapsed side thread is accepted only for exchanges the swarm answered for that backer, as it answered them: `Swarm.btw` keeps a seal of each question and redacted answer (`Btw.seal`, the last 200 per backer) and `Operations.say` asks the object (`vouched`) before any money moves; and `<btw_conversation>` tags inside questions and answers are made inert. A backer used to be able to write the agent's "Assistant" answers and present them as its own side conversation.
  - Settling a branch nets each backer's rows across open and team money before splitting the return, so moving branch money after the swarm switched steering modes no longer creates backing or team budget that was never paid.
  - The live stream (`/live`) has one upstream per swarm object: watchers share it and a new watcher gets the turn's frames so far (up to 2 MB) and then the live ones, where every watcher opened its own operator-key event stream to the agent's session; the upstream closes when its last watcher leaves.
  - `GET /hands` and `/machines` are cached for 10 s per isolate (an issuance drops the hands cache), and `/wait` checks that the swarm exists before it wakes its object.
  - The room socket: a `hello` is taken at most every 10 s per socket and its token's address is kept for 5 minutes in the object, `typing` is relayed at most every 2 s per socket, and a room takes at most 500 sockets. A backer posts at most 10 messages a minute in a room (`Swarm.paced`, 429 `rate_limited`, before any money moves). On production, before the hello fix, six hellos sent at once on one socket made seven presence broadcasts, because each read the socket's state before the session lookup it awaited; the object now marks the hello first.
  - Money added behind an idea the agent was already told reaches it again: `steered` keeps the weight it was told (`steered.weight`), and the next continuation includes a note whose weight grew by at least a quarter or $0.10 (rows from before the column are not resent); and a collapse stored while a continuation was being sent is no longer dropped, since only the notes in that prompt forget theirs.
  - A side question the swarm cannot pay for answers 409 `insufficient_balance` saying so, where it answered 409 `conflict` with the gateway's HTTP 402 text.
- A swarm whose agent session stops answering no longer waits forever (2026-11-05, swarm 29a779262595, whose session answered every request with 500 for over an hour after a heavy Code Mode cell wedged its recoveries, so the swarm kept polling a turn that never ended and did not continue when funded). `follow` and `advance` note when the session first answered 5xx (`unreachable`, cleared only when its events move again, since the wedged session also answered some reads with 200 and `running`); after 15 minutes without new events and another 5xx the swarm cancels the turn as far as the gateway still lets it, marks the round failed and drops the agent (`abandon`), so the next round's `lead` creates a new agent and sends it the swarm's brief, with the goal, notes and updates; the old agent's workspace stays behind.
- The room lives in the swarm's Durable Object (table `chat`): messages with author (null for the swarm's own), mentions and steer. The object announces money as it comes in (transfers), when the agent starts or gets back to work, each update (its start) and the end of the swarm. `GET /api/swarms/:id/chat` (and `/v1`) answers the newest 100 messages, `after` or `before` an id, the members and, with a token, `me`; `POST` posts (401 `unauthorized`, 403 `not_member`). `/chat/live` is a hibernating WebSocket (`acceptWebSocket`, `ping` answered by the runtime with `pong`): it sends every new message, presence (signed-in backers here and everyone watching) and typing; a client sends `{type: hello, token}` to be counted, which the object checks against `sessions`. The page reconnects after 3 s, catches up with `after` on every open, and polls every 15 s besides. The `/v1` socket is routed before `Rest`'s CORS middleware, which would wrap the 101. MCP's `swarm_chat` reads a room.
- Usernames were checked on 2026-11-02 under `vite` with a fresh local state and two signed-in test keys: `GET /v1/me` answered 401 signed out and the made name signed in; `@Georgios` was stored as `georgios`, the same name in other case for the second key was 409, `swarm`, `quark-1a2b` and `ab` were 400, the room's members, `me` and a member's mention used the username, a rename freed the old name for the other key, and clearing it brought back the made name, which still resolved as a mention. Headless Chrome on the fixture world: Pick a username, the reserved-name error, a save that renamed the visitor's messages, header and mention list.
- The fixture world has rooms (`Fixtures.room`, `say` and `chatter`): scripted conversations with steers on two running swarms, presence, someone typing and a new message every 15 to 25 s, and a fixture sign-in as the visitor, who has $1 not behind a note on "Benchmark every open-weight coding model".
- Checked on 2026-11-01 under `vite` with a fresh local state: two secp256k1 test keys signed in through `/v1/auth` (a replayed signature answered 409); a backer with $2.50 in (2 of it without a note) read the room as a member, mentioned themselves, was refused a steer without an amount (400) and one of $2.50 (409), steered $1.50 and then $0.50 behind the same idea in other case and spacing, which became one $2 note with 80% of the money, indexed for search, and was refused a third ($0.60 with $0 left); signed out and as a non-backer posting answered 401 and 403; a hand row with the second key's address made it a member with `machine` and $0 to steer; the WebSocket sent presence, `pong` and each message; `/v1` paged with `before`; MCP `swarm_chat` answered. Under `wrangler dev` with the build: the challenge and its preflight answered `*` for the wallet's origin, `/v1/swarms/:id/chat` allowed `authorization`, and `/v1/swarms/:id/chat/live` upgraded. Headless Chrome on the fixture world at 1440 and 390 px: the home page's two doors and tiles, a starter opening the budget step, the room's sign-in, @-completion, a mention, a steer with $0.50, Put new money behind it opening Back it with the note, the non-member gate, and the Answer and Back it tabs. Not exercised: Tempo Wallet's real sign-in prompt (a passkey needs a person), the round and money announcements (they need a funded round), and a hand issued while signed in through the gateway.
- Side questions (`/btw`, asked for on 2026-11-05: "add /btw support to swarms, so that I am doing some side-QnA with the swarm and then I can do /collapse to steer the swarm with my /btw thread transcript as we do in the CLI"). A side question goes to the swarm's agent through `POST /api/swarms/:id/btw` (`/v1` the same, `Operations.btw`, then `Swarm.btw`), which calls the gateway's `POST /v1/agents/:agent/side_question` (tempoxyz/gateway #281, nanocode-ts build-03046e3): the agent answers from its own conversation, without tools and without interrupting or steering its turn, as the CLI's one-off side question does, and the answer is paid from the swarm's money through the payer like any model call. Each question carries the thread's earlier questions and answers in the CLI's format (`Btw.ask`, the `EARLIER` prefix of nanocode's `bin/nanocode/src/side.rs`), and collapsing the thread steers the swarm with it: the room message is `@swarm Collapsed /btw: <the first question>` (`Btw.note`, at most 280 characters, the note the money backs) with the thread as `btw`, which the server checks matches the note. The swarm object keeps the CLI's collapse prompt (`Btw.collapse`: the note, the CLI's instruction and the `<btw_conversation>`) under `collapse:<note>` and sends it in place of the note's text, to a running turn as a steer or in the next continuation, and forgets it once sent. Only signed-in backers ask (401 signed out, 403 for others), at most 20 side questions an hour each (`Btw.perHour`, 429 after, chosen without measurement: each costs a model call over the agent's whole context); a swarm with no agent yet answers 409. The thread stays the asker's: it is not posted to the room or the feed until it is collapsed. Checked on 2026-11-05 with this branch under `vite` against the deployed gateway (a97170e2 and then c736909e) through a cloudflared tunnel, on a swarm funded by a 0.18 OUSD transfer from a throwaway wallet and 0.5 OUSD from the test wallet, whose agent was writing lighthouse haiku with 45 s waits between them: a side question answered "Six lighthouse haiku are now in /workspace/haiku.md …" in 5.1 s and a follow-up with the thread "/workspace/haiku.md" in 2.7 s while the turn kept running; signed out answered 401 and a signed-in stranger 403; a collapse whose note did not match its thread answered 400, and the right one 201, after which the agent's next prompt (its `input.accepted` event) was `Keep going.` followed by the whole collapse prompt with both exchanges. The thread was first a pane above the transcript's composer with `/collapse` and `/close`; the side pane below replaced it.

## Artifacts

- A swarm publishes artifacts so people can follow along without reading the transcript: a live dashboard of its numbers, a chart, a table, a write-up (the user's request of 2026-11-02). They are built on the gateway's existing public storage, so the gateway needed no change: an artifact is files under `artifacts/<slug>/` in the swarm's bucket and a manifest at the kv key `artifacts/<slug>` (`{ title, summary, entry, kind, updated }`), all public, in the swarm's app under the operator wallet. The agent writes them with `bucket_put` and `kv_set` ($0.005 and $0.0005, paid like every call); the prompt (`shelving`) explains the convention in full on an agent's first turn and when the swarm first learns of artifacts, lists the published ones in every continuation, and asks to keep a few current rather than publish many. A kv manifest gives a public, paged list by prefix, which the gateway has no bucket equivalent of (its `/p` lists only kv), and a version that grows with each update.
- `GET /api/swarms/:id/artifacts` (and `/v1`, MCP `swarm_artifacts`) lists them: `Artifacts.list` reads `/p/<operator>/swarm-<id>/kv?prefix=artifacts/` (up to three pages, cached 15 s per isolate) and `shared/Artifact.ts` turns each manifest into `{ slug, title, summary, kind, entry, form, version, updatedAt, url }`. A slug is `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}`; an entry is at most four segments of `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*`, so `..`, absolute paths and other escapes fall back to `index.html`. The form follows the entry's extension: page (HTML), image (SVG, PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP), markdown, table (CSV, TSV), json or text. The operator wallet comes from `/api/keys/me` once per isolate (`Gateway.wallet`).
- Files are served from Swarms' own origin at `/a/<swarm>/<slug>/<path>` (in `run_worker_first`), proxied from the gateway's `/p/.../bucket/...` with `If-None-Match` and `Range` passed on. The gateway's `/p` answers every object with `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox`, which blocks scripts, so an HTML dashboard cannot run there. Since tempoxyz/gateway 187b773 (2026-11-07) `/p` also answers HTML, JavaScript and XML objects, SVG included, as `text/plain`, so `/a` sets their type again from the file's extension when the gateway says `text/plain` (`Artifact.scriptedType`); until then every artifact page showed its own source, f7df8db3fe54's `autoresearch` chart and `progress` page included. Every HTML artifact is themed like the rest of Swarms (2026-11-07, the user's request: "make sure the theming on all artifacts is styled after the same way as other swarm UI"): `/a` streams an HTML answer through `HTMLRewriter`, which puts `<link rel="stylesheet" href="/kit/swarm.css">` before the page's first element and, at the document's end, a short `<style>` that keeps the background white, the text black and the body in Paradigm Serif whatever the page set. `public/kit/swarm.css` carries the app's `@font-face` rules (Paradigm Serif and SemiMono, from `style.css`), the room's palette as variables (`--fg`, `--muted`, `--faint`, `--surface`, `--wash`, `--line`, `--rule`, `--accent` #1a3d2c, `--good`, `--warn`, `--bad`, `--serif`, `--mono`, `--radius`), headings, tables, code and a few classes (`sw-page`, `sw-card`, `sw-rows`, `sw-stat`, `sw-big`, `sw-num`, `sw-chip`, `sw-bar`, `sw-grid`, `sw-muted`); the persona tells the agent to use them and set no fonts, background or palette of its own, in place of "system fonts or one good font … in light and dark". The autoresearch kit loads the same stylesheet and draws with its variables and an earthier branch palette. An artifact page has an opaque origin (`sandbox` without `allow-same-origin`), so its font requests are CORS requests from `null`: `_headers` gives `/fonts/*` and `/kit/*` `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. Checked in headless Chrome against f7df8db3fe54's pages (its own `progress` page, which sets `system-ui` and its own palette, and the `autoresearch` chart) at 1280 and 390 px: both rendered in Paradigm Serif on white with no sideways overflow; under `vite` the fonts were refused for want of that header, which `vite` does not apply. `/a` replaces it with `sandbox allow-scripts allow-popups allow-popups-to-escape-sandbox allow-downloads allow-modals` (never `allow-same-origin`, so the page has an opaque origin and cannot read the site's localStorage, where the session token lives, or its parent) plus `default-src 'none'`, scripts and styles from the swarm's origin, the gateway and four CDNs (jsDelivr, unpkg, cdnjs, esm.sh), `connect-src` and images from the swarm's origin and the gateway only, `frame-ancestors` the swarm's origin and `nosniff`. The page loads its own data files by relative URL (the CSP's origin rule covers `/a`), and can read the swarm's public API.
- The Artifacts tab (`Artifacts.tsx`) lists them and shows the chosen one (`?artifact=<slug>` in the URL): HTML in a sandboxed iframe with the same flags, an image as an image, Markdown rendered with relative links resolved against its folder, CSV and TSV and arrays of JSON objects as tables, other JSON pretty-printed. Reload adds a cache-busting nonce, Expand fills the screen (Escape closes it), Open opens the file. The page polls the list every 30 s while the agent works and every 60 s otherwise. The swarm's Durable Object reads the list at most every 30 s while a turn runs and at its end (`publish`): a new artifact is logged in the feed and announced in the room as a message with an Open button (`Said.artifact`, the `chat.artifact` column), and a new version in a later turn is logged.
- The agent learns the convention from its system prompt (2026-11-05; until 2026-11-08, when each swarm got its own instructions in place of the persona, next bullet): `Persona.ts` kept the operator wallet's gateway persona `swarm` (tempoxyz/gateway `Personas`: `POST /v1/personas/set` with `instructions`, which the gateway appends to the root agent's Responses `instructions` or Messages `system` as `# AGENTS.md instructions for persona swarm`; subagents do not get it). The text says what artifacts are for (progress at a glance, explanations, how close the goal is), gives the user's examples (a tree of ideas explored by subagents, a grid of C libraries being ported with each one's state), asks for taste (a headline answer to "how close are we?" first, the work left as visible as the work done, real numbers only, a restrained palette, 390 px to desktop, light and dark, one index.html reading a data.json) and gives the bucket and kv calls. `Persona.ensure` sets it when its text changed since the object last set it (0.0005 from the operator key, kv `personaSet`), so a new text reaches the gateway at the first turn after the deploy. A local run against the production gateway with another text overwrites the production persona, and production swarms do not set it back until their text changes; set it again by deleting `personaSet` or deploying a text change. Until 2026-11-06 `styled` called it only for an agent that did not have the persona yet, so once every running swarm's agent had it, a new text waited for a new swarm: two deploys after the Artifacts section gained how to read a file back, the gateway still held the 10,344-character text while three swarms ran. It now calls it first. `Swarm.lead` creates each new agent with `persona: 'swarm'` (falling back to none on a 409, when an older agent of the app holds the handle) and gives an existing agent the persona once with `PATCH /v1/agents/:id` (`styled`, kv `persona`). An artifact the agent publishes is announced in the room again (a row that opens it) and shows in the transcript as a card (`Shelved`: new or updated, its title and summary, and for the latest entry of a page or image a live preview, a sandboxed iframe at half scale that does not take clicks), and the whole card opens it in the Artifacts tab.
- Each swarm has its own instructions, fixed for its life, and Swarms sets no persona (2026-11-08, Georgios: "Swarms uses no shared persona at all"). Until then every lead ran with the operator wallet's one gateway persona `swarm` (`Persona.ensure` set it through `POST /v1/personas/set` whenever its text changed, `lead` created agents with `persona: 'swarm'` and `restyled` gave older ones the persona with `PATCH`), and the gateway read its text by name on every root call, so each edit of the text reached every running lead at once: it was edited 7 times on the night of 2026-11-07, and each edit changed the system prompt of every running lead and cost each one an uncached call (about $0.48 against $0.048 on f3a42cf87339's Opus 5.5 lead). Now `Persona.render(setup)` builds the text from the current sections with the swarm's own `# Models` (its template's `Setup.told`: the lead's harness and model, the subagents' harnesses and models and what a call of each costs; the old generic paragraph for swarms without a setup), and `Persona.keep` stores it in the swarm object's kv `instructions` once the gateway took it (the lead's creation, or the `PATCH` below), after which `Persona.frozen` answers that text whatever the code renders (it answers the current render until then). The first version froze the text at the swarm's first step instead, which a paused swarm's alarm or a page view's `heal` also runs, so every swarm that stepped while a build was live kept that build's text before its lead ever saw it; schema version 3 deletes kv `instructions` wherever the lead is not yet `instructed`, which only those step-time freezes wrote (between 03:32 and 03:41 UTC on 2026-11-08, when Swarms 9577fc8 and the marker build below were live). `lead` creates the lead with it as the agent's own instructions (tempoxyz/gateway's `agents.instructions`, migration 0081: appended to the root's system prompt where a persona's went, set once and never changed by anything else, and an agent with them takes no persona), so a lead created again later (after a lost agent, or the 15-minute unreachable rule) gets the same text, not a newer one, and a code change to the sections reaches only swarms created after it. A lead on the shared persona (a `Lead` record without `instructed`) gets the frozen text on its swarm's next turn, when `instructed` sends `PATCH /v1/agents/:id` with `instructions`, which the gateway takes once and which clears the agent's persona; a failed `PATCH` is tried again the next turn and never fails the turn, and a 409 (instructions already set) counts as done. That happens only when the swarm runs for its own reasons: nothing wakes, steers or funds a swarm for it, and the leads were paused, so their caches were cold anyway. `Gateway.persona`, `Gateway.restyle`, `Persona.ensure`, `Persona.name` and kv `personaSet` (deleted on freezing) are gone. `test/instructions.test.ts` checks the rendered text (every section, the template's models, under the gateway's 32,768-character cap), that a frozen text survives a later render, that a new or re-created lead is created with the frozen text and no persona and nothing calls `/v1/personas`, and that a lead on the persona is patched once and again after a failed patch. Checked on production on 2026-11-08 (gateway 12d43aa, version f720efaf, after its migration 0081; Swarms 9577fc8, version 5a9cd31a) with two throwaway Frontier Mix swarms funded in MACH from the test wallet's admin key: 30ae5277ed14 (1 MACH) created its lead with no persona and the 18,080-character text in `agents.instructions` (its `# Models` opening "Models (the swarm's Frontier Mix setup): you run on Claude Code with vercel/anthropic/claude-opus-5.5, about $0.038 a model call …"), and its update quoted `# AGENTS.md instructions for this agent` and that sentence from its system prompt; its two model calls cost $0.091591 for 18,166 tokens (uncached, 5.04 micro-dollars a token) and $0.012476 for 18,616 (0.67, cached). Then a build of the same commit with one sentence added to `# The work` ("Say plainly what you did and what you found.") was deployed from a scratch clone, never pushed (version 2052ea4c, live from 03:35:51 to 03:41:45 UTC; no other swarm was created meanwhile): 97370f4e75d0 (0.5 MACH), created under it, quoted the paragraph with the new sentence and its agent row held it, while 30ae5277ed14, given a note with 0.3 MACH three minutes later, quoted the same paragraph without it, its row unchanged, and its call cost $0.011705 for 18,849 tokens (0.62 a token, cached), where a persona edit cost f3a42cf87339's lead ten times its usual call. main was deployed again after it (version 1a9e7c41). Swarms whose lead was still on the shared persona at 03:50 UTC on 2026-11-08 (30): 236cb54576c8, 2452f9b2260d, 48dfb55dc725, c77fde6b935c, 8a1f4985a341, 29a779262595, 55d0e30060e2, c30244e83cb9, 79019a6087e5, 3e740359642d, 2d4a1a9790ba, bbff259f5abb, a8efb916f435, 33a66a5137ee, 3a05762b5254, ae64c263f6a1, 293f5683de4c, 71ae8f963b9b, 1cadc9cc84d4, f331c7adcb98, edbd09aeed99, c40012cbf6af, 2fb3495bdb89, 46e026f9e084, fc98b729378e, f57d8e240d4b, f7df8db3fe54, 44926f7cc4e7, 98eb38605e98, 3eaac25332a3. f3a42cf87339 moved on its first turn after the deploy, which its backers started (its gateway row then read `persona: null` with the 18,080-character text). The two throwaway swarms were deleted from D1 afterwards (their swarms, contributions, reports and search rows; both pages answer 404) and their three MACH channels closed with the test wallet's `requestClose` (1.64 of the 1.8 MACH came back; the check cost $0.159). The rest moved off the persona the same night without a turn, and the persona is gone (Georgios: "no shared persona at all"), through three one-shot deploys of a minute cron (`scheduled` in `index.ts` with `triggers.crons`), each removed by the next, since only the operator key, a Worker secret, may make these calls. Swarms 0af46ba (version 51c99869, 03:59 UTC) called a swarm object method on all 62 swarms each minute that, for a lead not yet `instructed` while no step ran (`stepping` unset, run not starting or working), ran `Turns.instructed` as a turn would: the frozen text (`Persona.render` with the swarm's setup), `PATCH /v1/agents/:id` with it, and kv `instructions` and `instructed` only once the gateway took it, with no turn, steer, alarm or phase change. Its first run, at about 04:04, logged `{"instruct":62,"left":[],"withoutAgent":21}`, and the gateway's agents on the persona went from 30 to 2: 3e740359642d and c77fde6b935c, swarms of local `vite` runs against the production gateway that are not in production D1, whose agents the operator key owns. b7811f8 (version aa9488f3) gave those two the generic text (`Persona.render(null)`, 17,798 characters; both answered with it and `persona: null`) and deleted the throwaway swarms' agents (agt_181c4068e09cb8f0ccc9416760ffa5f2 and agt_b88c7dd7f2c4e1d3ef7f8d33890e4879, `DELETE /v1/agents/:id` in their apps), after which the gateway counted no agent on the persona and none in those apps. 915cd57 (version 2c66478c) then deleted the persona (`POST /v1/personas/delete` with `{"name":"swarm"}`; the operator wallet's 17,747-character row last set at 02:32 UTC was gone by 04:15:42), and the next deploy dropped the cron (`crons: []`, then the key). Every swarm's D1 row (status, why, rounds, spend and money left, updated time) read the same before and after but f3a42cf87339's, which its backers had running and which was instructed already.
- Autoresearch (2026-11-06, the user's request: "teach the swarms about autoresearch somewhere and the classic diagram they have so that it's easy to get started w/ auto research on any of these for a given problem - artifacts help with presenting the progress and branches are the way that we know which parts of the design space have been explored"). The persona's `# Autoresearch` section teaches the loop of karpathy/autoresearch (its `program.md`, read on 2026-11-06):
  - Setup: one metric with its direction, from an evaluation fixed at the start; a fixed budget per experiment; a small surface the experiments edit; the baseline as the first row.
  - The loop, on a machine: commit an idea, run it with its output in a log file, read the metric, append a row to `results.tsv` (commit, metric, a cost column, status keep, discard or crash, description), then keep the commit or reset. Simpler wins, and the agent does not stop to ask whether to go on.
  - Branches map the design space: one subagent per region, each with its own `budget_usd`, machine and git branch `autoresearch/<region>`, and the loop in its task, since subagents do not get the persona. Each writes its own `artifacts/autoresearch/<region>.tsv`, so no two writers overwrite one file. A clear win is merged into main and the other branches restart from it; a region that stops paying off is marked a dead end and stays on the map.
  - The chart is the README's `progress.png` (drawn by its `analysis.ipynb`): every experiment a dot against its number, discarded ones grey, kept ones green and labelled, the running best as a step line, titled "N experiments, K kept improvements".
  - `public/kit/autoresearch.js` draws it. It is served at `/kit/autoresearch.js`, cached 5 minutes (`_headers`), and the artifact CSP allows it because it is Swarms' own origin. The agent stores a one-line `index.html` that loads it, a `data.json` and the TSVs. `data.json` has `title`, `metric` (the TSVs' column; when no column has that name the page reads a column named `metric`, else the second one, since f7df8db3fe54's agent wrote `metric` as the header and `speedup` in `data.json` on 2026-11-07 and its chart said "No experiments yet" over 11 rows), `better` (lower or higher), an optional `goal` and `baseline`, and `branches`, each with `name`, `explores`, `state` (exploring, promising, dead end or done), `found` and an optional `results` file. Rows come from `<name>.tsv` per branch, or `results.tsv` without branches; columns are found by header, and without a `status` column a row that beats the running best counts as kept.
  - The page shows a headline (the best value, its gain on the baseline, the counts, how far to the goal), the chart, the branch map and the ten biggest kept gains, in light and dark, and reloads its files every 60 s. Chart labels are placed biggest gain first, skip any that would overlap a placed one, stop at 24, and are left out under 560 px.
  - The persona went from 5,624 to 10,344 characters; the gateway caps instructions at 32,768 (tempoxyz/gateway `Personas.ts`).
  - Checked on 2026-11-06 in headless Chrome against a Node server that serves made-up results under `Artifacts.policy`'s CSP, inside an iframe with the Artifacts tab's sandbox flags (origin `null`). The cases were a 90-experiment single loop, four branches with a goal, a higher-is-better log without a status column and an empty log, at 1440, 1100, 1000 and 390 px, light and dark. There were no console errors apart from the 404 for an absent `data.json`, and no sideways overflow. Under `wrangler dev` the build served the kit as `text/javascript` with `max-age=300`.
  - Not run: an agent following the section on the gateway. That needs a deploy, which sets the new persona, and a funded turn.
- Tools and services (2026-11-07, the user's request that swarm agents know about and use paid services): the persona's `# Tools and services` section, after the machine-lease paragraph, tells the agent to use its harness's web search for current information, names the gateway's tool families (compute, kv, bucket, git, browser, email, phone), and says that other paid web services are found with the gateway's free `services_search` (tempoxyz/gateway f9fbb55: mpp.dev's directory and Mercator's registry, each endpoint's price and whether this caller can pay it) and called from a cell with `fetch`, which the gateway pays and charges to the payer as `agent.fetch`; it states `Paying.passable`'s limits ($0.25 a call, the larger of $1 and a twentieth of what the swarm raised an hour, half of that to one host, refused with `limit_reached`), asks for the price first, the cheapest service, one call before many, and to pass the guidance into a branch's task, since subagents do not get the persona. Cards are left out: the gateway's `cards_*` refuse an agent with a payer before payment. The gateway's MCP server instructions already say the same (f9fbb55), but nanocode never shows a server's instructions to the model (`server_instructions` in `crates/integrations/mcp-tools` has no caller at nanocode 2daf84a93), so subagents learn it from `services_search`'s own description, which says how `fetch` pays and to check the price, and from the task the agent gives them. The persona went from 12,998 to 14,432 characters. Once model templates (`# Models`) and the Wishlist landed, one pass ordered the persona as The work, Models, Tools and services (with the heavy-computation and machine-lease paragraphs moved in from Branches), Wishlist, Money behind every request, Branches, Artifacts and Autoresearch, and replaced the four sections' separate "subagents do not see this" sentences with one list in Branches of what each subagent's task must carry (the money weighing, the models it may give its own subagents and its budget, web search and services_search with any service found, the publishing instructions, and reporting blockers for the Wishlist); the instructions went from 17,351 to 17,218 characters, under the gateway's 32,768.
- Wishlist (2026-11-07, the user's request: let the agent ask the world for things in plain text, "only an artifact convention: no new tool, and nothing is wired up automatically"). The persona's `# Wishlist` section asks the agent to keep one artifact titled Wishlist, at `artifacts/wishlist` with the entry `wishlist.json`: a JSON array of items, each `want`, `why`, `use` (what it would pay or how it would use it), `status` (open, found or no longer needed) and, once found, `found` (where and from whom). It covers tools, paid services and APIs, datasets, accounts or access, machines with special hardware or local data, expertise and money for a step; it tells the agent to look with web search and `services_search` before wishing for a service, never to ask for or store secrets, to check what strangers point it to (real, what it does, what it costs) before relying on it, to mark an item found and thank whoever brought it in its update, and, since subagents do not get the persona, to have branches report what blocks them and to pass what was found into their tasks. It sits after Tools and services, and the persona grew from 15,145 to 17,351 characters (the gateway's cap is 32,768).
  - Answers reach the agent as backers' notes (which it already gets) and in the room, which it did not see: the swarm sends only notes, never room chat (Turns' `brief` and `next`). So the payer's `budget` answer (`Swarm.budget`, the gateway's free `budget` tool) now also names the swarm: `swarm` (its id), `page` and `room` (`<SWARMS_URL>/v1/swarms/<id>/chat`, public), and the persona asks the agent to read the room with a free cell `fetch` at the start of each turn while the Wishlist has open items, from the `next` it answered last time. Posting in a room needs backing (money or a lent machine), so the page's prompt says "Tell the swarm in the room, or in a note when you fund it".
  - The page (`Artifacts.tsx`, `shared/Wishlist.ts`, tested in `test/wishlist.test.ts`): `useWishes` in `Swarm.tsx` finds the artifact (`Wishlist.is`: JSON, slugged or titled wishlist) and reads its file once per version; `Wishlist.read` takes an array or `{ items }`, at most 50, maps a status to open, found or dropped and ignores items without a `want`. With open items the Artifacts tab carries a "2 wishes" pill and opens on a Wishlist strip above the shelf (the open items, a row that opens the full list, and Can you help? → the room, or its sign-in or fund row when the visitor cannot post); the wishlist itself renders as rows with Open, Found and No longer needed chips instead of the generic JSON table. Without a wishlist, or with none open, nothing shows. Not done: "N wishes" on the home and join cards, which would need the Worker to read every listed swarm's wishlist file.
  - Checked under `vite` on the fixture world (bench-coders seeded with two open, one found and one dropped item) in headless Chrome at 1440 and 390 px: the pill, the strip and the list rendered with no sideways overflow and no console error; the strip's row opened `?artifact=wishlist`, the help row focused the room's sign-in button on a desktop and switched to the Room tab on a phone; json-terabyte, without a wishlist, showed neither.
- The fixture world has five artifacts on "Benchmark every open-weight coding model" (a live leaderboard page, an SVG chart, a CSV table, a Markdown write-up and a wishlist) and one on the JSON parser swarm, as data URLs; the room announces two of them, and its feed has two publish lines and an update.
- Checked on 2026-11-05 with this branch under `vite` against the deployed gateway (c736909e) on the local swarm c77fde6b935c, whose agent was writing lighthouse haiku: its next turn gave the agent the persona (`GET /v1/agents/:id` answered `persona: swarm` with the operator wallet as scope, and `/v1/personas/get` the 3,617-character text), a team-budget steer asked for "a small progress artifact: how many haiku are done of how many planned, as a live page", and within two minutes the agent parsed its own file, published `haiku-progress` (an index.html and a data.json) and its manifest, and the page listed it, announced it in the room and showed it in the transcript and the Artifacts tab: a headline "51 of 54 haiku written" with the done count in one accent color, a progress bar with "94% complete · 3 remaining" and the update time, then a card per haiku, at 1440 and 390 px. Its update linked `/a/haiku-progress/index.html`, without the swarm id, so the text now asks the agent to name artifacts rather than link them. On 2026-11-06 the agent of f7df8db3fe54 changed its `data.json` by reading it back with `bucket_get` and writing that whole answer, the object's metadata with the file in `text`, so its page threw on `d.leaderboard` and said loading… for good; the text now shows how to read a file back, and the file was repaired with the operator key (the agent's own fields put back into the file, one `bucket_put`).
- Checked on 2026-11-02 under `vite` against a local stand-in for the gateway (a Node server answering `/api/keys/me` and `/p` kv and bucket reads the way the gateway does, `SWARMS_GATEWAY`): the list kept two good manifests and an `../../secret.html` entry as `index.html` and dropped a bad slug; `/a` answered the page with the CSP above, 301 for a slug without a slash, 404 for an encoded `../` and for a missing or private file; in headless Chrome the leaderboard page fetched its `data.json` and rendered it, while `localStorage`, `window.parent.document` and a fetch to example.com were all blocked; the Markdown write-up showed its relative `chart.svg` and its table. Headless Chrome on the fixture world at 1440 and 390 px: the tab, the room's artifact announcement opening it, each form, Expand on a phone, the dock, its More sheet and the room's unread count. Not exercised: an agent publishing an artifact through the real gateway (it needs a funded turn). Every list and file read goes through the gateway's `/p`, whose `PUBLIC_LIMIT` is 300 a minute per caller IP; this Worker's reads come from Cloudflare's egress, so many swarms and viewers at once could reach it (429 shows as "Too many artifact reads right now"), and caching the files in this Worker would be the next step.

- Surface (2026-11-09, Georgios and Žygimantas in #ai-agent: "a Tern-like dynamic ui" for swarms, then "open the pr"; the mock is https://slate.tehq.net/a/art_2w3cp705ahx5 and the walkthrough of four kinds of goal is in https://pyramid.tehq.net/threads/98cf5fa4-486d-4832-8294-fd5eb216d928). The agent lays out the top of the Results tab itself, as Tern's programs describe their UI as a tree of node kinds the terminal draws: the persona's `# Surface` section, after Wishlist, asks it to keep one JSON artifact titled Surface (`artifacts/surface`, entry `surface.json`), `{ nodes: [...] }`, each node `id`, `kind`, optional `title` and `wide`, in the shape the goal needs (a chart and the plan for a number to push, hypotheses and findings for open research, a range for an estimate, files and tests for a build), updated in place at least once a turn while running, under 12 nodes. Like the Wishlist it is only an artifact convention: no new tool, route or storage, and a swarm without one renders as before. `shared/Surface.ts` reads it (`Surface.read`, tested in `test/surface.test.ts`): ten kinds, `asks`, `stats`, `checklist`, `chart`, `bars`, `list`, `meter`, `range`, `tiles` and `text`, with aliases for the names agents reach for (`attention`/`needs`, `plan`, `budget`, `findings`/`hypotheses`/`sources`, `estimate`, `note`); unknown kinds, empty nodes, out-of-order ranges and repeated ids are dropped, values clamped, at most 12 nodes, and `asks` always first. `Surface.tsx` draws each kind as a card in a grid on the Results tab above what it made (`useSurface` beside `useWishes`, which now share `useJson`), `asks` and `stats` across the full width; an ask's button opens the backing box with its text as the direction (`steer`) or, with `action: chat`, the chat, so asking for money stays one tap. The Surface artifact is left off the shelf of what it made, as the Wishlist is. The persona grew by about 2,300 characters (under the gateway's 32,768), so each running lead reads its context uncached once after the deploy. Checked under `vite` with the fixture world, whose bench-coders swarm now has a Surface (asks, stats, plan, bars, chart and findings): at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone it drew every card with no sideways overflow and no console errors, and json-terabyte, which has none, rendered without it; `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and the node tests pass. Not done: the home and ideas cards do not read it, and nothing pushes a changed surface but the artifacts signal the page already follows.

## Contributor hands

- Anyone can lend a swarm their own machine as a nanocode hand. `POST /v1/swarms/:id/hands` (also `/api`, and the MCP tool `swarm_hand`) takes an optional `label` and answers the hand's `name`, `token` and `url`, plus both ways to attach:
  - `cli`: `nc hand '<url>' --token <token>` (nanocode 0.6.7), and `command`, the gateway's line that installs nanocode first.
  - `library`: a Node module that runs nanocode-ts's `Hand.connect` over its in-memory shell (`Connect.library`, release build-20f97e0, installed with `npm`).
  - `GET /v1/swarms/:id/hands` lists the issued hands and which are connected. The swarm page's "Connect your machine" box (`Connect.tsx`) issues one and shows both forms.
- A hand is an account hand of the operator key in the swarm's app (`POST /v1/hands?name=` with `Gateway-App: swarm-<id>`; see the gateway's AGENTS.md on account hands), so the agent and its subagents can use it in every turn, whichever contribution pays.
- Hands issued before `upgrade`, under a swarm's old pool key, stop attaching, and so do all hands when the operator key changes; contributors issue new ones.
- Hand pricing belongs to the gateway and the hand SDK: an owner prices their own hand (`nc hand <url> --token <t> --pricing prices.json` with `{"payee","describe","rules":[{"paths","ops","usd"}]}`, or nanocode-ts `Hand.connect(…, { pricing: { payee, describe, price(operation) } })`), the gateway's hub charges the agent that made each priced call before it releases the reply (operation `hand.use` on `/mcp`, so this app's payer pays it like any MCP call, at the price as `amount`), and the gateway pays the payee in USDC.e. So Swarms keeps no prices, meters nothing and sends no payouts (the user's decision of 2026-10-31, after a first version that metered and paid hands from the swarm's wallet). It reads `GET /v1/hands`: `requests` (gateway 8c471aa: the operations the hub dispatched, the hand's environment probe included, never counted again on a replay) and, from gateway `feat/paid-hands` (agent 14's branch, not deployed on 2026-10-31), `pricing` (`payee`, `describe`), `charges` (`count`, `usd`, billed to this account's agents), `earned`, `paid` and `cap`. `Hands.terms` turns them into the price summary, charges and earnings that the Connect box, the hands API and the agent's prompt show (`test/hands.test.ts`). At each turn's start `cap` sets every connected hand's cap to a fifth of what is left to spend with `POST /v1/hands/<name>/cap` `{ usd }`, which starts a new window each time, and a hand that joins mid-turn gets the same cap; it sends a cap only for hands whose listing has a `cap` field, so nothing reaches a gateway without caps.
- Lent hands are paid over MPP channels to their owners (2026-10-31, with gateway `feat/mpp-hand-billing`, not deployed that day): for a hand charge the gateway's payer request carries the owner's OUSD session challenge first, with `recipient` (the owner's address) and `hand`, and each channel snapshot names its `payee`. `Swarm.charge` then tries the swarm's wallet on a channel of its own to that owner first (source `hand:<payee>`, its mppx store `mppx:hand:<payee>:`, opened with 0.05 or the price, whichever is more, and topped up by 0.05, smaller than the gateway channel's 0.1 because it can pay only that owner), and only when the wallet cannot (no OUSD left, a USDC.e owner, a refused credential) falls back to the usual sources paying the gateway's challenge, which the gateway turns into its payout to the owner; a MACH pledge always falls back, because MACH cannot reach an owner. `Payer.offer` takes the recipient and now hands mppx a 402 with only the chosen challenge: before, a wallet paying the gateway's OUSD challenge on a hand charge would have signed the owner's OUSD challenge, the first in the list. The owner channels count in `pool_spent`, not in what the swarm may spend, since their deposits pay only their owner. At done `settle` closes them with `requestClose` from the wallet (no cooperative close: the gateway's `kv/get` route takes only its own channels), which the gateway's close supervision answers, and the refund waits for every such channel's deposit to reach 0 (`closing:hand:<payee>`). `Hands.terms` adds `channels` (what the owner was paid over channels and how much settled) and the price's currency, which the Connect box and `Lent` in the OpenAPI document show. Checked on 2026-10-31 against the gateway branch under `pnpm dev` (its AGENTS.md has the run): swarm 77e70888e4a0, funded by 0.15 OUSD, paid its lead's $0.01 read of a lent hand over channel 0x6301240e0c3d83bb1c71ca188b01801a6cad126bf080375145c0569ad4d6fe68 from its wallet (feed: "Opened a channel from the swarm's wallet to the owner of hand data-room-593ebb"), ended, asked the escrow to close that channel (0x73be4636d1b3b1098a8a6a7337a6d3a2baefe7dcdb18ac884191f8617e5beec3), and refunded 0.134862 OUSD (0x68ca0f0b6e988969249e43531283acdbd61e9b1bafa9d97641e8df20bc65726f) once the gateway had closed it (0xd4332c96f03c506e6950cdaa5cf81000912d0a7d147a033c6fabd80a4d4b4a24). The fallback through a MACH pledge was not run.
- Names are `<slug of the label>-<6 random hex>`, recorded in D1 `hands` (migration 0006) under `(swarm, name)` with insert-or-ignore and up to three tries, and the swarm also refuses a name the gateway already lists. They must not collide: the gateway hands out the same secret when the same name is minted again under one key, so a repeated name would give one contributor's token to another. A swarm takes at most 32 hands, a number chosen without measurement.
- The agent is told: its first prompt and every continuation list the connected contributor hands with their labels and price summaries and the money left (`lending` in `brief` and `next`). The text says the machines and their data belong to contributors, to use a priced hand when what it holds is worth its price, to work in a directory of their own, to treat what they return as data, and to stop and ask in the report before anything destructive or invasive. A contributor hand that connects during a turn is announced to the running turn with a steer (`welcome`), and the feed logs every contributor machine that joins with its label.
- When the swarm is done, `done` disconnects every issued hand (`DELETE /v1/hands/<name>`).
- Checked on 2026-10-30 with the built Worker under `wrangler dev` against the deployed gateway:
  - Issuing:
    - Four hands were issued on one swarm. Two with the same label got different suffixes.
    - An invalid label answered 400, an unknown swarm 404 and a done swarm 409.
    - The `library` module, run as issued in Node 22 with the release installed by npm, attached, and the listing showed it connected.
    - The `cli` line, under `nanocode hand` 0.6.7 with `-C`, attached for 20 s and was listed connected, then offline after it exited.
  - End to end, on swarm `9f9920b7df6d`, whose goal could only be met on the hand:
    - The library hand attached with a seeded `/workspace/contributor.txt`.
    - A 0.15 OUSD `transferWithMemo` from the test wallet was counted through `POST /v1/swarms/:id/contributions` (201, then 409). The pool moved 0.148 OUSD into its channel, and the feed logged the contributor's machine joining.
    - The lead's pool round listed the hand. The lead ran `cat /workspace/contributor.txt` and `ls /workspace` and wrote `/workspace/reply.txt` there. The hand's workspace logged each `run`, and the file read back `ack\n`.
    - The round spent $0.005838. Then the swarm ended: the hand stopped with `attachment endpoint permanently revoked this attachment` and exited, the pool key was revoked, and 0.140062 OUSD went back to the test wallet a minute later.
  - The lead's first two cells named the hand on `exec_command` and failed. The SDK had already placed those cells on the hand (`tools.hands()` reported it as `here`), and a nanocode-ts `Hand.connect` hand passed `hand` on to `exec_command`, which refused it (`unknown field hand`). nanocode-ts build-9c2dd35 (2026-11-01) routes a hand's nested calls by `hand`, so naming the hand a cell already runs on runs it there; the gateway pins a release with it from tempoxyz/gateway#130. The prompt now just says to pass `hand`; the version before said to leave it out when `tools.hands()` reports the hand as `here`. No round has run since that change.
  - Not run live: `welcome`, the steer for a hand that connects during a turn.
- Checked on 2026-10-31 on this branch, under `vite` against the deployed gateway: a hand issued on swarm 12d7b0f86f9e (`data-room-553b00`, now an account hand of the operator key in `swarm-12d7b0f86f9e`) and served by nanocode-ts's `Hand.connect` (release build-20f97e0) over its in-memory shell, seeded with `/workspace/data-room/q3.txt`, was listed connected; after a 0.1 pathUSD transfer the payer agent's first round ran `cat /workspace/data-room/q3.txt` there and reported both figures ($0.004144), and at done the hand was disconnected (the process exited) and 0.089829 OUSD went back (0x78a9e499b3274a9645194d508e2a570fabd746e8201e087b86b0a1d83cb13da7). A hand the gateway forgot after `DELETE` is no longer listed, so its `requests` read 0 afterwards.

## Feedback

- Anyone can comment on the site (the user's request of 2026-11-06, to dogfood nanocode-ts's feedback widget). Every page loads the gateway's `/feedback.js` in `index.html`'s head; it shows nothing until a page opens with `?feedback`, or after that browser sent a report. A click on any element writes a comment, and Send posts the report to `/feedback`, where the Worker serves nanocode-ts's `Handler.feedback` (`src/worker/Feedback.ts`, one per isolate) with the operator key, `SWARMS_GATEWAY_KEY`, through the `GATEWAY` service binding (`fetch`). Each report becomes the issue `git/swarms/issues/<n>` in the operator wallet's app `swarms-site`, the gateway's repository issue convention, numbered from 1; the console's Feedback tab at the gateway lists them to accept or close, and `/feedback/<n>` shows a reporter where theirs stands. `accept` takes only posts whose `Origin` is the site's own, no agent is woken (none can change this repository yet), and the operator key pays the gateway's `kv.set` price for each issue and number. Checked on 2026-11-06 under `vite` with this checkout's `.dev.vars`: `GET /feedback` answered ready for app `swarms-site` with the operator wallet, a post from another origin answered 403, and `/feedback/issues` 404 (no admin); the widget first threw `document.body.append` from the head, which nanocode-ts 788e8f8 fixed by waiting for `DOMContentLoaded`.
- The API and MCP take reports too (2026-11-07, the user's request after a session found Swarms bugs by reading a swarm's feed: "the swarm has tools for sending feedback, or the swarm user, and it could submit them"): `POST /v1/feedback` and the MCP tool `swarm_feedback` (`Feedback.file`: `message`, `kind`, `context` and an optional `swarm`) file a report as an issue of the same board through the same handler, as a post from the site's own origin whose page is the swarm's, and answer its id and status link. The MCP instructions say when to call it, and that a problem with a gateway tool belongs to the gateway's `send_feedback`. A swarm's agent already reports what breaks in the gateway's tools with `send_feedback`: on 2026-11-06 swarm agents filed fb_644e8201c07447deb2cacc81b50fbd0d (`kv_set` calls the payer refused, whose private keys were then public) and fb_93ac9252df644a6c95f2d98b92c6dfa9 (subagent budgets under $0.021 refuse every tool call), and nobody read them. The keys going public is Swarms' own `expose`, which publishes whatever the agent stored after each turn, and the agent is not told that.
