Such a report now shows in the transcript as a status line (`Trail.call`), and the gateway records the app, agent, subagent and turn of every report an agent sends (tempoxyz/gateway#327, migration 0057), so `select id, created_at, message, agent, turn from feedback where app like 'swarm-%' and resolved_at is null` on the gateway's D1 lists what swarms reported. Read that, and this board (the gateway console's Feedback tab for app `swarms-site`), when checking on swarms, before reading their feeds.

## Development

- Server-rendered pages give configuration the same 2500 ms budget as their other data reads (2026-11-19). A rejected configuration read previously aborted the HTML stream, and an unresolved read held the rendered content indefinitely after its opening bytes. The existing client bootstrap already retries when config is absent. `Clock.within` also clears a completed read's timer, including the model-price deadline, without closing live connections. Local checks: `pnpm_config_verify_deps_before_run=warn pnpm check` and `pnpm_config_verify_deps_before_run=warn pnpm build` passed using existing dependencies; pnpm warns that their build-approval metadata differs from the lockfile, and the reused nanocode-ts package is build-1ee1936 rather than the main lockfile’s build-c38629c, so these checks do not qualify that SDK upgrade for deployment. `node --test --experimental-strip-types test/*.test.ts` passed all 235 tests, including the configuration-failure regression that fails on the unchanged parent. Headless Chrome against `pnpm dev` and a local-only gateway returned server-rendered documentation without stalled config in 2923 ms; an injected client config failure recovered on its existing retry after 3016 ms. Documentation fixtures passed at 1440×900 and 390×844 touch with no browser errors, hydration warnings or horizontal overflow. This bounds the config await, not the whole response or upstream work; config can continue while its request context lives. Nothing was deployed, and no production percentile improvement is claimed.

- `pnpm dev` runs the Worker and the page. Set `SWARMS_STATE=<dir>` to keep local Durable Object and D1 state there, and `SWARMS_GATEWAY=<url>` to use a local gateway: only then, and only under `vite` serve, the Worker's `GATEWAY_URL` is that URL and `global_fetch_strictly_public` is off, because the flag sends fetches to the public internet, which a local gateway is not on. Under `vite` serve the `GATEWAY` service binding is dropped, so development calls the gateway by `fetch`. Builds and deploys keep `wrangler.jsonc` as it is. `pnpm db:migrate:local` (add `--persist-to <dir>` with `SWARMS_STATE`) applies `migrations/`. `.dev.vars` needs `SWARMS_SECRET`, `SWARMS_GATEWAY_KEY` and `SWARMS_URL`, a public URL of the dev server, because the gateway calls the pay route there: `cloudflared tunnel --url http://localhost:<port> --http-host-header localhost` (vite answers only its own host names, so the tunnel rewrites the Host header).
- `pnpm check` type-checks the Worker and the page. `pnpm build` builds both. `pnpm exec wrangler dev -c dist/swarms/wrangler.json` runs the build, where CORS preflights behave as deployed.
- Sign-in in development: the session origin is `SWARMS_URL` and Tempo Wallet's frame fetches `/v1/auth/challenge` from the page's own origin, so sign in on the page served at `SWARMS_URL` (the tunnel), not on localhost. A script can sign in on localhost: the message names `SWARMS_URL`'s host whatever host served it.
- Deploy: set `SWARMS_SECRET` and `SWARMS_GATEWAY_KEY` with `wrangler secret put`, then `pnpm db:migrate` (0028 adds `swarms.setup`, which the Worker reads, so it runs before the deploy; 0008 adds `sessions`, `steers` and `hands.contributor`; 0009 adds `names`; 0014 adds `swarms.creator`, `team` and `steering` and `steers.budget`, which the Worker reads, so it runs before the deploy; 0024 adds `contributions_backing` and the search rank and drops `swarms_problem`, and can run before or after the deploy, best before; 0026 makes `contributions.kind` nullable and adds `contributions_sponsored`, before the deploy that stops writing `kind`; 0027 drops the dead columns, after it), then `pnpm deploy`. The gateway must have agent payers (82d56b6) and public reads (5b5c47d); cooperative closes need its session account (70818b8). Since 2026-11-06 the Worker and the swarm objects reach the gateway through the `GATEWAY` service binding (`Gateway.call`). They use the same public HTTP API and key, only without the trip out to the internet and back; see the cold-path bullet under Research experience. Other outbound fetches still go to the public internet (`global_fetch_strictly_public`).
- No request awaits a promise another request started (2026-11-07, after the gateway's incident of that night, #415, where a per-isolate cache handed one request's in-flight background D1 read to later requests and those hung until their clients gave up, as `canceled` with no logs; Workers do not let one request's I/O be used by another). `Edge.cached` deduplicated reads by keeping each in-flight read's promise in `refreshing` and handing it to every request that came meanwhile, including the read a request started in `waitUntil` for a stale value. A `/branches` request (which waits up to 1.5 s for such a read) ended `canceled` in each production tail of the night, but one did again after this deploy (c36ab06f), when the audit's pages closed mid-request, so those were not shown to come from the sharing. Now a request with no cached value reads for itself, and while one request refreshes a stale value the others answer the stale value without waiting on it (`refreshing` holds only the keys being refreshed); `test/edge.test.ts` checks both, and its first test fails on the old code. The other per-isolate caches of the Worker that held promises keep settled values instead: `Gateway.wallet` (the operator's wallet), `Gateway.sponsorship` (the gateway's channel terms) and `Page`'s `index.html` and `preload.json`, whose `shell` also fetched `index.html` from the assets on every page even when it held it. The swarm object's own promises (`stepping`, `funding`, the payer queue, `signers`) live in one Durable Object and are left as they are; `Swarm.steady` holds a settled, cloned row.
- No code comments. Record decisions with their evidence here.
- Dead code is found, not guessed (2026-11-07): an export is dead when TypeScript's `findReferences` (a language service over `src` and `test`) finds no use outside its file, a local when `tsc --noUnusedLocals` flags it, and a CSS rule when a class it needs appears nowhere in `src` or `index.html` (no class name is built by concatenation, and template literals build only `brief-`, `panel-`, `tab-` and `swarm-` names, which count as used); a selector that needs a dead class can never match, so it goes, and a rule goes with its last selector. The scripts are `/tmp/swarms-opt/unused.mjs`, `deadcss.mjs` and `prunecss.mjs` (outside the repository). That night it removed 260 CSS selectors (233 rules: the old header nav, hero, tiles, constellation, slots, field inputs, machine tiles, steps, commit heads, sheets, fuel forms, quest emblems, the Ideas board, the old say buttons), `style.css` 2,396 to 1,588 lines and `room.css` 1,061 to 995, and the page's stylesheet from 120.2 KB (23.2 KB gzipped) to 100.0 KB (19.9 KB), on every page; the `Ideas` leaderboard component (the tab became Contributors; `Ideas.tsx` keeps `Presence`), `Bar`, `usePresence`, the research areas, `Wire.live`, `format.ratio`, `Gateway.revoke`, `Store.contributions`, and unused locals and parameters (the swarm object's `steer` took a sentence it never used). Checked by rendering every storyboard frame (225 shots, desktop and phone, `/tmp/pw/storyboard.mjs`) under `vite` before and after: 212 were pixel-identical, and the 13 that differed differed only in the fixture world's streaming transcript text.
- The swarm page loads no viem until someone pays (2026-11-07): `Contribute.send` imports `shared/Channel.ts` and `shared/Transfer.ts` when it runs, where `Contribute.tsx` imported them statically, and their `encodeFunctionData` and `keccak256` made the swarm page (and the home and join pages' idle prefetch of it) load viem's `call` chunk with its ABIs and errors, 31.4 KB gzipped, with the 6 KB `hash` and 1.9 KB `utils` chunks. The fund flow already awaited the wallet's own viem imports and a balance read between the tap and Tempo Wallet's approval, so the extra import changes nothing a payer sees. And the newest artifact's preview keeps its 200 px while the artifact list loads (an empty `shelved-peek`, only while `/artifacts` has not answered), where the transcript used to grow by 200 px when the list arrived: production's phone run of f7df8db3fe54 measured a layout shift of 0.21 at that moment.
- The swarm object writes less per gateway event (2026-11-07). A thought is buffered in the object's kv (`thinking`, `thinking:<who>`) from `reasoning.summary.delta` events and logged clipped to 1,500 characters (`Trail.thought`), and every delta rewrote the whole buffer however long it grew; `Trail.appended` stops growing it once its trimmed text is longer than 1,500, which logs the same line (`test/trail.test.ts` checks it against the unbounded buffer for leading, trailing and long tails), so a thought of L characters in d-character deltas costs at most about 1,500/d writes instead of L/d. Half of f7df8db3fe54's 337 logged thoughts were clipped at 1,500, so they had run past it. And the subagent roster (`crew`) is written only on a subagent's `added`, `status` or `completion` update, which are the only ones `Trail.subagent` changes it on, where it was written on every subagent event, tool calls and output deltas included.
- Markdown is parsed with GFM but never written back (2026-11-07): `Markdown.tsx` adds GFM's micromark syntax and its mdast `fromMarkdown` extension itself (`readGfm`, over `micromark-extension-gfm` and `mdast-util-gfm`, the two packages `remark-gfm` 4.0.1 wraps), where `remark-gfm` also registered GFM's `toMarkdown` extension, which pulled `mdast-util-to-markdown` and `markdown-table` into the swarm page for a serializer `react-markdown` never runs. The swarm page's chunk went from 237.0 KB (72.3 KB gzipped) to 215.5 KB (65.1 KB). Rendering a table, footnotes, strikethrough, task lists, autolinks, headings, code, quotes and lists and the last 16 agent messages of f7df8db3fe54 with `react-dom/server` gave the same HTML with either plugin (23 of 23, `/tmp/swarms-opt/md-compare.mjs`).
- Checked on production on 2026-11-07 after the deploys of 51a6767 through b9849e2 (versions b43e255f, 45e8426f, 257329f7), with `/tmp/pw/measure2.mjs` (cold, then warm, in headless Chrome; the phone run throttles to 150 ms, 1.6 Mbit/s and 4x CPU) before and after: the home page's JavaScript over the first seconds went from 223 KB to 176 KB transferred and its total from 348 KB to 298 KB, the same on `/join` (both also idle-load the swarm page's code), and a live swarm page (f7df8db3fe54) from 231 KB of JavaScript to 184 KB (223 KB to 171 KB before it hydrated) and 419 KB in all to 368 KB; that page's layout shift on the phone went from 0.214 to 0.003 and on a desktop from 0.061 to 0.0002. First paints moved within this network's run-to-run noise. Every listed page, the four newest swarms with their tabs and code pages, at 390 and 1,440 px, showed no console error, no `[object Object]`, `undefined` or `NaN`, and no sideways overflow (`/tmp/pw/prod-audit.mjs`); `/live` replayed f7df8db3fe54's feed, its room socket and the lobby answered `welcome`, and the home page and the swarm page made no request in 30 s with their sockets open.
- The swarm object is split by concern (2026-11-07), by pure moves, one commit each: `Swarm.ts` (2,498 lines, now 1,287) keeps the class, its RPC methods (every method `Operations` calls on the stub stays on it), its lifecycle (`step`, `advance`, `finish`, `lead`, `pause`) and its storage, and calls the rest as functions of the object: `Relay.ts` (the `/live` watchers, ring and heartbeat), `Paying.ts` (the payer behind `/pay`: `charge`, `attempt`, `vendor`, the outside-service limits and the channel bookkeeping), `Money.ts` (`refresh`, `refill`, sponsorships and hand caps), `Follow.ts` (following the gateway's event stream and `digest`), `Repo.ts` (the repository and code snapshot reads), `Branching.ts` (listing, budgeting and settling branches), `Chat.ts` (the room's messages and broadcasts), `Clock.ts` (durations several of them share) and `Words.ts` (text helpers). A moved method is `export function name(self: Swarm, …)` with `this` become `self`, and the class declares `ctx` and `env` public (`declare`) so those functions reach them; seven were renamed where a local or an import of the same name would shadow them (`channel` → `Paying.confirmed`, `room` → `Paying.headroom`, `crew` → `Follow.crewed`, `plan` → `Follow.planned`, `commits` → `Repo.history`, `owner` → `Repo.gatewayWallet`, `repo` → `Repo.located`). Two more moves followed the same night with the same proof: `Turns.ts` (the turn lifecycle: `step`, `initialize`, `advance`, `retry`, `lead`, `finish`, `pause`, `expose`, `sweep` and their helpers; `failures` → `Turns.failing` and `styled` → `Turns.restyled`, both shadowed by locals) and `Feed.ts` (the turn rows, the feed's search index and their SQL; `index` → `Feed.indexed`), leaving `Swarm.ts` at 916 lines; the harness then also asks both search routes, which answered the same before and after. Each move was made by a TypeScript-AST script (`/tmp/swarms-opt/move.mjs`, outside the repository) and proven three ways: an independent check that every moved body, turned back (`self` → `this`, `Module.name(self, …)` → `this.name(…)`), equals the old method text and that what stayed in `Swarm.ts` equals the old file less the moved parts (`verify.mjs`, `verify-rest.mjs`); `pnpm check`, the node tests and `pnpm build`; and a local run of the whole object against a stub gateway (`/tmp/swarms-opt/harness`: `vite` with `SWARMS_GATEWAY` at a node server that answers the gateway routes the object calls and streams a recorded-shape event stream of reasoning deltas, tool calls and results, a subagent's `added`, events and completion, a failed model call and the turn's result), which creates a swarm, contributes a MACH channel through `/contributions` (a Tempo transaction signed by a fixed throwaway key, which the stub registers), lets the object create its agent, run and finish two turns, then asks `/pay` twelve questions (a wrong secret, `budget`, another app, a request over the cap, a model call, a hand owner, outside fetches over and under the limit, two vendor charges, no challenge) and reads the detail, feed, room, `/wait`, `/live` (follow and replay) and the room socket. Its normalized output and the multiset of gateway calls were identical before the first move and after every one (two base runs were identical too).
- The payer's decisions have node tests (`test/paying.test.ts`, 2026-11-07): the per-call, hourly and per-host limits on outside services and their growth with what was raised, recording a paid outside call, the app and per-request cap refusals, the cumulative a voucher signs on an open channel (what the gateway requires, what it accepted plus one, or what was spent plus the calls in flight and this one, with in-flight calls older than 10 s forgotten), a token the swarm does not pay in, a closing channel's snapshot banking its spend and a source refused after two tries resting, and `rested`. `Paying.ts` imports `Gateway.ts`, whose `Edge.ts` imports `cloudflare:workers`, so the test maps that module to a stub with `module.registerHooks` before importing it, and drives the functions with a fake object (an in-memory kv and a client that records the credentials it is asked for). Mutating the voucher's `floor + 1n`, the per-call cap or the hourly divisor makes them fail.
- The payer answers without reading D1 and logs where its time goes (2026-11-07, after the gateway's traces of 2026-11-07 showed its ask to a swarm's `/pay` taking 604 to 2,914 ms, about 1 s at the median, while it holds each model call's first byte). Every ask is serialized in the swarm object (`Swarm.pay`'s `paying` chain), and each began with `found`, a D1 read whenever the object's 10 s row memo had lapsed or the swarm's epoch had moved, which `Money.refresh` does whenever the pool's figures change. `Paying.charge` now pays from the provisioned row the object last read (`Swarm.steady`, set by `found`), whose fields the payment uses (`id`, `wallet`, `sealed_wallet`) never change once set, and reads the row again only to word a refusal from the current `sponsor_left`; `raised`, which caps outside services per hour, only grows, so an older row only makes that cap stricter. Each ask logs one line, `{ pay, operation, paid, waited, worked, row, attempts, sign, confirm, room }` (ms: `waited` behind earlier asks, `worked` in `charge`, `sign` in the attempts, `confirm` waiting for a new channel's open to land, `room` reading the wallet's balance before an open or top-up), so the next running swarm shows in Workers Logs where an ask's time goes. Signing a voucher is local: 20 vouchers through `Payer.client` with a real gateway challenge took 0.6 to 6.8 ms each in Node and made no RPC call (`/tmp/swarms-opt/voucher-bench.mts`), so on an open channel with room the critical section is the row read and signing; an open or a top-up adds a balance read (260 ms from the Mac in the harness) and a transaction build. Serializing per channel or source instead of per swarm was not done: nearly every ask is a model call paid from the one pool channel, which must stay serialized because concurrent vouchers on one channel must be signed in increasing order, and the hand-owner channels it would free share the swarm wallet, whose opens and top-ups must not race either. `test/paying.test.ts` checks that a model call on an open channel reads no row and a refusal reads it once. The harness's output and gateway calls stayed identical.
- The payer against the real gateway, after that change (2026-11-07): a local `vite` of origin/main (9ec5573) in a scratch worktree, with its own `SWARMS_SECRET`, a gateway key of the test wallet (1 MACH limit, a day, approved with its admin key and revoked after) and `SWARMS_URL` at a `cloudflared` quick tunnel, ran a swarm the test wallet sent 0.1 OUSD to, whose agent on the gateway paid its model calls through `/pay` over the tunnel. In the swarm object 15 asks took 2 to 6 ms each (3 ms at the median, never waiting behind another), except the first, which opened the pool channel: 497 ms, 271 ms of it reading the wallet's balance. The gateway's own trace of each call (`/v1/traces/list`, its `pay` field) measured 156 to 547 ms for the seven voucher asks (297 ms at the median), round trips to the Mac through the tunnel, and 3,479 ms for the open, which the gateway lands on chain before it answers. So once the row read is gone the object's share of an ask is a few milliseconds, and the 604 to 2,914 ms the gateway measured in production before (about 1 s at the median) came from the transport and from asks queued behind each other's D1 reads, which the per-ask log line will show on the next running swarm. Not measured: production after the deploy (no swarm was running) and the same local run with the old code, whose D1 read is local and so fast there. The run cost the test wallet 0.038 OUSD (the gateway captured 0.035 of the 0.098 in the swarm's channel when it closed it, at the swarm wallet's `requestClose`, and the rest went to network fees and the 0.003 left in the swarm's wallet); the other 0.062 went back to the test wallet.
- Asks that arrive together queue in arrival order (2026-11-07). The queue behind `/pay` is `Paying.queued` (moved out of `Swarm.pay`, which keeps the secret check), so the node tests drive it: six asks of a lead and its explorers at once on one channel sign `signed + p1`, `+ p2`, … in arrival order; four asks that all carry the gateway's same snapshot sign increasing vouchers, each covering what was spent, the asks before it and its own price; and a hand owner's asks among the lead's are signed on the owner's channel without moving the pool channel's vouchers. Where the queue's time went, with the real queue and attempt code and stand-ins for what the object waits on (`/tmp/swarms-opt/queue-bench.mts`: a 70 ms row read, the production D1 reads measured from the Worker, a 2 ms signature, and the 226 ms an open's transaction build took beyond its balance read in the run against the real gateway, with the balance read itself real): six asks at once on an open channel finished after 89, 164, 239, 313, 389 and 464 ms (276 ms on average, each waiting behind the row reads ahead of it) with a row read per ask, and after 3, 6, 9, 11, 14 and 17 ms with the kept row. Asks that arrive while an ask opens the channel wait until the open shows on chain (`Paying.confirmed`, polling every 750 ms for up to 8 s), which the row change does not shorten; it happens once per channel, and in the run against the real gateway the lead's first call opened it before any other ask came. The queue stays one per swarm: a per-channel queue would let a hand owner's ask pass the lead's, but nearly every ask is a model call on the pool channel, whose vouchers must be signed one after another, and the asks on different channels still share the snapshot bookkeeping (a gateway snapshot can drop or confirm any of the swarm's channels) and the swarm wallet that opens and tops them up, which the voucher tests do not cover; after the row change an ask waits about 3 ms for each ask ahead of it. (Superseded on 2026-11-08 by lanes; see the next bullet.)
- The swarm's wallet pays on up to eight channels at once, its lanes (2026-11-08, Georgios: "for payments … parallelize tons and tons of shit given we have expiring nonces … this will stress test your design"). Lane 0 is the old `pool` source and the others are `pool.1` to `pool.7` (`Paying.lanes`, `Paying.most`), each its own mppx client and channel from the swarm's wallet to the gateway, so their opens and top-ups are separate expiring-nonce transactions (mppx's `ChannelOps` always uses TIP-1009 expiring nonces for session management) that land in parallel. The gateway's ask names its `subagent` and `tree` (now passed through `Operations.pay`), and `Paying.route` keeps each caller (`Paying.who`: the tree, else the subagent, else the lead) on a lane of its own while one is idle, opens a new lane when none is and the wallet can fund one beyond the lanes still opening (twice the larger of 0.1 and the setup's reserve, from the balance `Money.refresh` or the last read left in `self.held`), and otherwise shares the least busy lane; a burst of one caller's asks spills onto an idle opened lane, a retried ask goes back to the lane of the refused channel, and when the wallet cannot cover the price it prefers a lane whose deposit can. Each lane has its own queue (`inLane`), so vouchers on one channel stay in order while the lanes sign at once, and a lane that waits for its open to confirm or for a top-up's transaction blocks only itself; the global `self.paying` queue is gone. The wallet's balance is shared, so every open and top-up claims its amount (`claim`) in the same synchronous step that reads what is free, and the claim stays counted against the balance (`reserved`) until a balance read that started after the lane's deposit was seen to grow (`landed`, from the gateway's snapshot, `confirmed` or `Money.refresh`), or 30 s pass without it; concurrent balance reads share one request and an older read never replaces a newer one. A top-up adds the lane's last minute of spending when that is more than 0.1 (`flowed`). A refusal for voucher order ("Delta too small" and its kin, `Paying.ordering`) no longer parks a lane for ten minutes: before, any two such refusals in a row parked the swarm's only channel, so parallel subagents stopped every payment until it came back. Turns probe and settle their lanes in parallel, and `Money.refill` swaps pathUSD and USDC.e to OUSD at once. Checked on 2026-11-08 with `/tmp/frontier/paybench.mjs` (outside the repository): a simulated gateway (vouchers accepted per channel in arrival order after 20 to 90 ms of network each way, refused when not above the last or above the deposit, opens and top-ups landing 600 ms after they reach it, a JSON-RPC stub for the balance and channel reads at 60 ms) driving `Paying.queued` from N callers that each make model calls of 300 to 1,500 ms with holds of $0.0008 to $0.003, for 30 s from a $5 wallet. Before (fb8e0ac): 4 callers 3.8 calls/s (p95 1.2 s); 16 callers 0.4 calls/s, 4,045 of 4,073 asks refused because the only channel was parked; 64 callers 3 calls/s, p50 9.3 s, and two top-ups that overdrew the wallet. After: 4 callers 3.9 calls/s (p95 161 ms), 16 callers 15.7 calls/s (p50 124 ms, p95 181 ms, 2 ordering refusals), 64 callers 59 calls/s (p50 127 ms, p95 1.0 s, 270 ordering refusals each paid on its retry, 2 failed), and no overdraw in any run; with a $0.40 wallet, 16 callers ran on 2 lanes until the money ran out. Not run: real channels on Tempo, and the gateway's sponsorship quota (50 sponsored opens and 50 top-ups a day per payer), which lanes spend faster. Not run on production either: lanes pay only from the swarm's own wallet, which only stablecoin contributions fill, and the test wallet held 3.5 MACH and under 0.01 of any stablecoin that day (MACH contributions are channels the gateway pays from directly, which `Paying` never touches); after the deploy (version 7eaf1fe2) the one running swarm (f3a42cf87339) asked the payer only for `budget` in two minutes of `wrangler tail`, with no exception, and the pages answered 200.
- New swarms run on the cheapest models (2026-11-08, Georgios: "u can use the cheapest models we have"): the `cheap` template, now `Setup.fallback` and the API's default, leads with Codex on GPT-6 Luna and offers subagents GPT-6 Luna, Claude Haiku 5.5, DeepSeek V4.1 Flash and GLM-5.3 Flash on Codex or Claude Code, the four cheapest tool-calling models in `/v1/config` that day (about $0.0012, $0.0012, $0.0017 and $0.0018 a 50,000-token mostly cached call, against $0.038 for Opus 5.5). Existing swarms keep the setup they were created with. Checked on production on 2026-11-08 after deploying 7f2e944 (version 7eaf1fe2): `/openapi.json` names `cheap` as the default, the four models are in the gateway's `agents.models`, and a gateway agent of the maintainer's Codex key in app `cheap-check` with this setup (Codex on GPT-6 Luna, the four subagent models) ran one cell that spawned a Claude Code subagent on Haiku 5.5 and a Codex one on GLM-5.3 Flash, both of which answered, in a 27 s turn ($0.0018 and $0.0029 for the two subagents); the agent was deleted after.
- Checked on production on 2026-11-07 after deploying f6b13bc (version f564ee1d): `/pay` answered a wrong secret with 401 (the Worker's `do.pay` span 22 ms at the median over 20 asks, against 20 ms before), `/live`, the room socket and the lobby answered, and `wrangler tail` saw 38 events on the new version with no exception. No swarm was running (all 36 paused), so the per-ask log line has not yet been seen on production; `wrangler tail` on `/pay` while a swarm runs shows it.
- Checked on production on 2026-11-07 after deploying the split (b8a2f36, version a81268b6), with `wrangler tail` running: `/pay` on f7df8db3fe54 answered a wrong secret with 401 `Unknown payer secret.` for a model call and for `budget`, and a call without a challenge with its 401; `/live` replayed the feed, the room socket and the lobby answered `welcome`, the detail, feed, room, branches, hands, backers, machines, `/wait`, both searches, artifacts and the code page answered 200, and every page of the four newest swarms with their tabs, at 390 and 1,440 px, showed no console error, no `[object Object]`, `undefined` or `NaN` and no sideways overflow. The tail's 526 events in that window, all on the new version, held no exception and no error log (the 99 `canceled` were sockets and streams the test closed). No swarm was running (all 36 were paused), so the gateway called no payer of a running swarm in that window; the payer's paths were checked by the harness and the tests above instead.
- A model call the gateway cannot fit on any contribution channel no longer stops the swarm within a minute (2026-11-07, after swarm f7df8db3fe54 paused with money in its channels). That night 2 MACH were funded at 11:41:48 UTC; the agent created an H200 (its 300 s lease, $0.94, on the 20 MACH contribution's channel) and then renewed it twice within 32 s (11:46:27 and 11:46:59, $0.877786 each, both on the new 2 MACH channel), paying the machine up to 11:57:01, ten minutes ahead. The gateway prices a model call it pays from a contribution at the call's worst case (its ledger reserved $0.3775 to $0.3793 for each call of a 690,000-token DeepSeek V4.1 Flash conversation that cost $0.005 to $0.0065 cached) and needs that much free on one channel, so once the second renewal left $0.24 on the new channel and $0.34 across the older ones, no channel fitted and the gateway asked the payer at the call's small hold ($0.028451), which the swarm's empty wallet could not pay. The payer answered "try again in a moment", each such refusal failed a turn, and three turns in 64 s stopped the swarm (`rest`), whose machine sweep then terminated the H200 and the gateway credited the two renewals' $1.685 back (the `leases` rows of sb-qilTEQ3YAtKaoHl7ld0Nme, 11:48:04), leaving about $2.27 in channels and the swarm stopped. Now the payer records that refusal (`squeezed`, when the channels hold at least `perCall` but the gateway still asked) and names the largest channel; a turn that failed on it does not count toward the three errors (`squeezedTurns`, skipped by `failing`), the next turn waits one and then two minutes, the third such turn in a row shuts the machines down to return their prepaid time and goes on if the money that comes back is enough (or pauses for money), and the sixth stops the swarm as before (`Turns.squeezing`). A completed turn clears the count. `test/turns.test.ts` and a payer test cover it; both fail on the code before. Not changed here, and the gateway's to decide: that a contribution must hold a call's worst case, and that `Sponsors.sign` reads channel states from the isolate's cache on the first try and falls back to the payer without a fresh read when none fits, so a lease refund credited by another isolate (a terminate) can go unseen.
- Stopping the double reads on a swarm page's mount was considered and not done (2026-11-07): the first `welcome` refetches the team, branches and artifacts the page read at mount (about 0.4 s earlier, three small requests). The welcome's version is the swarm object's stamp, which moves with the object's storage, but those three come from D1 and the gateway, so a version match would not show that they are unchanged, and skipping their refetch could miss a change made between the mount read and the socket's acceptance. Holding those reads until the welcome would remove them, but the three parts load through three different mechanisms (`usePoll`, the roster loop and the branches store), each of which would need its own path for a socket that never opens.
- A Swarms deploy is a non-event for a running swarm (2026-11-08, Georgios: "everything is durable across deploys, Swarms included"). Drilled on production with a throwaway Frontier Mix swarm (e1baad6ba27d: Claude Code on Opus 5.5 leading, Codex on GPT-6.1 Sol subagents, E2B sandboxes, artifacts and a Wishlist), funded from the test wallet with 1.6 MACH in contribution channels and then, to put the swarm object's payer in the path (the gateway pays a MACH contribution's channel directly and asks `/pay` only for `budget` and when no channel fits, so with MACH alone the payer paid nothing: the tail held no `pay` RPC in the first 20 minutes), its MACH channels closed with the wallet's `requestClose` and 0.35 OUSD sent to the swarm's wallet. Unchanged `main` was deployed 12 times while the lead and its branches worked (`/tmp/night/sw62`, outside the repository: `watch.mjs` records `/live`, the room socket and every read, `tail.py` the Worker tail, `ledger.sh` the gateway's `charges` per update, `events.mjs` the agent's gateway events against the feed). What a deploy does to a swarm object: nothing at once. The running instance keeps the old code while it works; Cloudflare replaces it 1.5 to 5 minutes later, idle or mid-turn (resets at 05:05:45, 05:13:16 and 05:58:12 UTC mid-turn, 05:19:15, 05:27:18, 05:35, 05:52:03 and 06:03:31), which closes the room sockets (1006, the page reconnects and the welcome's version names the new boot) and makes every storage call of the old instance throw `Durable Object reset because its code was updated.`: the alarm that was following the turn threw it, and so did its own catch's `log`, so no spurious "hit an error" line was written, the runtime ran the alarm again on the new instance, and it went on following from the agent's saved cursor (logs and the cursor are written in one synchronous block, so they commit together).
  - Before (b7756f8, 05:00 to 05:47 UTC): the feed (249 entries) had no gap and no repeated row, every gateway `tool.call` had its row but the subagents' `wait` polls the narrator skips by design (and one subagent `submit_result` sent after the turn had ended, read with the next turn); the room's 22 messages reached every connected socket exactly once; each update's `spent` equalled the gateway ledger's net charges for its turn to the micro-dollar (0.262765, 0.027286, 0.259102, 0.184660, and 0.165156 after a failed turn whose 0.147936 the feed reported, also exact), across the mid-turn resets; idle pauses woke on new money after deploys (paused instance on old code at 05:08:39, "Back at work" 2 s later); the payer answered on the new instance right after a reset (05:52, its vouchers and then its refusals). Two things broke. `/live` streams outlived the instance that served them: the old instance stays up as long as a stream is open, its 25 s keepalives kept coming, but every entry is logged by the new instance, so a watcher (the swarm page while it runs, the X card, `/tg`) froze silently, missing seq 61 to 97 for 13 minutes and 129 to 165 later, until its 15-minute follow window ran out; the page's `EventSource` only reconnects on an error, and keepalives never stopped, so no watchdog could notice. And the Machines tab listed none of the swarm's machines: every E2B sandbox id (`iajm8koswc342dpzhhv41`, no `sb-` prefix; `auto` picks E2B) failed `Fleet.sandbox`'s `sb-` pattern, in the fold, the command tracker and a spawned job's log (not a deploy fault, found by the drill).
  - Fixed: each instance writes its boot id to kv `instance` when it starts, and `Relay.beat` closes every watcher of an instance whose `instance` is no longer its own or whose storage throws (`Relay.current`, logged as `{superseded, watchers}`), so watchers reconnect with `after` to the new instance within a beat and get the backlog (`test/relay.test.ts`, failing on the code before); `Fleet.sandbox` takes any sandbox id and a compute call's machine is a sandbox whatever its prefix (`test/fleet.test.ts`); and `Runs` keeps a tracker version (kv `runs:v`, written with the cursor) and tracks a feed again from the start once when it changes, so machines the old tracker skipped get their commands (`test/runs.test.ts`, failing before).
  - Thoughts and live offsets (found from the drill and the root's note on nanocode-ts build-5a43d4b): since that build the gateway's sessions send reasoning and tool-input deltas only as live frames (`live_deltas`), each with its `offset` in its item, and a stream resent after a reconnect starts again from an offset. Swarms read those deltas only from durable events, and asked for live frames only while a page watched, so it logged no thought at all: f3a42cf87339's feed held 52 and 38 thinking rows in the hours from 00:00 and 01:00 UTC on 2026-11-08 and none in the 2,240 entries after 02:00, and the drill swarm none in 249. And it relayed subagent thinking and tool input with `offset: null`, so a page appended a resent stream to what it showed. Now the object always follows the agent with `live=1` (`Gateway.stream`; `Relay.poke`, which reopened the stream when a watcher joined, is gone), every live delta carries its `offset` (thinking keyed by the reasoning item, `payload.item_id`, else the model call), and live thinking goes into the thought buffer at its offset (`Follow.pondered`, `Trail.placed`, kv `thinking:<who>` holding `{ done, item, text }`, still frozen once it passes 1,500 characters), which the model call's durable `assistant.message` or `model.call.completed` turns into the thinking row as before (`test/follow.test.ts`, both tests failing on the code before). The cost is that a running swarm now receives every delta whether or not anyone watches. Checked on production after the deploy of e702fb6 (version f47ce57e) with a throwaway free-template swarm (45b7202735c7, 0.1 MACH, $0.0078 spent): its feed logged 11 thinking rows, the lead's and its subagents', then the swarm was deleted (D1 rows and agent) and its channel closed.
  - After (4dd77e3 and 5037b44, 05:35 to 06:04 UTC): at the resets of 05:52:03 and 05:58:12 (the second mid-turn) the old instance logged `{"superseded":"a642237d","watchers":2}` and every `/live` watcher closed 18 to 25 s after the reset and reconnected, missing no entry (feed 249, live 246, the three before the first watcher connected); the four E2B sandboxes were listed with their 1, 8, 6 and 2 commands; feed, room and per-update spend checked as before. Not fixed here: a subagent whose model call the gateway answers 429 (the payer busy while the lead's uncached Opus call held most of a small channel) failed for good after three tries in 7 s ("durable operation recovery execution policy failed"), which an unreachable payer, which the gateway now answers as 429, would do too (reported to the nanocode SDK's owner); and the swarm's running `spent` (1.067745) read 0.0208 above the ledger's net (1.046905) because the wallet channel's figure is the gateway's last snapshot, taken with calls in flight, which a paused swarm does not read again. The drill cost the test wallet about $1.06 of model, machine and storage calls; its three MACH channels were closed with `requestClose`, about $0.13 of OUSD stayed in the channel the swarm's wallet opened to the gateway (only that wallet can close it), and the swarm was deleted afterwards (its D1 rows and its agent).
  - A paused swarm settles its spend (2026-11-08, after the drill above). Its running `spent` counts the wallet channel at `spent:pool`, the gateway's figure from the last payer ask's channel snapshot or the last `kv/get` probe's receipt, both of which count the holds of calls still in flight, and a paused swarm asked nothing and probed nothing again, so it kept that figure: the drill swarm read 0.0208 above the ledger, and on 2026-11-08 three paused swarms' `spent:pool` (the deposit less the `pool` they report, less their wallet's balance) stood above what the gateway had settled on chain for their wallet channel (`getChannelState`, read from the chain): c40012cbf6af by 0.008944 (0.597257 against 0.588313), 71ae8f963b9b by 0.016059 and 3a05762b5254 by 0.000351. Now `pause` sets an alarm 15 s ahead (`settleMs`), and every step of a paused swarm runs `Turns.settle`: once 15 s passed since the pause and the wallet channel's last payment, and the gateway lists none of the agent's subagents as pending, queued or running (`quiet`, else a minute later), it probes the wallet channel again even when no voucher was signed since the last probe (`Paying.probe` with `again`), so `spent:pool` becomes the receipt's `spent` with every call settled (plus the probe's own 0.0001 `kv.get`, which the ledger counts too), refreshes the row, and records the voucher it settled at (`settled:pool`), so a later pause probes again only if the channel signed since. A swarm paid only from contribution channels probes nothing: their spend comes from the gateway's sponsored-channel listing, which every refresh reads afresh. `test/turns.test.ts` checks the waiting and the once-per-voucher rule. On production after the deploy of 79b8efe (version 2f9bce36), a throwaway free-template swarm (aa2180751952, 0.065 OUSD from the test wallet) answered one sentence and paused idle at 06:50:06 UTC with `spent` 0.002488 (its model call and the turn-end probe); its settle probe followed at 06:50:20, 16 s after the turn-end probe, and `spent` read 0.002588, the ledger's net of every charge the swarm paid (0.002388 of model call and two 0.0001 probes; the 0.0015 of listings the operator key paid are not the swarm's). After a 0.05 MACH contribution woke it and it paused again, it probed nothing (the wallet channel had signed nothing since), and `spent` 0.005850 equalled the ledger's 0.00585; while that turn ran, `spent` read 0.028984, the contribution channel's listing counting a call's hold, and settled at the pause. The drift case itself (a call in flight at the pause) was not staged on production; the three paused swarms above still carry theirs until they pause again. The MACH channel was closed with `requestClose`; the swarm stays paused with its OUSD, which only its own wallet can return.

## Campaign experience (2026-11-08)

The user asked for Kickstarter-like backing, social participation, and visible progress toward breakthroughs, and authorized a direct default-branch push and deployment. The default swarm view is now Overview: mission, backing, latest finding, outputs, next-step context, research paths with existing funding controls, and community progress. Technical tabs are under Behind the scenes; old hash links still work. Following is explicitly device-local and appears under Following in discovery; it does not promise notifications. Discovery excludes clearly named operator test campaigns but direct URLs remain available. Payment approval and token information remain in checkout. Ordinary room chat no longer wakes an idle paid agent; explicit funded steering still does. Research completion is never presented as independent verification or a percentage toward a breakthrough.

- The site is built around backing an idea (2026-11-08, Georgios: "by default you just back an idea", "less text", "very normie friendly", then "the routes can be improved so that going back and forth always makes sense", "transcript, results, backers, code, machines, details are programmer stuff; normies want to chat with others, steer the research and read results", "no extra text", and no MACH or token details shown while every stablecoin, MACH included, still works). Routes: `/` home, `/ideas` all ideas (`?filter=running|money|waiting`), `/new` start one (`?q=<bank id>` for an idea from the bank), `/s/:id` an idea's results, `/s/:id/chat` its chat, `/s/:id?r=<slug>` a result opened full screen over the results, `/s/:id/code` its code (since the bullet on the Code tab below; until then it answered 301 to `/s/:id`), `/about` how it works, `/docs` for developers. Old links redirect with a 301 from the Worker (`Page.moved`: `/join` to `/ideas`) and in the app (`#room` to `/chat`, other hashes dropped). Every push records its depth in `history.state`, and every in-app back (`Back`, `back` in `router.tsx`: ← in the chat, the result viewer's ←, the idea page's ←) goes back in history when there is somewhere to go back to and otherwise replaces the page with its parent, so back never leaves the site and never loops; a link opened from a deep link reads ← All ideas, otherwise ← Back. An idea page (`Swarm.tsx`) is the title, one sentence of pitch, a backing box (`Box.tsx`: raised, backers' faces, money left, one line of state, $2/$5/$10/$25/Other, "What should it try next? (optional)", Back it · $5, Share) and two tabs, Results and Chat. Results (`Results.tsx`) is the latest finding, what it made (each opens full screen, `Viewer` in `Artifacts.tsx`), what backers want (their notes by money, each with + Back this), updates (backings and the agent's updates from `/v1/swarms/:id/updates`) and what it needs (its Wishlist's open items, pointing to the chat). Chat is the room without the team steering panel; @swarm still steers by opening the backing box with the text as its direction. On a phone the box folds to one Back it button until tapped, a Back it bar sticks to the bottom once it scrolls away, and the chat is full screen with ←, the title and Back it, which opens the box as a bottom sheet. The transcript, code browser, machines, backers list, details, side pane, branches and model picker are gone from the site (`Transcript`, `Side`, `Code`, `Repo`, `Machines`, `Connect`, `Contributors`, `Branches`, `Visual`, `Models`, `Campaign` and `Ideas` deleted); the API and MCP still serve all of it. New swarms use the default frontier template. Payment errors and room lines speak dollars: `Fuel` never names a token, and the room shows "@ada backed it with $5" (`Swarm.contributed`) and rewrites older lines that named a token. An idea page's server render seeds its updates and results (`updates:<id>`, `artifacts:<id>`), so the page does not shift when they load, and a page chunk that fails to load (an old page open across a deploy) reloads the page once (`page` in `pages.tsx`). Checked on 2026-11-08 under `vite` with the fixture world: the storyboard's 42 home, ideas, start, idea, chat, back-it and page states on 390×844 and 360×700 phones have no sideways overflow, no input under 16 px and no tap target with less than 24 px of reach but the room's names at the clipped top of its list; a script walked home → card → Chat → browser back → forward → Results → open a result → browser back → open and ← → ← Ideas, a deep link to a chat and its ←, and the old `/join`, `#room` and `/code` links, on a desktop and a phone, and every step landed where expected. `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and the node tests (114) pass. Deployed on 2026-11-08 (6ea3f53, then 657e4e1, e368dfc and the reload fix; versions a853a9ca to bea75d6a and after): on production `/join` answered 301 to `/ideas`; the home, All ideas, an idea, its chat and Start loaded at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone with no sideways overflow and no console errors, with layout shift 0 but 0.04 once on an idea page in a headless phone whose user agent the server took for a desktop (so it rendered the box open); and the swarm list read 4 running and 37 paused for money, none paused for anything else. A version of this commit wrote AGENTS.md back truncated to its first 140 lines (the tool reading it cut its output); the next commit restored it.
- Only money stops a swarm (2026-11-08, Georgios: "just only stop when we're out of money but otherwise we always keep going"). `Turns.finish` no longer pauses for `idle` ("Waiting for backers.") or `rest` (three failed turns): a failed turn waits 15 s and, from the third in a row, backs off from one minute doubling to 30 minutes (`Turns.backoff`), and the next turn starts. Six squeezed turns in a row count as out of money (`squeezing` answers `broke`), and a step that needs more than any source holds is out of money too (`needs` carries it), so `costly` is no longer written. The persona and every "keep going" tell the agent never to stop to wait for backers. Swarms paused as `idle`, `rest` or `costly` before this are woken the next time anyone lists swarms or opens one (`Operations.resume`, `Swarm.revive`, in `waitUntil`); a pause reads "Out of money. Back it to keep it going." or "Nobody has backed it yet." (`Phase.reason`), and `Status.standing` has only Running, Starting, Needs money and Not started. Branch settlement no longer announces unspent branch budgets in the room. "$9.69 left" on an idea is money the swarm still holds and spends: the swarm keeps working until it is gone.
- The chat and the Results tab were redesigned (2026-11-08, Georgios: "redesign the chat", "make it so shit doesn't move around like when i click results / chat", and "as a normie I expect to see the decision/subagent/swarm tree of all the things and the directions that have been tried, in an elegant way, instead of 'I merged the dense3 branch': Results at the top linking to the artifacts it produced, and right below the evolution of the ideas explored"). Results (`Results.tsx`) opens with what the swarm made, each a card with a live preview (a page artifact in a scaled sandboxed frame, an image as itself, other kinds as a glyph) that opens full screen; then How it got here (`Explored.tsx`), the swarm's tree of merged work, from `GET /v1/swarms/:id/progress` (`Operations.progress`, MCP `swarm_progress`, seeded by the server render as `progress:<id>`), which reads the swarm's own autoresearch artifact: `data.json` (metric, better, goal, branches with what each explored, its state and what it found) and `main.tsv` (every change to main, merges described as "merge <branch>: …", with the metric after each), parsed by `shared/Progress.ts`. The view opens with main's current value (1.40×), how many branches were merged and how many tried and dropped, a bar toward the goal and a step chart of main over time, then the trunk newest first: each merge is a node on the trunk with the branch's name, what it added and the value after it with its change (+1.3%), main's own changes sit on the trunk as On main, main's discarded tweaks fold into one line, and every branch main never merged forks off the trunk where it was tried (after the last merge before it in `data.json`'s order) as Tried, didn't beat main, with what it found; branches still running fork off the top. Until 2026-11-08 the tab read only the gateway's subagent list, which is flat and in which every branch reports the number it reached on the main it forked from, so the page said "16 directions tried, best so far 1.39× from verify attention softmax efficiency" when 1.39× was main after twelve merges (Georgios: "we want them to be like a tree of merged things", "this also seems like a bug in the whole way the thing works"). The persona now makes `main.tsv` part of the autoresearch convention, starts every branch from main's newest kept commit and lists main in `data.json`; a swarm without that record still shows its branches from the subagent list, newest first, without a best-so-far claim. Swarm f3a42cf87339 already kept both files, so its tree reads: start 1.00×, merges of moe, dense-fusion, attention, moe2, dense2 and moe4, a rerun, merges of moe5, attention3, attention4, dense3 and attention6, and main's stage tuning at 1.40×, with attention2, moe3, overlap and attention5 forking off as tried; then Backers want, Updates (folded, five shown) and It needs. The chat (`Room.tsx`) is a messenger: bubbles grouped by sender within five minutes, your own on the right in green, day dividers, the AI's updates and new results as cards from an AI avatar, backings as centred lines with + Back this, who is here at the top, a typing line, and one composer that grows with the text. Telling the AI is a mode, not syntax: the ✦ button (or picking AI from the @ list) turns the composer green and its send button into Back it, which opens the backing box with the text as the direction; @swarm still works and shows as @AI. Signed out the composer is one Sign in to chat button, a non-backer gets Back it to chat. Chat lines that named a token are rewritten to dollars and to "backed it with". Results and Chat are both kept mounted on wide screens and switched in place (`replace`, no scroll reset), the tabs stick under the header, and when the page is scrolled past them a switch scrolls back only to the tabs, so the title, box and tabs never move: measured on the fixture world at 1280×800 and 850×1000, the title, box and tabs kept the same position across Results → Chat → Results at the top, and when scrolled the tabs stayed at the same height after each switch. On a phone Chat is its own full-screen page (`/s/:id/chat`), so nothing under it moves. Checked under `vite` with the fixture world: the idea, chat and back-it states of the storyboard on 390×844 and 360×700 phones have no sideways overflow, no input under 16 px and no tap target with less than 24 px of reach. The room keeps its messages per idea in memory (`kept`), so switching back to the chat shows them at once.
- Each swarm keeps its work in one git repository, and its page has a Code tab (2026-11-09, Georgios: "each swarm gets a git repo", "each branch is a branch on that repo which then gets merged to main", then "Results Chat Code also - so we can see the code tree, and in the code tab it should also have git clone, and also show the README, like it usually shows in github. so maybe we can have the fund component as part of the results tab - the whole thing is full width, and there's a code tab there"). This supersedes the `main.tsv` convention of the bullet above and the 2026-11-03 rule that nothing in a swarm's messages asks for a repository.
  - The persona (`Persona.ts`, frozen per swarm when its agent is created, so only new swarms get it) has a `# Git` section in place of the `main.tsv` paragraph: one repository holds the swarm's code, data and write-ups, main is the best version so far, every branch of work is a git branch cut from main's newest commit (a subagent clones with `git_remote`'s URL and runs `git checkout -b <branch>`, or writes with `git_commit` without a machine), a branch that beats main is merged with `git merge --no-ff`, the merge's first line is "merge <branch>: what it adds" and a line "<metric>: <value>" gives main's number after it, and no branch is deleted or force-pushed, since unmerged branches are the directions tried. The autoresearch Branches paragraph names the branch as a git branch of the repository.
  - Every turn's message names the repository once it exists, and while it does not and the swarm holds at least $1.10, the first message and every later one ask the agent to create it with `git_create({ name, public: true })` before anything else (`Turns.repoed`). The agent creates it, not this app, so the swarm's own money pays Code Storage's $1 through the payer: the operator key's wallet held about 3.5 MACH and almost no stablecoins on 2026-11-09, so it cannot pay for every swarm's repository. Code Storage's commit route makes only single-parent commits, so merges need git on a machine.
  - The tree (How it got here) comes from git when the swarm has a repository: the swarm's Durable Object keeps a graph in its storage (`Repo.graph`, kv `graph`: the branches, main's first 100 commits from its head and each other branch's commits back to where it meets main, at most 40 branches and 50 commits each, messages cut to 2,000 and 600 characters and redacted), refreshed at most once a minute when `watch` runs or a read finds it stale (`Swarm.tree`), and reading only the branches whose head moved. `Operations.progress` hands it to `Progress.fromGit`, which walks main's first parents: a commit with two parents is a merge, named by its message ("merge x:", "Merge branch 'x'", "origin/x", "x - …") or by the branch that holds its second parent, with the number from its message (`measure`: "<metric>: <value>", any "name: number" line, or "1.31x" in the first line); every branch not merged forks off where it meets main's trunk, with its newest commit's first line as what it found, and a branch whose name a running subagent's role contains is still exploring. The autoresearch `data.json`, when there is one, gives the title, metric, goal and what each branch explores (`Progress.hint`). Without a repository, or when git yields no merges and no branches, the tree falls back to `data.json` and `main.tsv` (`Progress.parse`), then to the subagent list.
  - The page (`Swarm.tsx`) has three tabs under the title, Results, Chat and Code, across the full width; the backing box (`Box.tsx`) sits in the Results tab beside the results (`.idea-results`), and on Chat and Code the tab bar carries the amount raised and Back it, which opens the box as a sheet (on a phone the bottom bar does). Code (`Code.tsx`, kept mounted once opened, as the room is) is a GitHub-style repository page: the name, commits and files, `git clone <url> <name>` with Copy, the newest commit on main, the folder listing with breadcrumbs (folders first; a file opens in @pierre/diffs' `File`), and the folder's README rendered with `Markdown` below it. It starts from the snapshot the swarm keeps (`Swarm.code`, `/api/swarms/:id/code`), which `/s/:id/code`'s server render ships as `code:<id>`, and otherwise reads `log`, `files` and the README through `/api/swarms/:id/git/:read`; a swarm without a repository says the code shows up once the AI saves its first version, and a read that fails says so without the gateway's detail. The old `Code.tsx` and `Repo.tsx` (file tree, per-update diffs, full browser) described in the Code bullets above stay deleted. Checked on 2026-11-09 under `vite` with the fixture world, whose bench-coders swarm now has a repository and a git graph (`fixtures.git`, `fixtures.progress` through `fromGit`): the tree drew its merges from git at 1280×800 and 390×844, `/s/bench-coders/code` showed the clone command, the listing, a folder, `harness/run.py` highlighted and the README with its table, Chat and Code kept the raised amount and Back it in the tab bar, and no view overflowed sideways; `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and the node tests (132) pass. Deployed on 2026-11-09 (bd52119, version a478e0a8): `/s/06cf5d7c8f92/code` served in its server render and showed solve-p-np's clone command, its four folders and README.md, and its README rendered, at 1280×800 and as a 390×844 phone with the bottom Back it bar; f3a42cf87339's Results kept the box beside its tree and its Code tab said the code shows up once the AI saves its first version (it has no repository); no view overflowed sideways, layout shift was 0 (0.0011 on switching to Code) and no console errors. 06cf5d7c8f92's progress answered null: its graph needs a token renewal the treasury cannot pay (below), and it keeps no `data.json`.
  - Public reads of a swarm's repository need a fresh Code Storage token ($0.01 an hour), which the gateway buys only from the wallet that paid for the repository (its fee payer, a live key of the wallet that owns it, or the agent's payer). On 2026-11-09 every swarm repository with an expired token answered `/p` reads with 402 or 503: the gateway's fee payer held 14.53 pathUSD, 0.0007 USDC.e and no OUSD, and the DEX had no liquidity from pathUSD or OUSD into USDC.e (pathUSD into OUSD quoted at par), so it could not buy the USDC.e Code Storage charges, and the swarm wallets that paid for 48ba542731e3's and 236cb54576c8's repositories were empty ("The swarm's wallet cannot cover this service"). Two of those failures were the gateway's, fixed the same night in tempoxyz/gateway#585 (version 65ed2b5d): Code Storage offers OUSD, USDC.e and pathUSD, and the gateway picked USDC.e it could not buy and swapped before letting a MACH key use the swapper; after it, solve-p-np (owned by the operator key's wallet, renewed from its MACH) and wc (owned by the gateway's fee payer) answered 200. A repository a swarm's wallet paid for (48ba542731e3's, 236cb54576c8's) can be renewed only by that wallet, so it stays unreadable while the swarm is out of money, and its page shows the snapshot and graph the swarm last read. A new swarm's repository is paid for with the swarm's own money: the agent's `git_create` is charged $1 through its payer and the gateway's fee payer buys it from Code Storage, so the fee payer owns it and renews its token for public reads ($0.01 an hour while it is read).
- Backing in MACH is an approval, not a transfer (2026-11-10, Georgios, choosing among three designs: "it seems better to indeed do (3) so that the gateway does the channel opens etc obviously w/o us having to enumerate them all ahead of time", then "Yea make it automatic for backers. SGTM otherwise let's go", and, of a swarm he started to reproduce a 1,000-agent proof run on Boundless's Claude Fable 5.2: "a good example of the kind of thing that needs to be doable trivially, and ppl should be able to just come along and chip in"). A backer's wallet approves a spending key the Tempo gateway holds, limited to the amount (tempoxyz/gateway's Backings: `POST /api/keys/request` with `backs: { owner, app, tag }`), and the swarm's agent is paid from that wallet as it works: model calls from the wallet to Boundless over the key's own channel there, everything else over the key's channel with the gateway. Nothing moves at approval, what the swarm never spends never leaves the wallet, and revoking the key in Tempo Wallet ends it. This replaces the credit transfer for MACH of 1 or more; stablecoins, and MACH under 1 (the gateway's smallest key limit), are still sent as before and become the swarm's credit.
  - Flow: `POST /v1/swarms/:id/backings { amount, note? }` (`Operations.back`) asks the gateway for a MACH key that backs the operator wallet's app `swarm-<id>`, with the tag `Credit.tag(swarm, note)`, a deposit and top-up of a tenth of the amount (0.25 to 2) and 30 days, and answers `id` and `authorizeAccessKey`. The page has Tempo Wallet approve it (`Wallet.authorize`: `wallet_connect` with `capabilities.authorizeAccessKey`) and posts the `keyAuthorization` to `POST /v1/swarms/:id/backings/:request` (`Operations.approved`, which also takes `transaction`, an admin key's install), which completes the key at the gateway and counts a contribution of the approved limit: id `backing:<access key>`, the key in `contributions.channel`, no hash. A note counts only when it is the note the backing was prepared with (the tag). The button, amounts and words on the page are unchanged; the thank-you adds "It stays in your wallet until the AI spends it." When the approval cannot be asked for (the gateway refuses, or the wallet fails for any reason but the backer declining), the page sends the money instead, as before.
  - Money: `Money.refresh` reads `GET /api/keys/backings` beside the credit. `sponsor_left` and `sponsor_spent` (the columns MACH channels used) are what the backers' keys can still pay and what they paid, `pool_left` and `pool_spent` stay the credit, and a swarm's money left is their sum. `settle` counts a backing the gateway lists that D1 does not have (so an approval the gateway took but Swarms failed to record is counted within a refresh) and lowers an ended backing's contribution to what it paid, logging "…'s backing ended after paying $x; the rest never left their wallet". `raised` is therefore approved limits plus transfers, and falls when a backing ends unspent.
  - Agents are created with, and existing ones patched to, `payer: "backers"` (`Turns.credited`), which the gateway pays from the backers' keys and, when none can pay, from the app's credit, so swarms funded before this keep spending their credit. `Turns.broke` also takes the gateway's "None of this app's backers can pay".
  - Models: since tempoxyz/gateway d3b58e1 a model id's prefix is its provider, so the templates name Boundless's ids (`boundless/deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017`, `boundless/z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`, `boundless/z-ai/glm-5.3`, `boundless/moonshotai/kimi-k3.1-pro`, `boundless/anthropic/claude-opus-5.5`), which are also cheaper than the same models elsewhere (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash $0.24 and $0.96 a million against $0.30 and $1.20), and a new template `fable` leads with Claude Code on `boundless/anthropic/claude-fable-5.2` and offers Fable 5.2, Opus 5.5, Kimi K3.1 Pro and GLM-5.3 to subagents. Frontier OpenAI stays on Vercel. `/new` has a small "Runs on" select (Cheapest, Frontier Mix, Fable 5.2) and takes `?template=`; the model picker the site dropped on 2026-11-08 is otherwise still gone. A goal may be 12,000 characters and the question box 10,000 (they were 2,000 and 1,000, which cut the proof run's prompt at 1,000). A `boundless/` model has no fallback: a swarm on one that only has credit is paid by the gateway's fee payer, whose stablecoins nothing refills (about $7.5 on 2026-11-10), so such swarms stop when that runs out, while a MACH backing pays Boundless itself.
  - Not decided with Georgios: that only MACH is approved while stablecoins are sent; the "Runs on" select; that the default template names Boundless ids.
  - Checked on production on 2026-11-10 (Swarms 7cf2cce, version 78fc5ca9; gateway d8e5a04 through dff1251) with the test wallet's admin key signing the install transaction, since an admin key's approval cannot stand in for the wallet's own: swarm 3811c1ffdbb0 (template cheap, a 1 MACH backing with the note "Make it rhyme") counted raised $1 with the note at $1, started, logged "approved up to 1 MACH for the swarm to spend, with a note", and its agent stored a rhyming haiku; the key opened a 0.5 MACH channel with Boundless (0x69655b04fe744ea6eb98d7cd75774af96a45bb921b2d4aae652c7b5b65ee0250) and a 0.25 one with the gateway (0x7f35a762396307317c75d34cb58a559195b8452e0b1f037d6a51f679a4968a31), its model calls were `vendor` rows of Boundless with payer `backer` and its `kv_set` and `bucket_put` rows on the gateway channel, and its first update read "after spending $0.009965". Swarm 23375a753ab7 (template fable, a 6 MACH backing) ran Claude Code on Fable 5.2, created its repository ($1), started a GLM-5.3 subagent with a $0.50 budget that wrote a haiku for about $0.01, stored both and reported, the update costing $1.276186, then about $0.078 a turn. Revoking each key from the wallet paused its swarm as out of money within a minute and lowered the contribution to what the key paid ($1.905321 for the second; the first read $0.311979 for about $0.012 of charges, the gateway bug dff1251 fixed). Before gateway 8e0d84d the second swarm showed $3.08 spent and $2.92 left, since the gateway counted what Boundless only held; after it, $1.905321 and $4.094679.
  - Not run: Tempo Wallet's own approval from the page (a passkey needs a person), so the page's path ends at the same two routes the check used but its wallet call is untested; a stablecoin backing; two wallets backing one swarm; a backing that runs out while a turn runs. Both test swarms are paused and titled "e2e test", which the lists hide.
- The Fermat swarm (2026-11-10, Georgios: "I want us to spawn a Fermat last theorem swarm with frontier models similar to how the anthropic guys said they made it work in their fable 5.2", after "Safely scaling multi-agent teams with Claude Fable 5.2", claude.dev/blog/safely-scaling-multi-agent-teams-with-fable-5-2: a lead and 999 helpers, 60 statements, a one-sentence sketch, the definitions and 47 proved lemmas, a sandbox each with Lean and Mathlib, a git branch each and a shared database, and no other guidance). It is swarm 6f4541d2f211, "Prove Fermat's Last Theorem in Lean with a team of AI agents", template `fable`. His first try (9ff38a435eca, the day before) ran on DeepSeek with its prompt cut at 1,000 characters and spent its $25 on a goal that named files it did not have.
  - The kit. The blog did not publish its 60 statements or 47 lemmas, so the kit approximates them from anthropics/fermats-last-theorem (Apache-2.0, 6e837e75; 29,511 theorems, each stated in `Theorems/Thm_X.lean` and proved in `P2M/Sol/S_X.lean`, whose `import Theorems.Thm_…` lines are what it cites, and 1,450 definition modules): the 60 statements are its 49 landmark theorems (`html/data/meta.js`), the other theorems `PROOF-PATH.md` names and the largest theorems directly beneath them, each with its proof replaced by `sorry` and its imports replaced by the definitions that stood beneath it; the 47 given lemmas are the most cited theorems proved from Mathlib alone; and 400 more theorems ship with their proofs because 35 definition modules import them. `tools/check <Lean name>` builds a statement and passes only if its text up to the proof is unchanged (a hash in `statements.json`), `Lemmas/` declares nothing global, and `#print axioms` lists only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`; `tools/status` and `tools/final` go with it. 16 of the larger statements named, in their `attribute [-instance]` and `[-simp]` lines, constants that exist only in the removed proofs (2 to 27 each); a Lean probe on the built kit listed them and the builder dropped them. The scripts are in the swarm's repository under `provenance/`. Ten statements have nothing beneath them, eleven have 2 to 6 theorems, and the largest 29,488.
  - The repository and the image were made before the swarm had money, in its own app `swarm-6f4541d2f211` with throwaway keys of the operator's wallet (which is also the test wallet, so the app and its snapshots are the swarm's): `git/create` and a push of the 39 MB kit, then a Modal sandbox that built Mathlib from source (8,712 jobs, 40 minutes on 32 CPUs and 128 GiB; no prebuilt Mathlib matches Lean 4.33.1), the definitions and their 400 theorems (another 70 minutes, most of it a few definition modules that elaborate for 15 minutes each, one after another), the given lemmas and the 60 statements, and a snapshot, `im-94ZSXhaci35r7NpPmuU5tT` (23 GB of build products, good until 2026-12-10), which `IMAGE.md` on main names with the machine to start from it (4 CPUs, 16 GiB, `region: "any"`). On such a machine a small statement builds in 36 s and `tools/check` answers in 18 s. Making it cost $18.8 of MACH, more than half of it because the first sandbox was placed in `us-east` at 1.75 times the price: `compute_create`'s default region for a machine without a GPU is the one nearest the caller, so the persona now tells every agent to pass `region: "any"` (`# Tools and services`).
  - The goal (7,405 characters, which the 12,000-character limit of that day allows) gives the lead the blog's setup and nothing about the mathematics: the kit, the rules, helpers as `spawn_agent` subagents on Fable 5.2 with a budget each, one machine each from the snapshot, a branch each merged into main only after `tools/check` passes on the merged tree, coordination only through the kv store under `team/` and git, and a team sized by the budget tool (about $6 for the lead, about $8 a helper, more helpers whenever backers add money).
  - The run, checked: backed with 25 MACH from the test wallet (an admin key's install transaction, as in the check above) at 06:51 UTC, the lead read the kit, wrote its orders and assignments under `team/`, published a Surface, started two helpers 80 s later and waited on them inside its turn. `FreyPackage.of_counterexample` was on main 7 minutes after the launch, FLT for 5, 7, 11 and 13 wired to `flt_regular` and the three class-number statements after 13, and `ModularForm.S2_Gamma0_one_eq_zero` and `ModularForm.S2_Gamma0_2_eq_zero` (step 6 of the route) after 20. A fresh machine from the snapshot at main 6e95db8 answered `PROVED` for those three statements, each from the three standard axioms alone; four more are written and rest on statements not proved yet. It had spent $9.80 by then, about $27 an hour for a lead and two helpers on Fable 5.2 with a machine each. A $2 backing with a note ("Kit update on main…") reached the lead as a steer, which is how the image's second snapshot was announced.
  - Found on the way: a repository's publication belongs to the key that made it public, so revoking the throwaway key that created `flt` made it private and the swarm's clone URL answered 404 "No repository named flt" until the operator key made it public again (Swarms' own `expose` does that after a turn that used git); a gateway key whose whole limit sits in its gateway channel cannot pay a vendor's own charge (a `git_remote` token answered 402 "Code Storage was not paid"); and `GET /v1/swarms/:id/feed?limit=` without `before` answers the oldest entries.
  - Not done: the blog's safety layers (its sandboxes had no network and a classifier checked every tool call; these machines are online and nothing but the gateway's spending caps bounds them), so "do not open an existing formalization" is on the agents' honour; `tools/final` checks axioms and derives Mathlib's `FermatLastTheorem`, where the blog's grader ran leanprover/comparator (15 hours and 230 GiB on the original); and the statement lock is textual, so it trusts that `Lemmas/` declares nothing global, which `tools/check` only greps for.
- A swarm's page opens on Now: the lead's latest status, the swarm's own Surface and live traces (2026-11-10, Georgios, on a screenshot of the Fermat swarm's Surface: "this i do find kinda cool", and on Solve P = NP's grid of artifact cards: "this not as much, maybe this just needs a separate page, so main page is: 3 bullet point latest status update from the swarm leader, swarm, traces, and then results, chat, code you could keep"). The tabs are Now (`/s/:id`), Results (`/s/:id/results`), Chat and Code; the backing box sits beside Now, and the other tabs carry the raised amount and Back it in the tab bar.
  - Status (`shared/Brief.ts`, `Now.tsx`): at most three bullets under "Latest from the lead" with their age. The source is a `status` node on the Surface (new kind, aliases `now` and `latest`, three plain sentences; the persona's Surface section asks for it, so only swarms created after this have it, since instructions are frozen per swarm), else the newer of the latest update (its first three list items, or its first three sentences without headings and tables) and the Surface manifest's summary split at semicolons and sentences. A running swarm with no progress for 20 minutes adds "Stalled: its last progress was …".
  - Swarm: the Surface's nodes without the status node, or, for a swarm without a Surface, a list "Who is on what" built from its top-level branches (`Surface.crew`: working first, each with its state and the first line of its task or result). Tiles now keep 120 items (48 cut the Fermat swarm's 60 statements), `open` reads as quiet rather than a warning, and `proved`, `verified` and `in work` have tones.
  - Traces (`Traces.tsx`, `shared/Trace.ts`): the newest 60 feed entries in a box of at most 420 px (360 px on a phone) pinned to its bottom, one mono line each (time, agent, a mark for its kind, text), thoughts in italic serif, opened on a tap to the whole output (a compute call's stdout and stderr, else pretty JSON); Earlier loads 200 more and up to three pages load by themselves while fewer than 12 rows show. Chips filter to the lead or one top-level branch through the feed's `who`. While the swarm runs and the tab and the Now view are visible it follows `/live` from the newest seq it holds (never from 0, which would replay the feed) and shows what each agent is writing; otherwise a `changed` naming the feed reads what came after. A run of the same error or status line from one agent folds into one row with its count, and an error shows the gateway's reason from its detail: the Fermat swarm's feed held about 4,000 alternating "A model call failed." and "The run failed." entries.
  - Results lists page and image artifacts as preview cards and everything else (write-ups, tables, JSON) as flush rows with title, summary and age, then How it got here, Backers want, Updates and It needs; the Surface is no longer there. The server render seeds the Surface's JSON (`surface:<id>`, read beside the other page data within 1.5 s), so the status and the Surface are in the first paint.
  - The broken preview in that screenshot was the file itself: Solve P = NP's `gf3-parity/index.html` held its own `bucket_get` answer twice over (the read-back mistake of 2026-11-06). `/a` and `Artifacts.read` now unwrap a text file under 2 MB whose JSON names its own key and carries `text` (`Artifact.unwrapped`, up to five levels), so it renders as the page.
  - A turn whose model calls nothing can pay for now ends. The Fermat swarm's first turn (`agt_…:1`) failed every model call with "Boundless was not paid: No method found for challenges" from 07:40 to 17:49 UTC on 2026-11-10, about every 16 s, and the gateway kept the turn running, so the swarm read Working the whole time. `Follow.refused` counts the lead's model calls that failed with what `Turns.broke` recognises (now also " was not paid: "), a model call or tool result that went through clears the count, and at five `Follow.starved` cancels the turn and finishes it failed, which pauses the swarm as out of money. And `Turns.settle` no longer wakes a swarm it paused that way: the pause records what was left and what a step needs, and when what was left already covered a step (the money is there but could not be spent), only new money wakes it. Before that second fix the Fermat swarm went paused, Back at work, five failed calls, paused, every two and a half minutes (17:49 to 17:59 UTC).
  - Checked on 2026-11-10 under `vite` on the fixture world at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone (Now, one agent's traces, an opened trace, Results, an opened result, an out-of-money and a new swarm, Chat, Code): no sideways overflow, no console errors, no control under 24 px in the Now view; 135 node tests pass (`test/brief.test.ts`, `test/trace.test.ts` and additions to the surface, artifact, follow and turns tests). On production after 9835cd2 (version 8b38093d) and the settle fix (version 6c5efc47): `/s/6f4541d2f211` drew "5 of 60 statements proved on main" and "14 more wired to lower statements" from the Surface summary, its five Surface cards with all 60 tiles and its traces with layout shift 0 at both sizes; helper-3's chip showed its last 60 entries; the swarm paused at 17:58:59 UTC and was still paused at 18:01 with no new feed entry; Solve P = NP showed its latest update, Who is on what from eight branches and its traces, and its Results page the repaired GF(3) preview above six write-up rows. Not checked: a swarm whose lead writes the `status` node (none was created since), and `/live` following on production (no swarm was running).
  - Still open: the Fermat swarm shows $9.76 left that its backing keys cannot spend on a Fable call (a key's on-chain limit of 2.83 against a top-up of 2), so it reads Needs funding with money on the bar; the smaller deposit and top-up for new backings and the gateway's choice of key are not changed yet.
  - The page keeps its size while clicking around (2026-11-10, Georgios: "make the navigation on all pages stable so that things dont change in size … like the code when u click around … and also in traces switching between all, lead, helper-1"). Measured under `vite` on the fixture world with a script that reads the title, the tab bar, the shown panel and the document after each click: the Now, Results, Chat and Code panels were 1,414, 2,451, 692 and 386 px tall (Code 823 once loaded), the code frame changed with every folder and file, and the traces box went 420, 140, 174 px while switching agents. Now every panel is at least the window under the tab bar (`min-height`, the chat that height exactly), the code browser is one frame of fixed height (`--repo-height`: the path bar always shown, the folder list beside the open file or the folder's README on a desktop, each scrolling inside; on a phone the list, then the file with ← Files), the traces box is 420 px (360 px on a phone) whatever it holds and each agent's trace is kept while the page is open (`kept` in `Traces.tsx`, read again from its newest seq), and the header's links carry a transparent bottom border so the current one no longer sits a pixel higher.
