After: the code frame stayed 732 px through a folder, a file and the path bar, the traces box 420 px through every chip, and Chat and Code are both 785 px at 1440×900; on production after 6dac4d1 (version 80038e4c) the same script on `/s/6f4541d2f211` read the same sizes, and the header's links did not move between `/`, `/ideas`, `/new` and `/about`. Not changed: a page chunk that is not loaded yet (`/new`, `/about` clicked without hovering first) still shows an empty page for a moment.
  - Why the Fermat swarm reads $9.76 left and Out of money (read on 2026-11-10 at 19:00 UTC): the gateway lists its backings as 25 with 17.107445 charged and 2 with 0.15 charged, so 9.742555 is not charged. On chain the first key's remaining MACH limit is 2.834481, so about 5.06 of its uncharged money already left the wallet into its payment channels, and `Keys.spare` keeps one top-up (2) of the limit for the gateway's own channel, which leaves 0.83, under the 2 a Boundless top-up asks for (`orderChallenges` answers no challenge, hence "No method found for challenges"). The second key (1.85 left on chain) is never picked while the first shows more left. Not changed yet: the deposit and top-up `Operations.back` asks for (a tenth of the backing, 0.25 to 2) and the gateway's guard.
- The site lives at paradigm.xyz/swarms (2026-11-10, from the paradigm.xyz side: "paradigm.xyz/swarms is now reverse proxied to your worker … it shows your app's Nothing out here page, unstyled, since the app serves everything from the root. Could you rebuild it with a /swarms base path?"). The proxy is a Worker on paradigm.xyz that fetches `https://swarms.tempo-dev.workers.dev` with the path kept (its requests carry `cf-worker: paradigm.xyz` and the Worker's own host).
  - `shared/Site.ts` holds the base (`/swarms`): `strip` and `at` take it off and put it on a path, `home(env)` is `SWARMS_URL` (now `https://paradigm.xyz/swarms`) and `url(env, request)` the same, or the request's own origin with the base on localhost. The Worker's `fetch` strips the base and hands the request to the same Hono app, so no route changed. The built assets are named under the base (Vite's `experimental.renderBuiltUrl`, not `base`: with `base` the dev server answers a page asked through the assets binding with "The server is configured with a public base URL"), the catch-all asks the assets for a file the listed `run_worker_first` paths do not cover, and `public/kit/swarm.css` names its fonts relatively.
  - The pages: `router.tsx` puts the base on every link and navigation and reads the path without it, `api.ts`, `Code.tsx` and sign-out put it on every request, the sign-in URL is `/swarms/v1/auth`, the share link, the docs' addresses and the storyboard's frames use the site's address (`Boot.site`), and the feedback widget posts to `/swarms/feedback`. The Worker: canonical, Open Graph, player and oEmbed tags, the cards' links and requests (`frame.root`, `frame.site`), artifact addresses and their stylesheet, the OpenAPI server, MCP links, the agents' room address, the Telegram button and the sign-in message's origin (`https://paradigm.xyz`) all come from `Site`.
  - A page asked for on the Worker's own host answers 301 to its paradigm.xyz/swarms address (a page request without `cf-worker` whose origin is not the site's), since signing in there would name another site; the API, MCP, assets, artifacts and sockets answer on both hosts and with or without the base.
  - paradigm.xyz's proxy answers a WebSocket upgrade with 500 (`error code: 1101`, its Worker threw), while `/live` server-sent events pass through it. So the server render names the host the Worker was reached on (`Boot.socket`) and the page opens the lobby and room sockets there. If the proxy learns to pass upgrades, `Boot.socket` can go.
  - While here: the header read `location.search` during the server render, which had made `/new` render in the browser only since 2026-11-08; it reads `here()` now.
  - Checked on production on 2026-11-10 (ace1ea9, then the socket and redirect commits; version bfa83e06) in headless Chrome at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone on paradigm.xyz: home, All ideas, the Fermat swarm with its tabs, Solve P = NP's results with its artifact preview, Start and the docs rendered styled in Paradigm Serif with layout shift 0, no link or request outside `/swarms`, no console error, and the sockets open with their `welcome`; `/swarms/v1/config`, `/swarms/openapi.json`, a font, the kit stylesheet, an artifact file and a preview image answered 200, a swarm page named `https://paradigm.xyz/swarms/s/<id>` as canonical and `og:url`, and the docs name `https://paradigm.xyz/swarms/mcp`. 138 node tests pass (`test/site.test.ts`). Not run: Tempo Wallet's sign-in and a payment on paradigm.xyz (they need a passkey; the sign-in message now names paradigm.xyz, which must be what Tempo Wallet shows for that page), how the Telegram app and old X cards behave through the redirect, and BotFather's app address, which still names the old host.

A page that drew its own boxes keeps them, and an image cannot be restyled; the persona asks for HTML for anything with words. Only swarms created after this get the new instructions.

## State machine and bug audit (2026-11-10)

Georgios asked for the bugs and implicit state machines of the Swarms app and of the gateway routes it uses to be found and made explicit, and to merge the findings of a second session that had audited Swarms (42 findings, each with a reproduction). Five areas were fixed on branches and merged, each fix with a node test for it (the fixing agents reported each failing on the code before; that was not run again after the merge). The commit subjects of this batch say what each fix does; what follows are the decisions and what is still open.

- The pay flow is one store (`src/web/pay.ts`): a flow per swarm (`idle`, `busy` with its step, `done`, `failed`, and `unrecorded` while a payment the wallet made is not counted yet), with the allowed moves in one table. A sent transfer or a signed approval that the server has not counted is kept in `localStorage` (`swarms.owed`), so closing the box, switching swarms or reloading never loses it, and a retry posts the same hash or the same approval, never a second one. A refusal the server made (4xx) ends as `failed` with its reason; only an answer that could not be given (5xx, 429, no network) keeps the payment owed. `idle` and `failed` may go to `failed`, because the balance check runs before any step.
- The run record (`Phase.Run`) gained two things. A `waiting` run carries the prompt that was posted and not answered for (`sent`: its turn id, text and the notes it told), and the next attempt under that id posts it again word for word: the gateway answers a repeated id with the turn it already has, and a different text under the same id with 409, which Swarms took as accepted and then recorded notes as told that the agent never got. A money pause whose turn the gateway refused to pay while it listed enough money carries `wake`, ten minutes after the first refusal and twice as long after each further failed turn in a row, up to six hours; at `wake` the swarm reads its money and, when the gateway still lists enough, tries a turn. Before, such a pause went on only when more money was put in, so a few minutes of a model provider not being paid ("Boundless was not paid: fetch failed") stopped every running swarm for good. A pause for money the gateway does not list (left under the reserve) has no `wake` and goes on only with new money or a re-read that finds it.
- `Money.refresh` says whether it read the gateway (`known`). A failed credit or backings read used to stand in the row's last figures as if read, so a swarm woken by new money could pause again on the stale zero when the gateway answered 429. Now a turn does not start and a swarm does not pause on figures it could not read: `advance` retries in 30 s with one error row, a turn's end pauses for a shortfall only when it was read, and a paused swarm reads again a minute later.
- `Turns.broke` no longer matches "None of this app's backers can pay", which the gateway never says.
- A swarm's gateway team is named from its title with only the characters the gateway takes (`Team.named`); a title with "=", ":", "?" or parentheses made every first seat answer 502.
- Who posts and who steers is one function for the room's composer and the Worker (`shared/Door.ts`); the roster is read from the gateway's team on every guard; a gateway that cannot list the team answers 502 instead of "not on the team".
- A backing lowered to what it paid takes back what was steered with the rest, newest steer first, and lowers the budgets of the branches it was behind. Newest first was chosen without asking; the alternative is in proportion.
- Migration 0032 adds `hands.returned_at` (an evicted lent machine keeps its row, so its lender stays a rider). The Worker reads the column, so 0032 runs before the deploy. The swarm object's storage version went from 3 to 4.
- Checked on 2026-11-10 under `pnpm dev` on the fixture world: the storyboard's 59 frames at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone had no page error, no console error, no sideways overflow and no phone page scroll; the pay scenes `broke`, `deny`, `uncounted` and `backed` showed their alert with layout shift under 0.05. `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and 208 node tests pass.
- Not fixed, and why:
  - A backing is counted at its approved limit, whatever the backer's wallet holds (an empty wallet can approve $1,000, which then shows as balance and weighs its note), and a tenth of every backing, twice, is kept for a gateway channel that model calls cannot use, while the gateway lists it as left. Both follow from rides being approvals rather than transfers; the choice between making every ride a transfer to the swarm's credit and keeping approvals with a gateway change to their sizing is Georgios's and was not made.
  - The operator key's 30 management requests a minute at the gateway are shared by every swarm's credit and backings reads. Swarms no longer acts on a failed read, but still makes two such reads per refresh per swarm.
  - No gateway route lists the transfers credited to an app, so a transfer the gateway credited and Swarms never recorded cannot be found from Swarms.
  - A team seat with no limit can steer without bound.
  - A turn posted under an id the gateway refuses with a 409 of another kind (the session's model or provider changed) is still taken as accepted, and fails when its report is read.
  - `Operations.stop` reads a failed roster read as "not an admin" (403 rather than 502); a swarm object that never learned its id cannot be healed by a page view; a branch fund writes its ledger row before the gateway budget is raised, with no pending state.
- Not run: any of this on production, a real wallet through the new pay store, and a real gateway refusal followed by its `wake`.
- Deployed on 2026-11-11 at about 00:40 UTC (6946402, version 1b8017e7) after migration 0032, with `CI=1 CTX=tempo wrangler ctx exec pnpm run deploy`. On paradigm.xyz/swarms in headless Chrome at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone: home, All swarms, Start, About, the docs and three swarms (6f4541d2f211, f3a42cf87339, 06cf5d7c8f92) with their chat and code pages answered 200 with no console error, no 5xx, no sideways overflow and layout shift 0 (0.024 once). The list of 47 swarms read the same before and after (status, why, balance, riders), and the one running swarm, 06cf5d7c8f92, kept running and spending. The gateway's part (tempoxyz/gateway#613, a4d580d) deployed the same hour.

## One way in: every ride is a transfer (2026-11-11)

Georgios, asked whether every ride should become a transfer to the swarm's credit or approvals should stay with their sizing fixed at the gateway: "yeah simplify it all - steering should be applied fifo".

- A ride is a `transferWithMemo` of MACH, OUSD, USDC.e or pathUSD to the gateway's recipient with the swarm's memo, counted by its hash at `POST …/contributions`. That is the only way in. Gone: the approval of a spending key for MACH of $1 or more (`POST …/backings` and `…/backings/:request` under `/api` and `/v1`, `Operations.back` and `approved`, the page's `wallet_connect` with `authorizeAccessKey`, the `approved` kind of an owed payment, the docs and OpenAPI entries). An approval left nothing to send, but it needed a key, two channels, top-ups and a listing to say what it could pay, and the audit above found that an empty wallet's approval counted as money, that part of each one (half of a $1 approval) could never pay a model call, and that a tool call could end one for good. A transfer has none of those states: the money is the swarm's credit when the gateway has counted it. What is lost is that unspent MACH stayed in the rider's wallet; money sent is not returned.
- Approvals made before this keep working until they end (30 days at most, or when revoked): the agent's payer is still `backers`, which pays from them first and from the credit after, `Money.refresh` still reads them, and one that ends is still lowered to what it paid. On 2026-11-11 production held live ones on 6f4541d2f211 ($17.35 left) and 06cf5d7c8f92 ($11.23 left). An approval an older page had signed and not yet posted is dropped by the page (`pay.ts` keeps only sent transfers); nothing was approved by it, since only the removed route completed it.
- A key approved at the gateway for a swarm's app without Swarms (its `/api/keys/request` is open) is counted only at what it has paid, raised as it pays; before, it was counted at its limit the first time it was listed, so anyone could add balance and steering weight to a swarm with an empty wallet.
- Steering is first in, first out: a rider's money covers what they steered in the order they steered it, so when their money shrinks (an old approval ends having paid less than its limit) the idea or branch they steered most recently gives its money back first (`Store.lower`, by each idea's or branch's latest steer). This is what the code did; it was confirmed rather than changed.
- Checked on 2026-11-11 under `pnpm dev` on the fixture world: the storyboard's 58 frames at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone had no page error, console error or sideways overflow. `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and 205 node tests pass. Under `pnpm dev` with local D1 the removed routes answered 404, `/openapi.json` named no backing, and the call `swarm_contribute` prepared for 1 MACH to a local swarm passed an `eth_call` from the test wallet on Tempo (nothing was sent). Not run: a real wallet sending MACH of $1 or more from the page (the same code sent MACH under $1 and every stablecoin before).
- Deployed on 2026-11-11 at about 01:00 UTC (27e6303, version 9be755ae), no migration. On paradigm.xyz/swarms: `POST …/backings` under `/api` and `/v1` answered 404, `/openapi.json` named no backing, a transfer hash Tempo does not know answered 404 "no receipt", and the same pages as the deploy before, at both sizes, had no console error, 5xx or sideways overflow. The list of 47 swarms read the same as before both deploys but for what 06cf5d7c8f92 spent while it ran (25.01 to 25.44); its feed after the deploys held 62 entries, its errors the agent's own (a subagent out of its budget, cells that ran out of CPU, and one tool call cut off when the gateway's deploy restarted its session).

## Approvals are back: credit cannot pay for Boundless models (2026-11-11)

"One way in" above was reverted the same night, two hours after it shipped. Georgios: "go make this work now find bugs and optimize make sure swarms keep running".

- Why. A ride that is a transfer becomes the swarm's credit at the gateway, and the gateway pays a credit-funded agent's Boundless model calls (Claude Fable 5.2, the default template, and every other `boundless/` model) from its own fee payer, in stablecoins, while the rider's MACH sits at the gateway's recipient, which the Worker cannot spend. Read on Tempo at 01:55 UTC: the fee payer (0x4cc7…9965) held 5.21 pathUSD and 0.58 USDC.e; the recipient held 936.58 MACH, 39.09 OUSD and 51.17 USDC.e. The gateway's ledger had no row of a swarm's credit ever paying Boundless: approvals had paid it 25.85 MACH straight from riders' wallets. So with every ride a transfer, about $5 of model calls across all swarms would have emptied the account that also pays every sponsored network fee, after which no MACH-only key can open or top up a channel. The recommendation to make every ride a transfer was made without reading how credit pays Boundless.
- What is back: a ride of $1 or more in MACH is an approval (the page's `wallet_connect` with `authorizeAccessKey`, `POST …/backings` and `…/backings/:request`, `Operations.back` and `approved`, the docs and OpenAPI), paid from the rider's wallet to the model's provider as the swarm spends. Smaller MACH rides and stablecoins are transfers to the credit, as before.
- Kept from that change: a key approved at the gateway without Swarms counts only at what it has paid.
- What makes approvals work better than they did (tempoxyz/gateway#613, #614, #615, all deployed that night): a backing that cannot pay a call is rested, not retired; it rests 10 minutes, doubling to an hour, when Boundless says it has no room, instead of being picked again a minute later; it may use its whole limit less its gateway deposit for models, where a top-up was also kept back; and the gateway refuses to front Boundless while its fee payer holds under $3.
- The Fermat swarm (6f4541d2f211), paused since 23:32 UTC with $22.35 listed as left, ran again from 01:42 to 01:51 UTC on the new code, spent $4.07 from its approvals and pushed two merges, then stopped again when each approval left had less room than one call of its lead holds. It tries again by itself (the wake above). What its four approvals still list ($13.80) is partly out of reach: a call must fit in one approval, and the last part of each one, less than one top-up, cannot be deposited.
- An approval counts at what its wallet can pay when it is approved: the smaller of its limit and what the gateway lists for the key (what it paid plus what it can still pay, which the gateway caps by the wallet's MACH). An empty wallet's approval is refused with 409 `insufficient_balance` and counts nothing, where it used to add its whole limit to the swarm's balance and to its note's weight; one the gateway does not list yet answers 502 and the page posts it again. A wallet that empties after approving is still counted at what it held then, until the approval ends.
- Still wrong, and Georgios's or the gateway's to settle: credit that reaches the gateway's recipient does not come back to the fee payer by itself, so stablecoin rides on Boundless models are paid from a float the operator refills; `Backers.pick` at the gateway keeps its resting keys per isolate, so a key with no room still fails one call in each isolate before it is skipped.
- Checked on 2026-11-11 under `pnpm dev` on the fixture world: the storyboard's 59 frames at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone, no page error, console error or sideways overflow. `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and 210 node tests pass.

## Keeping swarms running (2026-11-11)

Georgios: "go make this work now find bugs and optimize make sure swarms keep running". What was found on production that night and what changed, besides the section above.

- A pause the gateway refused always tries again. One recorded before pauses carried a `wake` (the Fermat swarm's, from 23:32 UTC) is due ten minutes after it paused, and no `wake` is honoured further than an hour after the pause (`Phase.maxPatienceMs`, six hours at first: the count of failed turns in a row includes the ones the bug itself caused, so Fermat's second pause would have waited hours). Such a swarm says "Its last step could not be paid for, though it has money. It tries again by itself." on its page and in its feed, where it said "Out of money", and a turn started by such a retry does not announce "Back at work." or the same pause again in the room.
- `Money.refresh` asks the gateway for a swarm's backings at most once in ten minutes once a listing showed none alive (kv `unbacked`), and at once again when an approval is counted, so a swarm that only has credit makes one keyed gateway read per refresh instead of two; every swarm shares the operator key's 30 such reads a minute.
- `GET …/branches` answers the list the swarm last kept when the gateway takes more than 1.5 s to list the agent's subagents or fails to, whatever the list's age. On production during a gateway deploy the listing took 15 to 30 s and the route answered 502 after 14 s and 200 after 31 s; after the change it answered in 1.7 to 2.5 s cold.
- Checked on production on 2026-11-11 (Swarms 73c6a02, version 0c2f7575; gateway 24bddb2):
  - The Fermat swarm, viewed once after the deploy of 01:39 UTC, went from paused to working at 01:42:44 by itself.
  - A throwaway wallet with no MACH approved $1 for the e2e swarm 3811c1ffdbb0 through `POST /v1/swarms/:id/backings` and `…/backings/:request` (the key authorization signed with ox): the gateway took it and listed the key at once, and Swarms answered 409 `insufficient_balance` twice with the swarm's balance unchanged. The key stays listed for that app with nothing to pay.
  - A tail of the Worker over 100 s of reads of four swarms' eleven read routes held 113 events, every one `ok`, no exception.
  - Not run: an approval from a wallet that holds MACH, since no such wallet's key was at hand; a gateway refusal followed by a retry at its `wake` on production (the Fermat swarm's is due by 02:51 UTC).
- Later the same night:
  - A turn in which neither the lead nor a subagent makes a model call or gets a tool result for 30 minutes is cancelled and ends as failed, so the swarm starts another (`Clock.stuckMs`, chosen without measurement; `Follow.follow`, only while the gateway answers, since a gateway that fails for 15 minutes is the unreachable rule's). Before, such a turn was only reported after 20 minutes and waited for ever. Seen on 06cf5d7c8f92: its lead's `git_commit` of 02:14:12 UTC had no answer and the gateway's listing of its subagents timed out after 25 s, while the turn still read `running`.
  - No gateway call from the Worker or a swarm object waits more than two minutes (`Clock.gatewayMs`, an `AbortSignal.timeout` in `Gateway.send`); a call that hung held the swarm's alarm.
  - A turn is given up for lack of payment after eight refused model calls in a row, where it was five: with four approvals out of room, four refusals come before the gateway reaches the swarm's credit.
  - A paused swarm that is viewed reads its money again even while an alarm for a later wake is pending (`Swarm.heal` returned whenever any alarm was set, so once pauses set an alarm at their wake, the ten-minute look on a view and the one-hour cap never ran: the Fermat swarm stayed paused past 02:51).
  - Every gateway deploy resets the agents' sessions, and four of them in two hours (tempoxyz/gateway#613 to #616) cost 06cf5d7c8f92 two turns, each ended by the gateway with "The session was reset while driving this turn four times in a row". Batch gateway changes.
  - Checked on production at 03:00 UTC (Swarms 684bc6a, version afb3aa13; gateway 24bddb2): the Fermat swarm went back to work by itself at 02:55 once viewed, its four approvals were skipped after one refusal each (the rest the gateway now keeps in D1), every call then reached its credit, and the gateway answered "Boundless was not paid: the gateway holds too little to front this call until its operator refills it"; after eight of those it paused again at 03:00:16 as "Its last step could not be paid for, though it has money. It tries again by itself." 06cf5d7c8f92 was working again from 02:36. The gateway's guard reads the balance of the token the fee payer pays fees in (USDC.e, 0.55), not its largest (pathUSD, 5.21), so it refuses while USDC.e is under 3; it was left so, rather than deploy the gateway a fifth time.
  - So the Fermat swarm runs again when the gateway's fee payer (0x4cc7c5c526b16a0137270c48d84f826e16c99965) is refilled, which lets its $5 of credit pay, or when someone rides it with a new approval large enough for one call of its lead (about $3 held at once). Its four old approvals list $13.80 and can pay almost none of it.
  - Why approvals "ran out of room" (Georgios: "that seems self inflicted", then "all of this must work streaming w/o overcharging"). An approval paid Boundless directly, and Boundless holds each request's worst case before it runs: every byte of the body as an input token at the uncached price, plus the output limit (its `/mpp/setup`; asked without payment, Claude Fable 5.2 quoted $3.20 for a 400 KB conversation with 1,024 output tokens, a call that costs cents once cached). One approval had to cover that hold alone, for every call in flight on it, so four approvals with $2 to $4 left paid nothing. The gateway already charges its own callers the other way (hold the likely cost, charge what was used, cut a stream at what the channel can pay); approvals bypassed it. tempoxyz/gateway#617 (a draft, not merged, not run) sends an approval's model calls through the gateway's metered path and has the gateway pay Boundless from keys of its recipient wallet, the one wallet MACH's policy lets pay Boundless with the MACH riders paid. It needs those keys, which only the recipient wallet's owner can approve. With it, credit can pay for Boundless models too, so "One way in" can come back.

## Riders pay only the gateway (2026-11-11)

Georgios, after "Approvals are back" above: "cant we do without that pool?", "we should be able to not get paid on chain for every request and use mpp sessions", and then, of a side question: riders only ever pay the gateway and the gateway alone deals with providers; make it idiot proof.

- The rule. A rider's money goes to the gateway, which charges it for what each call used (its own sessions hold a call's likely cost, charge what it used and cut a stream at what is left). No rider, and no approval of a rider, pays a model's provider itself. Every stuck swarm of the night before came from breaking this: an approval paid Boundless over its own session, and Boundless's session asks each request for a voucher covering its worst case, about a hundred times what a cached call costs.
- So swarms run only on models the gateway pays its providers for. `Setup`'s templates name them: Claude Opus 5.5, Claude Fable 5.1, Kimi K3.1 Pro and GPT-6 through Vercel, DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, GLM-5.3 and GLM-5.3 Flash through Cloudflare. The default template is Claude Code on Opus 5.5 for the lead and every subagent (it was Fable 5.2, which only Boundless sells; Opus 5.5 costs the same on Vercel as it did on Boundless, Fable 5.1 is $10, $0.25 and $50 a million against 5.2's $8, $0.20 and $40). A `boundless/` id is refused in a custom setup (`Setup.direct`), is left out of `/v1/config`'s models and of the prices a setup is costed with, and a node test fails if a template names one.
- A swarm that ran on one is moved the next time it has money and is between turns (`Turns.remodel`): its setup is rewritten to the model the gateway meters (`Setup.moved`) and costed again, its agent is dropped so the next turn creates one on the new model with the brief (it keeps the repository, artifacts, notes and updates and loses the old agent's conversation), and its instructions are rendered again, since they name the models. On 2026-11-11 three swarms had such a setup: 6f4541d2f211 (Fermat) and the e2e swarms 3811c1ffdbb0 and 23375a753ab7.
- Every ride is a transfer to the swarm's credit again ("One way in" above, as it was written). What made it wrong two hours after it shipped was credit paying for Boundless models out of the gateway's fee payer; credit paying for a model the gateway meters costs the fee payer nothing, and the gateway's ledger shows credit paying 1,226 such calls on 2026-11-10. Approvals made before this pay through the gateway as well now that no swarm runs on Boundless, until they end.
- Known and accepted: Fable 5.2 is not offered; the gateway pays its providers in dollars from its own accounts while riders' MACH collects at its recipient, as for every other caller; what an old approval had already deposited with Boundless goes back to its rider's wallet when that channel closes and cannot pay for the swarm.
- Checked on 2026-11-11 under `pnpm dev` on the fixture world: the storyboard's 59 frames at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone, no page error, console error or sideways overflow. `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and 210 node tests pass.
- Not built yet, from the same request: a payment refusal that says why as a code rather than as the words "was not paid"; a canary turn per template; an alert when a swarm with money keeps being refused; the gateway listing which models it meters, so the guard reads that instead of the `boundless/` prefix.
- Checked on production on 2026-11-11 (Swarms 9e812e6, version f61bddea; gateway 126045b, which lists Vercel's three speeds of DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 1017): `/v1/config` listed 17 models, none `boundless/`; a custom swarm naming `boundless/anthropic/claude-fable-5.2` answered 400 with the reason; `POST …/backings` answered 404. The Fermat swarm (6f4541d2f211), paused since 03:00 UTC with $18.80 it could not spend, tried again by itself at 05:05:30: it logged "Moved the swarm from Claude Fable 5.2 to Claude Fable 5.1", created agent agt_a50ec6a4…, went back to work and started a helper. In the next six minutes the gateway's traces showed 16 `model.messages` calls and 23 tool calls of it, every one answered 200 and paid by its riders' old approvals through the gateway, where the hour before every call was refused. `GET /v1/health`, added the same hour, answered the Fermat swarm as refused before and `{"ok":true,"refused":[],"running":2}` after.
- Not run: a ride by transfer from a real wallet after this change; a swarm created on the new default from the start page.

## Fable 5.2 is back, behind the gateway (2026-11-11)

Georgios: "Yes please, I do want fable 5.2 back - can you plz go and do the whole fund flow again".

- The Fable template leads on `boundless/anthropic/claude-fable-5.2` again, with Fable 5.2, Opus 5.5, Kimi K3.1 Pro and GLM-5.3 for subagents. It is allowed because the gateway now meters it: tempoxyz/gateway#617 (merged, 6a83e9e) pays Boundless from lanes, keys the gateway holds, and charges the swarm's riders by what each call used, so no rider carries Boundless's hold. The gateway's model list says which models it meters (`metered`); `Gateway.prices` offers only those, a setup naming any other is refused, a template falls back to the metered stand-in (Fable 5.1 for 5.2) when creating, and a running swarm is moved the same way (`Setup.moved(setup, prices)`). The default template stays Opus 5.5 through Vercel.
- The lanes are three keys of the test wallet (0x9759…e122), 8 MACH each for 30 days, minted with its admin test key and set as the gateway's secret `BOUNDLESS_KEYS`. That is a stopgap with about $22 of room in all: riders' MACH lands at the gateway's recipient, and only that wallet can fund lanes from it. When the lanes are spent, Boundless calls are refused again and `/v1/health` will show it; the gateway still says `metered` then, so swarms are not moved off by themselves. Keys of the recipient wallet, with real limits, replace them by changing the secret.
- The whole fund flow on production, 05:34 to 05:41 UTC (Swarms 7ac9b88, version aaab7108): `POST /v1/swarms` with template `fable` made 1ecb2729f4d2 on `boundless/anthropic/claude-fable-5.2`; `swarm_contribute` prepared a 1 MACH transfer, the test wallet sent it (0x41a2be4233f0e51db990183ca1973cf5471edf5073aff69a8d10e0b039196137) and `POST …/contributions` answered 201 with balance 1.000000; the swarm was working 8 s later and answered "Hello — I'm running on boundless/anthropic/claude-fable-5.2 via Claude Code." Its turns cost $0.159720 (the uncached first call), then $0.071256, $0.072264, $0.0738, $0.074808 and $0.07568, each paid from the credit and each a few cents where Boundless would have held dollars from a rider; the gateway's credit for the app read deposited 1, spent 0.3766 mid-way. At $0.399112 left, under the $0.44096 one step can need, it paused as "Out of money. Ride it to keep it going." and `/v1/health` answered ok. The test wallet's MACH went from 43.75 to 20.41: the 1 MACH ride and the three lanes' deposits with Boundless, which come back when those channels close.
- A first try at the secret joined the three keys with no commas (`paste -s` joins each file apart), so the gateway found no lane and said `metered: false`; set again with commas, it said `true` within a minute.
- Left as they are: the Fermat swarm stays on Fable 5.1, since moving it back costs it its lead's memory again and its spending would empty the lanes in about an hour; the idle "keep going" turns of a finished goal cost $0.07 each on Fable 5.2 until the money is under one step.

## No swarm runs on Boundless until it charges by the stream (2026-11-11)

Georgios, after reading how Tempo's own mpp-proxy charges a session: "ok so lets not use boundless for our swarms until that's fixed".

- The Fable template is Claude Fable 5.1 through Vercel again, and every `boundless/` id is refused, whatever the gateway says of it (`Setup.direct`; `Gateway.prices` leaves them out, so `/v1/config` does not list them, a custom setup naming one answers 400, and a swarm still on one is moved the next time it has money). "Fable 5.2 is back, behind the gateway" above lasted an hour; the gateway's lanes (tempoxyz/gateway#617) stay in its code and do nothing without keys.
- What "fixed" means, for whoever picks this up: Boundless's MPP endpoint holds each request's worst case before it runs (every body byte as an input token at the uncached price, plus the output limit; $3.20 for a 400 KB Claude Fable 5.2 request). tempoxyz/mpp-proxy (`meterStream` and `payAsStreamed` in `src/mppx.ts`, `claudeStreamMeter` and `resolveClaudeUpfront` in `src/services/anthropic.ts`, read at e0e62c4) charges the stream instead: the prompt up front at real token counts with the conversation's previous prompt at the cached rate, settled to the usage of the first event, then output 256 tokens at a time from the channel's voucher balance, with a `payment-need-voucher` event when it runs short, and exact usage at the end. When Boundless does the same, a rider's money can pay it without being stranded, and the gateway's client has to answer those voucher events (it pays Boundless with a plain `fetch`; mppx's streaming transport handles them).
- The three lane keys of the test wallet were stopped at the gateway so their deposits with Boundless come back to it (below).
- An approval made before transfers became the only way in counts at what it can still pay: what it has paid plus what the gateway lists as left for it, which since tempoxyz/gateway#619 leaves out what sits in the key's old Boundless channel (`Money.settle`; it is lowered once and raised again only by what it goes on to pay, so the figure does not flap). The Fermat swarm showed $7.73 left at 06:26 UTC and could spend $0.47 of it: the rest was four approvals' deposits with Boundless, which go back to their riders when those channels close and can never pay the swarm now. `/v1/health` listed it as refused for that reason.
- Checked on production on 2026-11-11 (Swarms 232bdb0, version 47f37ec1; gateway e699676): `/v1/config` listed 20 models, none `boundless/`; a custom swarm naming Fable 5.2 answered 400 "swarms do not run on Boundless until it charges a call as it streams"; the three lane keys were stopped with `POST /api/keys/stop` and the test wallet's MACH went from 20.40 back to 42.12 within three minutes. The Fermat swarm, read once after both deploys, went from "$7.73 left" to balance 39.80, spent 39.27, $0.54 left, paused as out of money, and `/v1/health` answered `{"ok":true,"refused":[],"running":1}`. It had spent its $5 of credit and what its approvals could still pay the gateway between 05:05 and 05:57 UTC on Fable 5.1.

## Fable 5.2 comes through Vercel (2026-11-11)

Georgios: "do a few back and forths, optimize, then go and implement the most sensible and simple thing that works and is optimized in the gateway".

- Vercel sells Claude Fable 5.2 at the price Boundless does ($8, $0.20 cached and $40 a million tokens, read from Vercel's catalogue), and the gateway already served `vercel/anthropic/claude-fable-5.2` by id; it was only missing from the gateway's list, which is all the earlier sections of this file had read when they called Fable 5.2 Boundless-only. A paid call for it through the gateway answered from Vercel ($0.00116 for 25 prompt and 24 output tokens). tempoxyz/gateway#620 lists it.
- So the simplest thing that works needs no pool, session, hold or wallet: the Fable template leads on `vercel/anthropic/claude-fable-5.2`, and the default template is again Claude Code on Fable 5.2 for the lead and every subagent, as it was before that night. A swarm still set to a `boundless/` model is moved to the same model through Vercel. Riders pay the gateway by transfer and are charged by what each call uses, as for every other model.
- The design the night's questions led to is in the gateway's `FUNDS.md`: the gateway aggregates demand, customers hold a balance with it, it buys from each supplier itself, every link charges by use, and where a supplier billed in dollars sells the same thing at the same price it is the one to buy from. Suppliers paid on chain, Boundless among them, are for what only they sell, behind the gateway's own session, which waits on one approval from the owner of the gateway's receiving wallet.
- The Fermat swarm keeps Fable 5.1 on its current agent; a model cannot be changed on an agent, and a new one would lose the lead's memory a third time.
- Checked on production on 2026-11-11 after the deploy of 6f015b8 (version e4c21d9d), with two 1 MACH rides sent as transfers from the test wallet by its admin key through the public API. A swarm created with no template (ea6c8a485525) answered `frontier-anthropic` with `vercel/anthropic/claude-fable-5.2` for the lead and its subagents and `most` 0.44096; the transfer (0xec0180e063a5abbe7ad695bcab9fc9b1b7de35a6e73c2f3332c0080157a75b8b) was counted in 4 s, its agent started 3 s later and said it runs on vercel/anthropic/claude-fable-5.2; its first turn cost $0.196592 (uncached) and each later one $0.0072 to $0.0076, charged by the call; it paused at 08:06:33 UTC as "Out of money. Ride it to keep it going." with $0.439436 left, under the $0.44 a step can cost. The check swarm from the Boundless night (1ecb2729f4d2, set to `boundless/anthropic/claude-fable-5.2`, paused with $0.399) took a 1 MACH transfer (0xecc6048249b9a93d66daa0a878e51a4d02aa2192d8d6f887c39c5fa171c8674d): 2 s later it had a new agent, which said it runs on vercel/anthropic/claude-fable-5.2; its first turn cost $0.208562 and later ones $0.0097 to $0.018, and it paused at about 08:33 UTC with $0.434764 left. `/v1/health` answered ok with nothing refused throughout. Not checked: a ride from the page with a real Tempo Wallet, and a swarm started from the start page.

## Session resets and honest totals (2026-11-12)

- The "Solve P = NP" swarm failed nearly every turn from 08:25 PST on 2026-11-11 with "session was reset… four times in a row": its heavy code cells crashed the gateway host running its session, and each recovery re-ran the same cell. Two Swarms bugs kept it there. `Turns.failing` read at most `maxFailures` (3) turns, so `backoff` never passed one minute; it now counts up to 12, so the backoff reaches its 30-minute cap. And Swarms only replaced an agent when the gateway stopped taking messages, so it re-sent "Keep going." to the same dead session every two minutes. Now two failed turns in a row whose message says the session was reset (`Turns.resets`) drop the agent (`renew`), log why, and wake at once: the next step creates a new agent that keeps the repository, notes and updates but not the conversation. `renewedAt` keeps the new agent from being replaced on the old agent's turns. The gateway re-running a crashing cell on recovery is the gateway's (nanocode-ts) to fix.
- The home line and the share card's field total added up every swarm's `balance` (all riders ever put in, $288.55 on 2026-11-11) while about $11 was left. Both now add up `remaining`, floored at zero, and the card says "to spend".
- An update row's gist (`Answer.gist`) skips raw JSON lines (gateway errors such as `{"code":"over_limit",…}`) and fenced code, and shows inline LaTeX (`\le`, `\frac{a}{b}`, `\sqrt{x}`, Greek letters) as symbols. Opened update bodies still show LaTeX as written; rendering it needs KaTeX.
- Checked with `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and 215 tests.

## The 2026-11-10 audit, rechecked (2026-11-12)

- Each of the audit's 42 findings (40 distinct) was checked again at 71a4dc2. 36 were already fixed by the 2026-11-10 round and have regression tests. Approvals are gone, so the double-approval bug (#31) can no longer happen. Three are fixed now:
  - A team seat with no limit could put the swarm's money behind ideas and branches without end ($100 per message, repeated). `Store.allot` now holds a seat's 30-day allotments to `coalesce(limit, balance)` in the same insert ... select. `Team.roster` derives `seat.left` from the same bound, so `Seat.left` is never null in the API. The bound is balance rather than remaining, because the window counts allotments whose money was already spent.
  - `Operations.stop` read a failed team listing as "not an admin" (403). It now answers 502 through `Team.unlisted`, like every other guard.
  - New tests cover four lent-machine fixes that had none (a failed hands listing, a returned hand's lender, eviction without a listing, a lender seen on join), plus a paused swarm lowering a backing that ended while it was paused.
- Not fixed: a transfer the gateway credited but D1 never stored (#18). The gateway's credit route reports only totals, with no deposits by hash, so Swarms can't find the missing transfer. Recording the same hash again stays safe, and the page keeps unrecorded hashes (`swarms.owed`).
- The five fixer worktrees of the audit session (`swarms-fix-*`) and their branches no longer existed on gak-9.

## Credited transfers are recovered by hash (2026-11-14)

- Closes #18 of the 2026-11-10 audit: a transfer the gateway credited and Swarms never stored (the page lost the answer and its `swarms.owed` entry) is now counted. The gateway lists an app's credited transfers at `GET /api/keys/credit/deposits` (tempoxyz/gateway e44bcb7: `hash`, `log`, `token`, `from`, `amount`, `tag`, `created_at`, oldest first), which `Gateway.deposits` reads.
- `Money.recover` runs in `Money.refresh` after a credit read that succeeded with `deposited` above zero, and only when `deposited` differs from the value of its last pass (the object's `deposited` key), so the operator key's shared management limit pays one listing per change in what riders sent, not one per refresh. It groups the listed logs by hash (one transaction can carry several), skips hashes already in `contributions` (`Store.counted`, one `json_each` query), stores each missing one as `POST …/contributions` would without a note (the tag commits to the note's text, which the gateway never saw), records the new total, then tells the object (`contributed`, kind `new`), which wakes a paused swarm. A recovered transfer refreshes the row as a settled backing does. A failed listing or write leaves the key unchanged, so the next refresh tries again.
- A page that posts its hash after recovery stored it keeps its note: when `Store.give` finds the row already there and the request carries a note, `Operations.contribute` annotates it as a note on a counted transfer does, tells the object kind `noted` and answers `created: false`.
- The first refresh of every swarm after the deploy lists its deposits once (47 swarms on production, each at its own pace), and counts whatever the gateway credited that Swarms never stored.
- Checked with `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and 225 node tests; the two new ones (`money.test.ts` recovery, `funding.test.ts` race) fail on the code before. `Store.counted` was run against the tests' SQLite D1 with a mixed-case hash.

- Deployed on 2026-11-14 at about 21:56 UTC (3d6bf13, version 1fb88c43, after 415dc23d), from a clean worktree of origin/main with `CI=1 CTX=tempo wrangler ctx exec pnpm run deploy` (pnpm 10.34.9; no migration). The gateway's route went live first (tempoxyz/gateway e44bcb7, deployed by its workflow). On production: home, about and docs answered 200; the gateway listed the deposits of every listed swarm's app that the operator key could read before its management limit answered 429 (8 swarms with 9 credited transfer logs), and every listed hash was already in `contributions`, so nothing was missing to recover; within ten minutes the running swarm 06cf5d7c8f92 read `GET /api/keys/credit/deposits` through the service binding (200) and logged no recovery error. Not run on production: a recovery of a transfer actually missing, and the page-note race.

## Sign-in and rides through Tempo ID (2026-11-13)

The user's request: "migrate swarms to accounts at Alpha", following accounts' guides and its migration guide (tempoxyz/accounts2 `site/src/pages/docs/guides/migration.mdx`, `siwe.mdx`, `payments/request-payments.mdx`). `accounts` goes from 0.21.0 to 1.0.0-alpha.20261116025252-85382d1, the SDK of Tempo ID (with the signed-out `accounts.pay({ challenge })`, tempoxyz/accounts2#384, the frame's Account row that switches users in a popup, #388, and the payment popup opened at the click in browsers without IntersectionObserver v2, such as Safari, for an app Tempo ID does not trust, #405), and viem from 2.57.7 to 2.57.19, its peer. pnpm's release-age policy lists both and ox in `minimumReleaseAgeExclude`, since they were published that day.

- The Worker's sign-in is accounts' `siwe` plugin on its `backend` plugin (`Sessions.ts`), as the guide maps `Handler.auth`. The plugin's sessions live in the same D1 table `sessions` through `Store.from` (`takeItem` is `delete … returning`, so a challenge verifies once across isolates), and one instance serves an isolate. A session's id is the bearer token, so every session is still a token, 30 days, and nothing is a cookie. The routes are the "Write SIWE routes" recipe without cookies, under `/v1`'s CORS in `Rest.ts`: `POST /v1/auth/challenge` answers `{ id, message }`, `POST /v1/auth` takes `{ address, id, message, signature }` and answers `{ token }`, and `POST /v1/auth/logout` ends the bearer's session. A refused sign-in answers 401 with the plugin's reason.
- What breaks for callers. A script now posts the challenge's `id` with its signature. An access key's signature no longer signs in: the plugin refuses keychain envelopes, since an access key signs for an app and not as the account, where `Sessions.auth` had allowed them since 2026-11-06 for headless runs; a script signs with the account's own key. Tokens issued before the deploy do not read, so each page's next signed-in action answers 401 and opens the sign-in again (`renewing`), and scripts sign in again.
- A challenge request that carries an `Origin` must come from `SWARMS_URL`'s origin (the plugin's `origin`); one without it, such as a script's, gets a message for `SWARMS_URL`, as `Handler.auth` gave. Locally, sign in on the page served at `SWARMS_URL`, as before.
- The page creates `Accounts.create({ chain })` on first use, whose default sign-in is Tempo ID's dialog. Signing in fetches the challenge, the user's Tempo Account signs it on Tempo ID's page, and the page posts the signature. A ride is `accounts.pay` with the signed-in address as `account`: Tempo ID's payment page shows the amount and memo, the account's owner signs, and Tempo ID's sponsor pays the network fee, where Tempo Wallet's `eth_sendTransactionSync` paid it in the token sent. No sign-in asks for an access key, so nothing signs a transaction without the user.
- The ride box no longer checks the wallet's balance (the user's request: "This should just use the pay or deposit UX"). It showed "Not enough in your wallet." and refused to send when the chosen token held less than the amount; now every ride opens Tempo ID's payment page, which swaps from another token the account holds, or offers a deposit when none covers it. The fixture scene `broke` still fails the send, as a rider who closes the deposit would.
- Buy MACH from the payment page's deposit (the user's report: clicking MACH by card opened the payment page again in a popup, not Buy MACH with Apple Pay). accounts fixed it in tempoxyz/accounts2#375 (8201e42, the popup opens `/pay` with `method=mach`), published in 1.0.0-alpha.20261113032129-57efa59.
- The header shows the signed-in user's email (the user's request: "can we make this show the email of user"), after a username and in place of the short address. `dialog.signMessage` keeps no token, so signing in is `dialog.signIn({ challenge })` with the challenge fetched first: its session holds the signer (`address`), the signature (`challengeSignature`) and the verified `user.email`. The page then calls `accounts.signOut()`, which revokes the refresh token that sign-in kept, since Swarms uses only its own token. The email is kept with the face (localStorage and the `swarms_face` cookie, which `Page.face` reads back for the server render, so the header does not shift on load). It is shown only to that user; the server never stores it. The button cuts it at 24 characters, and at 11 on a phone, the width of a short address.
- One screen to ride and sign in (the user's request: "pay without being logged in first, and just-in-time authorize you onto the site"). Tempo ID's payment page and accounts gained it for this (tempoxyz/id#927, tempoxyz/accounts2#384): `accounts.pay({ amount, challenge, memo, to, token })` without `account` signs the user in or up on the payment page, pays from the account selected for the app, has the account sign the challenge, and resolves `{ address, challengeSignature, transaction }`. A ride without a session (`Wallet.send`) fetches the challenge, pays with it, and posts the signature to `/v1/auth`; a failed sign-in after a sent payment still lets the ride be counted, since counting needs no session. With a session, a ride names the account and asks for no signature. Starting a swarm is one screen too, whatever its steering: `Operations.create` no longer answers 401 for a team swarm without a session, which it had to because the server records the creator before the ride's memo can name the new swarm. Such a swarm has no creator until its first counted ride, whose sender becomes it (`Store.claim`, `coalesce`, so two first rides at once keep one), where an open swarm nobody started is led by its biggest rider. A signed-in start still records its creator at once. A sign-in through a ride learns no email, since Tempo ID gives the app none, so the header shows the short address until a sign-in through the dialog.
- The payment dialog opens at the click, and the challenge loads behind it (the user's report of 2026-11-13: "it takes a while to load"; the challenge took 0.13 to 1.2 s on production, measured with curl). As Tempo Wallet did with `wallet_connect`'s `auth` capability, the page names its routes once, `siwe({ auth: Site.at('/v1/auth') })` beside `dialog()` (tempoxyz/accounts2#461, tempoxyz/id#986), and `accounts.pay` without `account` and `dialog.signIn()` take no challenge: at the click the dialog shows Tempo ID's page while the SDK posts `{ chainId }` to `/v1/auth/challenge`, Tempo ID's page waits for the message before Pay or Approve, and the SDK posts `{ address, id, message, signature }` to `/v1/auth` and returns its `{ token }` as `auth`. The SDK posts verify itself (the user's choice, like the old `returnToken`), so the page stores `auth.token`. A failed challenge request closes the dialog before anything is signed or sent; a failed verify rejects `dialog.signIn`, and after a payment resolves with `authError` and the transaction, which Swarms still counts, leaving the rider signed out. The routes needed no change. The ride box still loads accounts and the memo code when it mounts (`Wallet.warm`), and fetches no challenge, so a signed-out view writes nothing. Before this, from 2026-11-13, the page fetched a challenge when the box mounted, kept it 5 minutes and passed the message to `pay`. Checked under `pnpm dev` with accounts 1.0.0-alpha.20261116025252-85382d1: the challenge route answered `{ chainId }` with `{ id, message }`, a preflight from another origin for `content-type` answered `*`, and a fresh key's `{ address, id, message, signature }` got a token; in headless Chrome a signed-out page fetched no challenge on load, and the click started the challenge request and `id.tempo.xyz/embed/pay` (with no `challenge` in its URL) 12 and 14 ms after the pointer went down, the frame showing Tempo ID's sign-in. The header's Sign in starts both at once too, about 300 ms after the click on a page without a ride box, where the accounts code loads at the click. Not run: a completed payment or sign-in through it. Release only after Tempo ID's production deploy of #986: an older payment page drops the late challenge, and `pay` then fails with `missing challenge_signature`.
- Tempo ID gives the page no account without a signature or an access key, so connecting is signing in (`connect` calls `signIn` when no address is known), and a remembered address is kept as it is instead of being checked against the provider's `eth_accounts`. Disconnect forgets the local state only; the user stays signed in to Tempo ID. Closing the dialog or rejecting on Tempo ID's page says "You declined the request in Tempo ID." The words "Tempo Wallet" became "Tempo ID" in the site, the docs page, OpenAPI and MCP.
- Checked on 2026-11-13 under `pnpm dev` with local D1: a fresh secp256k1 key signed the challenge and `/v1/auth` answered a 43-character token, with which `/v1/me` answered its address; a replay, another signer, an edited message and a challenge with `Origin: https://evil.example` answered 401, `Origin: https://paradigm.xyz` 200; after `/v1/auth/logout` the token answered 401, and the preflight answered `*`. In headless Chrome on the page served through an orb portal set as `SWARMS_URL`, Sign in posted the challenge (200) and opened Tempo ID's dialog for that origin, and closing it showed the decline. With a stored address, a ride of 1.5 OUSD opened `id.tempo.xyz/embed/pay` with the account, `amount=1.500000`, the memo, OUSD's address and `chain_id=4217`; without a Tempo ID session the frame offered its popup, which opened Tempo ID's login before `/pay`, and closing it showed the decline. The fixture world's "Check prompt…" frame rendered; the ride button says "Check prompt…" while Tempo ID's page is open (the user's request). `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and 215 tests pass.
- By hand on 2026-11-13, through the orb's portal with the production gateway key: the user signed in with Tempo ID, the header showed the account's email, and a $1 ride opened Tempo ID's payment page for MACH to the gateway's recipient, which offered a deposit for an empty account. Not run: a completed payment, Safari and Firefox, where the dialog falls back to a popup that the user opens with a click, and production.
- The header's Sign in opens Tempo ID's dialog at once (the report of 2026-11-23: Tempo ID was slow on click). A signed-out header loads accounts once the browser is idle after it mounts (`Wallet.warm` under `requestIdleCallback`, at most 1.5 s later), as the ride box does, so first paint does not wait on it, so `Accounts.create` preloads the dialog's hidden `/embed` frame and the click shows it. Before, the click loaded accounts' 21 chunks, created the instance, then loaded `/embed` and its 116 scripts and styles, twice, since the pinned accounts alpha's preload and the sign-in each load a frame when both start in the same tick (232 requests). Measured on 2026-11-23 in headless Chrome from Honolulu (Tempo ID answers from Cloudflare's Columbus data center there, about 150 ms away) on production: click to visible dialog 2,137 to 2,766 ms over 6 runs before, 54 to 82 ms over 4 runs with the instance warmed before the click (the deployed `warm` called at load, as this change does), with one frame and no request to id.tempo.xyz at the click. The passkey and email buttons then wait only for the challenge, ready 188 to 271 ms after the click. Cost: a signed-out view loads accounts and the hidden frame without a click. Not run: a completed sign-in.
- Signing in does not flash (the report of 2026-11-23: "a lot of flashing in Tempo ID and in Swarms during sign-in", two Brave screen recordings at 120 fps with reduced motion on). Three causes were Swarms' own. The reduced-motion reset gave every element `transition-duration: 0.01ms`, so Tempo ID's dialog, when it closed, transitioned its `left` and `visibility` for one frame and drew its card out of the top layer, at the top of the page behind the header; the reset is now `transition: none`, since no code waits for `transitionend`. After the dialog closed the header went "Sign in", then the email, then "@handle $balance", each a different width; `signIn` now reads the account and the balances before it sets the session, so the header changes once. And the dialog showed at the click without its SIWE message: Tempo ID's page drew a waiting state (another description, a busy Continue with email, the other buttons disabled, 22 px shorter) until `/v1/auth/challenge` answered 150 to 200 ms later; the Sign in button now requests the challenge when the pointer reaches it or it takes focus (`Wallet.prime`, through `Accounts.create`'s `fetch`, used once within 60 s), so the dialog has it at the click. The blank card at the click, the card that opened at 376 px and grew, and the waiting state itself are Tempo ID's and accounts' (fixes proposed there): Chromium paints no cross-origin frame that is off screen or hidden, so the preloaded frame drew and sized its request only once shown. Measured on 2026-11-23 in headless Chrome with reduced motion against production (`paradigm.xyz/swarms`, two opens and closes each, the page's dialog and `/embed`'s DOM sampled every frame): before, the dialog changed size twice per open, the embed changed state once (disabled to enabled) 230 to 290 ms after the click, and each close drew one frame of the card at the top; with this build served in place of production's assets, the first open changed size once and the embed's state did not change, and no close drew that frame. With the proposed Tempo ID and accounts changes too, the dialog showed 68 to 76 ms after the click at its final size and state and did not change. Not run: a completed sign-in in the browser, so the one header change was checked by reading `signIn` only.
- The header signs in as soon as Tempo ID returns a signature (the report of 2026-11-23: the header still said Sign in about 0.5 s after the dialog closed, while `/v1/auth` verified). The accounts `fetch` the page passes (`primed`) sees the verify POST, and `verified` in `wallet.ts` sets the address, pending, from the signature's address before sending it: the header shows the busy wallet button, with the remembered email, handle and balance when the face is the same address, and no layout class changes between Sign in and it. A verify that answers an error or throws puts the earlier state back, only while no session was set meanwhile. Its token still ends the pending phase as before. accounts moves to 1.2.20261123204450, the first release on npm after tempoxyz/accounts#1089 (5f35c8e, merged 2026-11-23 20:05 UTC; the earlier 1.2.20261123060341 predates it; its dist `Sheet.js` has `pointer-events: none` and no `left: -10000px`), which paints the preloaded frame while the dialog is closed. Checked: `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and 323 node tests pass; under `vite` on port 5190 headless Chrome opened the dialog on the click (400 px wide, 191 px high, in its popup-window state, since headless cannot embed the frame). Not run: a real sign-in through Tempo ID (it needs a passkey), so the blank card, the first-open height and the optimistic header were not seen end to end.

On production (`paradigm.xyz/swarms`, headless Chrome 158, 2026-11-23) Sign in showed `id.tempo.xyz/embed` in the dialog (400×471), whose response names `frame-ancestors https://paradigm.xyz`. On `http://localhost` sign-in still opened a popup with "Continue in the Tempo ID window": accounts' `Popup.http` sends a page on `http:` to a popup for a Tempo ID on `https:`, while `pay` and `funding.open` frame there already. That rule, not headless Chrome, is why the entry above saw the popup state. Tempo ID admits the frame (`/embed?client_id=http://localhost:5270` answers `frame-ancestors http://localhost:5270`), and in its frame on `http://localhost:5270` a CDP virtual authenticator created and asserted a passkey for `id.tempo.xyz`, and `SameSite=None; Secure` cookies, partitioned and not, were set, exactly as on `https:`. tempoxyz/accounts#1094 removes the rule; with it built and linked, the click on `localhost:5270` showed the dialog (a 390×471 bottom sheet at 390 px, the page not scrolling), `window.open` ran 0 times, and Continue with passkey ran the ceremony in the frame. accounts moves to 1.3.20261123214053, the newest on npm (its `gitHead` is accounts' main, e9dd35b); the next release after #1094 merges ends the localhost popup. Popups that stay by design: Continue with Google (Google refuses frames), Buy MACH's card checkout (a top-level page), the deposit frame for an address without a Tempo ID session in the browser (seen: `/embed/deposit` offered "Continue in a Tempo ID window to add funds"), Safari's creation of a new passkey account in a frame, and Safari and Firefox, which lack IntersectionObserver v2, for an app Tempo ID does not trust. `https://paradigm.xyz` is an origin client ID, so it is untrusted; trust needs a first-party or a verified `app_` registration through Tempo ID's Admin API, which is data, not code. Not run: a completed sign-in, payment or deposit with a real passkey or email code.
- The Cheapest preset's DeepSeek V4.1 Flash goes through Vercel (`vercel/deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017`), not Workers AI (`@cf/deepseek-ai/deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017`), at the same input and output price ($0.30 and $1.20 per million; cached $0.007 against $0.006). Workers AI limits inference requests per minute for the whole Cloudflare account, which every gateway agent shares, and answers `rate_limit_exceeded` (`3021`, `retry_after` 10). Measured on 2026-11-17 against production, 20 swarms started at once, each told to spawn 2 to 5 subagents (70 in all) that each reply with one word: on Workers AI 27 of 70 subagents completed within 10 minutes, and the other 43 stayed `running`, since every model call failed with 3021 and retried for minutes, some after their `submit_result` was already accepted. On Vercel, all 20 swarms spawned exactly the subagents asked for and 70 of 70 completed, none lost. The leftover errors on Vercel were the model's own (`spawn_agent` arguments with both or neither of `output_contract` and `output_schema`, retried by the agent), two runs that ended on the gateway's `D1 DB is overloaded` answered as 402, and one swarm whose lead got `is not a text model this gateway serves` for the Vercel id five times in four minutes. Swarms that already chose the Workers AI id keep it. `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and 226 tests pass.

## nanocode-ts build-878f938 candidate — 2026-11-17 PST

- The runtime dependency, lockfile and hand-install command use [build-878f938](https://github.com/nanocodexyz/nanocode-ts/releases/tag/build-878f938). The hand fixture uses `Connect.install` and `Connect.library`, as the real endpoint does. This candidate carries forward the local build-c9e92eb consistency patch without changing its source worktree. The verified release tarball's SHA-256 is `82a4fd83587fb35d4adda62564e64ec113a987541207a25a9ac3fc201bfa2d96`, SDK source `878f938275d47cf636242cd65425562cee0961f3`, and its main WASM's SHA-256 is `ac62b0c3c668a5f72cfd1ff235c9d1bad4df1ceff1243bb00c36e33e9b67b958`.
- `pnpm install --lockfile-only --ignore-scripts` with pnpm 12.14.0 failed `ERR_PNPM_MINIMUM_RELEASE_AGE_VIOLATION` for transitive `accounts@1.0.0-alpha.20261117214203-d38fbea` (published Nov 17 at 13:48:11.854 PST; cutoff Nov 16 at 19:47:39.758 PST). The workspace's release-age exclusions are unchanged; no exception or policy override was added. The lockfile refresh is manual and changes only the SDK URL and independently computed SHA-512 integrity: build-41b0f57, build-c9e92eb and build-878f938 have identical dependency, optional-dependency, peer and bundled-dependency declarations. This is not a successful protected install.
- `node --test --experimental-strip-types test/connect.test.ts test/hands.test.ts`: 6 passed. A scratch harness outside the repository compiled the freshly extracted verified SDK's WASM and exercised its actual `Hand.connect` and in-memory Bash workspace through a mocked WebSocket three times: each remote `exec_command` returned the expected text and closed cleanly, with fetch blocked and zero network requests.
