Type checks, the full suite, build, browser checks, gateway staging and owner-hand integration remain unrun; keep the candidate draft until those gates pass. Source candidate only; no deployment or paid traffic.

## A swarm's own locks are board claims (2026-11-20)

- Each swarm object keeps a board of tasks in its own SQLite (`Work.ts`, scope `swarm:<id>`, tables `work_tasks`, `work_keys`, `work_steers`), shaped like nanocode-ts's `Board` (post, claim with a lease, renew, release, finish, steer, heed, ready, held). Until the nanocode-ts build that exports `Board` is pinned, `Work.local` implements the same semantics over the object's own SQLite behind the `Work.Board` port, so swapping in `Board.open({ sql, scope, actor })` changes one line.
- The locks the object kept by hand are claims now, with no second path: the single-flight alarm step (`stepping`, a 5 minute lease renewed every third of it while the step runs; a second alarm that finds it held returns and leaves the alarm to the holder), the budget writes that ran one at a time on a promise chain (`budgeting`, `Work.serial`), and the soft locks in kv (`exposing`, `snapping`, whose `kv` timestamps are gone). Locks that only guard work in memory are labelled `lock: instance` and released when the object starts again, since nothing of its earlier life still runs; `snapping` is `lock: lasting` and throttles snapshots to one a minute as before, released early when a snapshot lands.
- Retries use the board's `Terms.retry` (backoffMs doubled per failed try, up to maxAttempts), steers wait to be heeded before their task may finish, and capped claims refuse with `Full`; `test/work.test.ts` covers these.
- Not moved yet: turn retry, backoff and patience, the stuck and stall timers, the steered notes, branch settlement and hand names. Hands and settlement are rows in the shared D1 database that the page and the API read across swarms, and backers' notes are D1 rows too, so moving them into one object's board changes what other readers see; they stay as they were until the board's tasks are mirrored where those readers look. The public `/v1`, MCP and page behavior is unchanged.

## Branch settlement is a board task (2026-11-20)

- Returning what a closed branch did not spend is the task `settle:<agent>:<branch>` on the swarm's board, run with `Work.once`: the swarm object claims it, writes the existing D1 settle batch (refund steers and the branch's `settled_at`), and finishes it. A finished task refuses every later claim, so a second settle for the same branch returns nothing; a settle whose D1 write failed releases its claim and the next sweep, stop or lead change settles it. The board, not `branches.settled_at`, decides whether a branch is settled.
- The Worker's stop and sweep paths reach settlement through the swarm object (`Swarm.settle`), so there is one path: `Branching.settle`. `Store.settle` is only the D1 write it makes.
- The refund steers have ids keyed by the task (`settle:<agent>:<branch>:<contributor>:<budget>`) and insert with `on conflict do nothing`, so a write that landed before its task could finish is not written twice when the task runs again. The `not exists settled_at` guard is gone.
- `settled_at` stays: the page, `/v1` and the lobby still read settled branches from D1, and the sweeps use it to pick which closed branches to settle. Branches settled before this change have `settled_at` and no board task, so the sweeps skip them as before.

## Lent hands are claims with a cap (2026-11-20)

- Each lent hand is the task `hand:<name>` labelled `hands: lent` on the swarm's hands board, a scope of its own (`swarm:<id>:hands`) because a claim's cap counts every held task in its scope, and the swarm's locks must not take hand slots. Issuing claims it with a cap of `Hands.limit` (32) and a lease of `Hands.unseenMs`; seeing the machine used renews the lease; returning it finishes the task, so a name is never issued twice.
- A stale hand is a claim whose lease lapsed: it no longer counts toward the cap. Before issuing, the swarm returns idle stale hands, and while the cap is still full, the idle hands whose leases end soonest; connected hands are never returned. This replaces the D1 `seen_at`/`created_at` arithmetic in `evictable`.
- Issuing runs in the swarm object (`Swarm.lend`, `Hands.issue`), so the count, the claim and the mint happen in one place; `/v1` hand issue only formats the answer. `Swarm.lent(swarm)` lists the hands and renews what it saw used, and replaces the listing-only `lent()`.
- D1 `hands` stays the read model for the page, `/v1` and riders (`seen_at` makes a lender a rider): its row is written when the claim lands (before minting, so a machine that connects at once finds it), deleted when minting fails (the task finishes failed), and marked returned when the task finishes. Returning writes D1 first and finishes the task second, so a failed write leaves the claim held and the next issue returns it again; the write is guarded by `returned_at is null`.
- Rows lent before this change have no task: the first issue or listing adopts each unreturned row as a claim whose lease runs from when it was last seen, or, when that is already past, as an open task: a stale hand that the next issue returns, or that claims its slot again once the swarm sees it used. Hands.test, lending.test and lent.test load Hands through the `cloudflare:workers` hook, since it now calls the gateway.

## The board opens once the swarm has its id (2026-11-20)

- `Swarm.board` reads kv `id` and opens `swarm:<id>` only then; before it, reading the board throws, so nothing a swarm claims lands under a shared `swarm:new` scope that a later id would orphan. Releasing the locks an earlier life of the object held (`Work.reclaim`) moved from the constructor into the first open, and `alarm` returns at once for an object with no id. Every caller (`heal`'s `exposing`, `alarm`'s `stepping`, `code`'s `snapping`) already runs only once the id is set.

## Turns are board tasks (2026-11-20)

- Each turn is a task `turn:<n>` (`turn:<n>.<k>` once an earlier one failed for good); the swarm keeps no copy of which task it is on and reads the live one (labelled `kind: turn`) from the board, so a wake from a pause or a restart picks up its tries where they were with `Terms.retry` `{ backoffMs: 15 s, maxAttempts: 8 }`. Claiming it is starting the turn, and the claim id is the gateway's idempotency id for the prompt, so a prompt sent again after a 5xx keeps its id while the claim lives (5 minutes while sending; a 409 counts as taken). A refused prompt, a model or agent error, or a turn that ends failed finishes the claim `failed`: the board opens the task again after its backoff (15 s doubling per try) and the swarm waits until then; after the eighth try the task fails and the swarm starts a new agent. This replaces `Turns.retry`, `backoff`, `failing`, `refusals`, `unreachable`, `reached`, `abandon`, `Lead.unreachable` and `Phase.patience`; a turn the gateway refused to pay for is a try the agent is not blamed for: it pauses for `Work.delay(paying, attempts)` (10 minutes doubling, capped at an hour as before) and never replaces the agent. A send claim the swarm still holds is renewed and the prompt sent again under the same id.
- A working turn's lease is 30 minutes and every progress event (`assistant.message`, model call, tool call…) renews it; the lease running out is what stuck means, so the turn is cancelled and finished failed. Stalled is a lease with a third or less left. `Clock.stuckMs`, `Clock.stallMs` and kv `progressed` are gone (migration 5 deletes the key); `working.progressed` is read from the lease.
- D1 stays the read model: the answer and the search index row are written after the turn task is finished, once per turn (`Work.once` keyed `d1:<task>#<n>`), so a step that is retried after a crash finishes the task once and projects once.

## Backers' notes are steers (2026-11-20)

- A note told to the agent is a steer on the board task `notes`, keyed `<note>:<weight>` so the same weight is recorded once. A note steered into a running turn is remembered for that turn and heeded when the turn ends; notes in a turn's prompt are heeded when the gateway takes it. A note whose steer is still unheeded at the next turn goes into that turn's prompt.
- The `steered` table is the projection written when a steer is heeded, and the page, `/v1` and the next prompt keep reading it as before.

## The board answers promises (2026-11-20)

- Every `Work.Board` method returns a promise, as nanocode-ts's `Board` does, and every caller awaits it (Turns, Follow, Hands, Repo, Swarm and the helpers `ensure`, `lock`, `reclaim`, `holding`, `serial`, `once`). `Work.local` still runs over the object's SQLite and answers each call on the next microtask.
- `Swarm.board` and `Swarm.slots` hand out `Work.opening(open)`: a board whose first call opens it (and, for `board`, reclaims the last life's instance locks), and whose every call waits for that open. Swapping in nanocode-ts is the one line inside each `open`: `Board.open({ sql, scope, actor })` in place of `Work.local(sql, scope, actor)`. Both now need the swarm's id; `slots` no longer falls back to `swarm:new:hands`.
- Since 2026-11-20 the board is nanocode-ts's own `Board` and `Work.local` is gone. `Host.open` (`src/worker/Host.ts`) opens `Board.open({ sql, scope, actor, module })` over `Sql.create(ctx.storage)` from `nanocode-ts/cloudflare`, with the board's wasm bundled into the Worker (`nanocode-ts/nanocode_wasm.wasm?module`). `Swarm.board` is scope `swarm:<id>` and `Swarm.slots` scope `swarm:<id>:hands`, both acting as `swarm:<id>`, so the object is the same principal in every life and the board's charter (written for the first opener) lets it manage both. `Work.ts` is now the real types (`Work.Board` is `Board.Board`) and the helpers `opening`, `ensure`, `lock`, `reclaim`, `holding`, `serial`, `once`, `until` and `delay`. Three behaviours of the real board changed callers: `heed` takes the seq of the last steer heeded (Turns passes the newest note's `seq`, and a steer's text is `body`); `renew` returns a `Verdict`, whose `Renewed.until_ms` `Work.until` reads; and a claim before a retry's backoff is refused as `Tasks`/`NotDue` without `untilMs`, so Turns falls back to the task's `dueAtMs`. Migration 6 drops `work_tasks`, `work_keys` and `work_steers`; nothing in them is carried over, since every task in them is either a lock (reopened at once by `reclaim` anyway) or a step its caller posts again. The tests run the real board over `nanocode-ts/node`'s `Sql.open` on temporary SQLite files (`test/board.ts`), and `test/host.ts` points `Swarm.ts`'s `./Host.ts` there, one file per Durable Object storage; `test/sql.ts` is gone. `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml` and `Connect.release` pin the nanocode-ts release build-b7a4da2 (2026-11-20, tgz sha256 36e13d84…7064), whose `Board` queues commands per handle, so the object's alarm, requests and timers may call one board at once; `Work.opening` only opens the board on its first call and tries a failed open again, and no longer queues calls itself.
- `working()`, which the page's synchronous reads return, stays synchronous: it reads kv `lease`, the turn lease's expiry as of the last claim or renew the swarm made. Whether the lease ran out is still read from the board. Reading events stays synchronous too: a progress event notes its time in kv `advanced`, and after each batch of events the swarm renews the lease once if that time is newer than the last renewal.

## Lent hands share the swarm's board (2026-11-20)

- nanocode-ts build-f4988c5 (tgz sha256 f4f374ed…a25f) lets a claim cap count only the held tasks with one label: `cap: { max, label: [key, value] }`. Lent hands now live on the swarm's own board (`swarm:<id>`) and claim with `Hands.cap`, `{ max: Hands.limit (32), label: ['hands', 'lent'] }`, so the swarm's locks and turns never take a hand's slot. `Swarm.slots` and the second scope `swarm:<id>:hands` are gone. `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `Connect.release` and `examples/board-swarm` pin build-f4988c5.
- No migration. D1 `hands` already holds every lent hand that has not been returned, and the first issue or listing on the new scope adopts each one, as it adopted the rows lent before hands were claims. Its lease runs from when the swarm last saw the machine, which is when the old claim was last renewed, so a hand that held its slot on the old scope holds it on the new one and no lent hand is lost. The old scope's tasks stay in the object's SQLite, unread. A returned name has a row but no task on the new board, so issuing also skips a name any row already has, which keeps a name from ever being issued twice.
- `examples/board-swarm` reads who finished a task from the board's `Task.worker` (the actor of the claim that finished or failed it, `agent:<name>`) instead of matching the workers' `finished` events. The swarm itself never kept that bookkeeping: Turns, Hands and the helpers read their own claims.

## Swarms are trees of Lean-checked tasks (2026-11-23)

The rebuild of DESIGN (design/DESIGN.md in the lead's workspace): one task tree per problem on the swarm's Board, riders put dollars and ideas on nodes, the Swarm object's own pool runs funded nodes as gateway subagents, and Lean decides what is proven. Lead rulings: one tree per problem (P vs NP first); the dispatcher-agent spawn path at launch (a gateway spawn endpoint is a follow-up); the verifier is GitHub Actions on the public proofs repository with toolchain and Mathlib pinned; free money stays the rider's (no ride-the-root after 14 days); settings runMin $0.50, runStep $5, runCap $25, 16 slots, ideaMin $1, triage at most 5%; bring-your-own-agent shows as coming soon; seed statements show "statement under review" until a person signs them off; everything real-money stays mock.

### Worker (core, tree/core)

- **Contract.** `src/shared/Tree.ts` is DESIGN 4.2's types without comments plus `status()` (4.3: node done is proven, failed or cancelled is a dead end, a run claimed/held/lapsed/stalled or any open verify is in flight, a run open/blocked/scheduled is funded, else proposed) and `headline()` (frontier = open lemmas whose needs are all proven; no denominator). Node ids in the API are bare nids; Board ids are `n:<nid>`, `r:<nid>:<k>`, `v:<nid>:<sha12>` (`:neg` for a refutation), `s:<task>`; the root is `root`, the triage node `triage`; other nids are 10 hex of `Store.noteId(parent + "\n" + title)`, the same as pnp's seed.
- **Board facts the design had to bend to.** Cancelling a task cancels its open descendants ("did not finish"), so settle tasks are top-level with a label `of=<task>` instead of children of the run or node they settle. A node task carries no Board `needs` (a claim to heed its steers or finish it would answer Waiting); its needs are an immutable `needs` label, and its runs carry the Board needs. A run is "over" for the pull rule once its settle task is done, so the next run can be posted before the pool finishes the old one. `Board.pool` has no wake hook and renews leases on its own timer, so pools poll the local board every 2 s and a run that sees no spend change for a lease is stopped as stalled. The pool cannot release a task, so a payment refusal releases the claim inside `run`, throws `LeaseLost`, and the pool's board is wrapped so `ready()` hides tasks resting until `rest:<task>`.
- **Ledger.** D1 `flows` (0033): every movement is one statement inserting both legs per contributor, guarded so the source covers every part or nothing is written, ids prefixed with the swarm and keyed by the settle task or tx hash (`on conflict do nothing`), all writes under `Work.serial('budgeting')`. Pro rata is integer micro-dollars, remainder to the largest share. Derived on read in `Flow.fold`: left, committed, pledged (pledge + move + own returns), inherited (grants in less returns to the parent), granted, spent, funders. `ideas` (0034), `swarms.archived_at` and `swarms.form` (0035).
- **Pull rule.** A node runs only with no open child (triage aside), its needs proven and no open verify. It pulls from its parent only when its weight share (recursively what the parent could pull) brings it to runMin, so a dollar on the root is not stranded in a leaf below runMin. Weight: triage weight when set, else 1 for a worker's split, a seed node, or one riders put at least ideaMin on; 0 for a bare proposal.
- **Commit then post.** `commit:<nid>:<k>` moves min(left, step) into the run bucket, then the run task is posted with that key; `Nodes.reconcile` on the object's first alarm posts a bucket with money and no task, cancels a run task with no commitment as `orphan`, settles a terminal run with money and returns money left on an ended node. Top-ups commit another step when the run has less than half a step left, up to runCap, and raise the subagent's budget.
- **Runner.** Dispatcher path: one gateway agent per swarm on DeepSeek V4.1 Flash with the frozen `Persona.dispatcher()` text; the prompt id is the claim; the Runner finds the subagent by role `n:<nid>#<k>`, sets `Gateway.budget` to the commitment, closes subagents no open run owns, and rotates the dispatcher after 200 runs once none of its runs is in flight. A takeover adopts by role. The spawn now goes through `POST /v1/agents/:a/subagents` first ("A run spawns its subagent in one call" below). Steers on the run are relayed with `Gateway.tell` (urgent from a step up) and heeded; steers left when the run ends go to the node for the next run. The dispatcher's spawn turn (`Gateway.turn` by the prompt id) is charged as spend from the run's bucket, so `/v1/health` balances to zero drift. The object's alarm runs at the soonest lease end, rest or triage time, and every minute while runs are open; its first alarm in a life releases run and verify claims an earlier life held.
- **Verify.** A proved or refuted result posts `v:<nid>:<sha12>`; the verify pool (4 slots) calls `Verify.request` with claim proof or refutation and the frozen statement and waits for `Verify.read` from `/v1/hooks/github`; it trusts a Check only with exit 0, the dispatched commit and `Check.statement` equal to the node's `stmt` label. A failed check steers the node with the log tail.
- **Triage.** Runs on at most triageShare of the root's pledges when there is something new (rider proposals or suggestions) every 30 minutes (`SWARMS_TRIAGE_MS` in tests) or when the root grew a step; actions are parsed strictly and bounded (grants at most a quarter of the root's money left per run, reject only when nobody but the author rode). Admins take the same actions by hand.
- **Archive.** Operator only (signed in as the gateway wallet, or the operator key as bearer): `Turns.sweep`, `Branching.release`, alarm stopped, `archived_at` set; rides answer 410; branch, hand and steering routes answer 410 (`gone`) on tree swarms and refuse writes on archived ones.
- **Facts kept in the object.** The delta log (`tree_deltas`: seq, at, node, event, text) is the source of Delta seq, node created/updated times and the burn series (Board tasks have no timestamps); weights are `tree_weights`; run spawn facts are `run:<task>` in kv.
- **Checks (tree/core after merging tree/pnp, tree/e2e, tree/web and tree/share, with share's frames, heads and announcements mounted).** `pnpm check`, `pnpm check:e2e` and `pnpm build` clean; `node --test --experimental-strip-types test/*.test.ts` 305 pass. `test/e2e/mock.test.ts` and `takeover.test.ts` pass; `test/e2e/tree.test.ts` under `pnpm dev` passes 12 of 13 scenarios including the tree and node pages at 1440x900 and 390x844 (no page error, console error or overflow). The one failure is the exact ledger check catching a single $0.02 metered call still in flight while an overspending node keeps running; DESIGN allows one metered call. A call that lands after a run settled is charged afterwards (`late:<run>` spend from the node, its other runs or its riders' free money), and a run's spend is read again after it is closed.

### Other streams (from their notes, design/notes/*.md)

- **pnp**: `Persona.rules/node/dispatcher/spawn/triage/output`, `Verify.request/read/result/seed/fallback`, `problems/pnp` (Lean 4.36, Mathlib fc4f59e5 by commit, 34-node seed with `reviewed: false`), the public verifier gakonst/swarms-proofs (`Check.lean` re-adds the theorem through the kernel and walks every constant for the axiom allowlist; native_decide's auxiliary axioms and sorryAx are refused; `leanchecker` per module because the whole package segfaults). Real runs 45139891659 (passed) and 45139896568 (sorryAx refused).
- **web**: TreePage, Tree, Node, Headline and grove.ts read only /v1 and patch rows from room-socket deltas; phone page never scrolls; archived swarms are read-only with their leftover money stated; Ride is the one-screen Tempo ID pay with the node's memo tag.
- **share**: swarm and node cards and frames (headline counts, one square per lemma, "Checked by Lean · <commit>", "statement under review"), `Og.frame`/`Og.head` and `Telegram.announce` for core to mount, copy without hype or tokens.
- **e2e**: mock gateway, model, chain and GitHub at the HTTP boundary, a pnpm dev harness and headless Chrome over CDP; the multi-process takeover test and the tree scenarios.

## Git writes are spend, and quiet nodes pause (2026-11-23)

- The proofs repository costs money on the gateway: git.create about $1 and each git.commit about $0.005 (`Verify.cost`). Each is a `spend` flow keyed so a retry never charges twice: `git:create` and `git:seed` on the root, `git:statement:<nid>` on the node. A charge comes from the first bucket that covers it, walking the node, its ancestors, free money and then the node with the most left; with none, the write is kept and tried again on the next alarm, so `/v1/health` never drifts by a git write. The mock gateway bills git.create and git.commit the same way, so the e2e ledger checks see them.
- Seeding is steps in kv (`proofsRepo`, `seedCommitted`, `seeded`), retried every minute while `seeding` is set, so a create that succeeded before a failed commit is no longer taken for a seeded repository. A node's statement is queued as `statement:<nid>` only when the node is first posted (a worker repeating a split re-posts the same children), committed by the alarm or right after posting, marked committed before it is charged, and cleared once charged.
- A node pauses after `quietRuns` (12, in `Nodes.Settings`) runs in a row that end without a proved or refuted claim or a split that posts a new child; a split that only repeats existing children is not a result, which ended a parent re-splitting without end once its children were proven. A fresh pledge on the node or a triage grant to it resumes it. The node shows "Paused: 12 runs without a result" (`Tree.Node.paused`).
- Seeded nodes say "Seeded by the swarm's authors" on the node panel, and tree rows show no `@seed` handle; `Summary` has no creator handle to name instead.

## A run's result folds once, as soon as its subagent ends (2026-11-23)

- Traced one run end to end under `pnpm dev` with the e2e mock gateway (create, plant, a lemma proposed with $3, the pool's claim, the dispatcher prompt, the subagent, the fold), with timestamps logged at each hop (scratch instrumentation, not committed). Create answered in 70 to 190 ms and planted the object in 15 to 60 ms; the ride's pledge to the run's claim took 14 ms when it created the pool. The subagent's end reached `Nodes.ran` 2.1 s and 9.9 s after the mock completed it: `watch` re-read the subagent listing on every stream wake, but `list` answered from its 2 s cache, so the wake the completion frame sent read the listing from before it and the loop slept again until the next frame, up to `streamUpMs` (120 s) on a quiet dispatcher. A wake that came while the loop awaited the listing was lost the same way. Now a frame carrying a subagent `status` or `completion` update drops the cached listing and counts as heard (`Runtime.heard`), and the loop reads again rather than waiting when one came while it worked. The same trace afterwards: 10 to 40 ms from the mock's end to the fold, across 13 runs.
- Every stream frame used to run `Nodes.topUp`, which takes the `budgeting` lock (a claim and a release on the board, then `Nodes.state` with a D1 read) whether or not anything changed, so the board's log grew by two entries per gateway event while a run was in flight, live deltas included: 120 log rows after two mock runs before, 211 after 13 runs now. `watch` now tops up only when the subagent's spend or the tree (`treeVersion`) changed since its last top-up.
- A run whose result was folded is finished from that fold. `watch` settles the run (moving what is left of its bucket back to the node) and folds its result (`Nodes.ran`) before the pool finishes the task, so a restart or a lost lease in between released the claim (`tick`'s first pass), and the next claim found no money in the run's bucket and finished a completed run `failed` ("No money is committed to this run."), retrying it twice more; a restart between the settle and the fold lost the result. The fold's outcome is now kept as `folded:<task>` in kv and a claim of a folded run finishes with it, and a run that was already spawned (`run:<task>`) is watched to its end even when its bucket is empty. `test/runner.test.ts` covers both, and both new tests fail on the code before.

## A posted run wakes the pool (2026-11-23)

- `Board.pool` claims new work only when it polls, every `pollMs` (2 s), or when one of its own runs ends, so a run posted while the pool was already running waited for the next poll: under `pnpm dev` with the e2e mocks and a 10 s run holding the pool, two lemmas ridden after it were claimed 1,747 ms and 1,542 ms after their runs were posted, against 5 ms for the first run, whose ride created the pool. nanocode-ts build-887f3cc (2026-11-20) gives the pool a `wake` subscription; the swarm is the only writer of its board, so its own `Runner.ensure`, which every path that posts a run or a check reaches (`tick`, `postVerify`, `verified`), now notifies the pools it already had. The same trace afterwards: 1 to 4 ms from posted to claimed. `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `examples/board-swarm` and `Connect.release` pin build-887f3cc (three commits after build-f4988c5: the wake, one read for every partition's refresh, and the nanocode 83c6938 wasm).
- With the pool woken, a run posted by a test's last step is claimed while `Runner.stop` stops the pools, and its watch started following the agent after `stop` had aborted the follow, which kept `test/api-tree.test.ts` from exiting; `stop` now aborts the follow again once the pools have stopped. `test/e2e/tree.test.ts` read the provable lemma's own pledges right after its ride and expected $5, which held only while its run waited for the pool's poll; it now also accepts the lemma already proven, whose money has gone back.

## Swarm nicknames (2026-11-23)

- A swarm can have a nickname, its page at `/swarms/<nickname>` (Georgios's spec: paradigm.xyz/swarms/<nickname>). A nickname is 3 to 40 lowercase letters, digits and hyphens that starts and ends with a letter or digit, unique across swarms (`shared/Nickname.ts`). Every top-level route is reserved (`Nickname.reserved`: a, about, admin, api, apple-touch-icon, assets, auth, board, chat, code, docs, favicon, feedback, fonts, health, ideas, index, join, kit, live, login, logout, mcp, me, n, names, new, node-modules, oembed, og, openapi, openapi-json, problems, public, results, robots, s, search, settings, sitemap, src, static, swarms, telegram, tg, v1, x), and so is any hex string of 8 or more characters, so a name never shadows a route, an asset folder or a swarm id; `test/nickname.test.ts` checks the list against `run_worker_first`, the listed pages and `public/`. Input is trimmed and lower-cased before it is checked.
- Names live in one table, `swarm_names (name primary key, swarm, current, created_at)` (migration 0036, additive, so the Worker before it keeps working), with a unique partial index on `swarm` where `current = 1`: a swarm has at most one current name, and every other row is a name it had. A swarm's nickname is derived in the row's select (`Summary.nickname`), not stored on `swarms`. A rename is one D1 batch (drop the new name's stale row, retire the current one, insert the new one), so a taken name rolls the whole batch back, answered 409. A retired name keeps sending its visitors to the swarm's current page until another swarm claims it, which deletes the old row. `POST /v1/swarms` takes `nickname` and inserts the swarm and its name in one batch, so a race on the name leaves no swarm behind.
- `PATCH /v1/swarms/:id { nickname }` (and `/api` for the page, and the MCP tool `swarm_nickname`) sets it, as one of the swarm's admins (`Team.admin`, the same check as the team edits), the operator's wallet, or with the operator's gateway key as the bearer; `null` drops it; an archived swarm answers 410. `GET /v1/names/:name` answers `{ name, nickname, swarm }`: the swarm a name names and that swarm's nickname now. The Reins sheet has the field for admins.
- Routing: `/<name>` (with `/chat`, `/code`, `/results` and `/n/<nid>`) renders the swarm server-side; `/s/<id>…` and a former name answer 301 to the current name with the rest of the path and the query (`Page.renamed`, before the page cache, whose key also carries the swarm a name resolves to, so a claimed name never serves the old swarm's cached page). Canonical, `og:url`, oEmbed, share and transfer links use the nickname; OG image keys stay `s/<id>`. The client resolves a name from the boot data, a module cache (`names.ts`) or `GET /api/names/:name`, and every swarm link inside the page is built from the route's base (`useBase`), so `/<name>/chat` stays under the name.
- A free name shows the new-swarm page in place, with the address under the heading: the idea of that id when there is one (`/pq-signatures` is Better post-quantum signatures), else the question prefilled with the name's words. Starting there sends the nickname; once the ride is sent the page reads the new swarm and swaps to it at the same address with no navigation and no spinner, and the server renders that address as the swarm from then on. A free page is never kept in the page cache. A free name with a sub-path, a reserved name and an invalid one stay 404. `/new?q=X` answers 301 to `/<slug(X)>` when that slug is a usable name (keeping its hyphens and other query parameters; the fixture world keeps `/new?q`), and idea cards link to `/<id>`.
- Checked on 2026-11-23 on `origin/main` 0ac4e5b: `pnpm check`, `pnpm build`, `pnpm check:e2e`, the unit tests, and `test/e2e/nickname.test.ts` under `pnpm dev` with the mock gateway in headless Chrome (set, rename, claim, 301s, oEmbed, the free and idea pages, and the create-at-name swap on the fixture world with a MutationObserver that saw no spinner or not-found page), with screenshots of the create-at-name, idea and nicknamed pages at 1440×900 and 390×844: no page errors, no console errors, no sideways overflow.

## A run spawns its subagent in one call (2026-11-23)

- `Runner.spawn` asks the gateway's `POST /v1/agents/:a/subagents` (https://github.com/tempoxyz/gateway/pull/719) for the run's subagent with its `budget_usd`, model, harness and output schema. Its idempotency key is `run-` and the SHA-256 of the run's task id, so a retry or a takeover after a crash gets the same subagent (200) instead of a second one. Before, every run cost a dispatcher model turn (Codex on DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, at least one model call) just to call `spawn_agent`. The Runner then polled the listing every `listMs` (2 s) to find the subagent and set its bound with `Gateway.budget` afterwards, so a quick subagent could spend before its bound was set. A subagent the route spawned carries its bound from creation: the Runner sends no `budget` call for it and records no dispatcher turn (`Spawned.turn`) to charge.
- The dispatcher path stays as the fallback, so this deploys before the gateway does. A 404 or 405 (a gateway without the route), or a 409 `no conversation to spawn from`, prompts the dispatcher as before and sets the bound after. A 409 `already starting` (the same key still spawning) is asked again every `listMs` until `spawnMs`. Any other failure goes to `refusal` as before. `SWARMS_SPAWN` is gone.
- The e2e mock gateway's spawn route keys by `key` and answers 404 under `spawnRoute: false`. `test/dispatch.ts` answers 201, repeats by key, and can answer 404, the 409 for no conversation and the 409 for a spawn still starting; `test/runner.test.ts` covers each.
- Measured on 2026-11-23:
  - Under the gateway's `vp dev --mode offline` with the route (real `AgentSession` and nanocode-ts runtime), the subagent existed 36 to 65 ms after the POST, five times, with `budget_usd` 0.25 on it. A repeat of the key answered 200 with the same id, and three concurrent calls with one key made one subagent.
  - Under `pnpm dev` with the e2e mocks, five tree swarms each way (root ridden with $10, then the time until `n:root#1` existed in the mock):
    - the route: 78, 93, 97, 139 and 146 ms;
    - the dispatcher path: 114, 121, 126, 153 and 155 ms, with the mock's dispatcher spawning at once, so this is only Swarms' own share (a turn, a listing and a budget call).
  - The dispatcher turn itself couldn't run offline. On production it is at least one DeepSeek V4.1 Flash call (0.57 to 1.68 s each, about 1.2 s for a Codex-sized prompt, from the gateway's own measurements), plus the session's wake, the tool call and up to one 2 s listing interval: about 1.5 to 4 s before a run starts (an estimate, not measured on production).
- Checked with `pnpm check`, `pnpm build`, `pnpm check:e2e` and `node --test --experimental-strip-types test/*.test.ts` (313 pass) on Node 24.
- A running task shows what it is doing (2026-11-23, Georgios: "I want it to show what it's doing"). A run carries an optional doing ({ text, at }, shared/Tree.ts), its worker's latest step in one line; the tree row shows it beside the title with a pulsing dot (under the title on a phone), and the node panel under its status. Nothing renders while the run is checking or when the Worker sends no doing. The fixture world gives every in-flight run one. Checked under pnpm dev on the fixture world at 1440x900 and 390x844: the line truncates on one row on a desktop and wraps under the title on a phone, with no sideways overflow.
- The Worker fills `run.doing` (2026-11-23). `Runner.follow` now splits the orchestrator's event stream into frames, and for a subagent event of an in-flight run (its `agent:sub` is registered as soon as the spawn returns) keeps one line: the newest heading of its reasoning summary, else the summary's last line (`Trail.gist`), or its tool call as the transcript names it (`Trail.named`), redacted and clipped to 120 characters, with the event's time. It lives in memory (`Swarm.treeDoing`), not in storage: `Nodes.view` lays it over the cached view, so the `/v1` tree and node reads carry it without bumping the tree's version, and a restart only loses it until the next step. A change goes out as a node delta with event `doing` on `/live` and the room socket, at most one a run every `doingMs` (2 s): the first at once, the rest held by one timer that sends the newest. A doing delta carries the current seq but no SSE id and no row in `tree_deltas`, so it never moves a reader's cursor or the node's updates; `Grove.patch` takes one at its own seq without advancing, and the node panel keeps it out of the update list. When the run's watch ends, its node ends or the runner lets go, the line is dropped and one more doing delta clears it.
  - Measured on 2026-11-23 under the e2e mocks (`pnpm dev`, mock gateway): a run that thought 30 times in 6 s pushed 4 doing deltas on `/live`, 1992 to 2006 ms apart (Thought 0, 9, 19, 29), each 18 to 209 ms after the thought, then the clearing delta 1.1 s after the last one; the room socket carried the same five. `test/e2e/tree.test.ts` (a running task shows what it is doing) checks the first thought on `/live`, the newest one on the node and tree reads, at most two pushes for ten thoughts within a second with the newest held, and the clear once the run ends.

## Paradigm's favicon (2026-11-23)

- The site's icons are paradigm.xyz's own files, byte for byte (`favicon.svg`, `favicon.ico`, `favicon-96x96.png`, `apple-touch-icon.png`), and `index.html` links the same four as paradigm.xyz does. The hrefs stay bare like the font preloads: the build's `renderBuiltUrl` prefixes public assets with `/swarms`, and the dev server serves them under it.
- `favicon-96x96` joins the reserved nicknames, since `public/` now answers it at the top level of the site.

## Riders never choose a model (2026-11-23)

- Starting a swarm or proposing a node offers no model, harness or template (Georgios: "We are never allowing the user to choose models. We always just say $1 minimum buy-in and otherwise it's a slider and they can go up as high as they want, like we had."). `/new` lost its "Runs on" list and `?template=`, and its web request carries no `template`, so the Worker's default applies; the node proposal form never had a choice. The amount is the slider from `bd01a76`, at least $1 on `/new` too (it was the template's step cost), and its range grows: the top mark doubles while the amount is at or above it, so there is no hard top, and typing an amount works as before. Dollars only. Checked on the fixture world under `vite` at 1440×900 and 390×844 (no page scroll, slider to $1,000 after dragging to the end of $500), `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and the node tests.
- A tree swarm runs any mix of OpenAI, Anthropic and Google agents, and riders never choose them (2026-11-23, Georgios: "when you create a board line the thing is able to spawn any combination of openai, anthropic or google agents. We never allow the user to choose models. Always $1 minimum buy-in, otherwise a slider as high as they want. Default to Fable 5.2 as the orchestrator agent, but the spawn_agent tool should VIA THE BOARD claim tasks and spawn agents with whatever the user prompts / steers with."). `POST /v1/swarms` with form tree ignores template, harness, model and subagents (an agent swarm still checks them) and sets `Setup.board`: Claude Code on Claude Fable 5.2 leads as the orchestrator, and its subagents may use every OpenAI, Anthropic and Google model `GET /v1/config` lists at creation, on Claude Code or Codex. The setup and the orchestrator's persona (`Persona.orchestrator`) are frozen at creation, so this reaches tree swarms created after it; earlier ones keep their dispatcher and worker model. The $1 minimum is unchanged (`ideaMin` and the web's slider).
- Each run claimed from the board asks the orchestrator for its harness and model before it spawns: a side question (`POST /v1/agents/:a/side_question`, outside its conversation, so picks add no context) with the node's title and words, the riders' unheeded notes, the run's budget and the offered models with what a call of each costs (`Persona.pick`). The answer is one JSON object; a model off the list falls back to Fable 5.2, and a harness the gateway doesn't run to Claude Code for an Anthropic model and Codex for the rest (`Runner.picked`). A fresh orchestrator has no conversation, and the gateway answers a side question then with 409, so the first pick is a turn, which also opens the conversation the spawn route needs; the Runner follows the orchestrator's stream during it, so the turn's end wakes it instead of the next 2 s listing. The pick is kept per run (`picked:<run>`), so a takeover spawns on the same model. The run is spawned through the spawn route with that harness and model and a bound of its commitment less what the pick cost, and the pick's cost is settled as part of the run's spend. The started delta names the model (Run 1 started on Gemini 3.8 Flash) and `run.model` carries it. A worker is told the orchestrator picked its model and which models its own subagents may use (`Setup.told` for board).
  - Measured on 2026-11-23 under `pnpm dev` with the e2e mocks, three fresh tree swarms (a scratch harness outside the repository): ride of the root to its run's subagent existing, 274, 279 and 290 ms, the first pick being a turn (2.1 to 2.3 s before the Runner followed the stream during it); a proposed lemma whose words ask for mock:model=vercel/openai/gpt-6-astra, proposal to subagent 256 to 259 ms through a side question, spawned on gpt-6-astra under Codex; the orchestrator on Fable 5.2 under Claude Code each time. On production a pick is one Fable 5.2 call of about 1.5k tokens in and 50 out, about $0.012 at $8 and $40 a million (an estimate, not measured), and its latency was not measured.
  - Checked with `test/runner.test.ts` (the orchestrator's setup, a rider's note in the pick, the spawn's model, harness and bound, `run.model`, the started delta and the pick's cost in the node's spend; the first pick as a turn with an answer off the list running on Fable 5.2; `picked`), `test/api-tree.test.ts` (a tree swarm's model fields are ignored, an agent swarm's still refused), `test/setup.test.ts` and `test/e2e/tree.test.ts` (the orchestrator and its offer, a lemma that asks for Gemini runs on it under Codex, every run on a listed model, the ledger balanced; a run's bound is now its commitment less its pick, and a node's spend is checked against what was committed to its runs).
- The board draws a swarm's tree as branches (2026-11-23, Georgios: "a centipede / tree of ideas splitting out", "sort board items hierarchically but also by funding inside each hierarchy", "a profile pic pill pattern with +9", "seamlessly fill with See More, showing the things that are the most important at each time by funding"). Branches.tsx replaces the outline rows: each level hangs off its parent's spine, siblings sort by money (subtree pledged, then spent), the top ones show and the rest fold behind "See N more" with their total, each item has a face pile of its riders (three faces, then +N), its money, a status mark and, while running, what it is doing and how long ago; every level ends in "Propose … $1+". On a phone the money and faces move under the title and the page itself never scrolls. Checked under pnpm dev on the fixture world at 1440x900 and as a 390x844 touch phone: no page scroll, no sideways overflow, no tap target under 24 px.

## A questions page (2026-11-23)

- `/faq` answers the questions a new rider asks, in three bands (Riding, Money, The work), each answer a line or two in the site's words: what a swarm and riding are, $1 minimum and no top, steering by adding an item or more money rather than by interrupting, the chat room, where the money goes (the gateway, as the swarm's credit; Swarms holds none), adding funds through Tempo ID (buying MACH with a card is the one place a token is named), money back only inside the swarm for now, no tokens and no returns, the orchestrator picking OpenAI, Anthropic or Google models per task, Lean against the frozen statement, and dead ends kept. Each claim was checked against README.md, the sections above (One way in, Riders pay only the gateway, Sign-in and rides through Tempo ID, Riders never choose a model) and the code (`Setup.board`, `Persona.pick`, `Operations.say` refusing steering notes on tree swarms). It renders on the server like `/about` (`Page.listed`, `run_worker_first`, `preload` in `server.tsx`), shares the about card with its own title and description, is a reserved nickname, and is linked from the header (desktop nav and the phone menu). The site has no footer, so it has no footer link.
- Checked under `pnpm dev` with the e2e mocks: `/swarms/faq` answered 200 with its title and text in the server's HTML; at 1440×900 and 390×844 the page did not scroll (document 844 px high at 844) and only `main` did, with no sideways overflow (scroll width 390).

## The account pill opens a menu (2026-11-23)

- The signed-in header pill (@handle and balance) is a button that opens a small menu on hover or tap: Add funds, Your profile, Sign out. It no longer links to the block explorer (Georgios: "Clicking the pill must never navigate to the block explorer"). Add funds opens Tempo ID's deposit page in place with the accounts SDK's `funding.open` for the signed-in address (`Wallet.fund`), in its frame dialog; on Tempo mainnet the hint is MACH with $25, which opens Buy MACH directly, and elsewhere the page lists every method. Hovering the pill preloads that frame (`funding.preload`), and the SDK instance is kept once loaded so the call opens within the click. The balance is read again when the page returns. In the fixture world Add funds adds $25 after a pause. Checked on the fixture world (`/s/p-vs-np?fixture&scene=in`) at 1440×900: the menu opens under the pill, no console errors.

## A ride ends in a cheer with share, invite and ride another (2026-11-23)

- Once a ride or a new board item is counted (`Fuel` sees `counted`), one sheet over the page says "You're riding · $5" with the swarm's title and its hype numbers (riders and dollars in, read again after the ride), and three actions: Share on X (an intent with the title, the amount, the riders and dollars, and the swarm's X card at `/x/s/<id>` as the link), Invite friends (copies the swarm's page address), and, on a tree swarm, Ride another part (scrolls the board into view, or opens the swarm's board when a phone shows a node). It lives at the app's root (`Share.Cheered`), so it survives the navigation a proposal makes to its new node; the node's Ride sheet closes under it. Esc, × or the backdrop closes it.
- A swarm page has a Share item in the header beside the account pill: on a touch phone with the system share sheet it opens that; otherwise a small menu with Share on X and Copy link.
- A ride refused for too little money offers Add funds on its alert, which opens the same deposit page as the account menu and clears the alert after.
- Checked on the fixture world under `vite` (`/s/p-vs-np?fixture&scene=in`): ride the whole swarm at 1440×900 shows the sheet centered; at 390×844 (touch) from `/n/root?ride` the sheet sits at the bottom, Ride another part opens the board, the page height stays 844 and its width 390; the intent's text is "I'm riding Does P = NP? with $5. 5 riders, $45.50 in." and its url `/swarms/x/s/p-vs-np`. No page errors.

## A swarm explains itself (2026-11-23)

- Every tree swarm gets an `about`: one plain paragraph on what it is trying to do, why it would matter and how anyone would know, for its page's About tab. `/v1/swarms/:id` (`Overview`, and `Detail` for the page and its server render) carries it, `null` until it is written; OpenAPI describes it. It is written once from the swarm's goal by a side question to its orchestrator (`About.ensure`, `Persona.about`), the same call a run's model pick uses, so it is charged to the swarm's own credit and never to the operator key. Its cost is a `spend` flow keyed `about` from the root (`Nodes.charge`, as git writes are), kept owed in the object's kv `aboutOwed` until it is written, so `/v1/health` does not drift.
- It is written in the background, never in a request: every read of the swarm (`Swarm.view`) hands `About.ensure` to `waitUntil`, which returns at once when the text is kept, and otherwise asks at most once in ten minutes, only for a tree swarm that is not archived, has an orchestrator, and holds at least $0.25 left in all. That covers new swarms (the first read after their orchestrator's first pick opened its conversation; a fresh orchestrator answers side questions 409) and existing ones (their next read) alike. An answer that is empty or looks like JSON is not kept, and every answer is redacted and cut at 700 characters. Swarms of the earlier single-agent form get none and stay `null`. Kept in the object's kv (`about`), so no migration.
- The e2e mock gateway answers the question with a short paragraph built from the goal (`Model.about`), and the fixture world's p-vs-np swarm carries one.
- Checked: `test/about.test.ts` (no question without money, a 409 retried only after ten minutes, the text kept and whitespace folded, the ledger's spend equal to the answer's cost, no second question once kept; `About.clean`), `pnpm check`, `pnpm check:e2e`, `pnpm build` and the node tests. Under `pnpm dev` with the e2e mocks, a new tree swarm ridden with $20 read `about: null` on its first read and the mock's paragraph about 3 s later, the server-rendered page carried it in its boot data, and `/v1/health` read drift 0.000000 with the gateway's spent equal to the ledger's (7.087). Not run: a real orchestrator's answer and its cost on production (a pick, the same call, is estimated at about $0.012 on Fable 5.2).

## A rider's profile read (2026-11-23)

- `GET /v1/riders/:who` (and `/api/riders/:who` for the page, `Operations.rider`) answers a rider's profile by username (with or without @, any case) or 0x address: `rider` (their account), `ridden` (every dollar they put in), `swarms` (each swarm they rode, started or suggested into, with the dollars they put in, its riders and dollars in, sorted by their dollars) and `items` (the board items they created or rode, with their pledged dollars, status and title, sorted by dollars). The dollars come from D1 in one batch (`Store.ridden`: `contributions` by contributor, `flows` pledges by node), the titles and creators from each tree swarm's own tree read (depth 64, at most 2.5 s each). It is read only and stores nothing. An unknown name answers 404; an address with nothing answers an empty profile. `test/riders.test.ts` covers a rider's lemma ride and proposal, another rider's root ride, a name lookup with @ and capitals, an unknown name and an empty address.
- `u` joins the reserved nicknames, and `/u/*` and `/me` join `run_worker_first`, for the profile pages that read it.

## A swarm's page lands in its chat (2026-11-23)

- Georgios: "make all of this swarms thing more social ... you see the high level metrics, you get hooked, you click, you fund it ... You can jam in the chatroom with other backers, but you don't steer the swarm live: either you create a new board item, or you further fund an existing idea ... For each swarm you can see the top backers", and "the whole UI must be focused around what a hype-chaser needs to read: how many people contributed, how much $."
- Both forms of swarm page (TreePage for tree swarms, Swarm for agent swarms) open with one results strip (`Strip.tsx`): the title and Ride it, then riders and dollars raised in 60 px figures (`contributors` and `balance` from `/v1`), then smaller facts (proven and in flight for a tree, standing and spent for an agent swarm), the latest finding in one line, and the top three riders ranked by what they put in (summed `contributions`, named from the tree's funders and the room's members), each linking to `/u/<handle or address>`. Under it one switcher: Chat, Board, Results, Code, About. Chat is the default panel.
- Routes: `/<swarm>` is the chat, `/<swarm>/board`, `/results`, `/code` and `/about` the other tabs, and `/<swarm>/n/<nid>` the board with that item open; `/chat` still answers as the chat. `Nickname.parse`, `Page.rendered`, `Share.page`, `Replica.readable` and the client router know the two new tails, and each renders on the server. Switching tabs replaces the address in place and keeps every visited panel mounted. The page is an app shell on every width: strip and switcher fixed, only the panel scrolls.
- Board: each item with children has its own fold control (a button with `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`), so the title selects and the button folds. What is unfolded is kept in the address as `?open=<ids>` (`?open=` folds all), carried when an item is picked. "See N more" is unchanged. The way to steer is "Add a sub-item $1+" at the end of every level and at the top of the item panel; the ride sheet no longer asks for "a note for its worker", the item panel no longer lists notes to its worker, and a tree swarm's chat has no @AI steering (`Room` `steer={false}`). Agent swarms keep their notes, their only way to steer.
- About renders `swarm.about` when `/v1` serves one, else the goal with the root item's words when they add to it, and when it started, raised, spent and left.
- The agent swarm page lost its cover tile, its pitch block, the sticky phone bar and the separate phone chat page; their CSS went with them.
- Checked on 2026-11-23 under `vite` on the fixture world (`/s/p-vs-np?fixture`, `/s/bench-coders?fixture`) at 1440×900 and 390×844: switching all five tabs kept the switcher at the same top (224 px) with the document exactly the viewport (900 and 844 px high, no sideways overflow); clicking a fold control flipped `aria-expanded`, showed its 5 children and wrote `?open=`, `?open=` alone rendered every item folded, and picking an item kept `?open`. `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and the node tests pass.
- A tree swarm's page at its nickname rendered only the loading orbit on the server: `Page.gather` put the name lookup (`name:<nickname>`) in the boot data and then `grove` started a fresh data object, so the client found no name until it fetched one. `grove` now adds to the same data; `/pequalsnp`, `/pequalsnp/board` and `/pequalsnp/about` now arrive with the strip, the switcher and the panel in the HTML. On a phone the strip keeps only riders, raised and the faces, so nothing clips at 390 px.
