- `/u/<username or 0x address>` is a rider's page, rendered on the server from `GET /v1/riders/:who` (seeded as `rider:<who>`): their face (`Avatar`), @name and short address, three numbers (dollars ridden, swarms, ideas they created), their swarms as rows with their own dollars on the right and the swarm's riders and dollars in under the title ("started it" when they did), then the board items they created or rode, each with its status mark, swarm and their dollars. Every row is the link to its swarm or node. An unknown name says "No rider by this name." `/me` answers 302 to your own page on the server when the face cookie names you, and in the page once Tempo ID has; signed out it offers Sign in. The account menu's Your profile goes there.
- Checked on the fixture world under `vite` at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone (`/u/ada?fixture&scene=in`, `/me?fixture&scene=in`, `/u/nobodyhere?fixture`): the document is 844 px tall at 844 (only `main` scrolls) and 390 wide, titles wrap under 390 px, no page errors.
- A node's riders and its "Idea by" link to each rider's page (`/u/<address>`), and the cheer's Ride another part opens the swarm's Board tab (`<swarm>/board`) when no board is on screen. Checked on the fixture world under `vite` at 1440×900: a ride from the swarm page's Ride it shows the centered sheet, Ride another part lands on `/s/p-vs-np/board`, and a rider row on `/n/root` opens their profile.

## The home is the site's board (2026-11-23)

- The home page leads with one line of what this is, the site's three numbers (riders, dollars raised, swarms working now) and then the site board: every swarm with at least $1 in it as a board item, in the same language as a swarm's own board (`Branches.tsx`): a face pile of its top riders with +N, its money, and a live line (what its runs are doing, or its proven and in-flight counts, with how long ago), sorted by money with open swarms before archived ones, six shown and the rest behind "See N more" with their total, ending in "Start a swarm $1+" (Georgios: "Homepage needs taste, right now you land on this and you see all these circles and you're like wtf", "board == the whole website, each individual swarm is inside a sub-board"). The wall of spirals is gone; each item keeps one 40 px spiral as its mark, sized by money, green for proven tasks, blinking for runs in flight. The intro, How it works and creed bands left the home (the header still links Swarm riders).
- `GET /v1/swarms` gains `riders` (distinct riders across visible swarms), `funders` (each listed swarm's top three riders by what they put in, with handles) and `heads` (each open tree swarm's tree headline, read with an 800 ms bound so a slow tree drops out instead of failing the list). A tree swarm's summary says `funding` and $0 spent while its tree has runs in flight, so the home reads tree swarms from their headline. All three are cached for the list's 3 s and served in the page's boot data, so the board, faces and numbers render on the server; the run's doing line is the only part read after load (one `/tree` read per working tree swarm, again when the lobby reports the swarm changed) and it replaces text in a line whose space is already there.
- `Status.test` also hides "L3 reliability …", "e2e test …", "… setup check" and "asdasd" titles. On production's list on 2026-11-23 (100 rows), the board keeps 12: $286.23 raised, 1 working, Megakernel ($94.52) first; the 88 dropped were test titles or under $1 (most are $0.08 to $0.10 "L3 reliability" swarms).
- Checked under `pnpm dev` on the fixture world (`?fixture`, `?fixture&scene=many`) and on a copy of Georgios's local state (the seeded P = NP tree swarm: 12 riders, $283, 69 proven and 5 in flight), at 1440×900 and 390×844: the server HTML carries the numbers and items, the phone page never scrolls (document 844 of 844, the board panel scrolls), no sideways overflow, no tap target under 24 px, "See 8 more" shows all 14 on `scene=many`. `test/store.test.ts` covers the riders count and the top three. `pnpm check`, the unit tests.
- All swarms (`/ideas`) is the same board without the fold: every swarm with $1 or more as a board item, then the ideas no one has started as items with their one-line pitch, then "Start a swarm $1+", with the All, Working now, Needs money and No riders yet filters as before. The card grid and its loading ghosts left the page (the start page keeps its idea cards). Checked under `pnpm dev` on Georgios's local state (P = NP and 17 ideas) at 1440×900 and on `?fixture&scene=many` at 390×844: the document stays 844 px high and only the list scrolls, no sideways overflow.

## The chat stays beside the board (2026-11-23)

- Georgios, on the agent swarm "Megakernel for Muse Spark Open's decoder layer (Frontier Mix)": "maybe the chat is on the right always on as a split and the left side is one of the other things? And when you collapse to single column view on mobile you default to chat. Also board here looks like shit."
- From 900 px up, both swarm pages (`Swarm.tsx`, `TreePage.tsx`) are a grid under the results strip: the left column is the switcher and the chosen tab (Board, Results, Code, About), the right column (`clamp(340px, 30vw, 480px)`) is the riders' chat, always shown, with its own scroll and its composer at the bottom. The switcher has no Chat tab there. `/<swarm>` (the chat route) shows Board on the left with Board underlined, so the same address works on both widths. Under 900 px the page is one column as before and `/<swarm>` opens on Chat. All of it is CSS on markup the server already renders (`data-desk` on the board panel when the route is the chat, `[hidden]` on the chat panel overridden from 900 px up, `data-tab` and `data-view` on the switcher), so nothing waits for a media query in script. The Room counts unread messages as read whenever it is on screen (`visible` is the chat route or not a phone).
- The agent swarm's Board lost its inline Ride box (it took a 380 px column); Ride it in the strip, Ride it to chat and every steer open the same Fund card as a sheet, and `?fund` or a prefilled note opens that sheet on load. `.idea-now` and `.idea-main` went with it.
- "Who is on what" is its own board (`Crew.tsx`, `shared/Crew.ts`), drawn like the tree's branches: a status mark (working pulses green, done is filled, failed is struck, stopped and out of budget are hollow), the title without a leading "autoresearch branch:", the branch's metric (`best_speedup` as `1.40×`, any other `best_*` number by name) and riders' faces and what it spent on the right, and one line under it. That line reads a JSON result's `summary` (or `result`, `outcome`, `finding`, `conclusion`, `answer`) and takes its first sentence that names no commit hash and no path ("Best commit 55a7f78…, copied to /w/attention4_best." is skipped for "Untouched copy measured 1.3586x; best measured 1.3697x"), else its first sentence; it used to print the JSON's first line, a lone `{`. Without JSON it is the result's first line, a failed branch's error, or a working branch's task. Working branches come first, then by spend; six show, then See N more with what the rest spent. A row with more to say opens in place to the whole summary and the branch's blockers. Production's Megakernel swarm (f3a42cf87339) has 18 top-level branches, 17 with a JSON result carrying `summary` and `best_speedup`, one failed with only an error; the fixture world has three branches shaped like them.
- Traces sit behind a Traces toggle under the board (with Live, Stalled or Idle), and their agent chips use the same cleaned titles. The board and the toggle render once the updates, artifacts and branches have loaded, so the toggle no longer jumps down as they arrive (it shifted the layout by 0.0097 to 0.0115 per load on `/s/mev-map?fixture`).
- The lead's idle replies are not findings (`shared/Chatter.ts`): a reply whose opening sentence says it is not starting or has started nothing, made no tool calls, can't make progress or take the next step, didn't check the budget, or is waiting. The strip's latest line and Latest from the lead skip them and show the latest real update, else the answer if it is not idle, else nothing (the strip used to fall back to the goal). In the chat a run of lead updates with the same gist (numbers ignored, every idle reply alike) folds into one row with a count: idle runs as one quiet line, "✦ I'm not starting anything this turn ×4 22:28". On production's Megakernel swarm 47 of its 50 updates are such replies (`/v1/swarms/f3a42cf87339/updates`, 2026-11-23); the latest real one is "I created the swarm's git repository and spent the new $5 on one more verify-attention branch, attention7."
- In the narrower left column the tree's buds put their money and faces under the title once `.grove-tree` is under 640 px wide (a container query), and the item panel beside it takes `clamp(280px, 38%, 420px)`. From 900 to 1279 px the chat is 320 px, the item panel `minmax(240px, 40%)`, the strip's big figures 44 px, and the top riders show as faces and amounts on the strip's line: with their names they wrapped to a second line once the tree's funders named them, which shifted the page by 0.39 at 1000×760.
- An opened board row shows the branch's whole summary in place of its one line, then its blockers; the panels keep a stable scrollbar gutter so the spend column is never under the scrollbar.
- Checked on 2026-11-23 under `vite` on the fixture world in headless Chrome at 1440×900 and as a touch iPhone 14 (390×844): on desktop `/s/mev-map` and `/s/p-vs-np` the document is exactly the viewport (900 high, 1440 wide), the board column is 900 px and the chat 432 px with its composer at the bottom, the switcher shows Board, Results, Code and About with Board underlined, and layout shift is 0.0003 (the header's sign-in, as before); at 1100×800 the tree and the item panel fit beside the chat. On the phone `/s/mev-map` opens on Chat, `/board` and `/s/p-vs-np/board` show the board, the document is 844×390 (no page scroll, no sideways overflow) and layout shift is 0. Not checked on production data in a browser: the board's parsing was checked against production's branch list with the unit tests.

## Board actions share one payment lifecycle and open as overlays (2026-11-23)

- Riding the swarm, riding an item and adding a sub-item run through one state machine (`pay.ts`: `next` moves an act through idle, signing, creating, paying, counting, owed, counted, failed and refused), one flow per slot (`slot` for a ride, `proposing` for a new item under a parent), shared by the board, the item panel and the strip, so a payment started in one place shows its state in the others. A payment that was sent but not counted is kept (`record`) and retried after 2, 5, 15 and 30 s; the board shows it as a pending row under its parent with Try again, and the item panel as an alert, so money that left the wallet is never shown as lost. The success sheet names the item that was ridden.
- Georgios: "when you're funding a swarm, I don't want you to hide what's behind; I just want it to overlay". Every ride and add state opens in `Overlay.tsx`: under 900 px a bottom sheet over a light dim (no blur), from 900 px up a 380 px popover anchored under (or above) the button that opened it, kept on screen as the page scrolls or resizes, with no dim. Escape, × and a tap outside close it. The board's "Add a sub-item" and the item panel's button open the same composer (`Compose.tsx`): an "Add under <parent>" heading, a focused title field ("Its title"), an optional detail and the $1+ amount; it has no Cancel button, since the overlay closes it. Suggest and Move stay inline disclosures in the item panel.
- Checked on 2026-11-23 under `vite` on `/s/p-vs-np?fixture` in headless Chrome at 1440×900 and 390×844: the strip's Ride it and a board row's Ride it open the sheet on the phone and the anchored popover on desktop with the board visible behind; "Add a sub-item" opens the composer anchored to its row on desktop and as a sheet on the phone, with the title field focused; Escape closes it. `pnpm check`, the unit tests and `pnpm build` pass.

## swarms.new serves the site at its root (2026-11-24)

Georgios, 2026-11-24: swarms.new should serve the site itself at its root (swarms.new/pequalsnp, swarms.new/faq), not redirect to paradigm.xyz/swarms, which keeps working as it does.

- The base follows the host, decided once in `shared/Site.ts`: `Site.prefix(url)` is `''` on `swarms.new` and `/swarms` on every other host (paradigm.xyz's proxy reaches the Worker on `swarms.tempo-dev.workers.dev`, localhost, tunnels). `under`, `strip` and `at` take a base and default to `Site.root()`, the page's own: the server render's `Boot.base` (through the render's `AsyncLocalStorage`), `window.__SWARMS_BOOT__.base` in the browser, else the page's own host. The Worker passes the request's base where it builds a path itself (renames, `/me`, `/a/:id/:slug`, `Boot.socket`, oEmbed's page key). `Site.url` is the request's origin on swarms.new, so canonical, Open Graph, oEmbed, player and share links, the docs' addresses, the OpenAPI server, MCP links, transfer links and the sign-in message's origin name swarms.new there and paradigm.xyz/swarms on paradigm.xyz. `SWARMS_URL` stays `https://paradigm.xyz/swarms`, the address for what has no request (the agents' room link, Telegram, proof URLs, feedback, the data cache's keys); `Site.home` no longer adds the base to a swarms.new `SWARMS_URL`, so making swarms.new canonical everywhere is that one variable.
- `Site.place` gives the Worker a request's path without its base and whether the page is stray: on swarms.new a page under `/swarms` answers 301 to the same path at the root, and elsewhere a page outside `/swarms` answers 301 under it, as before. `www.swarms.new` answers 301 to `https://swarms.new` with the path and query (`Site.www`).
- Built assets keep their `/swarms/assets/…` names (`renderBuiltUrl`, the modulepreload table, the font preload header and `/swarms/kit/swarm.css` in artifacts): every host answers a non-page path under `/swarms` by stripping it, and `/assets/*` is the assets binding's own path, so one build serves both hosts. The preload table's patterns take the base as optional. A cover saved with `/swarms` in its URL is stripped and re-based on the page.
- The page cache (`Edge.page`) keys a page by its origin, so swarms.new and the Worker's host never share a drawn page; `test/edge.test.ts` checks it. `test/hosts.test.ts` runs the whole Worker for both hosts: `swarms.new/faq` renders with `/faq` links, `"base":""`, `wss://swarms.new` and a swarms.new canonical; the proxied `/swarms/faq` keeps `/swarms` links and the paradigm.xyz canonical; the redirects above; and `/swarms/assets/a.js` and `/assets/a.js` reach the same asset on both.
- `wrangler.jsonc` has no `routes` yet: on 2026-11-24 swarms.new's nameservers were still Squarespace's (`nse1-4.squarespacedns.com`, and `https://swarms.new` answered 302 to paradigm.xyz from Squarespace), so the zone is not active on Cloudflare and a custom-domain deploy would fail. Once it is, add `"routes": [{ "pattern": "swarms.new", "custom_domain": true }, { "pattern": "www.swarms.new", "custom_domain": true }]`.
- Checked on 2026-11-24 under `vite` (port 5300) and `vite preview` of the build (port 5301) with `curl -H 'Host: …'`: on `localhost` `/` and `/faq` answered 301 under `/swarms` and `/swarms/faq` 200 with `/swarms/…` links and `"base":"/swarms"`; with `Host: swarms.new` `/` and `/faq` answered 200 with root links, `"base":""` and a swarms.new canonical, `/swarms/faq` 301 to `/faq`, the built entry chunk 200 at both `/swarms/assets/…` and `/assets/…`, `/openapi.json` named `swarms.new` as its server and the docs `swarms.new/mcp`; `www.swarms.new` answered 301 to swarms.new. In headless Chrome on the preview served as `https://swarms.new` (a local TLS proxy; `.new` is HSTS-preloaded) the home hydrated with root links, a click on About went to `https://swarms.new/about` in place, and Paradigm Serif loaded. Not run: production, sign-in with Tempo ID on swarms.new (its message names `https://swarms.new`, an untrusted origin client like paradigm.xyz) and a payment there.

## The site is one board of channels (2026-11-24)

Georgios: "the whole site is one big board, where each channel is a swarm, but swarms can also merge / read / write to other swarms if perms allow", "Channel = swarm, thread = board item", "You land on the homepage of a chatroom which has a description/topic and that's the #general chat, then you can swipe left to open the board, which shows the channels you can join, or create your own. The homepage is around CTAing." Justin Wang: "the viewer looks like you are looking through the Agent Swarm Discord ... All the natural UIs for chat apps like Discord and Slack apply, ported over but Paradigm branded."

- The shell (`Shell.tsx`) wraps `/`, every swarm page, `/ideas` and `/new`: a left rail and the page. The rail is the site's board: `# general` on top, then every channel (a swarm with $1 or more, test titles hidden, the home board's `board`) with its dollars and riders or "working now", working channels first, then by money, archived last and dimmed; "+ Start a channel $1+" (`/new`), "Browse every channel and idea" (`/ideas`), and the links the phone menu held (Swarm riders, FAQ, API, the essay). The rail is server-rendered from the same `swarms` listing the home used, which swarm pages now also put in their boot data (read in parallel with the page, 1.5 s bound), so it does not shift on load. A channel visited before shows a dot and a darker name when its `updatedAt` moved since (`swarms_seen` in localStorage), drawn after hydration in space the row already has.
- A channel's page is its chat. The header is the channel: `# title`, riders and dollars raised (30 px), the tree's proven and in flight or the agent swarm's standing and spent, Ride it, and the goal as its topic on one line with the top riders' faces. Tabs: Chat, Threads (the tree swarm's board, with the count of top-level items; Board on an agent swarm), Results, Code, About. Chat is a tab on every width now, the center of the page, instead of the 340 to 480 px column beside the board (`The chat stays beside the board` above). Messages read like Slack's: avatar and name on the first of a run, every message left-aligned, no bubbles; one's own messages too.
- Threads are board items. `/<swarm>/t/<item>` opens the item in a thread panel: from 900 px up a column of `clamp(340px, 31vw, 460px)` on the right of the channel, the height of the page, with the threads list still in the center; on a phone a full-screen sheet. The panel is the item panel as before (`NodePanel`: status, what it is doing, money, Ride it and "Add a sub-item $1+" through the shared overlay and payment state machine, its words, Lean statement, people, runs, updates, suggestions), under a header with Thread and the channel's name and a close. The rail lists the open channel's five richest top-level items under it as threads, with "All threads". `/n/<item>` still renders the same page and the page replaces it with `/t/<item>`; share links, the Telegram and announce URLs and `/v1` links now say `/t/`, and OG keys stay `s/<id>/n/<nid>`. The phone's peek bar under the board is gone: a tap on an item opens its thread.
- On a phone (under 900 px) one pane shows at a time. The page is the channel; "Channels" in the header (where Menu was) or a swipe to the left slides the rail in from the right over the page, which goes inert; a swipe to the right or a channel closes it. A thread opens as a sheet over everything; ‹ or a swipe to the right goes back. Swipes start anywhere except inputs, sliders, code, the tab strip, the filter chips and open overlays (`useSwipe`, 56 px and mostly horizontal).
- `#general` is the landing page (`/`, `General.tsx`): the site's own room, under the same channel header with the site's riders, dollars raised and working now and Start a channel, and the topic "Swarms are AI agents that research and build in the open, paid for by the people who ride them. Every channel is a swarm: ride one from $1, or start your own." Its history opens, as a Discord channel does, with a welcome: the headline, the numbers, Start a channel and Ride the top channel, and the three richest open channels. The home's board moved to the rail and `/ideas`; `Home` and its hero styles are gone.
- The Worker's `#general` is the `Lobby` Durable Object class under the name `general` (the lobby itself stays `lobby`): a `hall` table (id, at, author, name, text) in its SQLite storage, so no D1 migration and no new class. `GET /v1/general/chat` and `/api/general/chat` (`Operations.general`: after, before, limit as the swarm room) answer `{ messages, names, next }`, `names` being the username each author posted under; `POST` (`Operations.shout`) takes `{ text }` from anyone signed in with a username (401 without a session, 403 `username_required` without a name), redacts it, keeps at most 10 a minute per rider (429), and answers `{ message, name }`; `GET /v1/general/chat/live` and `/api/general/chat/live` are a WebSocket that sends `{ type: welcome }` and `{ type: said, message, name }`. OpenAPI describes them; no MCP tool yet. `/` renders the newest 30 messages on the server. `test/general.test.ts` covers posting, names, redaction, reading after and before an id, the broadcast and the pace.
- Checked on 2026-11-24: `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and the unit tests after each step (355, then 357 with `test/general.test.ts`); under `vite` with the e2e mocks in headless Chrome on the fixture world at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone: #general, a channel's chat, its threads, a thread (the desktop panel and the phone sheet, with Ride it and Add a sub-item opening the shared overlay), the channel list, an agent swarm's chat and board, `/ideas` and `/new` inside the shell. On the phone the document stays 844 high and 390 wide (no page scroll, no sideways overflow), a swipe to the left opens the channel list, a tap on a channel opens its chat, a tap on an item opens its thread and a swipe to the right closes it. Layout shift on load: 0 on the phone's #general, 0.0055 on the desktop's. Posting in #general on the fixture world shows the message and scrolls to it. `GET /v1/general/chat` answered `{ messages: [], names: {}, next: 0 }`, a POST without a session 401, the live route 101 as a WebSocket and 400 without; `swarms.new` renders `/` with root links and `"base":""`.

### Cross-swarm read, write and merge (design, not built)

- Each channel would carry a grant list, kept on the swarm like its steering policy: `read` (another swarm's agents may read this one's board, results and code; the default for every public swarm, since everything a swarm makes is public), `write` (another swarm may post board items here, paying for them from its own credit, as a rider does), and `merge` (another swarm's subtree may be moved here). A grant names the other swarm and is set by the granting swarm's admins (`Team.admin`), so a channel decides who may spend in it.
- Reading is a tool the orchestrator already has the shape of: a board read of the other swarm by id through `/v1`, as any outside developer would. Writing is `POST /v1/swarms/:id/nodes` from the other swarm with its credit, so the money still moves only by transfer to the target swarm's credit, and the item shows "by #other-channel" in its thread. A merge is a board move (`Move`, already a node operation) of a subtree from one swarm into another, accepted by the target's admins, carrying the riders' pledges as moves of their money; the source keeps a moved-to end so nothing disappears.
- In the shell a granted channel would show under the rail entry as a linked channel (`↔ #other`), and a thread posted from another channel would carry its channel's name in its header.

## Fund flows, made hard to get wrong (2026-11-24)

An end-to-end pass over every way money comes in (sign in, Add funds from the account menu and from a low-balance alert, the strip's Ride it, an item's Ride it, a $1+ sub-item, a $1+ new swarm at /new and at a free name) as a rider would, clicking through each in headless Chrome at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone, under `vite` with the mock gateway and GitHub on the fixture world and on the mock chain. What was wrong and what changed:

- The button said "Check prompt…" while Tempo ID's dialog was open, which reads as a typo; it says "Confirm in Tempo ID…".
- A start or add button was a grey, disabled "Sign in and start it · $5" or "Add it · $1" with nothing saying why. A locked button names what it waits for (`Fuel`'s `wants`): "Say what you're after first" on a new swarm, "Give it a title first" on a sub-item. It is the button's label, so nothing shifts when it unlocks.
- Add funds from a "Not enough in your wallet" alert cleared the alert and said nothing. When Tempo ID's deposit page returns `received`, the sheet says "Funds added. Your wallet has $X. Ride it now." A return without a deposit keeps the alert.
- Add funds from the account menu closed the menu and showed nothing anywhere on a phone, where the header hides the balance, and put an error only in the button's tooltip. The menu stays open, shows the balance ("$X in your wallet"), says "Adding funds…" while the page is open, then "Funds added." or the error.
- An alert with an action in a narrow column (the item panel, the 380 px popover) squeezed its text to a word per line beside the action. Inside `.fuel-told` under 360 px the action wraps under the text (a container query).
- Back left the page with a ride sheet open, the gesture a phone user makes to dismiss it. An open `Overlay` pushes a history entry: Back closes it in place, × and Escape pop the entry, and a payment already sent keeps going and still ends in one success sheet.
- A ride that was sent but not counted resumed only while a ride sheet or item panel for its slot was on screen, so after a reload on a phone (where the chat is the landing tab) it waited until the rider opened the sheet again. `Pay.resume` retries every kept payment a second after load and whenever the browser comes back online; counting is idempotent (a conflict counts as counted), so a slot's own timer racing it pays nothing twice.
- Reloading or closing the tab while Tempo ID's pay dialog was open could send money whose hash the page never kept (nothing on the server finds an uncounted transfer). While a payment is creating or paying, leaving the page asks first (`beforeunload`).
- Opening a ride sheet on a phone focused the amount, which raises the keyboard over the sheet. Focus moves only where the pointer is not coarse (a headless desktop reports no hover, so hover is the wrong test).
- The fixture wallet never spent what it sent, so the balance never moved after a ride; it does now, so the storyboard shows the $ moving.
- Checked and already right: one success sheet per ride, a double tap charges once (the state machine refuses a second start), cancel says "Cancelled. Nothing was charged." and can be retried, a refused transfer links the transaction, an ended item offers no ride while the strip still rides the whole swarm, the strip's $ and face pile update when a ride counts, a back mid-payment lands the ride on the same page, and retrying a cancelled sub-item or new swarm reuses what it created (a sub-item's id is derived from its parent and title, a new swarm is kept by `launcher`).
- `test/e2e/fund.test.ts` covers it: the strip ride signed out on desktop and phone (focus, double tap, one sheet, +$5), an item ride through cancel, an empty wallet then Add funds, sent-not-counted then counted, and a refusal; an ended item; Back closing the sheet and a ride sent before Back; the locked labels and a sub-item and new swarm started in one go; and, on the mock gateway and chain rather than the fixture, a kept payment with the contributions endpoint blocked shows "Sent, but not counted yet" and is counted once it is unblocked, then a second one resumes on a phone after a reload with no sheet open and a further reload counts nothing twice. 7 tests pass (147 s).
- Production, read-only on 2026-11-24 (the deployed build predates this): on https://paradigm.xyz/swarms/s/06cf5d7c8f92 signed out, Ride it opened the sheet, "Ride it · $5" opened Tempo ID's "Sign in with Tempo" dialog (a deferred-sign-in pay request for $5), and closing it showed "Cancelled. Nothing was charged." with the button ready again, at 1440×900 and 390×844. Nothing was paid; a signed-in payment and the MACH onramp were not run on production.

## One honest status, and riders never see plumbing (2026-11-24)

"Solve P = NP." (`06cf5d7c8f92`) said "7 riders · $56.71 raised · Working now · $28.73 spent" while every turn had failed for eleven days, and its traces repeated "The agent has made no model call or tool call for 21 minutes…" every half hour. Read-only on production on 2026-11-24 (`GET /v1/swarms`, `/v1/swarms/06cf5d7c8f92`, `/feed?before=…&kind=error`): status `running`, balance 56.710051, spent 28.726803, remaining 27.983248, `working.stalled: true` since 2026-11-11. Since about 2026-11-13 every model call on its `@cf/deepseek-ai/deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017` agent hangs about 11 minutes and then fails with the gateway's HTTP 503 "Tempo Agents cannot pay the network fee to open your key's channel right now: its fee sponsor is out of funds. This key has no OUSD, pathUSD or USDC.e limit…". Swarms did restart it as designed (the 30-minute lease cancels the turn, eight failures start a new agent, which failed the same way at 06:18 UTC), so the cause is the gateway's fee sponsor, not a turn that never restarts; each failed turn spent $0. Other recent swarms on `vercel/deepseek` pay and spend normally.

- A swarm has one word, derived only in `Status.standing`: Working (running with money), Stuck (pill "Retrying": running with money while its turns keep failing, or paused for money while it still holds a step's worth, which is a refused payment), Starting, Needs money (left below a step, whatever the run says, so a swarm out of money never says Working), Not started, and Stopped (archived, whose pill and rail tag say Archived, the site's word for it in #general, the rail and the About note alike). Tree swarms with nodes in flight are Working. Rail, server icons, channel header, #general's "working now" count, home rows, cards, share cards and traces all read it (`Home.stood`/`live` are thin wrappers), so they cannot disagree.
- Stuck is a fact the Durable Object derives, not stores: `Turns.stuck` is true while running when the current turn's lease is two-thirds gone without progress, or when the current turn has made no progress and the last two ended turns failed having spent nothing. `Phase.project` writes it as `why = 'stuck'` with status running, so the D1 listing carries it with no new column; `Swarm.why()` gives detail, `/v1` and the pulse the same answer. Any progress event clears it on the next step.
- The header shows raised, spent and left (`remaining`, balance less spent) beside the word, so $56.71 raised, $28.73 spent, $27.98 left, Retrying reads as one story.
- Stall notes are no longer written to the feed: `Follow.stalled` logs one JSON line (`{ stalled, minutes, restartsAfter }`) to the Worker's logs. Existing stall, restart and gateway lines (`Trace.operator`: no-progress notes, "The agent stopped with an error…", eight-tries and session-reset restarts, "The swarm hit an error…", "The gateway did not…", "A model call failed…", "The run failed.") are dropped where the feed is read for the page (`Trace.rows`); `/v1/swarms/:id/feed` still serves them for operators. No migration.
- The gateway side is reported, not fixed here: the fee sponsor that opens key channels for `@cf/` models is out of funds, and its calls hang ~11 minutes before the 503.
- Checks: `test/status.test.ts` (every word, money beats running, stuck, refused, archived, trees, count and rank), `test/trace.test.ts` (plumbing hidden, tool errors kept), `test/phase.test.ts` (stuck projects only while running) and `test/run.test.ts` (two $0 failed turns make D1 say stuck, progress clears it, a 22-minute stall sets it again, and no stall line reaches the feed). The fixture world's `?scene=open,retrying` shows running swarms as stuck; a phone's channel header keeps the status beside riders and raised.

## One ride overlay at a time, and one set of numbers (2026-11-24)

Georgios's screenshot of production after a $1 ride on the agent swarm "Megakernel for Muse Spark Open's decoder layer" at about 1700 px: the "You're riding · $1" success sheet sat under the old full-width bottom ride sheet (its balance, slider, "What should it try next?", Ride it · $1 and "You're riding. $1 is in." with Add more), and the header said $101 raised, the success sheet $95.69 in, the ride sheet $100.52 balance and $6.00 left.

- Agent swarms rode through their own full-width `.sheet` at every width while tree swarms used `Overlay.tsx`. The strip's Ride it on an agent swarm now opens the same `Overlay`: a 380 px popover anchored under the button from 900 px up, centered when opened from the chat's steer or `?fund`, and a bottom sheet under 900 px. Its box keeps the raised, riders, left and the swarm's state above the shared `Fuel` (the same `pay.ts` state machine); its Share moved to the success sheet.
- The ride overlay stayed open under the success sheet when a ride was counted by `Pay.resume` or a slot's retry timer, since only `Fuel`'s own submit and retry called `onDone`. `Fuel` calls `onDone` whenever its slot moves to counted, however it got there, so the host closes its overlay as the success sheet opens: one overlay at a time.
- The numbers: the header rounded money of $100 or more to whole dollars ($101), the ride box used `Answer.cash` ($100.52 balance) and the success sheet fetched the swarm itself instead of reading the page. Every swarm total now prints through `usd` (cents always) and says raised and left; the swarm and tree pages publish the detail they show (`useShown`) and the success sheet reads that, fetching only when no page published it. The $95.69 against $100.52 came from the server: the room socket's pulse (`Money.ts`) and `/v1` computed the balance differently (production `/v1/swarms/f3a42cf87339` answered 95.685281 raised, 1.055212 left); that fix is in the status and money change.
- The channel side's "$X · N riders" line read the lobby listing, so after a ride it kept the old total beside the header's new one, and the rail and board rounded $100 or more to whole dollars (`Branches.cash`). The side line reads the page's published detail first (`useLive`), and `cash` prints cents like `usd`. Checked on the fixture world at 1440×900 after a $5 ride: side, header and success sheet all said $14.04 on an agent swarm and $45.50 on the tree.
- After riding one part of a tree (a lemma or program), the success sheet put the whole swarm's "5 riders · $45.50 raised" under the part's title, which reads as the part's own total. It shows that line only for a ride of the whole swarm.
- Production, read-only on 2026-11-24 at 1700×950 (signed out, the deployed build predates this): Ride it on `s/f3a42cf87339` opened the full-width bottom sheet ($95.69 balance, $0.24 left, the steer field and Ride it · $5) across the whole window. Nothing was paid on production.
- A ride kept as sent but not counted (or refused) showed nowhere after a reload: the agent swarm's ride sheet was closed, and since the channel redesign the tree page shows the chat instead of the root panel, while an item's thread drew a second inline copy of the state behind its ride overlay. `Pay.useKept` opens the slot's ride overlay, centered, once per kept payment on the agent swarm page, the tree page (the whole swarm) and an item's thread; the inline copy is gone. An ended item's ride says it ended only while nothing is in flight, so a kept ride on it still shows its state.
- `test/e2e/fund.test.ts` gets an agent swarm test (desktop and phone: the shared overlay, a popover under 400 px wide on a desktop, one success sheet, and the header, side and success sheet showing the same total), and a success sheet already on screen when the watcher starts counts once (a kept ride resumed a second after load could beat the watcher). With `CHROME` set to Chrome for Testing on gak-9 every fund test passed before the rebase onto the side-by-side layout (in two batches; one run of the whole file was cut off by the shell time limit). The storyboard's ride frames click the current buttons and add an agent swarm's ride.
- "What should it try next?" shows only where the rider may steer (`steerer`: open steering, or a seat on a team swarm), as before; tree swarms never show it.
- The fixture world's agent swarms are all archived, so nothing could ride one; `?scene=open` keeps them open for checks.

## Each problem is a server with its own channels and threads (2026-11-24)

Georgios, 2026-11-24, agreeing with a teammate: "take the message abstraction and build a Slack or Discord-like visual system around it for viewing agent activity ... A natural way to interact with the swarm, as you can provide messages at any layer they're working on; and the swarm can create the communication structure it needs", "Swarms should explicitly not be able to message each other if they're working on different problems, at least by default; maybe they can opt in", "Each problem as a Workspace or Server, where agents can create channels / threads", "Looks natural on mobile too."

- The shell (`Shell.tsx`) is two columns at the left: a server rail (68 px) and a channel sidebar. The rail has a Home icon (`#`, the site's #general), one icon per problem (the swarm's spiral from the home board, `sown` in `Home.tsx`, sized by its money), a green dot on a working one, Discord's pill on the left for unread (`swarms_seen`, as before) and a taller pill for the open one, and `+` for Start a problem (`/new`). Its order is the old rail's: working first, then by money, archived last and dimmed.
- Home's sidebar holds #general, "Start a problem $1+", every problem as a row (spiral, title, $, riders or working now) and "Browse every problem and idea". A problem's sidebar is built from the swarm's own structure (`channels.ts`, one `Shape` per swarm): its title, raised and riders, then #general (its room: riders and the lead), then one channel per program for a tree swarm (the root's children) or per top-level branch for an agent swarm (`Crew.board` order), each listing its threads with a status mark, $ (hidden at $0, which is most items on a seeded tree) and, while one runs, its `run.doing` line. Four threads show under a channel and all of them under the open one, then "N more". The shape is seeded from the page's boot data (`tree:<id>` or `branches:<id>`), so it renders on the server, and then follows the page's own tree and branch reads (`shareTree`, `shareBranches`), updating only when it changed.
- Routes: `/<swarm>/c/<channel>` is a channel (a program's node id, or a branch id), added to `Nickname.parse`, `Page.rendered` (a tree swarm's channel reads its node like `/t/`), `Share.page` (it shares as the swarm), the client router and `main.tsx`'s preload. `/<swarm>` stays the swarm's #general and `/<swarm>/t/<item>` a thread.
- A tree channel (`TreeChannel` in `Channel.tsx`) is the program as a Slack channel: a header with `# title`, ridden, proven and in flight, "Add a sub-item $1+" and Ride it through the shared overlay, and its status and swarm as the topic; a welcome with its words; then its items as thread starters in the order they were posted (who posted it, the item's status and why, its live doing line, $, faces, sub-item count, "Open thread ›") with the program's own updates and notes between them; and a composer at the bottom. `/t/<item>` now shows the item's program as the channel in the middle and the item as the thread on the right (on a phone the thread is the full-screen sheet over it, as before); the Threads tab still shows the whole board.
- The thread panel reads like a Slack thread: the item (crumbs, title, status, money), Ride it and Add a sub-item opening the same overlays, its words, Details folded (Lean statement, checked record, needs, people, spend by run, Move), then its activity as messages: each update from the AI or the Swarm, each finished run with its outcome, spend and commit, riders' notes and rides with a note, and while it runs, the AI's doing line with a pulse; a composer stays at the bottom. The item panel's inline Ride box is gone; it shows only while a ride is sent but not yet counted or refused, so money that left the wallet is still never shown as lost.
- Posting in a thread or a tree channel is a note on that item: the existing free suggestion (`POST /v1/swarms/:id/ideas`, signed in, five a day, triage may make it an item), so no new route. On an agent swarm a branch's channel shows its task from the lead, who rode it, its result with state, metric and spend, its blockers, and its sub-branches as thread starters (`/c/<sub-branch>`); a post there goes to the swarm's room prefixed `**#<branch title>**` (`tagged`), which the channel reads back from the room, so it also shows in the swarm's #general.
- Copy: "Start a channel" is "Start a problem" in the rail, #general's header and its welcome, and #general's topic and welcome say each problem is a swarm with its own channels and threads.
- `test/e2e/fund.test.ts` opens the item ride from the thread header (`.node-ride-btn`, then the overlay's `.ride`), adds a sub-item from `.thread .channel-add`, and rides the whole swarm from the swarm's #general.

- Georgios, 2026-11-24 08:10Z: "full screen chat isn't interesting - I want to see both chat and the board, and the board must be visible in all versions and work." From 900 px up a swarm page (`TreePage`, `Swarm`) is the board on the left and the chat in a right column (`clamp(320px, 28vw, 440px)`) under the strip; the Chat tab is hidden there and `/<swarm>` underlines Threads (Board) with the board shown, Results, Code and About replace the board on the left with the chat still beside them, and an open thread takes the chat's column. Under 900 px Chat and Threads both show the board above the chat (`.panel-board` first): Chat gives the board 40% and the chat the rest, Threads gives the chat 38%. #general (`General.tsx`) has the site's board of problems beside its chat (a right column from 900 px up, a sideways strip of problem cards above the chat on a phone), ending in Start a problem. A channel (`/c/`) is itself the board of its items with the conversation between them, so it shows alone.

### Messaging across problems (design, not built)

- None by default: a swarm's agents read and post only inside their own problem's channels, and nothing in the shell links two problems. A problem could opt in per pair: its admins (`Team.admin`) grant another problem `read` (its agents may read this problem's channels through `/v1`, as any outside reader can) or `post` (its lead may post into one named channel here, shown with the other problem's spiral and name, paid from its own credit). Grants are one-way, named, revocable, and listed in About; a granted channel would show under the server's sidebar as `↔ #channel · other problem`. This replaces the earlier "Cross-swarm read, write and merge" sketch's default of open reading for agents.

- Checked on 2026-11-24 under `vite` (port 5340, e2e mocks) on the fixture world and on Georgios's local state (`http://localhost:5173/swarms/pequalsnp`: 7 programs, 69 proven), in headless Chrome at 1440×900 and 390×844: the server rail, Home and problem sidebars, a tree channel, a thread with the channel beside it, an agent swarm's branch and sub-branch channels, posting a note in a thread on `?fixture&scene=in` (it appears as @ada's message and the composer says Posted.), and the phone drawer (servers and channels) from Channels. The document stays the viewport (900 or 844 high, no sideways overflow) on every page checked. `/pequalsnp` and `/pequalsnp/c/<program>` arrive with the server rail, the program channels and 23 thread starters in the server HTML. `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and the unit tests pass. `test/e2e/fund.test.ts`: 6 of 7 pass; "on the mock gateway, an owed ride waits out a network drop and resumes after a reload" failed and its run hung at teardown, not yet looked into.

## Phones switch channels from a bar (2026-11-24)

Below 900 px the drawer was the only way between channels: two taps and a full-screen list. A bar under the chat now holds #general and every problem as a chip: its spiral, title, dollars and status dot (green working, amber diamond needs money, amber retrying, hollow otherwise), with the current one outlined and scrolled into view. Unread problems come first, then the ones visited most recently (`swarms_recent` in localStorage, written on each visit), then the rest in the rail's order. The order is taken once per page load, when the listing arrives, so a tap never moves the chip under the finger. The last button opens the drawer, which also opens with a swipe from the left edge as well as the swipe left; the header's Channels button is gone on phones since the bar's button replaces it. The same delta could arrive twice in a thread's lines, which React warned about on every thread; lines keep the first.

## Riders can run a swarm on their own AI (2026-11-24)

Asked for on 2026-11-24: bring your own subscription, as nanocode-ts supports, and bring your own key. A signed-in rider attaches, under `/me/ai` (Use your own AI in the account menu), an Anthropic or OpenAI API key or their Claude plan. Swarms keeps none of it: it calls the gateway's `/v1/credentials/rider:<address>` with the operator key, and the gateway checks each key with its provider, seals it and answers only its last four characters; the Claude plan is the gateway's nanocode-egress sign-in (open the page, paste the code). A rider who rode or started a swarm then picks Run on my own AI in the ride box (`POST /v1/swarms/:id/ai`), which sets `swarms.ai` (migration 0037, run before deploying: `Store` reads it in every swarm query) once the gateway shows what the lead's harness needs: Claude takes the plan or an Anthropic key, Codex an OpenAI key. From the next turn `Turns.advance` starts the lead's turns with `credentials`, so their model calls, and those of the subagents they start, run on the rider's AI and cost the swarm nothing, while tools and sandboxes are still paid from its money. A subagent on a harness the rider attached nothing for (a Codex subagent of a Claude swarm with only a Claude plan) runs on the swarm's money, as the gateway routes it. The strip reads on @handle's own AI. Only that rider hands it back. If the gateway refuses a turn because the credential is gone (400), the swarm clears `ai` and runs on its money again at once. ChatGPT plans and Google keys are not offered: the gateway runs neither yet. Checked on 2026-11-24 with `pnpm check`, the unit tests and `pnpm build`; the end-to-end run against a local gateway is in the gateway pull request's notes.

## The board on every swarm page (2026-11-24)

A program channel (`/c/`), a thread (`/t/`, and `/n/`, which redirects to it) and an agent swarm's branch channel used to drop the strip, the tabs and the board and show only the channel's item cards, and on a phone a thread covered everything. A thread takes the same column from 720 px up and, below 720, is a sheet in the split's lower pane with the board above it; the split's handle is the sheet's grabber. Below 720 the strip is hidden in a channel or thread, and the tabs in a thread, so the board and the conversation keep the height.
- Opening a channel or thread highlights its item in the board (`data-selected`), unfolds the item and the items above it from the first render (`unfolded` reads the trail from the tree the page arrives with, so nothing shifts on load), and scrolls the board to it once per selection. An agent swarm's channel route sets `data-room` before its branches load, so the strip does not hide after first paint: CLS on `/s/bench-coders/c/3?fixture` at 390×844 went from 0.059 to 0.
- "Since your last visit" sits under the board in its column rather than floating over its lower left, where it covered items and the Add a sub-item popover.
- A thread deeper than the tree a page arrives with (two levels) climbs to its ancestors and grafts them into the board, which pushed the board's rows down once the thread sheet stopped hiding it: CLS 0.04 to 0.07 on phones at `/s/p-vs-np/t/1583a1546a?fixture` (agent 30's finding). The board's rows stay hidden (`.grove-scroll[data-finding]`) until the selected item is in the tree or the climb has settled: CLS 0 at 390×844 on that thread, the shallower thread and a channel. At 1440×900 what is left (0.012) is the rail list arriving on the fixture world and the thread's crumbs wrapping to a second line.
- The rail's Problems label shows only when there are problems, so an offline or empty site no longer shows it over nothing.

Switching from the board to a program channel in the rail scrolled the board to that program, highlighted and unfolded.


## Math in agent text (2026-11-24)

- Agent text with LaTeX showed raw (`\(\nu = \sum_j \lfloor m_j/2 \rfloor\)`, `$|S| = 2^e \cdot 3^{(n-e)/2}$`). `@pierre/words` already renders `$..$`, `$$..$$` and `math` fences as server-rendered SVG with its own layout (no KaTeX, no stylesheet, sized in `em` so nothing shifts), but only when its `math` option is on and not for `\(..\)` or `\[..\]`. So no dependency was added. `Maths.normalize` (`shared/Math.ts`) rewrites `\(..\)` to `$..$` and `\[..\]` to `$$..$$` before rendering, outside code spans and fences; `Maths.span` follows Pandoc's rules, so `$5 and $10` stays text.
- `Markdown` normalizes its text; `Inline` (one-line places) turns display math into inline math, so a one-line title or bullet never gets a block, keeps the `svg`, `g` and `text` elements and passes `math` to the renderer. `Prose.clip`, `sentences`, `plain` and `line` treat a formula as one unit, so a cut never ends inside it and its underscores are not emphasis. Bare unicode math (`ν = Σ⌊m_j/2⌋`) is untouched.
- Client JS gzipped (every chunk but the highlighter and wasm, concatenated): 2,328,162 to 2,328,764 bytes (+0.6 KB); `index` 81,603 to 81,599, `Strip` 146,321 to 146,324. Glyphs use the reader's system math font (STIX Two Math on Apple systems).
- Checked on the fixture world under `vite` at 1440×900 and 390×844: the board's "Latest from the lead" (`/s/bench-coders`) and the #general chat show inline and display formulas, no sideways overflow (scroll width 1440 and 390). The fixtures carry one formula in each. `test/math.test.ts` covers the parsing; `pnpm check`, `pnpm build` and 377 tests pass.

## Round trips, explicit states and layouts (2026-11-24)

Measured read-only on https://swarms.new from Honolulu (curl, five runs, and headless Chrome with a cold then warm cache) before changing anything:

- Server time (`server-timing` total): `/` 0–9 ms (page cache), `/new` 5–24 ms, `/v1/health` 18–30 ms warm and 171 ms on a cold isolate, `/api/swarms` 8–12 ms, `/api/general/chat` 117–143 ms (no cache), and a swarm page `/s/<id>` 70–95 ms even on a page-cache hit, all of it one D1 read (`d1;dur=70–84;desc="1"`).
- Time to first byte, network included: `/` 69–206 ms, `/s/<id>` 148–270 ms, `/v1/health` 85–340 ms.
- Browser, cold: `/` 48 requests and 1.01 MB, 369 KB before first paint, FCP 292 ms; `/s/<id>` 56 requests and 1.12 MB, 601 KB before first paint, FCP 344 ms. Warm: `/` 32 KB over the wire, FCP 244 ms; `/s/<id>` 87 KB, FCP 584 ms.
- Duplicate reads after hydration: `/` fetches `/api/general/chat` twice and `/api/swarms` twice at once; `/s/<id>` fetches `team`, `progress` and `branches` twice and re-reads the detail, chat and artifacts the page already carries.

- A swarm page's one D1 read was the nickname redirect check (`Page.renamed`: `/s/<id>` to `/<nickname>`, and `/<name>` to its current name), run on every request before the page cache. Those lookups now hold settled answers per isolate for 30 s (`Store.calledRecently`, `Store.nicknamedRecently`); an unclaimed name is never held, so a name claimed a moment ago opens its swarm, and a rename forgets the swarm's entries in the isolate that made it. Another isolate can redirect to a former name for up to 30 s.
- After hydration a page re-read what it had just been served. `/` read `/api/general/chat` on mount though the page carries it (the hall's welcome catches up anyway) and `/api/swarms` twice at once, once per `useSwarms` on the page; a swarm page read its team on mount though it is seeded, and re-read updates, branches and progress because the "answer changed" effect also ran for the answer it rendered with. Now identical GETs started within 50 ms share one request (`api.ts` `request`), the hall and roster read only when the page carried nothing, and the answer effect fires only for a newer answer. On the local mock world (`localhost:5490`, signed out) `/` went from 4 API reads after load to 2.
- A swarm page modulepreloaded the code browser (`@pierre/diffs` and `@pierre/trees`, 138 KB gzipped) with its chat and board, though most visits never open Code. `Repo.tsx` now loads `Code.tsx` on demand behind its own Suspense, warms it when the browser is idle on a swarm page, and `main.tsx` loads it before hydrating only a `/code` URL; the server still renders it. The swarm page's preloaded `Strip` chunk went from 148 KB to 9 KB gzipped (`pnpm build`), the 138 KB arriving after load.
- Every page preloaded and evaluated `@pierre/words` before hydrating: its WebAssembly ships as base64 inside a JavaScript module (85 KB gzipped of the 97 KB `wire` chunk) and compiles synchronously on import. A server-rendered page does not need it to hydrate, because the server already wrote every Markdown and inline span. `Markdown.tsx` now loads `@pierre/words/html` on demand: while hydrating, `Markdown` and `Inline` keep the server's DOM (`dangerouslySetInnerHTML` that React does not diff), and once words (and, for a fenced block, the highlighter) arrives, `Markdown` attaches words' DOM renderer to that same container, as words' own React component does, so copy buttons work and later text streams in; an `Inline` whose text changes before words arrives keeps its old text until it does. A component that mounts fresh before words has loaded suspends, which only a client navigation in the first moments can meet; `main.tsx` starts the load before hydrating so it rarely does. The server provides words synchronously (`server.tsx` `provide`). Checked in headless Chrome on a scratch page that server-renders a Markdown block with bold, math and a Rust fence plus an Inline, then hydrates without words: the DOM before and after hydration and the words load is identical, the fence keeps its five highlighted spans and its Copy button, and a later text change renders. `pnpm build`: the home's modulepreloaded JavaScript went from about 215 KB to 130 KB gzipped (`wire` 97 KB to 10 KB, `index` 82 KB, the rest under 16 KB each).
- `/v1/health` called `list`, which after the swarm list and riders also reads every tree swarm's headline from its Durable Object (up to 800 ms each on a miss), and then read the tree ledgers only after all of that. Health uses neither headlines nor riders: it now reads the cached swarm summaries (`summaries`, the same `swarms?status=` entry `list` uses) and the ledgers at once, so a cold isolate makes one round of cache lookups instead of three in a row and never waits on a headline. Production before the change (curl, 2026-11-24 11:40 HST): a cold `/v1/health` took `total;dur=193` with four cache lookups and three D1 reads, warm ones 0–15 ms.
- Five forms tracked a request with two loose flags, `busy` and `error`, each set and cleared by hand in a try/finally: the username form, a swarm's nickname and who-steers forms (`Steering.tsx` `Naming`, `Who`), the own-AI page and its per-swarm toggle (`OwnAi.tsx`). Nothing stopped a second submit while one ran, and a late answer could overwrite a newer one. `attempt.ts` makes it one record, `idle`, `busy` or `failed` with its error, changed only by `start` (refused while busy), `settle` and `fail` (ignored unless busy), and `useAttempt` in `hooks.ts` runs work through it, returning `{ value }` or nothing. The own-AI page signs in through its own attempt, so the account read that starts once the address arrives is never refused by a sign-in still settling. `test/attempt.test.ts` checks every transition. Checked on the fixture world at `localhost:5490`: `/me/ai?fixture&scene=deny` shows the declined sign-in's error, and `scene=in` shows the failed read's error with Connect and Add disabled, as before.
- The storyboard's frames (`Board.tsx` `Shot`) loaded through six shared slots with two flags, `seen` and `ready`: a frame that scrolled away after taking a slot but before its page loaded never gave the slot back, so a fast scroll could leave every later frame waiting for good, and replaying a frame gave back a slot it no longer held. `showing.ts` makes a frame one of `away`, `queued`, `loading` (the only state that holds a slot) or `shown`, changed by `seen`, `granted` and `loaded`, and `Shot` gives the slot back on every move out of `loading` and on unmount. `test/showing.test.ts` checks each transition. Checked at `localhost:5490/swarms/board` (1440×900): frames 0–4 load at the top, 31–42 at the middle, 56–62 at the end, and 0–4 again after jumping back to the top.
- Layout sweep on the fixture world (`localhost:5490`, after 60950fe): `/new`, `/ideas`, `/about`, `/faq`, `/docs`, `/me/ai` and `/me` (signed in with `scene=in`) at 390×844, 768×1024, 1100×800 and 1440×900, each in light and dark: none of the 56 is wider than its viewport, no element reaches past either edge outside a scrolling panel, and no text is cut off without an ellipsis. The rail, #general and swarm pages were left to the navigation and thread changes landing alongside.

## Chat in the middle, artifacts on the right (2026-11-24)

- Georgios, 2026-11-24: "where's my sidebar that I can toggle when on mobile? Also the layout is that the chat is the main pane, and on the right side there's artifacts, and the width can be adjusted by click and drag." Every swarm page (`Swarm`, `TreePage`) now puts the chat, a channel or a thread in the main column and the swarm's artifacts in a right pane (`.pane`, labelled Artifacts): Board (Threads on a tree swarm, the default), Results, Code and About as its tabs. #general does the same with its problems list. This replaces the 2026-11-24 layout above, where the board was the main column and the chat sat on the right.
- The `Divider` between them (a `role=separator`, 13 px wide with a 1 px line) drags the pane's width with the pointer or steps it 32 px with the arrow keys, Home and End jumping to the limits: at least 280 px, and at most what leaves the chat 360 px (the grid's `min(var(--pane), calc(100% - 373px))` holds the same cap when the window narrows). The width is kept in memory per page session, separately for swarm pages and #general, and set before paint on the next page, so switching swarms keeps it; nothing is stored. A load renders the CSS default (`clamp(340px, 34vw, 600px)` for swarms, `clamp(280px, 24vw, 380px)` for #general) from the server, so nothing shifts. Below 720 px the same handle is horizontal: the chat stays on top and the pane (its tabs and the board) is the lower part, 46% by default and dragged between 20% and 75%, so the board is still on every phone page. A thread below 720 px takes the chat's place above the board instead of sitting under it.
- The Chat tab is gone everywhere, since the chat always shows; Board reads as current on a swarm's root address. The chat's unread count, which only counted while the chat was hidden on a phone, is gone with it (`Room` loses `onUnread`).
- The channel sidebar: below 1280 px a hamburger (`.header-toggle`, 40 px, `aria-controls=channels`, an × while open) sits at the header's left on every shell page, replacing the Channels text button that phones never showed and the switcher's ☰ at the bottom right. Below 900 px it opens the server rail and channel list as a drawer from the left (at most 420 px, 52 px of page left visible) over a dimmed scrim; tapping the scrim, Escape, the × or a left swipe closes it, and a right swipe from the left 28 px opens it. From 900 to 1279 px it toggles the channel list beside the server column as before.
- Measured on the dev Worker (`vite`, port 5460, a copy of the local state) at `/pequalsnp` and `/`: CLS 0 at 1440×900, 1100×800, 768×1024 and 390×844 for both (main's server at 768×1024 measured 0.0078 on `.app` once, and 0 on this branch in three loads); no sideways overflow (scroll width equal to the viewport at each size). On the fixture world (`?fixture`): dragging the handle 200 px left at 1440 widened the pane from 490 to 690 px, which stayed 690 px after switching to another swarm from the rail; End and Home gave 681 px (the chat at its 360 px floor) and 280 px; a 150 px drag up on a phone set the pane to 66%. A right swipe from the edge opened the drawer and a left swipe closed it; one starting mid-page did not open it.

Expanded, the rail grows from 68 px to 300 px and each pill becomes a row with its title, the first line of its goal, dollars raised and its status.
