## Local-first client store (2026-11-24)

Georgios: "make the app Linear-style local first, inspired by Zero and Linear's sync engine." Every page reads one client store, the server streams what changed, and a revisit asks the network nothing.

- How Zero, Replicache and Linear do it, and what was taken. Replicache keeps a key-value view on the client, pulls patches since a cookie, and runs named mutators locally first (pending), then rebases them on the server's answer, which carries the client's last mutation id so a replay never applies twice. Zero adds queries the server keeps live and an IndexedDB store. Linear bootstraps from a snapshot, persists normalized models in IndexedDB, and stays current through a socket of deltas tagged with a global sync id, catching up from the last id on reconnect. Swarms already had the pieces on the server: the room socket's `welcome` names the swarm object's stamp (`version`, the detail and chat ETag), `pulse`, `changed` and `delta` stream each change, and `/v1/swarms/{id}/deltas?after=` is the tree's whole delta log by seq. So no new dependency and no server change: `src/web/local.ts` (one module, about 450 lines) is the client half.
- The store (`local.ts`): rows keyed as `usePoll` already keyed its data (`<swarm>`, `node:<swarm>:<nid>`, `updates:<swarm>:<nid>`, `room:<swarm>`, `rider:<address>`, `tree:`, `burn:`, …), each `{ value, at, version, state }` with `state` one of `fresh` (loaded or kept current by the stream this session), `stored` (read back from IndexedDB, not yet confirmed) or `stale` (known changed). It lives in memory and is written behind to an IndexedDB database `swarms` with one object store per entity (`swarms`, `nodes`, `messages`, `riders`, `runs`, `views`, plus `meta` for each swarm's version and delta cursor and `outbox` for queued mutations), at most 300 rows each, newest kept. The server render's boot data seeds it as `fresh`; the fixture world stays in memory.
- Reads: `usePoll` is now a `useSyncExternalStore` over its row (the server snapshot is the boot data, so hydration matches), fetches only when the row is not `fresh`, and `Local.load` shares one request per key, so the channel pane and the thread pane that ask for the same node make one request. Its `sessionStorage` copy (`keep`) is gone: everything persists.
- Sync: a `welcome` at the version the store last saw confirms it (stored rows at that version become fresh, nothing is refetched, no stir); a different version marks the swarm's rows stale (mounted ones reload, as the old welcome stir did), except node rows when the store holds a delta cursor: those catch up with one `/deltas?after=<cursor>` read (pages of 500, at most 4) applied as the socket's deltas are. Reads whose source the version does not cover (team, branches, artifacts, burn, code, progress; see the 2026-11-07 entry on double reads) are reloaded on every welcome, as before. Live `delta` events patch every cached node and its updates, not only the selected one, and mark a node stale for the events that change its funders, runs or steers (proven, ended, started, funded, moved, steered), as TreePage's selected-node handler did. `pulse` patches the cached detail and `said` the cached room even while nothing shows them; a `changed` part nobody shows is reloaded on its next mount. The cursor only moves on deltas the store applied or a tree read while the socket is live, so it never runs ahead of a cached node.
- Measured on the local fixture world (`pnpm dev` with the mock gateway, the P = NP tree swarm, headless Chrome at 1,440 × 900, a click on a sidebar link timed to the frame where the pane has its data and no request in flight; `measure.sh` outside the repository), before (c9c617e) and after: a channel's first visit 4 requests to 2 (the node and its updates were read twice), a thread's first visit 2 to 2, every revisit 4 or 2 to 0 (settled 35 to 83 ms, now the paint itself, 14 to 31 ms), and after a reload every channel and thread already visited 4 or 2 to 0, behind one `/deltas` read at load.
- Reactions and thread replies (e66da88): a `said` with a `parent` is a reply, so the store adds it to that thread's row (`replies:<swarm>:<parent>`, the `messages` table, what `GET /v1/swarms/{id}/chat/{message}` serves) and never to `room:<swarm>`, which the server lists top-level only. `reacted` and `replies` replace a message's `reactions` or `replies` summary in the room row and in every held thread row (as its parent or one of its replies); a message the store does not hold changes nothing and wakes no reader. A reaction calls `touch()`, so it moves the swarm's version: a stored room or thread whose socket missed a reaction is refetched on the next `welcome`, never shown stale. `test/local.test.ts` covers the routing.
- Prefetch: the router already called its warmers on a link's hover, focus or touch to load page code and a swarm's detail. The detail now warms through `Local.warm` (one shared request per key, nothing when the row is fresh, so the old 10-second throttle map is gone), and a link to a tree swarm's channel or thread (`/c/<id>`, `/t/<id>`, `/n/<id>`, by `/s/<id>` or nickname) also warms that node and its updates under the keys `TreePage` reads, so the click finds them fresh. Only once the stored detail says the swarm is a tree, so an agent swarm's link never asks for a node it does not have.


## Threads and reactions in the room (2026-11-24)

Georgios: "for the chat allow emoji reactions, thread replies etc — the idea is that each top-level thread I direct to the swarm creates a new board item and then you get a thread; each swarm can have many threads, or just humans jamming — so it feels like Slack/Discord thread + replies where humans and agents jam together."

- A reply is a `chat` row with `parent`, the id of its thread's first message; a reply to a reply joins the same thread (`Chat.root`). The room (`GET /v1/swarms/:id/chat`) lists only first messages, each with `replies` (`count`, the last reply's `at`, and the latest three repliers newest first, `null` for the swarm's own updates) when it has any, so a busy thread costs the room one line. `GET /v1/swarms/:id/chat/:message` reads a thread (its first message, replies oldest first, paged with `after`, the members and `me`). Posting a reply is `POST …/chat` with `parent`, under the room's rules (riders only, a username, 10 a minute shared with the room, redacted); `@swarm` in a reply is 400, since a steer or an item belongs to the room. Nothing derived is stored: the summary is one grouped query over the partial index `chat_thread (parent, id)`.
- Reactions are `chat_reactions (message, emoji, author, at)`, one row per person and emoji, `WITHOUT ROWID`. `POST …/chat/:message/reactions` `{ emoji, on }` adds or takes back the caller's, idempotently, from twelve emoji (`Reaction.emojis`: a fixed set keeps validation a lookup and every client able to draw them), riders only, 30 a minute (`paced(…, 'reacted')`). Messages carry `reactions` (emoji with who, oldest emoji first) only when they have any.
- Live on the room socket: `said` carries `parent` for a reply and is followed by `replies` with the thread's new summary; `reacted` carries a message's whole reaction list after a change, so a client replaces rather than counts. Both bump the room's stamp, so the edge's tagged room reads do not serve stale threads or reactions.
- A message to the swarm on a tree swarm becomes a board item: ✦ or `@swarm` in the room opens the add-sub-item card (`Compose`, under the root, the message as title and words, $1 and up, the same pay flow), and `POST /v1/swarms/:id/nodes` takes `said`. The proposal keeps it, and only when the ride makes the node (`Riding.pledge`) does the room get the message, under the proposer's name, with `node`: an unpaid proposal says nothing. From then on `Nodes.emit` posts the node's funded, started, progress, checking, proven, ended, steered and moved updates as the swarm's replies in that thread (not `posted` or `doing`), redacted. The page opens that thread when the message arrives instead of jumping to the item. Plain messages stay chat and get threads like any other. Agent swarms keep ✦ as a steer.
- The page: hovering (or tapping, on a phone) a message shows react and reply; reactions are chips with counts that toggle your own (optimistic, put back on failure) and name who reacted; a thread shows as faces, a count and the time of the last reply under its message, and opens in the artifacts pane's place on desktop and as a sheet on a phone (`Thread.tsx`), with its first message, the item card when it made one, the replies and a composer. The picker keeps the six last-used emoji in localStorage first.
- Measured on gak-9 under node:sqlite (`~/swarms-work/threads-scratch/bench.ts`, 50 runs each): the room's newest 100 read in 0.22 ms with 5,000 messages and no threads, and in 3.0 to 4.0 ms once 3,000 replies and 2,000 reactions sat on those 100 (0.44 ms for the rows over the partial index `chat_top`, about 1 ms for the thread summaries, the rest building reaction lists); before `chat_top`, the same read scanned the replies above the newest first message. A thread of 30 replies read in 0.18 to 0.31 ms, a reply with its summary broadcast took 0.06 to 0.08 ms and a reaction toggle 0.04 to 0.10 ms.
- Checked on 2026-11-24 with `pnpm check`, `node --test` (`test/threads.test.ts`: threads, refusals, redaction and pacing, reactions and their stream, the paid and unpaid proposal) and `pnpm build`, and in headless Chrome under `pnpm dev` on the fixture world at 1440×900 and as an iPhone 14: on Does P = NP? a thread opened in the right pane and as a sheet with no sideways overflow, a reply updated the count and faces under its message live, a reaction toggled in the room and the thread together, the picker added 👍, and ✦ "Try a SAT encoding…" opened Add it to the board with the message filled in, paid $1 in the fixture, posted the message with its item card and opened its thread; on Benchmark every open-weight coding model a reply opened and posted in the agent swarm's room. No console errors. Not run: a real swarm's runs posting into a thread (covered by the unit test through `Nodes.emit`), and production.

## Admins edit a swarm's title, nickname and picture (2026-11-24)

Georgios, 2026-11-24: "allow the swarm creator to edit the title / slug (e.g. https://swarms.new/s/06cf5d7c8f92 should be pequalsnp) and other metadata like the swarm's picture on the leftmost bar".

- `PATCH /v1/swarms/:id` (and `/api` for the page, and the MCP tool `swarm_edit`) takes any of `title` (1 to 80 characters, as on create), `nickname` and `picture` (`{ emoji, color }`, one emoji by grapheme from `Picture.colors`, or `null` for the pattern), as one of the swarm's admins (`Team.admin`), the operator's wallet or with the operator's key, the same check as the nickname. `Operations.edit` writes them in one D1 batch (`Store.edit`), so a nickname another swarm holds (409) leaves the title and picture as they were. A new title is re-indexed for search, and the swarm object (`restyled`) bumps its stamp (so the detail's ETag changes), tells the lobby and pushes `detail` to its room, so every open page and pill shows the edit; the lobby's change key now also carries the nickname and picture.
- An uploaded picture is `PUT /v1/swarms/:id/picture` with the image as the body, checked by its bytes (`Picture.sniff`: PNG, JPEG, WebP or GIF; SVG is refused, since it can carry script) and at most 64 KB. R2 is not bound in `wrangler.jsonc`, so it lives in D1, in `swarm_pictures (swarm, emoji, color, type, data, at)` (migration 0038, additive; a check makes a row either an emoji on a color or an image), apart from `swarms` so the list's `select s.*` never carries image bytes. The summary carries only `picture: { emoji, color }` or `{ url: /v1/swarms/<id>/picture?v=<at> }`; `GET` on that url serves the bytes with `nosniff` and `default-src 'none'`, immutable for a year at its current `v` and 60 s otherwise. `DELETE` drops it. `test/edit.test.ts` covers who may edit, validation, the 409 rollback, the search row, the upload's type and size checks, serving by version and switching back to an emoji.
- The page (`Edit.tsx`): a swarm's admins (`Team.admin` through the roster the page already reads) get a pencil beside the channel header's title. It opens a card with the picture (sixteen emoji on nine colors, Upload a picture, Use the pattern), the title and the address (`swarms.new/<nickname>`, checked with `Nickname.invalid` as typed). Save is optimistic: the title, address and picture change at once in the local store's detail and listing rows, so the header, the pill, the switcher chip and the palette show them, and a new address replaces the URL (`App`'s nickname effect); the pencil says Saving… until the server answers, and a refusal (409 for a taken address) puts back what the server holds and reopens the card with the draft and the reason. An upload is cropped to a 128×128 square in a canvas and sent as WebP (PNG where the browser cannot encode WebP), so it stays far under 64 KB. A picture shows in the pill, the phone's switcher chip, the palette and the card instead of the spiral (`Picture.tsx` `Badge`). The state is one record, closed, open (with the error of the last try) or saving.
- Checked on 2026-11-24 under `vite` (port 5610, e2e mocks): with the operator key against the dev Worker, `PATCH` set title, nickname and emoji at once (401 without a session or key), a taken nickname answered 409 and left the title as it was, a 109 KB PNG answered 400, an SVG 400, a 33,788-byte PNG was stored and `GET` on its url returned the same bytes with `immutable`, `nosniff` and `default-src 'none'` (60 s at a stale `v`), `/s/<id>` answered 301 to `/edit-check`, and `DELETE` dropped it. In headless Chrome on the fixture world signed in (`scene=in`) at 1440×900 and 390×844: the pencil opened the card (a bottom sheet on the phone, no sideways overflow), choosing an emoji and violet, retitling and setting `pequalsnp` showed the new title and the pill's emoji 60 ms after Save while Saving… showed, then moved the page to `/swarms/pequalsnp`; a 900×600 PNG chosen through the file input became a 128×128 preview and then the pill's picture. Not run: the 409 rollback in the page (the fixture world has one editable swarm; the rollback path is the same `apply(before)` the code runs on any refusal), and production.

## One column of problems, and tabs when chat and artifacts don't fit (2026-11-24)

- Georgios, 2026-11-24: "get rid of the second column" and "no split UI on mobile; go to tabs when smaller; default tab is chat". The channel sidebar between the pills and the chat (the swarm's name with its dollars, riders and status, `# general Riders and the lead`, `‹ #general on Swarms` and the footer links) is gone at every width. The layout is the pill rail, then the chat, then the artifacts. This replaces the channel sidebar described in the two sections above.
- The swarm's dollars, riders and status appear once, in the strip. The chat header no longer shows the rider count either. Switching channels and threads inside a swarm is a dropdown on the chat's own title (`ChannelMenu`): `# general ⌄` in the room's header, and the channel's title on a branch or program channel. It lists #general, then the Branches or Programs with their threads (four each, the open one in full), with the same rows the sidebar had. It is one record (`{ at: 'closed' } | { at: 'open', path }`): it closes on a pick (the path changes), Escape or a press outside it. Which channel and thread are open reaches it from the `Shell` through a context (`Here`), so no page passes it down. The rail stays a list of problems only, so nothing it shows repeats the strip.
- The footer links (Swarm riders, FAQ, API, Swarm riding by Paradigm) sit at the bottom of the rail under its toggle, in a sticky `.servers-end`. Collapsed, they are short (Riders, FAQ, API, Essay, with the full name as their label and title); expanded and in the phone drawer, they are the full text. Below 900 px the drawer is the rail alone, always with its rows, and the header's hamburger shows only there. From 900 px the rail is always on the page and its chevron expands it.
- Chat and artifacts sit side by side with the drag handle only when the page beside the rail (`.hub-main`, a size container named `hub`) is at least 1000 px wide: from a 1069 px window with the rail collapsed, or 1301 px expanded. Narrower, and on every phone, the swarm page is one pane at a time with the tabs Chat (the default, at the swarm's own address), Board (Threads on a tree swarm), Results, Code and About. They are the same links as the right pane's tabs, so a reload lands on the same tab. A channel and a thread take the chat's or the board's place in the same pane. Because the switch is a container query, the server's HTML is already in its final layout: nothing flashes, and expanding the rail can switch a window between the two layouts. The `Divider` is horizontal only now; its vertical phone split, `--split` and the 719 px media query are gone. In the split, the Board tab is the one drawn as current on the chat's address (`data-default`), since the chat is beside it.
- Measured on the fixture world under `vite` (port 5600, e2e mocks) in headless Chrome, on `/`, `/s/bench-coders`, `/s/p-vs-np` and `/s/bench-coders/c/3`. The chat's left edge and width were the same on all four at each size: 1440×900 105+787 px (artifacts 473), 1100×800 93+586 (artifacts 357), 900×800 93+768 in tabs, 768×1024 and 390×844 touch the full width in tabs. No sidebar column at any size. No sideways overflow, and the document is the viewport's height. Load CLS was 0.0027 or less (0 on 768 and 390). With the rail expanded at 1440, the chat is 337+555. #general below 1000 px shows the chat alone, as phones did before.

## Every route answers as documented (2026-11-24)

Georgios: "make EVERY sub-endpoint work".

- `test/e2e/endpoints.ts` (`pnpm endpoints [base]`) sweeps every route the Worker serves on one base (`SWARMS_BASE`, default https://swarms.new; https://paradigm.xyz/swarms works too): the pages, `/x`, `/tg`, `/og`, `/oembed`, artifacts and feedback, every `/api` read the web app makes, every `/v1` route against `/openapi.json` (a small JSON Schema check of each 200 body), the five sockets (to their `welcome`), the two event streams (to their first event), and `/mcp` (`initialize`, `tools/list`, then every reading tool with real ids and every writing tool with no arguments, which must be refused). It finds ids itself (a swarm with a machine that ran commands, its newest room message, a branch, an artifact, a rider, a nickname, a tree swarm and its root). Writes are probed only with bodies that must be refused (400 or 401) or that change nothing (`/transfer` only prepares a link, an unsigned GitHub hook counts nothing); the hand token route is listed but not called, since anyone may call it and it lists a machine on the swarm. With `SWARMS_TOKEN` it adds the signed-in reads and refused writes. Each GET is timed `SWARMS_SAMPLES` times (3 by default); the table gives the median, the Worker's own `server-timing` total, and the run p50 and p95 against a 300 ms target, leaving out the long polls. `test/e2e/endpoints.test.ts` asserts every row passes; `SWARMS_BASE=local` instead starts `pnpm dev` on 5620 with the mock gateway on 6620, signs in, names the rider, makes a nicknamed agent swarm and a tree swarm, rides both and posts in the room, and sweeps that.
- First sweep of production (https://swarms.new, 2026-11-24, from dev-georgios, 2 samples): 170 routes, 18 rows off. Most were the sweep's own wrong expectations (a single-segment path is an unclaimed nickname's page and answers 200 by design, `/feedback` is the feedback board's JSON, tree-only routes answer 404 on an agent swarm, `/transfer` with no body prepares a link). The real ones: `/v1/swarms/{id}/chat/{message}` and its reactions answered "No such route" (threads were not deployed yet); and one probe was a mistake: `POST /v1/swarms/06cf5d7c8f92/hands` with `{}` issued a hand token, so that swarm's Machines tab lists an unconnected `hand-b341c6` (started 2026-11-24 22:41 UTC). The sweep no longer calls it. Wall p50 62 ms, p95 1,003 ms; server p50 0 ms, p95 428 ms. Slowest by the Worker's own time: `/api/riders/:who` 762 ms and `/v1/riders/:who` 508 to 745 ms (91 D1 queries, one per swarm gakonst rode), `GET /v1/swarms/{id}` as an event stream 880 ms to its first event, `/oembed` 321 ms (two D1 queries on the primary and the swarm object), and every route that reads D1 off the read session paid the primary round trip, 150 to 180 ms (`d1.primary`, against 8 to 20 ms on the `eeur` replica).
- The local sweep on main found `setup.template` "board" (a tree swarm's) missing from the OpenAPI enum: `Setup.kinds` is now the one list of templates a setup can name, and the schema reads it.
- More reads go through the D1 read session (`Replica.readable`): `/ideas` (the renamed `/join`), `/u/:who`, tree routes (`progress`, `tree`, `nodes/:nid`, `nodes/:nid/updates`, `deltas`, `ledger`, `proofs`), a thread (`chat/:message`), `/riders/:who`, `/names/:name`, `/general/chat`, `/me/swarms/:id`, `/oembed` and `/og/*`. Each of their swarm lookups already falls back to the primary when a replica has not seen the swarm, and a writer's bookmark cookie keeps read-your-writes.
- Your own AI degrades instead of failing: the gateway's credentials routes (tempoxyz/gateway#720) are not deployed, so `/v1/credentials/rider:0x…` answered "No route answers GET …" and `/me/ai` showed that text. `Gateway.credentialed` asks the gateway once per isolate and per gateway URL every 5 minutes, with no key, for `/v1/credentials/rider:0x000…0`: a 404 whose detail starts "No route" means not offered, anything else under 500 means offered (401 there means the route exists), a 5xx keeps the last answer (false before any). `/v1/config` carries it as `ownAi`; every `/v1/me/ai` route and turning a swarm onto your own AI answer 503 `unavailable` while it is false; `/me/ai` renders "Your own AI is not available yet." on the server from the boot config (and switches to it if a call answers that 503), and the swarm page leaves out the Run on my own AI switch. The mock gateway's `credentialRoute: false` answers as the real gateway does today; `test/e2e/mock.test.ts` checks both answers.
- A rider's profile read each of their swarms with its own query (`Store.find` per swarm, about 100 in parallel for gakonst); it now reads them in one (`Store.findAll`, `where s.id in (select value from json_each(?))`).
- The account menu leaves out Use your own AI while `ownAi` is false. Checked under `pnpm dev` with the mock gateway's `credentialRoute` off and on, in headless Chrome at 1440×900 and 390×844: off, `/v1/config` answered `ownAi: false`, `/me/ai`'s server HTML held "Your own AI is not available yet." (and not the sign-in prompt), no console errors, no sideways overflow; on, `ownAi: true` and the sign-in prompt as before.
- The sweep's expectations, corrected after that first run, then passed on production as deployed (an older main) on both bases, 181 routes each, 3 samples: https://swarms.new wall p50 41 ms, p95 590 ms, server p50 0 ms, p95 235 ms; https://paradigm.xyz/swarms wall p50 37 ms, p95 410 ms, server p50 0 ms, p95 166 ms. Sockets are opened where the page opens them (the server render's `Boot.socket`, `swarms.tempo-dev.workers.dev` behind paradigm.xyz, whose proxy still answers an upgrade with 1101). Over 300 ms there: the riders reads (above), `GET /v1/swarms/{id}` 421 ms of Worker time (a primary D1 read and the swarm object), MCP `swarm_artifacts` 1,248 ms where `/v1/swarms/{id}/artifacts` answered from the edge cache in 15 ms, and every socket: its 101 took about 700 ms from FRA (curl, three runs) while the Worker's own span for it was 117 ms, and the welcome follows at once.
- `GET /v1/swarms/{id}` (and MCP `swarm_get`) spent 750 to 840 ms in the swarm object on 06cf5d7c8f92 (`do.view`), against 140 ms for the page's `/api` detail: it shipped every update's whole text (478 updates, about 650 KB of text, 137 KB in the newest 100) over RPC only to cut each to its excerpt in the Worker. The object now cuts them (`view({ excerpts: true })`, the same `Answer.excerpt`), so the answer is unchanged and the RPC carries about 107 KB. Not measured on production yet.
- A swarm's artifact list was kept 15 s in the isolate and the colo cache and then read again from the gateway while the reader waited, three `/p/…/kv` pages at about 470 ms each: MCP `swarm_artifacts` took 1,248 and 1,423 ms of Worker time on two sweeps while `/v1/swarms/{id}/artifacts` answered from the isolate in 0 ms. It now goes through `Edge.cached` (fresh 15 s, then served stale for up to 10 minutes while one background read refreshes it), so only a list nobody read for 10 minutes makes its reader wait. The swarm object's own read after a publish still goes to the gateway.
- After, on main under `SWARMS_BASE=local` (signed in, 3 samples): 205 routes, 0 failed; wall p50 8 ms, p95 19 ms, server p50 1 ms, p95 7 ms (local D1, so these say nothing about production's round trips). Not checked: production after these commits, which needs a deploy.

## Search problems from anywhere, and a first-run welcome (2026-11-24)

Georgios, 2026-11-24: "make onboarding and search through problems sick: when you onboard (and in the normal view) you can search titles fuzzily".

- Search is one palette (`Search.tsx`), opened by ⌘K or Ctrl+K on every shell page and by the search pill under #general in the rail (a row with ⌘K when the rail is expanded, and the top of the drawer on a phone). It matches problem titles and goals and the channel and thread titles of every swarm whose shape the page knows (`channels.ts` `useShapes`); the first query also reads the shapes of the top eight problems not yet seen (their tree at depth 2, or their branches), once a session. With no query it lists the top problems in the rail's order. Each result shows its picture or spiral (or # and the item's mark), the title with the matched letters underlined, the goal excerpt around the match or the channel path, its dollars and its status. ↑ and ↓ move, Enter opens, Escape and a tap outside close; a phone gets the palette full screen with Cancel. Nothing matching offers Start a problem $1+ with the query, which `/new?q=` now puts in the question box (a full page load still answers 301 to the name's page, as before).
- Matching (`shared/Fuzzy.ts`, `test/fuzzy.test.ts`): each word of the query must match, in any order, accents folded; a substring scores by length, a word start and how early it sits, and a word of three or more letters may match its letters in order with gaps (bonuses for word starts and runs, a cap on the span). When any candidate matches every word as a substring, the in-order-only matches are dropped, because "lean" matched "Cook–Levin: SAT is NP-complete" by its letters.
- A first-time visitor on #general (no `swarms_hello` cookie; `shared/Hello.ts`) sees the welcome (`Hello.tsx`) where #general's intro was: one line on what Swarms is, "What do you want to see solved?" over a search box, the top five matching problems as they type, Start a problem $1+ with the query when nothing matches, and "Start <query> as a new problem" under the matches. The server reads the cookie into `Boot.hello` and the page cache's variant (only for `/`), so it renders in place with no shift. Its ×, any link in it, Enter on a result and leaving #general set the cookie for a year, and it never shows again. `test/hello.test.ts` and `test/page-server.test.ts` cover the cookie and the server render.
- Checked on 2026-11-24 under `vite` (port 5610, e2e mocks) in headless Chrome: ⌘K opened the palette with the input focused; "np" found Does P = NP? (NP underlined) and no in-order-only match; "lean" found P = NP by its goal; on the P = NP tree's channels and threads ArrowDown then Enter opened `/s/p-vs-np/t/a2c3be6dd7` and closed the palette; "bench" found the agent swarm at 390×844, full screen, no sideways overflow. The server render without the cookie holds `class="hello"` and with `swarms_hello=1` holds the old welcome; load CLS 0 at 390×844 with and without the welcome, and at 1440×900 0.0055 both with and without it (the dev server's late stylesheet, as measured before) and 0 on a second load. Dismissing set `swarms_hello=1` and the reload showed the old welcome.
- Checked on production (2abe416, GET only, Playwright Chromium from dev-georgios, non-GET requests aborted; the only ones were Tempo ID's own analytics beacons), 2026-11-25, at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone: without the cookie the server HTML of / holds `class="hello"` and with `swarms_hello=1` does not; the welcome shows with load CLS 0 on both, "np" lists Solve P = NP., and × sets `swarms_hello=1`. Ctrl+K opened the palette in 21 ms with the input focused (the drawer's search pill on the phone, 470 ms including the drawer), "np" found Solve P = NP., Enter opened `/s/06cf5d7c8f92` and closed it, no sideways overflow. Signed out, a swarm page has no edit button or card. `/me/ai` says "Your own AI is not available yet." in its server HTML and on the page (`/v1/config` `ownAi: false`). `/u/<gakonst>`: HTML in 92 to 130 ms from the colo cache (6 to 8 ms of Worker time), LCP 1,328 ms on the phone. Hovering a rail swarm fetched `/api/swarms/:id?feed=0`; the click routed in 45 ms and painted its title 148 ms after the click with no document load, and Forward came back in 13 ms. Reactions and thread replies render on the deployed build in the fixture room (`/s/p-vs-np?fixture`: 4 reactions, 2 thread chips, the thread opens with its 3 replies on both sizes); production's rooms have no reactions or replies yet, so none were seen on live data. Screenshots: `~/swarms-work/a67/prod/` on dev-georgios.
- Found there: a problem whose goal is only its title showed it twice in the palette and the welcome (Solve P = NP. over Solve P = NP.). A goal line that repeats the title, ignoring case, accents and punctuation (`Fuzzy.same`), is now left out (`test/fuzzy.test.ts`).

## Owed payments retry from the store, and every funding flow's failures are covered (2026-11-24)

- A transfer the wallet sent but the server could not count yet (`owed`) was retried by a timer in `useFlow`, so the retry ran only while a component showing that slot was mounted: an uncounted sub-item whose sheet closed waited for `Pay.resume` or a reload. `Pay.move` now schedules the retry itself whenever a slot enters `owed` (at `retries[tries]`) and clears it on any other move, so the store retries with nothing on screen and `useFlow` only reads the flow. `test/pay.test.ts` checks that a 502 `upstream_failed` on counting leaves the slot `owed` and the store counts it after `retries[0]` with no component.
- `test/e2e/fund.test.ts` adds, at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone: a sub-item and a new swarm through a cancel, an empty wallet then Add funds, a transfer sent but not counted at first, and a refusal (one cheer each, one swarm with one ride after a refusal); Add funds from the account menu declined (says Cancelled, the balance unchanged, retryable, a tap elsewhere closes the menu); and an agent swarm ride cancelled, short then funded, and refused, with the raised total unchanged when nothing was paid. A Chrome DevTools command that never answers now fails after 60 s (`test/e2e/chrome.ts`) instead of hanging the run.
- `test/e2e/fund.test.ts` on main (2abe416 plus the sponsor cap), whole suite with `--test-concurrency=1` on the mocks, 2026-11-25: 16 of 17 passed. The refused new swarm failed on both viewports: the start lands on the new swarm's page at once and its ride sheet should reopen there with "Sent, but it cannot be counted.", but the fixture world built every agent swarm archived (`archivedAt` ten hours ago, since the fixture swarms are old), including one just created, so the page treated it as read-only and never opened the sheet. The real Worker never archives a new swarm. The fixture's `create` now makes it unarchived; after, the suite passed 17 of 17 (about five minutes on dev-georgios), with the refused start's sheet showing the alert and its transaction link at 390×844 (`fund-new-refused-phone.png`).

## A swarm's detail says why its last model call failed (2026-11-24)

"Solve P = NP." (`06cf5d7c8f92`) showed only Retrying while every model call failed the same way for days, and the reason was readable only by paging `/v1/swarms/:id/feed` for operator lines the page hides. The latest feed on 2026-11-24 (read-only, `GET /v1/swarms/06cf5d7c8f92/feed?before=999999999999&limit=200`) held 47 such failures between 10:29 and 23:16 UTC, the last one `Messages HTTP 503` "Tempo Agents cannot pay the network fee to open your key's channel right now: its fee sponsor is out of funds…".

- The swarm object keeps the last `model.call.failed` the gateway reported as `failure` in its kv (`Follow.refused` writes it, `Follow.failure` reads it): `{ at, code, message, route, status }`. `route` is the gateway's transport word (Messages, Responses), `status` the HTTP status when there is one, `code` the error's `type`/`code` (or a stream error's code), and `message` the error's own message out of its JSON body, redacted (`Trail.redact`) and clipped to 600 characters. "the turn was cancelled", which the swarm causes itself, does not replace it.
- `GET /v1/swarms/:id` (`Overview`) and the stamped detail (`Detail`) carry it as `failure`, null until a call fails; it stays after the agent goes on, so readers compare its `at` with the feed. Documented in the OpenAPI schema. No migration: it lives in the Durable Object's kv.
- Checks: `test/follow.test.ts` (a 503 with a JSON body, a cancelled call that does not replace it, a stream error and a 402 problem body).


## A gateway that cannot pay its own fees is not the agent's fault (2026-11-24)

The same swarm's agent was replaced about every four hours while nothing was wrong with it. Each model call hung about 11 minutes and then failed with the gateway's 503 "…its fee sponsor is out of funds. This key has no OUSD, pathUSD or USDC.e limit, so it cannot pay the fee itself…"; one such failure per turn never reached the eight unpaid calls (`Follow.starved`) that end a turn as refused, so the turn ran to its 30-minute lease, ended as "made no model call or tool call", was blamed on the agent, and after eight blamed tries (`Turns.retry`) `exhausted` started a new agent. The fee payer the gateway documents (0x4cC7C5c526B16a0137270C48D84f826E16C99965) held 0.000092 pathUSD and no OUSD or USDC.e on Tempo mainnet on 2026-11-24 (read with viem's `balanceOf` over https://rpc.tempo.xyz). The key whose channel cannot open is the backer's MACH-only pledge key (see the channel-open entry above), not `SWARMS_GATEWAY_KEY`, which has stablecoin limits.

- `Turns.broke` counts "cannot pay the network fee" as unpaid, and `Turns.finish` treats a failed turn that saw an unpaid model call as refused (unless the swarm's balance grew meanwhile): it pauses for money with a wake, like any refused payment, and its attempt is not blamed, so it never counts toward replacing the agent.
- `Turns.sponsorless` recognises the fee sponsor's message. One such failure ends the turn at once (`Follow.starved`) instead of waiting out the lease, since retrying inside the turn cannot help, and the pause carries `sponsor: true`, whose reason (`Phase.reason`) reads "Waiting on the gateway's fee sponsor: the gateway cannot pay the network fee for its AI calls right now, though the swarm has money. It tries again by itself." The ride box shows a refused pause's reason while the swarm is Stuck instead of the generic retry line. Wakes back off from 10 minutes (`Turns.paying`).
- The gateway side is not fixed here: its fee payer needs funds, and its 503 comes only after its own retries (about 11 minutes).
- Checks: `test/run.test.ts` (a lease-ended turn after unpaid calls pauses as refused; a sponsor failure pauses with `sponsor`, says so and keeps the agent), `test/follow.test.ts` (one sponsor failure starves the turn), `test/turns.test.ts` (`broke` and `sponsorless`).

- A fee-sponsor pause's wake no longer doubles without end (2026-11-25): `Turns.paying` doubles from 10 minutes per refused attempt, so a sponsor outage of a day pushed the next try out to the 60-minute patience cap (`Phase.maxPatienceMs`) and the swarm sat out most of each hour after the sponsor was funded. A pause with `sponsor` now waits `Turns.wakeIn(attempts, true)`, at most 15 minutes (`Turns.sponsorWaitMs`); other refused payments keep the doubling. `test/turns.test.ts` checks both schedules and `test/run.test.ts` that a sponsor pause wakes within 15 minutes.

## Sockets skip the app's middleware (2026-11-24)

- Measured from FRA (curl `--http1.1` upgrade, three runs each, GET only) against production (c3b41cd): the five sockets answered 101 after 583 to 1,281 ms while the 101's own `server-timing` said the Worker was done after 104 to 124 ms (`do.fetch` 111 to 116 ms for a room: the swarm objects and the lobby sit near IAD, about 100 ms from FRA). The same on `swarms.tempo-dev.workers.dev`, so not the custom domain. Cloudflare's own Durable Object chat demo, with its room created from IAD and opened from FRA, answered 101 in 130 to 145 ms. The difference is what the Worker does with the object's 101: Hono's `c.res` setter rebuilds the response (`new Response(res.body, res)`) whenever an earlier middleware has a response, and the timing middleware builds another to add `server-timing`, so the socket reached the client through two or three wrappers, where the demo returns the object's response itself.
- The Worker's `fetch` now answers a GET with `upgrade: websocket` to `/api|v1/swarms/live`, `/api|v1/general/chat/live` and `/api|v1/swarms/:id/chat/live` (with or without the base) before the Hono app, returning the object's own response untouched (`upgraded` in `index.ts`). A room still checks the swarm exists (on the D1 read session, the primary on a miss) and answers 404 without opening a room. Sockets carry no `server-timing` any more. A plain GET of those routes still goes through the app (400). `test/sockets.test.ts`. Under `SWARMS_BASE=local` (workerd, port 5631) the sweep passed, 231 routes, all six sockets to their `welcome`. Not measured: production, which needs a deploy; if the 101 still lags the Worker's span by about 500 ms there, the next suspect is the hibernation API (`acceptWebSocket`), which the demo does not use.
- paradigm.xyz/swarms answers every upgrade with 500 `error code: 1101` after about 800 ms, so its proxy Worker receives Swarms' 101 and then throws. That Worker belongs to paradigm.xyz and is not in the gakonst or paradigmxyz repositories gh can search. The fix is there: return the upstream response itself when `response.status === 101` (or `response.webSocket` is set), before rebuilding it to rewrite headers, since `new Response(body, { status: 101 })` without the `webSocket` throws. Until then `Boot.socket` keeps pages on `swarms.tempo-dev.workers.dev`.
- The slowest reads on production then, medians of five from FRA, and what main already does about them: `GET /v1/swarms/{id}` (and its event stream's snapshot) 539 ms, 479 in the Worker, `do.view` 400 to 480 ms: main cuts the update excerpts in the object (b2bec85). `/v1/riders/{who}` and `/api/riders/{who}` 670 to 680 ms, 91 D1 queries on the primary: main reads the swarms in one query (f313232) through the read session (8bdbeaf). `/u/{who}` 767 ms though its headers left after 30 ms: the body waits for the same rider read. `/oembed` 376 ms, its D1 reads at a primary's distance (about 160 ms each): main reads it through the read session (8bdbeaf), leaving `do.view` (about 117 ms). `/{nickname}` unclaimed 388 ms, two D1 reads of about 165 ms: a name no swarm holds misses the replica and is asked again on the primary, by design, so a name claimed a moment ago is found; main leaves it so.

## Adding a hand needs a sign-in (2026-11-24)

- `POST /v1/swarms/:id/hands`, its `/api` twin and the MCP tool `swarm_hand` now answer 401 `unauthorized` (a Problem) unless the caller is signed in (any rider's session from `/v1/auth`, read with `Sessions.who`, which covers the swarm's owner) or sends the operator's gateway key as the bearer token (`Operations.keyed`). Before, anyone could issue a hand token, which lists a machine on the swarm; the route sweep's anonymous `POST …/hands` with `{}` issued one on a live swarm that way. The check comes before the body is parsed, so an anonymous caller learns nothing about the swarm or the label. The web app's Connect box already issues through `signed`, which signs in first.
- Checked with `test/unhand.test.ts` (anonymous answers 401 and mints nothing at the gateway, a signed-in rider and the operator get a hand) and `test/lending.test.ts`, which now issues as a signed-in rider.

## Owners can remove a hand (2026-11-24)

- `DELETE /v1/swarms/:id/hands/:hand`, its `/api` twin and the MCP tool `swarm_hand_remove` (`Operations.unhand`, `Swarm.unlend`, `Hands.remove`) remove a lent hand. Only the swarm's admins (its owner and team admins, `Team.admin`), the operator's wallet signed in, or the operator's gateway key as the bearer token may: anonymous answers 401, any other rider 403, a malformed name 400, a hand the swarm never issued 404, an archived swarm 410.
- The gateway revokes it first (`DELETE /v1/hands/<name>` under the swarm's `Gateway-App`, which closes a machine attached with that token and drops the token: tempoxyz/gateway `src/Hands.ts` `revoke`). A gateway failure answers 502 and changes nothing here, so the owner can retry; a gateway 404 means it holds nothing more to revoke, and the removal goes on. Then the hand's lending claim is returned (`free`), its D1 `hands` row is deleted, so it leaves `GET /hands`, and the swarm object drops it from its connected hands and remembers its name in `removed`, which `fleet` filters, so it also leaves `GET /machines` (the Machines tab) though its feed rows stay. Clients get `changed` for hands and members.
- The operator's way without a browser session: `curl -X DELETE https://swarms.new/v1/swarms/<id>/hands/<name> -H "authorization: Bearer $SWARMS_GATEWAY_KEY"`.
- Checked with `test/unhand.test.ts` against the mock gateway: refusals (401, 403, 400, 404) revoke nothing; an admin's removal revokes at the gateway and the hand leaves both `hands` and `machines` while the other stays; a second removal answers 404; the operator removes the last one; a gateway 500 answers 502 and keeps the hand listed; a gateway 404 still removes it.

## The route sweep never writes to a live site (2026-11-24)

- `test/e2e/endpoints.ts` sent writes to whatever base it swept: refusals it expected (`POST /v1/swarms` with `{}`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`), but also `POST /v1/auth/challenge`, `/v1/auth/logout`, `/v1/hooks/github` and MCP's writing tools with no arguments, and once an anonymous `POST …/hands` that issued a real hand. Now every mutating request (`POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`, and every MCP tool not marked `readOnlyHint`) goes out only when the base is local (`Endpoints.local`: localhost, 127.0.0.1, [::1], 0.0.0.0 or *.localhost), or when `SWARMS_SWEEP_WRITES=1` and it targets a throwaway swarm the sweep created itself (`run({ made })`, a `/v1` or `/api` path under that swarm; MCP writes never go to a live site). Every other one is a row with status skip and note `skipped (write)`. The gate is one check at the top of `call` and one in `mcp` (`Endpoints.unwritable`). Reads, sockets, event streams and MCP's `initialize`, `tools/list` and read-only tools still run everywhere.
- The sweep now expects anonymous `POST …/hands` and `DELETE …/hands/:hand` (both twins) to answer 401, and signed in a bad label 400 and removing an unknown hand 403 or 404; on a live site all of them are `skipped (write)`.
- `test/sweep.test.ts` is the guard: it runs the whole sweep with `fetch` and `WebSocket` stubbed against https://swarms.new and https://paradigm.xyz/swarms, signed out, signed in, and signed in with `SWARMS_SWEEP_WRITES=1` but no swarm of its own, and fails if any mutating request (other than MCP's reads) would be sent; with a swarm of its own it allows writes only under that swarm, and against 127.0.0.1 every write still goes out.
- The throwaway swarm (2026-11-25). With `SWARMS_SWEEP_WRITES=1`, a signed-in `SWARMS_TOKEN` and the operator's gateway key in `SWARMS_OPERATOR_KEY`, the sweep makes its own swarm (`Endpoints.make`, `POST /v1/swarms`, template cheap, no money) on a local base, or on a live site only with `SWARMS_SWEEP_LIVE_WRITES=1` as well (`Endpoints.throwaway`). Every write probe aimed at the swarm under test then goes to the throwaway instead (`Endpoints.retarget`), reads stay on the swarm it found, and `Endpoints.exercise` makes real writes there: a message, a reply in its thread, a reaction, a rename, a hand issued then removed, and reads the thread back. Last, in a `finally`, it archives the swarm with the operator key (`POST /v1/swarms/:id/archive`) and checks that a rename then answers 410. MCP writes still never go to a live site. `pnpm endpoints` on production without those variables sends no write, as before.
- Signed-in sweeps (2026-11-25). With `SWARMS_TOKEN` the sweep first reads `GET /v1/me`: a token that is not a live session is one failed row ("not a live session") and no signed-in probes, instead of a page of 401s. A live session adds the signed-in reads and the rider's own `/v1/riders/:me` and `/u/:me`; its writes stay `skipped (write)` on a live site. Not run against production: a session there takes a signed `POST /v1/auth`, so it is checked with the stubbed sweep (`test/sweep.test.ts`) and under `SWARMS_BASE=local`.
- Under `SWARMS_BASE=local` the sweep now makes, writes to and archives a throwaway on every run (`test/e2e/endpoints.test.ts` passes the mock gateway's key as the operator), and covers every tree-swarm route (`/api` and `/v1` `tree`, `proofs`, `ledger`, `deltas`, `nodes/:nid`, `nodes/:nid/updates`, the four node writes, `/s|x|tg/:tree/n/:nid`, `/me/swarms/:tree` and MCP `swarm_tree`). Run on 2026-11-25 on main: 249 routes, 0 failed, wall p50 9 ms, p95 35 ms. What a local world still cannot reach: a machine's commands, logs and export (no machine runs commands on the mocks) and the branch writes (no subagents); production covers the machine routes.
- `test/sweep.test.ts` adds: when a throwaway is made, on which bases and with which variables; that writes move to it and reads stay; that an allowed live sweep sends one `POST /v1/swarms`, writes only under its swarm and archives it last; and that a dead token fails its row.
- Production GET sweeps, 2026-11-25, from dev-georgios (`pnpm endpoints https://swarms.new`, 3 samples, no writes). On 2abe416 at about 07:45 UTC: 201 routes, 0 failed, 9 over 300 ms; wall p50 55 ms, p95 343 ms; server p50 10 ms, p95 204 ms. Over 300 ms besides the sockets, `/v1/swarms/:id` and its stream, `swarm_get` and `swarm_machines` (another change's work): the two long polls (`/wait` and MCP `swarm_wait`, 1 s by design) and MCP `swarm_branches` at 1,525 ms of Worker time, which is the branch list's 1.5 s `patience` for a slow refresh; six later reads of `/v1/swarms/{id}/branches` 6 s apart (each past the 5 s fresh window, so each refreshed) took 125 to 165 ms of Worker time (`do.subagents` 107 to 121 ms), and the second sweep had it under 300 ms, so it was one slow subagent listing, not a route to fix. The second sweep at about 08:40 UTC, after a deploy of a newer main: 13 failed, every read of one swarm's view answering 500 (`/api|v1/swarms/:id`, `/answer`, `/backers`, `/x/s/:id`, `/tg/s/:id`, `/oembed`, the card), and MCP `swarm_get` saying "The RPC receiver does not implement the method \"viewed\"": the Worker calls the swarm object's new `viewed` while the objects run a version without it. Not fixed here; reported to the change's owner.

## A swarm's detail always carries its failure and says why it is stuck (2026-11-25)

- The post-deploy sweep of 2abe416 (2026-11-25 08:01 UTC) failed `GET /v1/swarms/{id}` on "Solve P = NP." (`06cf5d7c8f92`) with `$.failure: missing`, on swarms.new and paradigm.xyz/swarms. One read at 08:02 still had no `failure` key; from 08:03 on, 40 reads in a row carried `failure: null`. The Worker builds the detail from the swarm object's `view`, and for a minute after the deploy the object still ran the code from before 16aed7a, whose view has no `failure`, so `JSON.stringify` dropped the undefined field. `Operations.get` and `Operations.stamped` now write `failure: view.failure ?? null` and `reason: view.reason ?? null`, so the detail matches its schema whichever version the object runs.
- `failure` was null although the feed held a fee-sponsor 503 every ten minutes, because the object only keeps failures it sees after 16aed7a. `Follow.recalled` reads the last `model.call.failed` back from the feed (its `error` in the row's detail, skipping "the turn was cancelled") the first time a swarm with no kept failure is viewed, and keeps it, or null when there is none, so the scan runs once per object.
- `reason` was null while `why` was stuck, since only a pause had a reason. `Swarm.reason` (used by the view and the room's pulse) now gives a stuck swarm one too (`Phase.stuck`): the fee sponsor's line when its last failure is the sponsor's 503 (`Turns.sponsorless`), the same line c83c2a5 gives the sponsor pause; "Its model calls keep failing: the gateway answered 503. It tries again by itself." for another failure; "Its last turns made no progress. It tries again by itself." with none. The ride box shows a stuck swarm's reason as it already did for a refused pause.
- Checks: `test/run.test.ts` (a stuck swarm's reason with no failure, a 500 and the sponsor's 503, and null once it progresses; the feed read-back skips a cancelled call and runs once), `test/nickname.test.ts` (`Operations.get` carries `failure` and `reason` as null). Not measured: production, which needs a deploy; then `curl -s https://swarms.new/v1/swarms/06cf5d7c8f92 | jq '{failure, reason, why}'` should show the sponsor's 503 and its reason while the sponsor is empty, and the sweep's `/v1/swarms/:id` row should pass.

## Faster reads: feedback readiness, empty hands, unchanged swarm views (2026-11-25)

- Measured from FRA against production (2abe416, reads only, 12 samples each, `fetch` from node, server = the Worker's `server-timing` total): `GET /feedback` p50 27 ms, p95 3,117 ms (server p95 2,970; the post-deploy sweep had 1,667 median); `GET /v1/swarms/{id}` p50 287, p95 706 (server 264 / 686); its event stream's first event p50 651, p95 1,765; `/v1/swarms/{id}/hands` p50 217, p95 468; MCP `swarm_get` median 311, max 573. Test swarm 06cf5d7c8f92.
- `GET /feedback` is nanocode-ts' `Handler.feedback` readiness check, which asks the gateway's `/api/keys/me` once per isolate and holds the answer. The gateway's `/api/keys/me` took 0.6 to 5.5 s, and requests spread over many isolates, so most GETs paid it. The handler's `fetch` (`Feedback.send`) now answers `/api/keys/me` from `Gateway.wallet`, which already keeps the operator's wallet in the colo cache for a day (`Edge.cached`), and goes to the gateway only when that has no wallet. Filing a report (`POST`) runs the same check, so it skips that call too.
- A swarm object's `view` costs one round trip from the Worker (about 110 ms from FRA: the objects sit near IAD) plus its payload: `/updates?limit=1` (3 KB) took 112 ms in the object span, `limit=50` (143 KB) 210 ms, and `GET /v1/swarms/{id}`'s view (478 updates with excerpts, 110 KB) 190 to 510 ms. `Swarm.viewed(shape, known)` answers `null` when the object's stamp is quiet and equals `known`, and the full view otherwise; the Worker (`Operations.viewed`, used by `load`, `ledgered` and `backers`) keeps the last quiet view per swarm and shape in the colo cache (`Edge.held`/`Edge.hold`, 60 s) under the current minute, and sends its version. So an unchanged swarm costs one round trip with no payload, and the answer is the view the object would have given, by the same rule as the detail's ETag (`sd`: quiet stamp, same minute). `about` and `failure` now bump the stamp (`viewKeys`), since the view shows them. `test/viewed.test.ts`.
- `/v1/swarms/{id}/hands` read the hand rows inside the swarm object. Now the Worker reads them on the read session together with `Store.exists`, and asks the object (gateway listing, `seen` marks) only when the swarm issued any hand, so a swarm with none answers without the object.
- MCP's reads come back from the D1 primary (`d1.primary` 165 to 680 ms in `swarm_get` and `swarm_backers`) though `Mcp.handle` gives them `Replica.read`, while the same reads over `/v1` come from `d1.eeur` in 10 to 15 ms. Not fixed here: the cause did not show in the code, and it needs a deploy to test.
- Checks: `pnpm check`, `pnpm test`, `pnpm build`; the route sweep under `SWARMS_BASE=local` (workerd, 5 samples): 236 routes, 0 failed, wall p95 23 ms, server p95 9 ms (local D1 and objects, so it shows the routes still answer, not production's round trips). Expected on production after a deploy: `/feedback` under 50 ms once the colo holds the wallet; `GET /v1/swarms/{id}`, its event stream and `swarm_get` about 120 to 150 ms in the Worker for a quiet swarm (one object round trip), a changing one as before; `/hands` for a swarm without hands about 20 ms. Not measured: production, which needs a deploy.

## The swarm's About tab lists its machines, and its admins remove one (2026-11-25)

- The web app had no way to call `DELETE /v1/swarms/:id/hands/:hand` (fb0d20b), and no machines view since 6ea3f53, so removing a hand such as `hand-b341c6`, which an anonymous sweep issued on "Solve P = NP." (`06cf5d7c8f92`), needed curl with the operator key. The About tab now shows a Machines list under who steers (`src/web/Machines.tsx`), read from `GET /api/swarms/:id/hands` and reread when the room says `hands` changed; it shows only when the swarm lends at least one hand and is not archived. Each row is the hand's label (its name on hover) and connected or offline.
- The swarm's admins (`Roster.admins`) and the operator's wallet (`Config.credit.owner`, the same address `Operations.owned` lets through) see Remove on each row (`Fleet.removes`). Remove asks first in place ("Remove it?" or Keep); confirming drops the row at once, sends the DELETE through `Api.unhand` (signed, so an expired session signs in again), puts the row back and shows the server's problem in an alert if it fails (403, 502 when the gateway did not revoke), and rereads the list either way.
- Checks: `test/fleet.test.ts` (`Fleet.removes`: an admin in any case, the operator, not another rider, not signed out); headless Chrome on the fixture world (`/s/bench-coders/about?fixture&scene=in,open`) at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone: the list, Remove, the in-place confirm, and no Remove signed out (`scene=open`). Not checked: a real removal, which needs a deploy and an admin session; then Georgios removes `hand-b341c6` from https://swarms.new/s/06cf5d7c8f92/about and `GET /v1/swarms/06cf5d7c8f92/hands` no longer lists it.

## Socket upgrades wait on the Cloudflare account, not the Worker (2026-11-25)

After 46dc47d production (2abe416, version f0b41f6e) still answered WebSocket upgrades from FRA (curl `--http1.1` upgrade, GET only) in 0.73 to 2.2 s on swarms.new and 0.78 to 1.28 s on swarms.tempo-dev.workers.dev: `/v1/swarms/live` 0.73 to 2.09 s, `/v1/general/chat/live` 0.72 to 2.19 s, `/v1/swarms/06cf5d7c8f92/chat/live` 0.73 to 1.55 s, with about one in ten at 0.14 to 0.17 s. The time is spent before the Worker runs, not in the Worker, the objects or the hibernation API, so no change to `index.ts`, `Lobby.ts` or `Swarm.ts` can remove it.

- Workers Observability (tempo-obs `cf_workers_query`, env dev, by each request's `cf-ray`, with the send time recorded on the hand, clock NTP-synced): each upgrade's stateless invocation ran 96 to 132 ms (one round trip to the object near IAD; rooms add the D1 check, about 11 ms on the replica), the Lobby object's own invocation 0 to 2 ms of wall time, and the invocation ended within 5 ms of the moment curl saw the 101. So the Worker started 560 to 1,080 ms after the request reached FRA. A plain read through the same object (`GET /v1/general/chat?limit=1`) took 141 to 157 ms end to end and its invocation ended within 3 ms of the client's first byte, as it should.
- The delay comes from the WebSocket handshake itself, not from returning a socket: `/x/card.svg` (no I/O) took 31 to 73 ms plain and 566 to 695 ms with `Connection: Upgrade`, `Upgrade: websocket` and the `Sec-WebSocket-*` headers, while its invocation ran 5 to 7 ms of wall time and ended within 2 ms of the client's first byte. A missing room (404, no object opened) took 0.76 to 1.69 s. `Upgrade: foo`, `Upgrade: websocket` without `Connection`, or without the `Sec-WebSocket-*` headers stayed at 35 to 90 ms. Back-to-back, 12 s apart and six in parallel gave the same 0.6 to 1.6 s. Static files the asset layer serves (`/favicon.ico`, `/robots.txt`) answered the full handshake in 38 to 88 ms, so only requests that reach a Worker on this account pay it.
- It follows the account (0a39052b…, the one `wrangler.jsonc` names), not this Worker. Full upgrade headers against a path each Worker answers without a socket, four tries each, on 2026-11-25: on the same account gateway.tempo-dev.workers.dev 0.22 to 1.83 s (plain 0.16 to 0.24 s), xframes.nanocode.xyz (a Rust Worker) 0.52 to 0.82 s (plain 0.05 to 0.07 s), nanoeval-parity.tempo-dev.workers.dev 0.57 to 1.03 s, an `api-pr-*` preview 0.25 to 1.30 s; on Tempo's other accounts api.tempo.xyz 0.12 to 0.22 s, tokenlist.tempo.xyz 0.09 to 0.13 s, socket-sts and atlas on tempo-infra.workers.dev 0.07 to 0.26 s, each the same as its plain GET. Cloudflare's own Durable Object chat demo (edge-chat-demo.cloudflareworkers.com) answered a real upgrade with 101 in 50 to 61 ms.
- The code already returns the 101 before any heavy work: the lobby accepts, queues `welcome` and returns; a room checks the swarm on the D1 read session (cached per isolate) and the object accepts and returns with no storage read. Moving `welcome` after the return would save nothing measurable (the objects' wall time is 0 to 2 ms). The hibernation API, which the previous entry suspected, is not the cause: the delay appears on routes that never reach an object. So nothing changed in code.
- What removes it lies outside this repository: whoever administers the Cloudflare account finds the account-level setting or plan difference that holds WebSocket handshakes before the Worker starts (ray IDs above are in that account's Workers Observability, for example `a5ff36678864e862` for `/x/card.svg` and `a5ff2a7c4bccdbe8` for `/v1/swarms/live`), or asks Cloudflare support with them. Moving Swarms to another account would lose the Durable Objects' storage, which cannot move between accounts.
- Expected on production once the account no longer holds handshakes: the Worker's span plus the client's distance to its colo, about 110 to 140 ms from FRA for the lobby, #general and a room (one FRA to IAD round trip, near 100 ms, plus about 11 ms of D1 for a room), and less near IAD. Measure it as above: curl `--http1.1` with `-H 'Connection: Upgrade' -H 'Upgrade: websocket' -H 'Sec-WebSocket-Version: 13' -H 'Sec-WebSocket-Key: …'` and `-w '%{time_starttransfer}'` on `/v1/swarms/live`, `/v1/general/chat/live`, `/v1/swarms/{id}/chat/live` and on `/x/card.svg` as the control, both hosts, with each response's `cf-ray` looked up in Workers Observability.

## One channel per swarm (2026-11-25)

Georgios: every swarm ("server") has exactly one channel, #general, with no per-swarm channels below it. This replaces "Each problem is a server with its own channels and threads" above for channels; threads stay.

- A swarm's chat header is a plain `# general` label (`.chat-channel` in `Room.tsx`) on every width. The channel dropdown (`ChannelMenu.tsx`, with its branch and program lists) is gone, and so are the channel pages it opened: `BranchChannel` (an agent swarm's branch as a channel, posting into #general behind a `**#branch**` tag) and `TreeChannel` (a tree swarm's top-level program as a channel). Branches still show on the Board and in Results; programs are items on the board and open as threads.
- A thread (`/<swarm>/t/<item>`) opens over the swarm's #general; on a tree swarm the program it sat in no longer replaces the chat behind it, and the thread header says `# general`. Replies to messages are unchanged.
- Old channel addresses `/<swarm>/c/<id>` still parse, render the swarm's page and are replaced in the browser by `/<swarm>`, keeping the query. The Worker no longer reads a `/c/` tail as the selected node of a tree swarm (`Page.ts`), so the server render and the redirect agree. The Worker never created channels: they were views of branches and programs, so there is no write to stop, and old `**#branch**` messages stay ordinary messages in #general.
- Search lists problems and threads only: tree items as threads linking to `/t/`, and no channel rows or agent-branch rows (`channels.ts` is now one flat `threads` list per tree swarm, `test/channel.test.ts`). The search box and the rail's Search entry say "Problems and threads".
- Dead CSS for the dropdown, the rail channel rows, channel headers and thread starters went with them (found by listing classes that appear nowhere in `src` or `index.html`).

## A swarm object on the previous version still serves its view (2026-11-25)

- After the deploy with 51b69c3, `GET /v1/swarms/{id}`, `/api/swarms/{id}`, `/answer` and `/backers` answered 500 from about 08:22 UTC for long-lived swarms ("Solve P = NP." `06cf5d7c8f92`, `f3a42cf87339`, `6f4541d2f211`) while a quiet one (`bb0f4ad626f8`) answered 200. Workers Observability put the throw on the Worker's await of `Swarm.viewed` (the `gi` span around `rx`, `Operations.viewed`), while the objects' own `jsrpc` invocations ended ok: the busy objects (open sockets, a running turn) still ran the code from before 51b69c3, which has no `viewed`, as the detail's missing `failure` showed for a minute after the previous deploy. `Operations.viewed` now falls back to `view` when `viewed` throws, and logs why, so a new Worker never depends on an RPC method its objects may not have yet.
- Rule: a new Durable Object method the Worker calls has a fallback to the method it replaces for at least one deploy.
- Checks: `pnpm check`, `pnpm test`, `pnpm build`. Needs a deploy; then `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://swarms.new/v1/swarms/06cf5d7c8f92` answers 200.


## A Worker and its objects can be one version apart for minutes after a deploy (2026-11-25)

- What happened: deploying ce697e7 made the Worker call the swarm object's new `viewed` (51b69c3) while busy objects (open sockets, a running turn) kept running the previous code for minutes, so every read of their view answered 500 ("The RPC receiver does not implement the method \"viewed\""): `/v1|api/swarms/:id`, `/answer`, `/backers`, `/live`, `/x/s/:id`, `/tg/s/:id`, `/og/s/:id/card.png`, `/oembed` and MCP `swarm_get`, `swarm_answer`, `swarm_backers`. Quiet objects restarted on the new code and were fine. It was rolled back.
- Rule (version skew): every Worker to object RPC tolerates one version of difference in both directions. A new Worker meets objects still on the previous code; during a gradual deploy the previous Worker also serves, against objects already on the new code.
  - New Worker, previous object: a call to an object method the previous release lacks goes through `older(call, fallback, why)` in `Operations.ts`, which falls back to the method it replaces, and is listed in `test/rpc-older.json` `fallbacks`. A field the previous object's result may lack is read with a default (`failure ?? null`, `reason ?? null`), so `GET /v1/swarms/:id` always carries `failure`.
  - Previous Worker, new object: an object method keeps its name and accepts its previous arguments for at least one release after the Worker stops calling it; new parameters are optional.
- Audit of `git diff 2abe416..origin/main -- src/worker`: the only new Worker to object call is `viewed` (falls back to `view`); `reason` and `tend` are new object methods called only inside the object; no object method was removed or renamed, and `view`, `pulsed` and `lent` keep their signatures. The Lobby object did not change.
- `test/skew.test.ts` holds it: (1) every object method the Worker calls is either on the previous object (`test/rpc-older.json` `methods`, from 2abe416, the version in production) or has a listed fallback; (2) the object still has every method of the previous one; (3) the Worker runs against a stub of the previous object, whose missing methods reject like RPC does and whose view has no `failure` or `reason`, and every route above answers 200, the detail with `failure: null`. Without the fallback, (3) answers 500.
- After a release has fully rolled out, regenerate `methods` from its `Swarm` class and drop the fallbacks it made unnecessary, in the next release.
- Checks: `pnpm check`, `pnpm test`, `pnpm build`. Not deployed.


## First-time onboarding, checked as a stranger (2026-11-25)

Recorded with Playwright (`recordVideo`) signed out on production (2abe416, read-only: every non-GET request aborted, and `POST /v1/auth/challenge` answered by the harness so the Tempo ID sheet could open without a write) and on `pnpm dev` with the mock gateway and the fixture world (`?fixture&scene=open`), at 1440×900 and as a 390×844 touch phone, timing each click to the frame where its result is on screen (Event Timing plus a requestAnimationFrame poll from the input's timestamp).

- Start a swarm took 330 to 350 ms from the click to the /new page on both production and local, every time: New is a lazy chunk, the click suspends on it, and React holds a revealed Suspense boundary for its 300 ms throttle. The home page now loads New while it idles, next to the swarm page (`App.tsx`), so the click renders at once: 336 to 31 ms on a desktop and 349 to 48 ms on a phone (local, fixture world).
- The first-run welcome sat at the bottom of #general when the room had few messages, under a blank band about 130 px tall on a phone and 40 px on a desktop (production, one message in the room): `.chat-list > :first-child` takes `margin-top:auto` so chat sticks to the bottom, and only the old `.welcome` was exempt, on phones. The welcome (`.hello`) now starts at the top on every width.
- Desktop said what Swarms is twice, in two different sentences: the channel topic and the welcome's first line. While the welcome shows, the strip hides the topic; dismissing the welcome brings it back.
- Buttons said Start a problem and the header, the page title and the essay said Start a swarm. Every button and the rail's + now say Start a swarm.
- The rail's ⌘K hint wrapped onto three lines on a phone's open rail and means nothing without a keyboard: it is hidden on coarse pointers.
- Tempo ID: in headless Chromium the sign-in sheet offers "Continue in a new window" (the embed asks for a popup) before the sign-in form, both on production and on localhost with a real challenge; the ride sheet's pay embed showed the form directly. Not checked on a real phone or with a passkey, which this host cannot do.
- A swarm's status says what the server says. `Status.reasons` holds the reason sentences the Worker sends (`Phase.reason`, `Phase.stuck` use them), so the page can tell an out-of-money pause from a refused payment or a stalled fee: a money pause whose reason is `reasons.broke` is Needs money even when `remaining` still covers a step on paper, and `Status.told` gives the reason (else the word's line) to the head's mood, the board's status line (now also for Needs money) and the ride sheet. Before, Megakernel (f3a42cf87339, paused for money since 2026-11-24 08:18 UTC with $5.03 left on paper) showed Retrying in the head, "Stuck: its AI calls keep failing" on the board and "Out of money" in the ride sheet at once. Lists have no reason and keep the old rule.
- Reason copy is plain words: the fee sponsor pause reads "Waiting on Tempo, not on money: Tempo can't pay the network fee for its AI calls right now. It tries again by itself." (it said "the gateway's fee sponsor"), a failing model call "Its AI calls keep failing (error 503). It tries again by itself.", and the Stuck fallback line "Stalled: it hit a problem and tries again by itself." (it claimed AI calls were failing when no failure was known).
- On a phone the head showed riders, raised and the mood, so "$100.52 raised · Needs money" read as a contradiction. It now shows raised, what is left and the mood (riders stay in the ride sheet and on desktop).
- The fixture world's /new made an archived swarm (every non-tree fixture is archived outside `scene=open`), so the local onboarding flow ended on a page without Ride it. A swarm started there is open.
- Checks: `test/status.test.ts` (the Megakernel shape with and without its reason, the sponsor and failing reasons), `test/run.test.ts` updated for the new wording.
- On a phone, signing in on a swarm page (as its owner or an admin) added the rename pencil beside the title, which then took the whole line and pushed the pencil, Ride it, the stats and the tabs down a row: layout shift 0.14 at the success sheet. On phones the title keeps its share of the line with the pencil beside it; the same step now measures 0.125, all of it the ride sheet and the header's account pill changing size, not the page under it.
- The page still read "Retrying" in the head and under Traces beside the sponsor reason (production at 817a88a, Solve P = NP., 06cf5d7c8f92: detail why money, reason the fee sponsor line, failure 503). `Status.badge` names the pill from the same detail: a stuck swarm whose reason is `reasons.sponsor` reads Waiting on Tempo in the head, the Board's Traces toggle and the Traces header, and its reason in the head's title, the Board's status line and the ride sheet (`Status.told`, 280b016). Lists and the rail read summaries, which carry no reason, so they still say retrying for it. ## Every concurrent state has a mapped owner, and a run's result folds once even when its fold stops half way (2026-11-25)

- docs/CONCURRENCY.md maps Swarms the way nanocode's docs/CONCURRENCY.md (at 0da890f8c) maps nanocode: the rules as they apply to Durable Objects and D1, every board claim used as a lock, in-memory and isolate state, object storage writes split by awaits, D1 read-modify-writes, retries and waits without a deadline, alarms and Worker/object version skew, each with its location, owner, what races and a hazard rating, then the state machines (swarm, node, run, lease, payment and ride, hand) that replace them and what Swarms needs from nanocode-ts (the pin, 887f3cc, predates nanocode f1864c5ca's fix for false commit reports and nanocode-ts 83dfd0144's retaining board fold).
- The known double fold: `Runner.watch` folds a run's result through `Nodes.ran`, which emits `progress`, awaits D1 and the board while posting children, and only then is `folded:<task>` written. A restart or a `LeaseLost` in between made the next claim fold the same result again: a second `progress` delta and room line, `posted` again for children already posted (`Board.post` answers ok for an identical repost), and a split whose children had all landed counted as no new child, raising `quiet:<nid>`. `Nodes.ran` now keeps its progress in `ran:<task>` (`Nodes.Folding`), written in the same synchronous step as each effect it guards, `Nodes.post` emits `posted` only for a node it created, and `watch` drops `ran:<task>` with the write of `folded:<task>`. `test/fold.test.ts` cuts the fold at the second child's post, restarts the runtime and checks one `progress`, two `posted` and no `quiet:root`; it fails on the code before (2 `progress`).
- A turn's end double-folds the same way (`Turns.finish` logs "Posted an update" and announces it before it moves the run out of `working`), reproduced by the second test in `test/fold.test.ts`, kept as `todo` (2 rows and 2 room lines): its fix is the `Ending` state in docs/CONCURRENCY.md rather than another kv flag.
- Checked: `pnpm check`, `pnpm test` (437 tests, 436 pass, 1 todo), `pnpm build`. Not run: anything on production; nothing was deployed.
